# marvin — Working Journal

Running log of planning, decisions, open questions, and work-in-progress for marvin. Newest entries at the top. For *what marvin is* (purpose, subsystems, interfaces, milestones), read [`spec.md`](spec.md) — this journal does not duplicate it.

---

**2026-08-08 — the STRUM BAR chip finally lights up, and it needed no new asset: the colour was already in the scheme, unreachable.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- **The lit colour existed all along.** `SCHEME_PILL_ZINC_800` carries base `#27272A` (zinc-800) *and* background `#00B6DE` (cyan-500 quantized) — exactly the mockup's `bg-zinc-800` → `bg-cyan-500` pair from `PlayerPerformance.tsx`. But it was applied to a **pill**, and `leWidget_SkinClassic_DrawStandardBackground` only ever fills from `base`; only the *button* skin swaps base↔background on pressed. So the chip could never show cyan, and the old code compensated by swapping between two schemes whose bases are both `#27272A` — changing nothing but the caption colour. Checked before assuming a new scheme was needed; none was, so no design-zip edit and no Generate for this.
- **Now the same idiom as the frets**, whose comment already spelled it out ("idle colour in BASE and its lit colour in BACKGROUND"): a toggleable `add_button` with `LE_WIDGET_IGNOREEVENTS`, driven by `setPressed(strum)` + explicit invalidate. Two indicators on one card that behave identically now share one mechanism instead of two.
- Caption dropped (Greg's call) and width 45 → **68** (`STRUM_W`, 1.5×). The colour *is* the state, so IDLE/ACTIVE text was asking the eye to read where a wider bare chip is seen at a glance. `s_cap_strum` and its two design strings are no longer referenced; the strings stay in the design (they cost nothing and a caption may come back).
- `s_strum_pill` (a `leWidget *`) became `s_strum_chip` (a `leButtonWidget *`) — worth the rename because the type genuinely changed, and the alternative was casting back and forth at every use.
- **Widening it broke its alignment, because the old position was only right-aligned by coincidence.** The literal `x = 198` was flush with the metric column solely because `198 + 45 == COL_X + COL_W` (243) — so growing the chip to 68 pushed it 23px past the card's content edge. Now derived as `COL_X + COL_W - STRUM_W`, which makes the width the only thing to tune and puts it on the same edge the state pill already uses (`STATE_X + STATE_W == 243` too). The general shape: **a literal coordinate that happens to satisfy a constraint records the answer, not the constraint**, and the next size change silently breaks it — the same failure as the pixel column table and the advance-vs-ink gutter.

---

**2026-08-08 — perf-log capture types for 2-player gameplay: the note bands are now tagged per highway, and the region stream grew from one slot to three. NOT YET ON HARDWARE.**

- **The bands were already 2-player-correct and completely unlabelled.** `CV_MARVIN_CFG_2P_LEFT` has held the left-highway rects since 2026-07-14, and `game_controller` swaps to it on `GP_SCREEN_in_song_2p` (`cvcfg 2pl` forces it by hand) — but both configs emitted `PERF_STRIP_SENSING`/`STRIKE`, so **a capture could not say which highway it was reading**. Fixed by carrying the kind in the geometry config (`sensing_kind`/`strike_kind` on `cv_marvin_v1_config_t`): 1p keeps 0/1, 2p-left ships new `SENSING_2P`(5)/`STRIKE_2P`(6). No new gating, no producer logic — the rects and the switch already existed; only the tag was missing.
  - **This also fixes a latent viewer bug rather than just adding a label.** `app.js` takes the *first* record of a kind as that kind's canonical `(x,y,w,h)` and positions the card from it, so a mid-capture 1p→2p swap drew the left-highway pixels at the 1p position, silently. One kind per geometry makes that assumption true again, and both pairs can be shown in one session.
- **Two-player replaces one scoreboard with two, and there was exactly one region slot — occupied.** `PERF_CMD_REGION_STREAM` drove a single `s_region_*` rect (the 1p scoring block), so the two amp panels could not both stream. Now `PERF_REGION_SLOTS` (3) independent slots, each with its own enable + rect, each fixed to a kind: 0 → `REGION`, 1 → `SCORE_2P_LEFT`(7), 2 → `SCORE_2P_RIGHT`(8). Defaults mirror gameplay's `AMP2P_BLOCK` — `(128,164,68,78)` / `(515,164,68,78)`.
  - **Two records, not one stitched 136×78.** Both sides fit a single strip (31.8 kB < the 65,000 B cap) and stitching would halve the record count, but a strip carries one `(x,y)` — the viewer would glue the right panel 387 px from where it belongs, and `amp2p.py` reads per side anyway. Separate kinds also mean either side can be dropped alone.
- **No schema bump, and the command didn't grow.** A new strip kind is forward-compatible by the policy in `perf_log_records.h`, and `slot` took over the command's spare `reserved` byte — still 14 bytes, and `slot 0` keeps its old meaning, so the payload layout is unchanged. Host mirror (`records.py`) tracks it with a `REGION_SLOTS` table that the CLI, the web API and the front-end labels all read from.
- **No rate control — Greg's call, and the reasoning is worth keeping.** A divisor byte would have let the scoreboards run at 10-15 Hz (plenty for digit OCR) while a band stream kept 60 Hz. Not needed, because **bands and scoreboards will be captured in separate sessions**; over-subscription drops at the strip pool and is already counted in `DROP.dropped_strip`. Per-frame cost if you ignore that: bands 14.9 + 20.9 kB, scoreboards 15.9 + 15.9 kB — 2.1 MB/s and 1.9 MB/s at 60 fps respectively, against the ~3.9 MB/s the wire sustains. Either alone is comfortable; both together is not.
- **`PL_STRIP_QUEUE_DEPTH` 4 → 6** (now derived as `2 + PERF_REGION_SLOTS + 1` so it can't fall behind the slot count again). Worst case rose to 5 strips per frame against a 4-deep queue; each pool slot is ~65 KB of DDR BSS, so the insurance costs ~130 KB of 240 MB.
- **Found on the way: the offline viewer has never been able to render the score region.** The front-end registry called kind 3 `"score"` while the PNG route matches `Strip.kind_name` (`"region"`), and a loaded capture's records carry no inline pixels — so every fetch 404'd, invisibly (live mode works, it paints from `bgr_b64`). Aligned the id, and the invariant `registry id == kind_name` is now a test that builds a capture containing **every** registry kind and asserts the route serves each one.
- **Also found: the viewer served stale JS, and had done all along.** Greg restarted the server and refreshed and the new checkboxes weren't there. Not a UI bug — Starlette's `StaticFiles` sends `ETag`/`Last-Modified` but **no `Cache-Control`**, so the browser is entitled to reuse `app.js` on *heuristic* freshness without asking, and `index.html` has no cache-busting query. A front-end edit could therefore survive both a reload and a server restart, silently, which makes every future UI change untrustworthy. Ruled out the server first (one `app.js` on disk, editable install, served bytes contained the new labels, top-level eval clean under a stub DOM) before touching anything. Fixed with `Cache-Control: no-cache` on the static mount and the index route — *revalidate*, not don't-store, so the ETag still answers 304 and the cost is one conditional request per load. Capture pixels keep their `immutable` long cache: they're content-addressed by (capture, epoch, kind). Both halves are now asserted in `test_api.py`. **A stale copy already in the browser needs one hard reload to break out** — the new header can't invalidate what the browser won't re-ask for.
- Files: `perf_log/{perf_log_records.h,perf_log.c,perf_log.h,perf_log_rx.c}`, `detector/cv_marvin_v1.{c,h}`; host `tools/marvin-perf` (`records.py`, `cli.py`, `web/{live,api,server}.py`, `web/static/app.js`, README). No MCC/Legato. 169 host tests pass; verified end-to-end on a synthetic capture (all four kinds decode, extract to per-side PNGs, and serve over the offline route). **Pending Greg's MPLAB build**, then: `cvcfg 2pl` shows both band pairs positioned correctly in one session, the two `2P SC` toggles render the amp panels ~387 px apart, and `dropped_strip` stays at zero for whichever combination is actually used.

**2026-08-08 — STOP gets a real icon and the disabled SELECT SONG gets its own scheme. Two design-zip edits; NEEDS AN MGS GENERATE before it builds.**

- **STOP had no symbol because no asset existed** — the image set was `BUTTON_FACE_START` / `SELECT_SONG` / `CHECK` / `CLOSE` / `HAMBURGER` plus nav icons, so `run_state_show` had nothing to show and set the image to `NULL`. START's symbol is an **image**, not a font glyph: 14×14 RGBA, and decoding its alpha confirms a **solid** triangle in pure white — the `fill-current` trap the 2026-08-05 entry documented for `play.svg`.
- **The mockup settled the two open questions.** `NowPlaying.tsx` renders `<Square className="w-3 h-3 fill-current" />` against `<Play className="w-3.5 h-3.5 fill-current" />` — so STOP is **12px, not 14**, deliberately, because a filled square reads heavier than a triangle of the same bounds (69% coverage vs 30% even at the smaller size). Greg's instinct that it needed filling was right; lucide ships `square.svg` as `fill="none"`.
- **Icon colour is baked, so it follows the caption not the scheme:** `#FFCAC5`, BUTTON_EXPERT's `text`. Worth noting the firmware's STOP diverges from the mockup here (mockup is `bg-zinc-800 text-zinc-400`); left alone as a pre-existing decision, but it means recolouring either button now means re-rendering its icon too.
- **`SCHEME_BUTTON_DISABLED` added** because no stock scheme was dark enough in both slots: `TOGGLE_OFF` was the closest and Greg still read it as too bright on button *and* text. Cloned from `SCHEME_BUTTON_MODE` with base `#1F1F23` (between the zinc-900 card and the enabled `#292829`, so the button recedes without vanishing) and text `#52525B` (vs enabled `#9C9EAD`). `background` set to `#27272A` deliberately per the skill's button rule, even though a disabled button is never pressed.
- **Composer was open and silently reverted both edits — and the scripts still reported success.** `add_image.py` printed `manifest 26 -> 27 / applied` and `add_scheme.py` `verified: 74 -> 75`, while the zip on disk held 26 and 74. `audit_refs.py`'s asset counts caught it, because they are read from the zip as it exists rather than from what the script built. Recovery was free: all 145 members were byte-identical to HEAD (Composer had only re-serialized the container, which is why `git status` said modified while the *size* matched HEAD exactly), so `git checkout --` lost nothing. **Skill updated** with the "applied is not evidence" rule, the audit-counts tell, and the diff-members-not-the-file recovery check.
- Not done: a dimmed `SELECT_SONG` icon. The face image is still dropped while disabled, which is right for now — it is a fixed `#D4D4D8` bitmap that would otherwise be the brightest thing on a card where everything else just receded.

**2026-08-08 — START/STOP latched in STOP after a song because the dashboard feed drops events, and status is an edge not a sample. Select-song is now gated during a run.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- **`DashboardFeed_PostStatus` went through a drop-on-full queue.** `post()` is `xQueueSend(..., 0)` on a 16-deep queue drained by a **priority-2** task, while the controller (priority 4) posts up to four telemetry events per 300 ms poll during gameplay. A full queue is ordinary there, not exceptional.
- **Drop-on-full is right for telemetry and wrong for status, and that is the whole bug.** Playtime/score/streak are *samples* — a lost one is superseded 300 ms later. A status is an *edge*: `finish("READY")` is the last status of the run, so dropping it left the dashboard holding `s_run_active = true` with nothing ever to correct it, and the button stayed STOP until the next run. Status now also goes through a **depth-1 `xQueueOverwrite` mailbox** the consumer reads after draining the shared queue — cannot be crowded out by telemetry, still wait-free (so the header's actuation-path contract holds), and the queue copy avoids tearing the 24-byte string. The classification (edge vs sample) is now written into `dashboard_feed.h` so the next event type gets the same question asked of it.
- **First diagnosis was wrong and worth recording.** `finish()` published the status *before* clearing `s_busy`, and observers read `GameController_IsBusy()` when handling it — an obvious race. But the controller is priority 4 against the feed's 2, so it is never preempted by the queue send and `s_busy = false` always lands first. Real ordering hazard, not this bug. **Fixed anyway** (clear then publish) and every terminal path now routes through `finish()` — `ensure_wii_connected` reports its reason through a `const char **` instead of publishing "NO WII"/"PAIR WII" itself, which had the same publish-while-busy shape. Checking the priorities is what killed the plausible-but-wrong answer.
- **SELECT SONG is gated while a run is in flight** (`run_state_show`), since the selection is an input to the run. **Clearing `LE_WIDGET_ENABLED` alone would have been a trap:** Legato's button paint has *no* disabled styling — the flag only gates picking — so it would have looked live and silently done nothing. It also takes a muted scheme and drops its face image, mirroring what START already does.
- **The disabled scheme has to be picked by its `LE_SCHM_TEXT` colour, not by its name.** The button skin draws the caption from that field, and the first pick — `SCHEME_NAV_BUTTON_UNSELECTED`, which *sounds* like an inactive button — has text `#D4D4D8` (luma 212) against `SCHEME_BUTTON_MODE`'s `#9C9EAD` (158): choosing it by name made the disabled caption **brighter** than the enabled one. Greg caught it. Now `SCHEME_TOGGLE_OFF`: text `#71717B` (luma 113) so the caption visibly dims, and base `#27272A` close enough to `#292829` that only the text moves. Measured the RGB_888 rows out of `le_gen_scheme.c` rather than trusting the names. No new scheme, because that means an MGS design-zip round-trip plus a manual Generate; worth doing properly if a real disabled scheme is wanted.
- The dashboard is the only caller of `UiManager_OpenSongSelect`, so gating this one button really does close the path — no nav-drawer route to check.

**2026-08-08 — axis gutter 26 → 30px: the tick labels were clipped because the earlier fit check measured *advance* instead of *ink*.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- Greg saw the `k` in `1.2k` losing a few pixels. **The two entries below both claim "4 chars = 20px in a 22px gutter → OK"; that check was wrong** — it summed glyph *advances*, which is not how wide a string paints.
- In `DejaVuSansMono_9`, `'k'` has bearingX 1 + width 5 = **ink right edge 6 against a 5px advance**, and `'%'` is 0 + 6 = 6. Both overhang by a pixel. So `1.2k` sums to 20px of advance but inks **21px**, and right-aligned in a 22px label the tail falls outside. `100%` on the utilization axis was clipping identically — same bug, both charts, one shared constant.
- **The lesson is the one the activity-log columns already taught, in a subtler form:** measuring the font was right, measuring the *wrong metric* still put a hard-coded number in the layout. Advance is where the next glyph starts; `bearingX + width` is how far the ink reaches. For a right-aligned label the second is what has to fit.
- `AXIS_W` is now 30 (labels 26px, 5px slack over the 21px worst case), and the comment records ink-not-advance so the next person doesn't re-derive it from advances. Knock-on checked with real metrics: the bar slot goes 51 → **50px**, and the longest node name "Lightshow" inks **46px**, so the labels still fit; the sparkline just loses 4px of width.

---

**2026-08-08 (later still) — the utilization history chart autoscales its ceiling with zero pinned, and the mockup turned out to be no authority on this.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- **What the mockup actually does** (`~/Downloads/Marvin/src/app/components/screens/BusStatsScreen.tsx`): the utilization chart is `<YAxis domain={[0, 60]}>` — hardcoded — while the TX chart is a bare `<YAxis>` with **no domain**, i.e. Recharts autoscales it. So the TX chart was always meant to autoscale (the earlier change matches its intent), and the 0..60 utilization domain was chosen to flatter the mock generator `25 + Math.random()*18` = 25–43%. It sits in the middle third of that domain *because the fake data was five times our real figure*. Nothing to defer to; our sim (`250 + rnd()%190` permille) had faithfully copied the fake data's range.
- **Why utilization needed different treatment from a rate:** a percentage has an absolute reference — 100% is saturated — so a fully autoscaled axis can imply a busy bus where there is none. A frames/s axis has no such reference. Greg named this exactly ("don't want to think 2% usage is 100%").
- **The widget's existing autoscale is the wrong kind here.** `Sparkline_SetAutoscale` fits the plotted window's own **min..max**, so the bottom edge is not zero and a 5.9→6.4% wiggle becomes a mountain range. `min_span` widens the window but never anchors it.
- **So: fixed mode with a moving ceiling.** Added `Sparkline_SetScale` (ceiling only; zero stays pinned, since fixed mode plots 0..scale) and `Sparkline_PlotMax`. Floored at **10%**, which is what bounds the magnification: at 6.1% the trace sits at 61% height, honest and readable, and a fraction of a percent cannot be blown up to fill the card.
- **`Sparkline_PlotMax` rather than the newest sample, and that distinction is load-bearing.** The chart shows 40 samples of history, so the ceiling has to cover every sample still on screen; scaling to the latest value would clip older, taller history flat against the top edge as soon as the trace dropped.
- **A separate ladder from the TX chart's**, because the tick text is a percentage: every rung is a multiple of 20 permille (`{10,20,30,40,60,80,100}%`) so the midpoint is a whole percent. `nice_ceiling` would have been free to pick 250 permille, whose midpoint is 12.5% — five glyphs in a four-glyph gutter. Widest label is now `100%`, 20px of 22.
- Damped with the same constants as the TX axis; 19.5↔20.5% boundary flutter relabels the axis **once in 20 refreshes**. Also took the mockup's `(msg/s)` unit into the TX chart title, since that axis now shows bare numbers.

---

**2026-08-08 (later) — the TX axis scale is now damped asymmetrically, and the ladder was refined to track max closely.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- **Greg's ask: instant up, damped down, and the scale should still closely track the actual max.** Those two pull against each other — close tracking means a fine ladder, and a fine ladder means more boundaries for a fluttering max to cross.
- **The coarse ladder had to go first.** 1-2-2.5-5-10 overshoots by up to **2×** (1001 scaled against 2000, half the plot empty), which is the opposite of tracking max. Rungs are now `{1, 1.2, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10}` held in tenths: overshoot is ~1.33× worst case and usually ~1.2×. Odd rungs still get skipped (1.5×10 = 15 would label its midpoint 7 against a gridline at 7.5).
- **The damper is a held peak, not a filter on the displayed value:** take a new max immediately (a burst is never clipped), hold ~2 s, then shed half the remaining gap per refresh. `step` is forced to at least 1 — integer division of a gap below `DECAY_DIV` yields 0, which would park the scale above a quiet bus forever.
- **Flutter protection turns out to come entirely from the hold, not the decay rate.** Simulated 2% flutter across the 1000 boundary (985↔1010, the worst case): because rising is instant, every spike re-arms the hold, so the decay branch never runs and the axis changes **once in 40 refreshes** — against an undamped scale that flips 1000↔1200 *every* refresh. A one-refresh dropout to 0 moves the axis not at all. That decoupling is what allowed tuning the decay purely for settle time.
- **So the decay rate was chosen on settle behaviour alone.** Burst to 900 then idle at 20: quarter-gap crawls down 16 steps over 14 s; half-gap settles in **7 s over 9 steps** (1000 → 500 → 250 → 150 → 80 → 50 → 40 → 30 → 20), which reads as settling rather than drifting. Picked half-gap.
- Upward tracking stays tight: a climb through 10/50/120/240/480/900/1400 labels 10/50/120/250/500/1000/1500 with no lag.

**2026-08-08 — TX RATE BY NODE gained an autoscaling y axis, and the bars now scale to the labelled ceiling instead of the raw maximum.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- **The bars were already autoscaled; what was missing was any axis at all.** Heights were scaled to `max_rate`, so the chart showed which node was busiest but not *what any value was* — purely relative, with the numbers only available in the table's TX RATE column.
- **The coupling is the part that matters:** adding labels without changing the bar scaling would have drawn the tallest bar at full height against an axis claiming a round ceiling — a chart that actively lies, which is worse than one that says nothing. Both now derive from one `nice_ceiling(max_rate)`.
- **`nice_ceiling` rounds up a 1-2-2.5-5-10 ladder, with two constraints that came out of testing.** Rungs are held in *tenths* so 2.5 can be one — without it a 240/s node scales against 500 and leaves half the plot empty, and ~240/s is exactly the fretboard's stream rate (240 → 250, tallest bar 96% instead of 48%). And an **odd rung is skipped, not rounded**: 2.5×10 = 25, whose midpoint tick would label as 12 against a gridline at 12.5. Swept 0..200k exhaustively plus the rest of the uint32 range in steps — zero odd ceilings, zero ceilings below their input, zero ticks wider than the gutter.
- **Gutter arithmetic, measured not assumed:** `AXIS_W` is 26 px, labels get 22, and `DejaVuSansMono_9` advances 5 px → **4 glyphs**. `fmt_axis` is capped at 4 characters for every uint32 (`999` / `1.0k` / `20k` / `200k` / `4G`), verified across the sweep. The bar area loses those 26 px, so the slot goes 54 → 51 px; "Lightshow" is 9 chars = 45 px, so the node names still fit.
- Ticks reuse the utilization chart's exact placement, font and scheme so the two cards read as one system. `set_text` invalidates through the string callback, so the new labels need no extra damage handling.
- **Related, not done:** chart 1 is still a **fixed 0..60%** domain, and now that utilization is measured honestly (~6%) its trace will hug the floor. `Sparkline_SetAutoscale(min_span)` already exists on the widget, so it's a small change — but a history plot whose scale shifts under you needs a decay rule to not be jarring, which is a design decision rather than a wiring one.

---

**2026-08-08 — `fmt_count` gained a G step, because the ladder stopped at M and 1e9 would have clipped the tile.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- K and M were already there; **G was not**, so a `uint32_t` count past 1e9 rendered `4294.96M` — **8 characters**. Measured the fonts rather than eyeballing them (the same trap as the activity log's hard-coded pixel columns): every glyph in `DejaVuSansMonoBold_24` advances **14 px** and the KPI value label is 137 − 2×16 = **105 px**, so exactly **7 characters** fit. 8 needs 112 px → clipped by 7 px. The node table is safe either way (`DejaVuSansMono_14`, 8 px advance, 133 px column = 16 chars).
- **Reachable, not theoretical:** `tx_total`/`rx_total` are bus-wide sums, so ~12 days at the current ~930 frames/s and **under a day** on a busy bus. The baselining above resets them on marvin restart, which is what keeps it from being a routine sight.
- With the G step the widest possible output is `999.99M` = 7 chars = 98 px, so the ladder is now **width-safe by construction across the whole uint32 range** rather than by luck. Checked every boundary (0, 999, 1.0K, 999.9K, 1.00M, 999.99M, 1.00G, 4.29G) against both tile widths.
- Used **G** rather than B: a bus screen already shows byte rates, and "4.29B" would read as bytes.

---

**2026-08-08 — the byte rate now includes preamble/SFD, so `util = bytes/s × 8 / 10 Mbit` reconciles by hand.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- **Greg read 68.8 kB/s next to 6.1% and couldn't square them.** He was right to try: 68.8k × 8 / 10 Mbit is 5.5%, not 6.1%. The 0.6-point gap was the **8-byte preamble + SFD charged per frame** — the MAC generates it, so it appears in no frame length, and `util_permille` included it while the displayed byte rate did not. At ~930 frames/s that is ~8 kB/s of pure framing.
- **The tile said "wire" and wasn't**, which is the actual defect — a figure that can't be checked against the one beside it invites exactly this question every time someone looks. Fixed by counting preamble into the byte rate rather than by explaining the discrepancy in a caption.
- **Both now derive from one clamped `wire_bt`**, so the identity holds exactly at every traffic level rather than approximately: verified 0.0% / 6.0% / 43.0% / clamped-100% (where the byte rate lands on exactly 1,250,000 B/s = 10 Mbit/s), and across a 10 s window. `T1S_PREAMBLE_LEN` (8 B) is now the single source and `T1S_BT_PREAMBLE` derives from it, so the two uses can't drift.
- **The simulator was already consistent** (`permille × 1250`), which made this worse, not better: the mismatch appeared only on live data, so sim mode would have hidden it from anyone checking the arithmetic.

---

**2026-08-08 — marvin 40 tx/s against fauxmote 20 rx/s is correct: half of marvin's TX is addressed to a node that isn't on the bench. No code change (Greg's call).**

- **Follower RX is already own-MAC + broadcast only, and it's enforced in hardware, not software.** All six followers pass `promiscuous = false` to `TC6Regs_Init` (fauxmote `mf_t1s.c`, plus guitar / lemmy / lightshow / fretboard `t1s_follower.c`/`t1s_detector.c`), so the LAN8651 address checker never hands another node's frame to `TC6_CB_OnRxEthernetPacket`. A follower *cannot* over-count. **marvin is the only promiscuous node**, deliberately — that is what lets it measure the whole segment (see the entry below).
- **The gap is two independent 50 ms floor-refresh loops, each 20 frames/s**, going to different destinations: `FX_REFRESH_MS` (`net/fauxmote/fauxmote_link.c`) → fauxmote id 1 on `0x88B7`, which fauxmote counts and reports as its 20/s; and `FBL_HEARTBEAT_MS` (`actuator/fretboard_link.c`) → the **guitar node id 3** on `0x88B5`, which isn't on the test bus, so nobody receives or counts it. 40 = 20 + 20, exactly.
- **Both loops re-send unconditionally on timeout, and `node_for_type()` is a pure static-table lookup with no presence check** — so marvin transmits to an absent node indefinitely, ~20 PLCA transmit opportunities per second at present.
- **Decision: leave it.** Considered gating sends on presence (frees the TOs, makes the totals reconcile) and rejected it — presence is a 2 s heartbeat timeout, and withholding a button *release* from a guitar that has gone heartbeat-quiet while still alive is a worse failure than transmitting into the void. Also considered counting TX-to-absent-nodes as a separate figure so the screen explains its own gap; not worth the surface area now that the arithmetic is written down here.

---

**2026-08-08 — node totals now baseline on first heartbeat, so the bus view means "since marvin came up"; `t1s reset` re-zeroes on demand.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- **Greg reloaded marvin and TOTAL TX/RX immediately read ~100k.** Not a counter bug: a follower reports *its own lifetime* totals in the v2 heartbeat, and marvin was displaying them raw. Those counts outlive marvin restarting, so the screen paired a five-second UPTIME with an hour of fauxmote's traffic. The two tiles were answering different questions.
- **Fix is a per-node baseline, not a reset.** The first heartbeat after boot captures `base_tx/rx/crc/sym`; every later report is shown relative to it. Marvin's own counters already zeroed at boot, so this makes the whole view consistently session-relative and coherent with UPTIME.
- **A node restarting is the case that breaks a naive baseline**: its totals drop below the stored base, and the subtraction would wrap to ~4 billion. Totals going backwards is now the restart signal — recapture the baseline, and reset `prev_tx/rx` at the same time or the next interval reports the whole jump from zero as one second's traffic. `sub_base()` saturates as a second line of defence for crc/sym, whose narrower counters can move independently.
- **`t1s reset` (console) zeroes marvin's counters and re-arms every node baseline** — each node re-zeroes on its next heartbeat, so the window starts at the command rather than at each node's boot. **Uptime restarts with it**, since it is the window the totals cover; leaving it would recreate exactly the incoherence this entry is about. **Presence deliberately survives** — this forgets traffic, not who is on the bus. No lock: every field is a single aligned word, so a concurrent frame can cost the reset one count, which is not worth touching the RX hot path for.
- **No screen control for it.** The bus screen has no interactive widgets at all today (`ScreenBus_SetInput` only gates picking), and the baselining alone fixes what Greg actually hit. A touch affordance is a follow-up if it's wanted.

---

**2026-08-08 — bus utilization was ~2× wrong and blind to PLCA, and is now measured from wire bytes on marvin alone. New §9 in [`t1s-podl-link.md`](../../../docs/t1s-podl-link.md) is the durable write-up.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- **The old figure was `Σ(every node's tx_rate + rx_rate) × 64 bytes`, wrong three ways.** (a) **~2× overstated:** a frame occupies the medium once but appears in its sender's `tx` and every listener's `rx`, and marvin is promiscuous so it counts everything. Occupancy must count transmissions only. (b) **64 bytes assumed** — true for our control traffic today, and badly wrong the moment a fretboard ADC stream appears. (c) **no PLCA accounting at all**, which is the whole picture at our traffic levels.
- **Greg's instinct that marvin can compute it all was right, and better than expected.** `TC6Regs_Init(..., promiscuous=true)` means marvin's MAC copies *every* frame on the segment, and `TC6_CB_OnRxEthernetPacket` hands it an exact length **before** marvin's ethertype/source filters discard most of it. Marvin never hears its own transmissions, so `rx + tx` on marvin is the entire bus, counted exactly once, with **zero dependence on follower telemetry**. Byte counting now happens at exactly two points: the single `send_to_node` TX site and the top of the RX callback.
- **The two directions report different lengths for the same frame**, which would have been a silent ~7% error. RX `len` includes pad and FCS (`MAC_NCFGR.RFCS` defaults 0 → the MAC relays the FCS); TX passes neither (the MAC pads to 60 and appends FCS, `QTXCFG.MACFCSDIS` defaults 0). TX counts are grown to match.
- **PLCA cycle structure, from the datasheet + Microchip KBs:** BEACON 20 BT (five N symbols, 2 µs) + 16 BT short silence, then one TO per node — unused = 32 BT (`PLCA_TOTMR` default) of *full* silence, used = the transmission plus a 16 BT short silence. So an idle cycle is 20+16+8×32 = **292 BT = 29.2 µs**, a ceiling of ~34,250 cycles/s, spent whether or not anyone transmits. Notably **the 96 BT interframe gap does not apply per frame here** — PLCA separates transmissions with the short silence and the next TO, so the first draft of this math would have over-charged every frame by 12 bytes.
- **So "utilization" had to be defined, not just corrected.** It is now the share of line time carrying frames, and nothing else. That reads ~0% on today's bus *by construction* — the rest is beacon and mandated silence, not spare capacity. `to_used_permille` (transmit opportunities that carried a frame) is the honest headroom figure, because a PLCA bus saturates when a node wants to transmit more often than its TO comes around, not when the bit rate nears 10 Mbit/s.
- **Verified the arithmetic numerically before trusting it** (throwaway host harness over five traffic profiles). Idle fauxmote-only: 0.0%, 256 B/s, 34,238 cyc/s. Seven nodes + 30 cmd/s: 0.2%, 2.8 kB/s. Fretboard stream (1000 × 526 B): **43.0%** — so the gauge's 45%/70% bands become meaningful exactly when real traffic arrives. It also **caught a defect**: an oversubscribed bus clamps `frame_bt`, collapsing the derived cycle count while the frame count stays high, which produced **TO used = 228%**. Clamped. Also added a `dt_ms > 10 s` resync — bit times overflow a `uint32` past ~7 minutes and the bring-up retry loop skips `recompute_rates` entirely.
- **Display consequence: a whole-percent readout renders every real value as `0%`,** so the KPI card now shows one decimal and carries the absolute wire byte rate (`fmt_bps`, scaling B/s → kB/s → MB/s) — that is the figure that actually moves on an idle bus. The console `t1s` command gained wire B/s, PLCA cycles/s, and TO-used. **The titlebar's T1S BUS tile is left reading 0%** — its `value_buf[4]` holds three characters, and widening it is a layout change on a tuned widget for a number whose true value is zero.
- **Deliberately not done: the hardware PLCA counters.** `CTRCTRL` (0x0020) `TOCTRE`/`BCNTRE` expose `TOCNTH/L` (transmit opportunities) and `BCNCNTH/L` (beacons), clear-on-read, which would make the cycle rate measured rather than derived and double as a PLCA-health check. Adding periodic SPI register polling to a service loop that is marginal enough on the current test rig to need its SPI clock backed off is a risk to take deliberately, not incidentally. Recorded in §9. There are **no octet counters** in the MAC statistics block (MMS1 0x288–0x293 is frame and error counts only), so software byte accumulation was always required.

---

**2026-08-08 — bus stats: fauxmote read 4 tx/s against marvin's 2 rx/s, and no frames were being lost. Marvin's self row simply wasn't counting heartbeats.** NOT YET ON HARDWARE.

- Fauxmote's 4/s is correct and is two independent 500 ms timers: the `0x88B6` heartbeat (`T1S_HB_INTERVAL_MS`, `mf_t1s.c`) and the `0x88B7` STATUS uplink (`MF_STATUS_PERIOD_MS`, `mf_link.c`). Its `s_tx_count` covers both, per the 2026-08-04 v2-heartbeat contract.
- Marvin's `rx_frame` handler `return`s out of the `0x88B6` branch after stamping presence and parsing the v2 telemetry, **before** reaching `s_rx_count++`. So the self row counted only the 2/s of STATUS. Half the arriving traffic was invisible to the counter that was, in fact, the traffic *carrying the other node's numbers* — the 4/s displayed for fauxmote got there over the frames marvin declined to count.
- **The asymmetry is the bug, not the omission.** Node rows come from each follower's own all-ethertype `rx`/`tx` totals, so the self row has to be counted the same way or the two columns can never be compared — which is the whole point of putting them side by side. Added `s_hb_rx_count`, and folded the three-channel sum into `self_tx_total()`/`self_rx_total()` so `recompute_rates`, `GetSelfStats`, and `GetBusStats` cannot drift apart again (they each had the expression inline, which is how one of them could have been fixed alone).
- Knock-on: `util_permille` understated bus load by the whole heartbeat population (7 nodes × 2/s once everything is up ≈ 14 frames/s unaccounted), and `err_rate_ppm` was dividing by that same short denominator. Both read correctly now.
- The channel-specific accessors (`T1SLink_RxCount` / `CtrlRxCount`, used by the `t1s` and controller console commands) keep their old per-channel meaning; only the aggregate changed.

---

**2026-08-08 (night, after the commit) — activity log: a draggable scrollbar, font 12 → 16, ring 500, and the column table now derives itself from the font instead of being hard-coded pixels. Scrollbar NOT YET ON HARDWARE.**

- **The scrollbar is a control, not an ornament, and that is why it went in over the simpler "arrow if there is more" idea.** At 500 entries and a 42px pitch the content is ~21,000 px tall against a 644 px viewport — **33 screenfuls**. A 1:1 drag cannot realistically reach the oldest line, so before this there was no way to get there from the panel at all. A touch anywhere on the track positions **absolutely** (thumb centred on the finger), so one gesture reaches either end. An arrow would have told you more exists without giving you any way to go and see it, and could not show how far in you are.
- Geometry, all derived rather than placed: track 8 px wide, inset `LL_PAD_X` from the right edge, 6 px clear of the message column — which gives up **one character** (87 → 86) to make room. Thumb height is the visible fraction of the content with a **28 px floor**, because 15 of 500 rows is 3% of the track and would otherwise be a 19 px sliver: 30 rows → 329 px, 60 → 164, 150 → 65, 500 → 28. Suppressed entirely when the content fits (`max_scroll() == 0`), so an empty log shows no furniture.
- **Costs no extra frame.** It is painted at the end of `ll_paint`, after the rows so a row can never overdraw it, and the widget already repaints in full on every scroll step — so this is a few hundred pixels added to a paint that was happening anyway.
- `LogList_Dragging()` already gates the screen's poll, and a bar drag sets `tracking` too, so a scroll still suppresses the refresh repaint rather than fighting it.
- **The card's bottom border and both bottom corners were being eaten by the list, and it was one cause not two.** The card spans y 76..783 with its 1px border *on* those rows and radius-4 arcs in the last 4 (780..783); the list spanned y 140..**783**. It is opaque and a later sibling, so it won everywhere it reached — flattening the border and squaring both arcs. `TABLE_H` now stops **`CARD_R` short, not 1px short**: ending one row above the border would still have squared the arcs, since they occupy the last `CARD_R` rows. 644 → 640, so the card's own zinc-900 shows for three rows below the list (invisible — the list fills with the same colour) and the border and arcs survive. Still 15 rows.
  - The general shape to watch on any screen here: **an opaque square child over a rounded bordered card must inset by the border on the sides and by the radius at the corners it touches.** The x inset (+1/-2) was already right; only the bottom was assumed.
- **Greg's two questions about entry and auto-refresh found a real bug — the list would have frozen the moment the ring filled.** Answers were "no" and "yes, until it silently stops":
  - **`LogList_SetCount` keyed everything off the row count, and the count is pinned once the ring is full.** `log_ring_count()` is `min(seq, LOG_RING_ENTRIES)`, so after 500 lines it never changes again — and `SetCount` opened with `if (n == w->count) { return; }`, skipping the invalidate. So new lines would stop appearing, **while `refresh_counters()` kept updating the header** — numbers moving over frozen rows, which reads worse than nothing. Present since the screen was written; going 100 → 500 only delayed it from ~1 minute of uptime to ~10.
  - The same count delta was also the position-hold input (`n - w->count` as "how many arrived"), so a scrolled reader would have drifted a line at a time once full: the content shifts, the delta reads zero, the offset never compensates.
  - Root cause is that the count is not the change signal — **the sequence number is**, and only the screen knows it. `LogList_SetCount(count)` is now `LogList_Prepend(count, inserted)`: it always invalidates (the caller only gets there because `seq` moved) and takes the shift explicitly. `inserted >= count` means everything being read has rolled out of the ring, so it lands back at the newest.
  - **Entry now always lands on the newest line** (`LogList_ScrollTop` from `pull_new_lines(true)`), and unconditionally — the sequence number not having moved is no reason to skip it when the scroll position still needs resetting. Holding position is right *while* the screen is up, so arriving lines do not yank what is being read, and wrong on entry: this is a "what is happening now" view, not a document to resume.
  - Both paths now go through one `pull_new_lines(bool to_top)`, so the entry and poll cases cannot drift apart. The poll still early-outs on an unmoved sequence number *before* taking the render lock, so an idle screen is still free.

- **The pixel column table was a trap waiting to spring, and bumping the font is what sprang it.** `LL_COL` held hard-coded x/width sized for `DejaVuSansMono_12`'s 7px advance, so changing the row font would have left every column mis-sized with nothing to warn about — and the screen's own headings, placed from `LogList_ColumnRect`, would have drifted from the rows they label. Columns are now declared in **characters** (`LL_CH_TIME` 12, `LL_CH_LEVEL` 5, `LL_CH_SOURCE` 11, gap 2) and resolved to pixels in `layout_columns` from the advance `char_w()` already measures. Changing `ROW_FONT` now reflows rows and headings together.
- **`DejaVuSansMono_16` (height 20, advance 10) at `ROW_H` 42.** The message column takes the remainder, so the larger text is paid for in message characters rather than in a broken layout:

  | font | advance | message column | rows |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Mono_12 (was) | 7 | 976 px = **139 ch** | 17 |
  | **Mono_16 (now)** | 10 | 874 px = **87 ch** | **15** + a 14px sliver |
  | Mono_18 | 11 | 840 px = 76 ch | 14 |

- **87 characters was chosen on evidence, not by eye.** Estimating expanded length for the tree's 189 distinct log messages (tag stripped, format specifiers widened): median **32**, p90 66, p95 72, max 121. So 87 ch shows **98.4% of message types whole** — three truncate. 139 ch was 100% and 76 ch (Mono_18) drops to 95.8%, which is why 16 rather than 18. Headings went `_9` → `_12` and the title `Bold_14` → `Bold_16` to stay in proportion.
- **Ring depth is not RAM-constrained, and the real limits are elsewhere.** Measured from the map, `log_ring.c.o` contributes **15,604 B** of `.bss` — that is 100 × **156 B** plus the 4-byte sequence counter, so an entry is 156 not the 160 the field list suggests (XC32 packs `log_level_t` to one byte with `-fshort-enums`). `.bss` sits at 6.31 MB inside the 216 MB cacheable `ram` region, i.e. **210 MB spare** — nothing like the `ram_nocache` pressure the canvases are under.

  | entries | RAM | vs today |
  |---|---|---|
  | 100 | 15.2 KB | — |
  | 500 | 76.2 KB | +61 KB |
  | 1000 | 152.3 KB | +137 KB |

  **Set to 500.** `log_ring.c.o` .bss went 15,604 → **78,004 B** = 500 × 156 + 4, exactly as predicted, and `.bss` is now 6.37 MB of 216 MB. `.region_nocache` is untouched at 36,432,480.
  - **`log dump` needed a bound to go with it**: 500 lines at ~100 chars is ~4 s of serial at 115200, and the old default was "everything held". It now prints `LOG_DUMP_DEFAULT` = 40 unless given a count, with `log dump all` for the whole ring, and the header reports `n of held` so a truncated dump is never mistaken for the whole thing.

  What actually bounds it: **`log dump` over serial** (~100 chars/line at 115200 ≈ 8.7 ms/line, so 1000 lines is 8.7 s — its default should be capped rather than "everything held"), and **scroll reachability** (1000 rows × 42 px = 42,000 px of virtual content, which no 1:1 drag will cross). `log_ring_counts` walking every entry twice a second is O(n) but immaterial at these sizes. So: pick depth on how far back you want to *read on the panel*, and raise it freely up to a few hundred.

**2026-08-08 (night, last) — System Info: a node tap cost 186 ms and now costs 59.5 ms (3.1×). It is the only repaint in marvin that is user-visible LATENCY rather than background load. Targeted damage is the default; the probe found a stall it would otherwise have introduced.**

```
marvin> system probe
  node switch (full panel) = 185715 us   6 iters
  full panel  1280x800 (1024000 px) = 174941 us
marvin> system refresh targeted
marvin> system probe
  node switch (targeted)   =  59527 us   6 iters
  full panel  1280x800 (1024000 px) = 173103 us
```

**TARGETED IS NOW THE DEFAULT: 185,715 → 59,527 µs, 3.12× on the switch and 3.59× on the paint alone.** The targeted paint is 28% of the full-panel paint, against 19–30% predicted from the damage-rect count, and 59.5 ms landed inside the predicted 44–63 ms band. Tap latency goes from ~190 ms to ~65 ms. The per-rect overheads did not eat the win.

- **Correct by construction, verified in the vendor code rather than by eye:** every mutation `show_detail` makes damages itself — `set_text` → `leFixedString_SetFromChar` (preinvalidate + invalidate), `setScheme`, `_leWidget_SetVisible` (invalidates on *either* transition, so a card being hidden damages the area it vacates), and `_leImageWidget_SetImage` (invalidates the old rect and the new). The whole-panel invalidate was carrying none of the load its comment claimed.
- **Greg's objection to the probe — "switching between detail panels doesn't tell us anything because you always have to go back to system" — is right about the scenario and it exposed a real bug.** The repaint *work* transfers, because the real grid→tap→detail path calls the same `show_detail(n)` on the same tree and waits for one frame; the only extra is a canvas bind and the 5 ms poll. But the probe always moves to a *different* node, and the common real pattern is **back to the grid and re-tap the same card** — where every setter early-outs, so targeted mode queues **no damage and produces no frame**, and `UiManager_WaitFrameAfter` blocks until some unrelated layer paints (the grid titlebar's 10 Hz pulse, ~100 ms) or times out at `RENDER_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS` = 8 s. Whole-panel mode could never hit it because it always damaged something. So the fastest case was about to become the slowest.
  - Fixed by detecting it where it is knowable: `show_detail` compares the requested node against the one already on the tree (guarded by `s_detail_valid` so the first call is not mistaken for a re-show) and sets `s_skip_wait`, which `view_task` honours. The flag is passed to the task rather than binding inline so `bind_view` keeps running on the context it always has. A re-tap of the same node is now genuinely instant instead of merely fast.
  - Worth recording as a pattern: **making damage precise makes "nothing changed" a reachable state, and any code that waits on a frame has to handle it.** Nothing else in the tree waits on a frame this way, but `UiManager_WaitFrameAfter`'s contract should be read with this in mind.
- **Correcting a premise, because it changes what the probe would have to do: the grid and the detail view are on SEPARATE canvases, not one.** `ScreenSystem_InitSurface` assigns `CANVAS_SYSTEM` → `s_fb` and `CANVAS_SYSTEM_DETAIL` → `s_fb_detail` — two full-screen framebuffers on Legato layers 7 and 8, the second of which is the ninth layer-screen that forced `ram_nocache` 32 → 34 MB on 2026-08-06. So **returning to the grid costs nothing**: its canvas still holds its boot paint and `bind_view(VIEW_OVERVIEW)` is register writes. A grid→detail→grid round trip is exactly one detail repaint, which is what the probe already times; bouncing off the grid inside the probe would measure `gfxcSetLayer` and nothing else.
- **Real tap measured, three runs: 80.6 / 77.7 / 100.4 ms — mean 86.2, and the paint is only 73% of it.**

  | | mean | min | max | spread |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | last real tap | 86,234 µs | 77,744 | 100,392 | **26%** |
  | node switch (probe) | 63,370 µs | 59,609 | 66,127 | 10% |
  | full panel (probe) | 181,332 µs | 177,575 | 183,922 | 4% |

  Against the old whole-panel behaviour the operator would have felt roughly 181 + 23 ≈ **204 ms**, so the change is about **2.4× on perceived latency**, and 86 ms is under the ~100 ms threshold where a transition reads as instantaneous.
- **The 13–41 ms gap between probe and reality is quantisation and lock contention, not paint — and run 3 proves it.** That run has the **lowest** probe figure (59.6 ms) and the **highest** real tap (100.4 ms); anticorrelated, so its extra 41 ms cannot be drawing. The known contributors, all before the frame can finish:
  - `_SYS_INPUT_Tasks` on `vTaskDelay(10)` — up to 10 ms to dispatch the touch
  - `_LEGATO_Tasks` on `vTaskDelay(10)` — up to 10 ms before the frame even starts
  - `WaitFrameAfter` on `RENDER_POLL_MS` — up to 5 ms to notice it finished
  - the titlebar tick's *damage* landing in the same frame as the detail repaint — the pulse dot is small, but a `tiles_refresh` was measured at ~20 ms on 2026-08-07 and fires every `SAMPLE_MS` = 500 ms

  Worst case ~45 ms, typical ~13 ms — which brackets all three observations.

  **Correcting myself: the titlebar tick does NOT hold the render lock across that ~20 ms paint.** It holds it only across the widget edits — `tiles_refresh` (setString + sparkline push + invalidate) and `setAlphaAmount`, i.e. microseconds — and releases before the paint, which then happens in `LEGATO_Tasks` like any other. So the tick is not blocking taps by holding a lock, and the ~20 ms figure is paint cost, not lock-hold time.
- **The real mechanism by which ANY lock holder hurts touch latency, which is worth more than the titlebar question: `UiManager_RenderLock` suspends `SYS_INPUT_Tasks` as well as `LEGATO_Tasks`, and its back-off loop re-suspends both every 1 ms until an in-flight paint finishes.** So while a long paint runs, a holder can spend tens of iterations repeatedly stopping the task that dispatches touches.
  - **And that suspend looks unnecessary.** `leInput_InjectTouchDown` only queues an event under `leEvent_Lock()` — it writes `_state.touch[id]` and pushes onto the event list, and **never walks or mutates the widget tree**. Picking and dispatch happen in `leEvent_ProcessEvents()` inside `leUpdate`, which the lock already stops by suspending `LEGATO_Tasks`. Nothing that takes the render lock in this tree calls `LE_MALLOC`, so the allocator is not shared with the injection path either.
  - Dropping `vTaskSuspend(s_input_task)` would therefore be **one line in our own file** and would help every holder — the titlebar tick, the bus and log polls, the anim ticker, the probes — rather than just one. Not taken unilaterally: it is a change to the primitive guarding the whole UI, justified by reading vendor code, and the failure mode if the reading is wrong is rare corruption rather than an obvious break. Wants a decision, not a guess.
- **Moving the titlebar tick into Legato's own context: cheap but not the answer here.** The `update()` vtable route the 2026-08-07 entry proposed is dead — patch #15 compiled that walk out, the same trap that had silently killed song_list's fling — so it would mean calling the tick from `_LEGATO_Tasks` in generated `tasks.c`, a file that carries no marvin patches today, for ~5 lines plus a permanent re-apply burden. It would remove 10 lock acquisitions a second and the back-off churn, but it would **not** fix the tap variance, because the titlebar's damage still lands in the same frames as the detail repaint.
- **n = 3 with one outlier does not support building anything.** The free discriminator is `titlebar tiles off` (the ~20 ms paint) versus `titlebar pulse off` (a 12×12 dot) and a dozen taps each.
- **`RENDER_POLL_MS` 5 → 1** (ours, not generated): ~2.5 ms of pure average latency for two reads per poll, on a wait that only runs during a view switch or at boot. Taken. The two `vTaskDelay(10)`s are in generated `tasks.c` and would each need a re-apply note, so left alone.
- **The free experiment that would settle the outlier: `titlebar pulse off`, then tap and read `system probe`.** If the ~100 ms cases disappear, the variance is the titlebar tick holding the render lock and the fix is to stop that tick contending on the tap path (or to move it into Legato's own context, which the 2026-08-07 entry already proposed for a different reason). Costs nothing to try — the switch already exists.
- **But the probe genuinely is not the real number, for a reason that has nothing to do with which canvas is bound — and that gap is now closed.** It runs under `UiManager_RenderLock` (LEGATO_Tasks and the input task suspended) and calls `leRenderer_Paint()` synchronously, so it excludes `view_task`'s wake-up, `WaitFrameAfter`'s 5 ms poll granularity, and DDR contention from the capture DMA, T1S and USB — none of which the render lock stops, as `render_probe.h` already warns. **`system probe` now also reports `last real tap (grid->detail)`**, timed in the live path from `show_detail` entry to the bind that puts the node on the panel. That is the figure to quote for responsiveness; 59.5 ms per switch is the floor it sits on.

- **186 ms per tap, and the real figure is a little worse**: `WaitFrameAfter` polls on `RENDER_POLL_MS = 5`, so the observed grid→detail transition is the repaint rounded up to a poll plus scheduling — call it ~190 ms. The card's pressed border gives immediate feedback so it reads as "loading" rather than as a dead tap, but it is a fifth of a second.
- **94% of it is the full-screen repaint** (174,941 µs); `show_detail`'s own work is the remaining 10,774 µs. That works out at **~142 µs per rewritten widget**, which is far too much for a string compare and a damage push — the likely explanation is that a label's damage rect is computed by *measuring* the string (`_leLabelWidget_GetTextRects`), and `leFixedString_SetFromChar` both preinvalidates and invalidates, so every label measures its text **twice** per switch. That cost stays in targeted mode, but at 6% of the total it is not the thing to chase.
- **Splitting the 175 ms paint: ~28 ms is the invariant floor** (fill + scratch→canvas, both measured today) **and ~147 ms is content** — text, the two AA cards, the QR image.
- **So the targeted-damage estimate is 44–63 ms depending on how the ~76 rects tile, i.e. 3–4×**, taking the tap from ~190 ms to ~50–70 ms. Both terms of the floor scale with damaged area, so narrowing the damage is the only thing that gets under 28 ms at all.
- **Two per-rect costs to watch when the number comes in, because they could eat the win:** `leRenderer_Paint` runs an `invalidateWidget` pass **from the layer root over the whole widget tree per damage rect**, and each rect gets its own scratch→canvas blit. At 76 rects over ~150 widgets that is ~11,000 widget visits plus 76 GFX2D blit setups — order 1–4 ms on the estimates above, so it should not dominate, but if `targeted` comes back anywhere near `full panel` this is where it went.
- **Order matters for the follow-up:** narrowing the damage also reduces the *text* drawn, so the scheme-blend-table route must be measured after this, not before, or it will be credited with work the damage change already removed.

- **Why this screen is different from every other repaint measured today: the operator waits for it.** `bind_view` deliberately holds the grid on screen until the detail repaint lands (`view_task` → `UiManager_WaitFrameAfter`), because the detail canvas is one tree re-pointed at the tapped node and binding early would show the *previous* node. So the grid→detail transition cannot be faster than the repaint. Everywhere else a slow repaint costs CPU; here it costs responsiveness.
- **`system probe` measures the real thing** — `show_detail()` to a different node plus the frame that resolves it, which is exactly what a tap pays. It alternates nodes rather than repeating one, because `lestring_set_utf8` skips identical writes and re-showing the same node would make every iteration after the first nearly free and flatter the number badly.
- **The lever is almost certainly the same one the bus screen found on 2026-08-07, and the justifying comment is wrong in the same way.** `show_detail` ends with a whole-panel invalidate under: *"the strings above were written straight into their leFixedStrings, which does not damage the widgets"*. It does — `set_text` → `lestring_set_utf8` → `leFixedString_SetFromChar`, which **preinvalidates and invalidates unconditionally** (that is the very property `lestring_set_utf8` exists to work around), and each of the eight `setScheme` calls damages too. So the whole-panel invalidate is very probably redundant. `system refresh full|targeted` A/Bs it; default stays `full`, so nothing changes until measured.
- **Why targeted should genuinely help here, rather than just re-merging into a full-screen rect:** `addDamageRectToList` only combines rects that **intersect or contain** one another — non-intersecting rects are pushed back as separate entries, so scattered damage stays scattered rather than collapsing to a bounding box. A node switch rewrites **~76 widgets** (11 scalar strings + 8 schemes + PROSE 8 + STORY 5 + STORY_ITEMS 5 + BUILT 8 + NODE_BUS 7 + the 8×3 rail), which at label-sized rects is **~19–30% of the screen**.
- **And the floor it would escape is worth stating, because it is invariant:** any *full-screen* repaint on this hardware costs at least **~28 ms** — 1,024,000 px of GFX2D fill at the measured 7.6 ns/px (7.8 ms) plus the scratch→canvas blit at 19.7 ns/px (20.2 ms). Content optimisation cannot get under that; only narrowing the damage can, since both terms scale with damaged area.
- **Correcting a lever I nearly recommended: `accent_corner` is NOT in the tap path.** The journal's ~15 ms soft-float per-ray ellipse figure is `PanelAA_EnableLeftAccent`, and reading the code it is applied to the **grid's** eight node cards (`add_node_card`), not the detail view — whose only accent is an `add_capsule`, already on the integer `aa_shape` path. The grid is painted once at boot and never repainted ("entering it is a bare bind"), so that 15 ms is **boot cost**, not per-tap. Still worth converting for boot time; irrelevant to tap latency.
- **Third lever, smaller: the detail view is the densest label text in the UI** (~76 rewritten widgets), so the scheme-blend-table route from the entry above would apply the measured 1.38× to its text portion. Worth roughly a tenth of the repaint, and only worth doing after the damage question is settled — narrowing the damage reduces the text drawn too, so doing it in the other order would over-credit the table.
- **Also added while in here: `s_detail_node`.** Nothing tracked which node the detail tree was pointed at, so the probe had nothing to restore to.

**2026-08-08 (night, later) — "how do we speed up text for everything?" The answer splits cleanly in two, and MGS turns out not to be involved at all. song_list converted; the label route costed but not built. NOT YET ON HARDWARE.**

- **MGS cannot emit blend tables, so this is entirely our problem — which makes it easier, not harder.** `schemes.json` contains **zero** occurrences of "blend" or "lookup" (its scheme keys are just the 16 colour slots), and every generated scheme ends `0, NULL` for `blendTableCount` / `blendTables`. Legato models the feature; MGS does not expose it. So no design edit and no regen is available *or needed* — the tables have to be built in our code, and that means we also control `base`, which sidesteps the problem below.
- **The two halves of the UI need completely different work, and only one of them is cheap:**

  | | how text is drawn | to use the lookup path |
  |---|---|---|
  | **custom widgets** (log_list, song_list) | our own glyph loop | pass the flat background to `TextLut_DrawLine` — **done** |
  | **Legato labels** (dashboard, bus, system, keyboard, titlebar) | `leLabelWidget` → label skin | the widget's **scheme** must carry the table |

- **song_list is converted, and it is the one that actually mattered per frame.** It repaints in full on every scroll step *and* now on every fling frame at 30 Hz, so its text cost is paid continuously while the operator interacts. Its `draw_str` already resolved alignment by measuring and moving `x` before drawing, so it was already single-line left-aligned by the time it hit the renderer — exactly the shape the lookup path needs. The background is a parameter rather than read from the scheme because **this widget is transparent** (`SongList_SetTransparent(s_list, true)`), so its own scheme's BASE is not what shows through: a normal row's glyphs sit on the dialog's zinc-900, a selected row's on the zinc-100 selection fill, and the ramp is only valid if that is right.
- **The label route works, the plumbing is already wired, and there is one trap that would have shipped a visible bug.** `legato_widget_label_skin_classic.c:197` already calls `leUtils_GetSchemeLookupTable(widget.scheme, TEXT, BASE)` and hands the result to `leStringRenderer_DrawString`, which honours it. But it asks for a table matching **the scheme's own TEXT and BASE** — and an audit of every `SCHEME_TEXT_*` says **23 of 24 have BASE at `0xC8D0D4`, Legato's default light grey**, because a transparent label never reads BASE so nobody ever set it. Building tables from that would ramp every glyph toward light grey on near-black cards: visible haloes on every screen, and invisible to every static check. Same failure class the `mgs-legato-design` skill records for `background` being left white.
  - Because the tables must come from our code anyway, the fix comes free: marvin owns a **RAM copy** of each scheme with BASE set to the real background and the table attached, and screens point their labels at the copy. `sizeof(leScheme)` is **712 B** (11 colour modes × 16 slots × 4 B + count + pointer), so ~8 wrapped schemes is ~5.7 KB plus 512 B per colour pair. No design edit, no regen, and each screen's own `add_label` helper already knows its card colour — so it is roughly **one line per screen**, not per call site.
- **Expected payoff, so this is not started on a hunch.** At the measured ~31 µs/glyph blended vs ~22 µs looked up (~9 µs saved), the bus screen is the only label screen where it is worth much: ~600 glyphs repainting per refresh at 2 Hz ≈ **~1.1% CPU** against its measured ~7.3%. The dashboard is a few tenths; the system detail, keyboard and song-select chrome are static after a tap and gain nothing. **So the honest answer to "everything" is that only two widgets in marvin are text-bound, both are custom, and both are now done** — the label work is a real but modest follow-up, best gated on a bus-screen measurement rather than done wholesale.

**2026-08-08 (night) — the three-way text A/B ran, and it says the glyph path is DDR-transaction bound, not ALU bound. `lut` is 19.8% off every log repaint and stays the default. Recommending we STOP short of blit-shift, and naming the thing that is worth more than it.**

| `log text` | list repaint | delta | what it isolates |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ustring` | 77,820 µs | — | baseline (Legato's string renderer) |
| `walk` | 76,512 µs | **−1,308 µs (1.7%)** | the redundant string measuring |
| `lut` | 62,445 µs | **−14,067 µs (18.1%)** | the per-pixel glyph path |
| | | **−15,375 µs (19.8%) = 1.25×** | both |

- **My "redundant string measuring" hypothesis was essentially wrong, and it is worth saying so plainly.** I called it one of three stacked inefficiencies: `drawUString` runs `GetLineCount` + `GetRect` + `KerningRect` + `GetLineRect` before drawing a glyph, per cell, 68 cells a repaint. It is worth **1.7%** — noise. Those passes only walk advances out of the glyph table; they touch no pixels, and on this platform anything that touches no pixels is nearly free. `walk` is kept anyway, because it is the *vehicle* for `lut` (the lookup path is unreachable through `drawUString`, which ignores the table), not because it pays for itself.
- **The per-pixel path is the whole win: 18.1%, and it lands where the coverage model said it would.** Taking the non-text floor out (fill 6,256 + scratch→canvas 15,807 = 22,063 µs), the text portion goes **55,757 → 40,382 µs = 1.38×** — against **1.22× predicted** from counting non-zero bytes in the font's raster blob. First prediction this session to land close, and the reason it did is that it modelled a *mechanism* (transactions per glyph) rather than extrapolating a rate measured for something else, which is how the previous three went wrong.
- **So: this text path is DDR-transaction bound, not ALU bound, and the residual says so quantitatively.** Removing a destination read got 1.22×; removing *three* `leColorConvert` calls and a four-multiply blend per pixel on top of that got only the remaining **1.13×**. Per-pixel arithmetic is close to free here; per-pixel DDR touches are the cost.
  - **This does not contradict the aa_shape entries above, and the distinction matters for the next reader.** Those saw 20–36× from removing float maths because Legato's vector rasteriser ran `Atan2` **per supersample — 8× per output pixel**. The rule is arithmetic *density per DDR touch*: at ~8 transcendental ops per pixel the ALU dominates, at ~1 blend per pixel the memory system does. Both entries are right about their own regime.
- **`lut` stays the default.** 19.8% off every repaint of this screen for 2.5 KB of ramps and one cache; `log text` keeps all three paths switchable so the next person can re-derive this instead of trusting the table.
- **Recommending we NOT build blit-shifted scrolling, having measured it properly.** It is still a real 3.4× (copy 14,943 + text 2,375 + fill 368 + band blit 929 ≈ 18,615 µs, ~54 fps), but:
  - **80% of a shifted step is the GFX2D copy itself**, so the ceiling is ~67 fps no matter how cheap text becomes — the copy cannot be avoided, only the text it replaces.
  - The drag is now **62.4 ms/16 fps, already better than the 89.7 ms/11 fps Greg called "working smoothly"**. The remaining win buys *momentum* on the one screen where momentum is not wanted.
  - Against that: a custom damage path that has to tell Legato "the rest of me is already correct", an unverified overlapping-copy direction (`GFX2D_BUFFER.dir`), and shear on a single-buffered live canvas. That is gold-plating a solved problem.
- **What is worth more, found while checking the above: every Legato label in marvin is leaving the same 1.38× on the table, and the plumbing is already wired.** `legato_widget_label_skin_classic.c:197` already calls `leUtils_GetSchemeLookupTable(widget.scheme, …)` and passes the result to `leStringRenderer_DrawString`, which — unlike `drawUString` — **honours it**. It returns NULL for one reason only: no scheme in this design carries blend tables (`grep -c leBlendLookupTable le_gen_scheme.c` = 0). So the dashboard, the bus table's ~60 labels, and the system screen would all get it *without touching a widget*, as a data change rather than a code change.
  - The obstacle is that `SCHEME_*` are `const` in generated code, so the tables cannot be attached at runtime in place. Two routes, neither explored yet: find out whether MGS can emit blend tables at all; or have marvin own RAM copies of the schemes with tables attached and point widgets at those (~6 KB of schemes plus ~512 B per colour pair). **That is the next thing to measure, not the blit.**

**2026-08-08 (later still) — `log probe` measured the GFX2D rates and my fill estimate was 4× too high. Blit-shift is worth 3.5×, but the text path is the better first move and it is now A/B-switchable. NOT YET ON HARDWARE.**

```
marvin> log probe
  list   1246x644 (802424 px) =  71886 us   17 rows
  PANEL  1280x800 (1024000 px) = 131822 us
  gfx2d fill 802424 px =   6091 us (   7 ns/px, w)
  gfx2d copy 802424 px =  15788 us (  19 ns/px, r+w)
  => shift+1row ~20016 us vs 71886 us full  (3.5x)
```

- **Correcting myself again, and this one matters: the GFX2D fill is 7.6 ns/px, not the ~30 ns/px I took from `render_probe.h`.** So the fill in a list repaint is **6.1 ms, not 24 ms** — 8.5% of the frame rather than 27%. Where the 30 ns/px came from is now clear: `RenderProbe_WidgetUs` times a whole `leRenderer_Paint`, which is fill-into-scratch **plus the scratch→canvas blit plus frame overhead**. That is a fair number for "what a damaged pixel costs", but it is not the fill rate, and I used it as one. Third estimate of mine this session to be wrong by a wide margin; all three were wrong in the same way — reasoning from a number measured for a different purpose.
- **A new item falls out of the same measurement: the scratch→canvas blit is ~15.8 ms, about 22% of every list repaint, and it is a floor.** Legato paints into scratch and blits the damaged rect to the canvas, so *any* repaint of that area pays it. It is not avoidable by drawing less cleverly — only by damaging less area.
- **Breakdown of the 71.9 ms:** fill 6.1 ms (8.5%, hardware), scratch→canvas ~15.8 ms (22%), **text ~50 ms (70%), CPU, one pixel at a time.** Text is the target, and that was the answer to Greg's "how much effort" question — not the blit.
- **Blit-shift verdict: 3.5×, so it clears the bar** (copy 14.9 ms + one row's repaint 4.2 ms ≈ 19.1 ms vs 71.9 ms, ~52 fps). Deferred behind the text work, with two unknowns still open and recorded so they are not forgotten: the probe copies the rect **onto itself**, which measures throughput but does *not* exercise the overlapping src/dst hazard a real shift has (`GFX2D_BUFFER.dir` exists for it — `GFX2D_XY00`..`XY11` — semantics unconfirmed); and `XLCDC_BUF_PER_LAYER = 1`, so a shift moves pixels mid-scanout and may shear for a frame.
- **Reading the text path turned up three stacked costs, all fixable in our tree with no vendor patch.** Per covered glyph pixel, `leRenderer_BlendPixel` does: a DDR **read**, `leColorConvert` on the destination, `leColorConvert` on **the source colour — constant for the whole string, recomputed every pixel**, a four-multiply blend, `leColorConvert` back, and a write. Legato already ships the alternative (`leFont_DrawGlyph_Lookup`: index a 256-entry table by the glyph's coverage byte, plain `PutPixel`, no read and no arithmetic). And separately, `drawUString` runs `GetLineCount` + `GetRect` + `KerningRect` + `GetLineRect` — three or four full measuring passes over the string — **before drawing a single glyph**, per cell, 68 cells a repaint, for single-line left-aligned monospace text.
- **But I checked the lookup path's own cost before believing in it, and it is not the slam dunk it looks like.** It writes *every* pixel of a glyph's ink box including the transparent ones, where the blend path touches only covered pixels. Counting non-zero bytes in `DejaVuSansMono_12`'s raster blob: **6,644 of 10,900 = 61% coverage** (and only 7.8% fully opaque, so nearly every covered pixel really does blend). So transactions only go 13,288 read+writes → 10,900 writes, **1.22×**. Everything else it saves is arithmetic, and whether that matters depends on whether ~590–940 cycles/px is DDR-bound or ALU-bound — which is a question for the panel.
- **So it ships as a three-way runtime switch rather than as a claimed improvement**, the house pattern (`bus refresh`, `titlebar pulse`, `gamma`). `log text <ustring|walk|lut>`, then `log probe`:
  - `ustring` — today's `leStringRenderer_DrawUString`. Baseline.
  - `walk` — our glyph walk, Legato's per-pixel blend. **ustring − walk = the redundant string measuring.**
  - `lut` — our glyph walk + the blend lookup table. **walk − lut = the per-pixel path.**

  Two subtractions, three numbers, and no need to guess which of the two costs is real. Default is `lut`; if the measurement disagrees, the default flips.
- `ui/gfx/text_lut.{c,h}` holds it: an 8-entry cache of `leBlendLookupTable`, ramps interpolated per channel in 888 and converted once into the render mode (lerping packed 565 would band), built lazily on first paint because the render colour mode is only known inside one. 16bpp render modes only — the table's entry width has to match what `leFont_DrawGlyphData_Lookup` indexes — falling back to `walk` otherwise. `log text` reports tables built and refusals, so exceeding the cache shows up instead of silently degrading.
- **Two Legato bugs found while reading, worth knowing:** `drawUString` **ignores `req->lookupTable` entirely** (only the `leString` variant honours it), so every custom widget in this tree that sets that field — song_list since it was written, and the log list until now — has been setting it to no effect. And `drawUString`'s space-skipping tests a `codePoint` variable it never assigns inside its loop, so it draws spaces after all. Neither is worth patching a generated file over; the glyph walk sidesteps both.

**2026-08-08 (later) — momentum back in the song list without giving back patch #15's 3.85%, and the one measurement that decides blit-shifted scrolling. NOT YET ON HARDWARE.**

Greg, on restoring the fling: *"Removing it was a significant reduction in processor usage, so I don't want to just blindly put it back."* Right instinct, and the answer is that the walk and the fling need very different things — **O(every widget) × 100 Hz unconditionally** versus **O(the one thing moving) × only while it moves**. Patch #15 is untouched.

- **`ui/ui_anim.{c,h}`: a ticker task that blocks on a semaphore whenever nothing is animating.** Idle cost is not "small", it is *nothing* — the task is not runnable, so it is cheaper at rest than even a registry-based walk polling at 100 Hz would be. A release with velocity registers a step; the task steps every `UIANIM_STEP_MS` under `UiManager_RenderLock` and re-blocks the instant every step reports finished. 30 Hz rather than 60 because the song list repaints in an estimated 20–26 ms, so 33 ms leaves headroom instead of pinning the render task for the duration of a fling.
  - Chosen over the two alternatives (extend patch #15 to walk a marvin registry inside `updateWidgets`; or patch `_LEGATO_Tasks` in `tasks.c`) because it needs **no generated-file edits at all**, which is the project rule, and because both of those poll at 100 Hz even with nothing registered. The cost is a third render-lock holder, which is mild — the titlebar already takes it 10×/s permanently.
- **`sl_update` became `songlist_step`, and the `update` vtable override is gone.** Keeping both would double-step if any of the four `MARVIN_ANY_UPDATING_WIDGET` widget types is ever enabled in MCC; with the override removed that slot is the base no-op and there is exactly one driver. The physics is unchanged — it was already measured against `xTaskGetTickCount()` rather than the tick period, so a paint slower than the step period drops frames without changing the distance travelled.
- **The log list stays drag-only.** At 89.7 ms a repaint it flings at 11 fps, which is ~11 visible steps across a whole fling — a slideshow, and it would pin the render task for the full second. Momentum there is a paint-cost problem, not a mechanism problem.

**Answering Greg's three questions about blit-shifted scrolling, from the code:**

- **"Is Legato using the 2D engine for clearing the screen, or is this pixel by pixel?" — the 2D engine, and this changes where the cost actually is.** `leRenderer_RectFill` → `_RGBFill` → `leGPU_FillRect` → `DRV_GFX2D_Fill` → `PLIB_GFX2D_Fill`, i.e. hardware, and `gfxGPUInterface` in `drv_gfx2d.h` really does install `DRV_GFX2D_Fill`/`DRV_GFX2D_Blit`. Worth noting `DRV_GFX2D_Fill` does **no cache maintenance at all** — it programs the engine and spins on `GetGlobalStatusBusy` — so it never had the 2 MB walk that patch #14 had to fix in `Blit`. Two consequences: the ~24 ms of fill in a list repaint is already as fast as it gets and can only be *avoided*, not optimised; and the ~66 ms of text is **CPU, one pixel at a time** — 8bpp antialiased glyphs blended with `lookupTable == NULL`, because `grep -c leBlendLookupTable le_gen_scheme.c` is 0. The text is the whole target.
- **"Changes to Legato code?" — none.** `PLIB_GFX2D_Copy(dst, dst_rect, src, src_rect)` is the within-buffer shift primitive, `GFX2D_BUFFER.dir` (`GFX2D_XY00`..`XY11`) expresses the scan direction an overlapping copy needs, and `DRV_GFX2D_Blit` is already patched for row-granular cache maintenance. The shift plus narrow damage lives entirely in our widget and a surface helper — the same direct-surface-write precedent as `AaCorners` / `video_frame`, and the same narrow-damage idiom as `bar_set_height`.
- **"How much effort?" — the mechanism is about half a day, but two unknowns decide whether it is worth spending, and one of them is now measurable.**
  1. **GFX2D copy throughput on this surface.** The shift *replaces* ~66 ms of text with a full-area copy, so it only wins if the copy is cheap. If a copy runs at the fill's ~30 ns/px, a scroll step goes 89.7 → ~29 ms (**3.1×**, ~34 fps). If it costs 2× because it reads as well as writes, ~53 ms (**1.7×**) and it is probably not worth it. Datasheet numbers will not answer this: the surface is uncached strongly-ordered DDR with the LCDC concurrently reading ~360 MB/s of it.
  2. **Tearing.** `XLCDC_BUF_PER_LAYER = 1` — single-buffered, so a shift moves the pixels being scanned out. Every existing repaint already blits into the live canvas unsynchronised, but none of them moves 800,000 px at once, so a shear for one frame during a fast fling is plausible and only testable by eye.
- **So `log probe` now measures unknown 1**, for about 20 lines: `PLIB_GFX2D_Copy` of the list rect onto itself (same pixel count and the same overlapping access pattern, without changing what is on screen) and `PLIB_GFX2D_Fill` over the same rect as the write-only reference, both with the busy-wait the driver uses — timing the register writes alone would have been meaningless. It prints a verdict line comparing `copy + one row's repaint` against the full repaint it would replace. **Under about half means build it.**

**2026-08-08 — Activity Log screen: a 10th layer-screen showing the last 100 serial lines. CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE and working; 1% idle. Two findings on the way in, one of which is a pre-existing regression in song-select's scroll.**

- **Measured on hardware, and the pre-build estimate held for once.** Greg: working well, drag "definitely increases load, but it is working smoothly", `health` reports **1%** for `LEGATO_Tasks` with the screen static.

  ```
  marvin> log probe
    list   1246x644 ( 802424 px) =  89662 us   17 rows
    PANEL  1280x800 (1024000 px) = 151620 us
  ```

  | | µs | ns/px | |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | list | 89,662 | **111.7** | 11.2 fps while dragging |
  | PANEL | 151,620 | 148.1 | titlebar + card + list together |

  **Predicted ~92 ms at 116 ns/px before the build; measured 89.7 ms at 111.7 — 2.6% off.** Worth recording *because* the last several estimates in this journal were wrong by wide margins: the difference is that this one was extrapolated from a *measured* per-pixel rate on the same hardware (the bus refresh) rather than reasoned from first principles about what ought to be expensive.
- **1% idle is the design working, not luck.** The poll compares `log_ring_seq()` and returns before taking the render lock when it has not moved, so a quiet system queues **no frames at all** on this screen. Compare the bus screen, which repaints on a 1 Hz timer whether or not the numbers moved.
- **Splitting the 89.7 ms: ~24 ms (27%) is the background fill at the established 30 ns/px, and ~66 ms (73%) is text.** That puts glyph work at roughly **590–940 cycles per glyph pixel** depending on how the glyph box is counted — the *same order* as the ~950 cycles/px this journal measured for the titlebar card on 2026-08-07, which contained no glyphs at all and was attributed to soft-float `sqrtf`. Two unrelated paint paths landing at the same per-pixel cost suggests the floor is the **blended write into uncached scratch**, not the geometry maths in front of it. Stated as a hypothesis, with a test that needs no new code: `log probe` right after a reboot (ring nearly empty, most rows blank) against `log probe` with 100 lines held — same rect, same fill, an order of magnitude fewer glyphs.
