- **Context for that: `LE_FONT_BPP_8` and no blend lookup table exists anywhere in this design.** The fonts are `Style: Antialias` / 8 bits per pixel, and `leUtils_GetSchemeLookupTable` returns NULL for every scheme here — `grep -c leBlendLookupTable le_gen_scheme.c` is **0**. So every antialiased glyph pixel in marvin's *entire* UI takes the arithmetic blend path rather than a table lookup. That is a design-wide property and not specific to this screen; noted as a lead, not touched.
- **A trap avoided by reading the generated struct instead of the header comment.** `le_gen_assets.h` documents `DejaVuSansMono_12` as `Height: 37`, which would have made every row's text sit hard against its top edge. The actual `leRasterFont.height` member is **16** (baseline 12) — the comment's 37 is not the field `font_h()` reads. Centring is correct as a result, but the two numbers disagree by 21 px and only one of them is the one the renderer uses.
- **The `TAG: message` convention is not a habit, it is an invariant — and that is measured, not assumed.** Extracting all 185 distinct `LOG_*` format strings in the tree and running the real `split_tag` over them on the host gives **185/185**, 32 distinct tags, zero untagged. So the mockup's Source column costs nothing to populate. Two things the naive rule would have got wrong, both found by running it rather than reading it:
  - **Two tags contain a space** (`QSPI verify`, `QSPI bench`), so "reject spaces in a tag" — my first rule — would have dropped 15 lines to Unknown. Space is a tag character; what actually delimits is *a separator followed by a space*.
  - **One line uses `|`, not `:`** — `"HM| %s"`, health_monitor's table continuation, which is what `health` streams its whole per-task report through. Accepting `|` is the difference between `health`'s output being attributed and being 30 lines of Unknown.
  - The rule that survives: first `:` or `|` at index 1..16, followed by space or end, with only alnum/`_`/`-`/space before it. It correctly takes the *first* colon in `TC358743: init: VI_MODE failed` and correctly declines nothing.
  - Cross-checked the display-name table the same way: **32 tags emitted, 32 mapped, no gaps and no dead rows.** An unlisted tag renders as-is rather than as Unknown, so a new subsystem gets a sensible row the day it is written.
- **`update()` is never called in this build, so song-select's kinetic scroll has been dead since re-apply patch #15.** `updateWidgets` early-returns under `!MARVIN_ANY_UPDATING_WIDGET`, which is the whole point of that patch — but `widget_song_list`'s `sl_update` is where its release fling lives, and it is only reachable from that walk. The drag itself still works (input events dispatch normally and `sl_touchMove` invalidates directly); what is gone is the deceleration after the finger lifts. **Not fixed here** — it is a behaviour change on a working screen and belongs in its own change, with a decision about *which* mechanism drives it. Recorded because it is invisible: nothing warns, and the screen looks correct until you fling it.
- **The log list is therefore drag-only by design, not by omission**, and the arithmetic says that is the right call anyway. It has no partial-damage scheme — a new line or a drag step repaints all 1246×644 = 802,424 px, which measured 89.7 ms, i.e. **11.2 fps**. A fling would have been asking for animation at that rate. In practice the drag reads as smooth because Legato coalesces the invalidates from several touch-moves into one frame; the finger leads the content rather than the content stuttering.
  - **Row-quantising the scroll would NOT help, and it is worth writing down why**, because it looks like the obvious optimisation: only invalidating when `scrollY / rowHeight` changes would cut *invalidates*, but the frames are already coalesced down to ~11/s by the paint time itself, so it removes no work and only makes the scroll snap. The genuine levers are fewer glyph pixels (a narrower message column) or a cheaper glyph path (blend tables, above) — neither worth taking while the screen feels right.
- The poll also skips its repaint **while a drag is in flight**, rather than fighting the scroll for the render lock; the lines are not lost, the next poll after the finger lifts picks them up.
- **Capture taps `log_emit` with `va_copy`, not by formatting once and printing the result.** DBGU output stays byte-for-byte what it was, including lines longer than the ring's 144 chars. The sink formats straight into the ring slot — no stack scratch buffer, so no logging task grows its stack by a line's worth. Lines that give up on the log mutex are deliberately *not* captured: their arguments are already lost, so there is nothing to capture.
- **Readers do not take the log mutex, and must not.** A reader that did could queue behind a task parked in polled DBGU output (~10 ms for a full line at 115200). Instead an entry with sequence number S always lives at index `(S-1) % 100`, so a reader locates a line from one volatile read; the slot is marked `seq = 0` before it is reformatted and the copy is rejected if the sequence number no longer matches. The only race that exists — the oldest slot being recycled mid-copy — comes back as "no line here" rather than a torn one.
- **Timestamps are uptime, not the RTC.** `RTC_TimeGet` is available and used elsewhere, but the clock is only right after `time set`, and the pre-scheduler `log_printf` calls from `SYS_Initialize` happen before anything reads it. Uptime is monotonic, needs no peripheral access on the log path, and reads 0 for the boot lines, which is the truth.
- **Memory: Greg raised `ram_nocache` 34 → 40 MB in MCC before this landed** (`ddram.ld` `LENGTH = 0x02800000`, `plib_mmu.c` `addr < 0x228U`), so the 10th canvas needs no further squeeze. Measured after the build: 34,384,480 + 2,048,000 = **36,432,480 of 41,943,040 (86.9%)**, 5.26 MB spare, and the map confirms `screen_log.c` contributes exactly 2,048,000 B and nothing else moved. (Correcting myself: I read the pre-change figure as 34,122,336 while planning — `0x20caa60` is 34,384,480, and the predicted total was low by that same 262,144. Conclusion unaffected.) That supersedes §0 of `ui_compositor.md`, which called the splash's 4 MB the preferred reclaim — it is not needed, and reclaiming it would have meant the log screen could not paint until after the reveal, since the splash is on screen while the other screens paint behind it.
- **One collision worth knowing about, because the symptom is a warning and not an error: `LIST_H` is FreeRTOS's `list.h` include guard.** A screen defining `#define LIST_H (CARD_H - ...)` as a layout constant redefines it, and whichever came second wins depending on include order. `screen_song_select.c` has had the same `#define LIST_H` since it was written, for the same reason, and never tripped it — it does not pull in `list.h`. Renamed to `TABLE_H`/`TABLE_Y` here; the general lesson is that short generic layout macro names in a screen are a coin flip against FreeRTOS and Legato headers.
- **Where it stands: `add_layer.py <zip> LOG --like BUS` added `SCREEN_LOG` / `PANEL_LOG` as layer 9** (`audit_refs.py` clean, 10 layers, empty root panel), Greg's MGS Generate landed it (`LE_LAYER_COUNT` 10, `Marvin_PANEL_LOG` declared), and the firmware **builds and links clean with no warnings**. Layout confirmed on the panel: card 1248×708 at y=76, table 1246×644 at y=140, 17 whole rows at 36 px with a 32 px partial 18th (a free scroll affordance), message column 970 px = 138 chars at the font's 7 px advance, right edge 1230 inside a 1246 px widget.
- **Left as follow-ups, none blocking:** ~~song-select's dead fling~~ (fixed the same day — see the entry above); whether the ~590–940 cycles/glyph-pixel floor is the uncached blended write (the empty-vs-full `log probe` test above); design-wide blend lookup tables; and extracting song_list's scroll machinery into something the two lists share, which was deliberately kept out of this change.

**2026-08-07 (evening, later) — first look on hardware: the `%` was clipped, the readout sat wrong, and CPU reads 13%+. Layout fixed and the gradient dropped; the 13% is NOT accounted for by anything this change draws.** Greg's three observations from the panel.

- **The `%` was clipped because a glyph's advance is not its ink width.** `%` at `DejaVuSansMono_12` advances **7 px but is 8 px wide** (`bearingX` 0), and the unit's box was sized to the advance, so Legato clipped the last column. This is a new failure mode next to the `fit_font` one from 2026-08-06 — that was a box too short for its *font*, this is a box too narrow for its *glyph* — and the same trap sits one character away in the caption: `P` and `T` at `Mono_9` are 6 px wide on a 5 px advance, so "CPU" survived only because it ends in `U`. Widths are now sized to ink (`UNIT_INK`), and the caption right-aligns to the unit's **ink** edge so the two agree exactly at x=53.
- **The readout looked wrong because stacking two label boxes edge-to-edge stacks their internal leading, not their text.** Legato puts a single line's baseline at `y + h/2 − baseline/2 + baseline` and glyphs rise cap-height above it, so boxes at y=4 and y=16 left an **8 px** hole between the caption's baseline and the value's cap — against the mockup's ~2 px (`leading-none` + `mt-0.5`). The block is now positioned from **ink landmarks**: cap heights 7 and 13, a 3 px gap, the whole 23 px stack centred in the 42 px card (9 above, 10 below), and the unit solved onto the value's baseline (both at card-relative y=32). Boxes overlap as a result, which is fine for transparent labels and is why the constants are derived rather than hand-placed.
- **Gradient dropped** — Greg's call and the right one, so `Sparkline_SetStyle` loses its `area` flag and the per-pixel ramp goes with it (−544 B of text). But it is worth recording that **it was never the CPU cost**: the wash was ~800 blended pixels per tile at 1 Hz, ~1,600/s.
- **The 13% cannot be attributed to pixels, and the arithmetic is the argument.** Everything this change draws, per second: one merged damage rect of 353×42 for the panel fill (14,826 px), two 158×42 card fills (13,272), eight radius-12 AA corner boxes (1,152 px, each with a soft-float `sqrtf` on an FPU-less core), ~1,000 glyph pixels and 46 antialiased line segments — at **1 Hz**; plus the LED's 12×12 at 10 Hz (1,440 px/s). Call it **~32,000 pixels per second**. For that to be 13% of an 800 MHz core, each pixel would have to cost ~3,250 cycles. It doesn't, even writing to strongly-ordered uncached DDR.
- **What this change *did* multiply is the frame rate, and that is what to test first.** `leRenderer_Paint` at `LE_PREEMPTION_LEVEL 0` runs a whole frame to completion in one call: all **9 Legato layers** walked with two driver ioctls each, and per damage rect an `invalidateWidget` pass **from the layer root over that layer's entire widget tree**. The base view previously damaged about once a second (dashboard telemetry) or never (System Info is static); the LED pulse makes it **10×/s**. 13% at 10 frames/s is 13 ms/frame, which is the right order for that fixed cost — so the pulse tick, not the plots, is the suspect. `TICK_MS` in `titlebar.c` is the single knob: setting it equal to `SAMPLE_MS` turns the pulse into a 1 Hz step and isolates it in one flash.
- **Also worth ruling out before blaming this change at all: there was no CPU readout before it.** `HealthMonitor_CpuPermille()` is new, and the supervisor's `SUP: idle=..%` line only prints at DEBUG and only every 10th sample, so 13% may be marvin's resting load (capture + Legato + T1S + USB) surfaced for the first time rather than created. **The `health` console command settles it definitively** — it prints per-task CPU% over a 500 ms window, so it will name the owner: `Titlebar` itself, `LEGATO_Tasks`, or neither.
- **If it is the frame rate, the fix is to move the pulse into Legato's own `update` vtable hook** rather than to slow it down: driven from inside the render task it needs no `UiManager_RenderLock` round trip and no third lock holder, and `leUpdate` hands it `dt` for the phase. Deliberately not done yet — changing the tick mid-diagnosis would confound Greg's next measurement.

**Same evening — `health` says `LEGATO_Tasks` owns the 13%, which narrows it to two mechanisms; added a `titlebar` console command to tell them apart instead of theorising further.** Greg's reading after the gradient came out: unchanged at 13%, attributed to the render task. That rules the plots' pixel work out (the gradient's removal moved nothing, as predicted) and leaves exactly two ways the render task can be spending it — one of which this change did not create.

- **Mechanism A — the fixed 100 Hz idle walk, which is pre-existing.** `_LEGATO_Tasks` in the MCC-generated `tasks.c` calls `Legato_Tasks()` → `leUpdate(0)` on a flat `vTaskDelay(10)`, **whether or not anything is damaged**. And `updateWidget` ([legato_state.c](../default/src/config/default/gfx/legato/core/legato_state.c)) recurses over **every widget on all nine layers with no visibility check and no early-out** — hidden canvases included. marvin's population is ~800–900 widgets (the bus layer alone runs to 200 labels + 96 widgets), so that is ~85,000 vtable calls a second over ~180 KB of widget structs against a 32 KB D-cache, i.e. mostly cache misses. Order 3%, and **this change grew it**: the titlebar went from 7 widgets to 18, ×5 instances, so 35 → 90. Real, but a 6% share of a cost that was already there.
- **Mechanism B — frames, which this change did create.** With `LE_PREEMPTION_LEVEL 0` a whole frame runs to completion inside one tick, so the render task's share is frames/s × cost-per-frame and the damaged *area* barely enters into it. The pulse damages the dot 10×/s, and that is exact rather than estimated: all 20 of the smoothstep's 100 ms steps produce a different alpha (`255 253 243 229 211 192 174 156 142 132 128 …`), so none are absorbed by `setAlphaAmount`'s no-change guard. Against a base view that previously damaged about once a second, that is a 10× increase in frames.
- **The two are distinguishable by one number, so `titlebar` now reports it.** `titlebar [pulse on|off] [tiles on|off]` toggles the two damage sources at runtime and prints `frames/s` sampled over the same 500 ms window `health` uses, so the two commands' readings compose: `titlebar pulse off` → `health`. Expected outcomes are unambiguous — frames/s drops to ~1 and `LEGATO_Tasks` falls sharply ⇒ mechanism B; frames/s drops but `LEGATO_Tasks` stays near 13% ⇒ mechanism A, and the 13% was always there. Runtime switches rather than a `#define` because each answer otherwise costs a reflash, and A/B-ing live is how the rest of this screen's questions got settled (`gamma`, `ScreenBus_SetSimulated`).
- **The tick now also skips the render lock entirely on a tick with nothing to change**, deciding before it acquires rather than relying on `setAlphaAmount`'s internal guard, which only fires after the lock is already paid for. That is what makes `pulse off` genuinely free instead of merely quiet.
**2026-08-07 (night) — measured it, and the answer is not the titlebar: every Legato frame on marvin pays ~8 ms of cache maintenance on a framebuffer that is not cacheable. NEEDS A DECISION — the offender is MCC-generated.** Greg's A/B on hardware:

| pulse | tiles | total CPU | frames/s |
|---|---|---|---|
| on | on | 13.7% | 10 |
| on | off | ~11.7% | 10 |
| off | off | **3.85%** | **0** |

- **Divide it out and the per-frame cost is absurd: 7.85% over 10 frames/s = 7.85 ms *per frame*, to repaint a 12×12 dot.** The tile refresh costs ~20 ms. Baseline with zero frames is 3.85%, which is mechanism A (the 100 Hz `leUpdate` walk) and roughly the ~3% predicted.
- **Root cause, found by reading `DRV_GFX2D_Blit`** ([drv_gfx2d.c](../default/src/config/default/gfx/driver/processor/gfx2d/drv_gfx2d.c)): before every copy it runs `dcache_CleanInvalidateByAddr(dest->pixels, dest->buffer_length)`, and `dest` is **the whole canvas pixel buffer** — `gfxPixelBufferCreate` sets `buffer_length = width × height × bpp`, so 1280×800×2 = **2,048,000 bytes**, no matter how small the damaged rect is. `dcache_CleanInvalidateByAddr` walks that in 32-byte lines: **64,000 iterations of `cp15_dcache_clean_invalidate_mva` + `__DMB()`**. At ~100 cycles a line on an 800 MHz ARM926 that is **8.0 ms** — against 7.85 ms measured. The source buffer's clean is harmless by comparison (Legato sizes the scratch buffer to the rect).
- **And it is pure waste, not merely mis-sized: the canvases live in `.region_nocache`**, which `MMU_Initialize` maps `TTB_SECT_STRONGLY_ORDERED` (non-cacheable, unbuffered). There are no dirty lines to write back. Every Legato repaint spends ~8 ms issuing cache maintenance against 2 MB of memory the cache never held.
- **This is not a titlebar problem, it is marvin's whole repaint path.** It is paid by the dashboard's 1 Hz telemetry, the bus screen's 1 Hz refresh, every nav-drawer slide step, every touch-drag frame — and it retroactively explains the 2026-07 drag-latency entry, which measured the lag correctly, attributed it to "paint cost per frame", and could not see that most of that cost was independent of the pixels painted. A ~9× overshoot on `LE_SCRATCH_BUFFER_SIZE_KB` (4096 for a full screen) is what makes the damage rect small and the fixed cost dominant.
- **`titlebar probe [iters]` was added to confirm it directly**: it times `leRenderer_Paint()` for a 12×12 damage and for a 158×42 one (19× the area) under the render lock — legitimate, since the lock's whole contract is that LEGATO_Tasks is suspended and no paint is in flight. If the two come back nearly equal, the cost is the fixed 2 MB walk and not the pixels.
- **`titlebar probe` confirmed it on hardware, and the model now closes.** `dot 12x12 = 7447us`, `card 158x42 = 15153us` — the 19×-larger rect costs the *same* 7.45 ms fixed plus 7.71 ms of real drawing. Reassembling: `3.85% baseline + 10×7.45 ms (pulse) + 2×7.71 ms + 7.45 ms (tiles)` = **13.58% against 13.7% measured**. The fixed toll is the 2 MB cache walk; predicted 8.0 ms from 64,000 lines at ~100 cycles, measured 7.45 ms.
- **Second finding from the same probe, separate problem: the card's own drawing is 1186 ns/px — ~950 cycles a pixel.** That is our code, not the vendor's: soft-float `sqrtf` per pixel in `AaCorners` across four radius-12 corner boxes (no FPU on this core), 8× supersampled vector AA over 23 line segments plus round caps, and every write landing in the uncached scratch buffer. It is why the tiles cost 2% at merely 1 Hz. Not addressed here; worth its own pass, and `titlebar probe` measures it.
- **Fix: patch #14, Greg's call among the three options below.** Options as they stood:
  1. **`CONFIG_CANVAS_GPU_BLIT_ENABLE = false`** (an MCC *config* value — the sanctioned lever). `GFXC_BufferBlit` falls back to a row-wise `memcpy`, which needs no cache maintenance at all since both buffers are non-cached. Small rects become nearly free; a full-screen repaint gets slower (2 MB of uncached `memcpy` instead of 2D DMA), so this trades boot/transition cost for steady-state cost.
  2. **Don't route the LED through Legato at all** — draw the 12×12 disc straight into the canvas surface. No damage, no frame, no blit, ~144 pixel writes, and it is in *our* code. Fixes the pulse specifically and permanently, and the precedent exists (`UiManager_CutModalCorners`, `AaCorners`, `video_frame.c` all write surfaces directly).
  3. Patch the driver to clean only the destination *rect* — correct and general, but a hand-edit of a generated file, needing a re-apply note like the `tasks.c` ones.

  **MEASURED END TO END on the bus screen, and the accounting finally closes.** `bus probe`:

  | | old | new |
  |---|---|---|
  | refresh @1 Hz | 160,715 µs → **16.07%** | 18,588 µs → **1.86%** |
  | titlebar | ~1.6% | ~1.6% |
  | baseline (100 Hz `leUpdate` walk) | ~3.85% | ~3.85% |
  | **steady total** | **~21.5%** | **~7.3%** |
  | observed peak | 35% | ~11% |

  The refresh alone is **8.6× cheaper**. Correcting myself: last entry I put ~7% on the bus refresh — it is **1.86%**, and the rest was the titlebar plus baseline. That is the fourth estimate of mine in this session to be wrong by a wide margin, which is the whole argument for the probe.
  - Read the per-widget lines as **worst case**: the probe invalidates the entire widget, while the refresh damages a band. `txbar 5777 µs` is a full 46×202 repaint where the refresh touches ~46×10 of it — which is why REFRESH is far below the sum of the parts. The sparkline at 5450 µs is the one that genuinely repaints in full.
  - **With the refresh fixed, Legato's own 100 Hz idle walk became the biggest single item** — see re-apply patch #15. It calls a no-op on every widget on every layer, 100 times a second, because none of the four widget types that override `update()` is enabled in this build.

  **CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE: the bus screen's `LEGATO_Tasks` share drops 35% → ~11% with targeted refresh.** (Greg, on the sampling: "when it hits within 500ms window" — the refresh is a 1 Hz burst and `health` averages over 500 ms, so the reading depends on whether the burst lands inside the window. Same artifact as the titlebar's 1–6%.)

  - **~7% above baseline is left, and I am not going to guess at it again.** 70 ms per refresh over ~160,000 damaged pixels is ~437 ns/px, far above the measured 30 ns/px fill rate, so it is not fill. `bus probe` now times **one whole refresh plus the frame it causes** — the number the 1 Hz task actually pays, and the only one that responds to `bus refresh full|targeted`. That plus the existing per-widget lines should locate it in one flash instead of another round of arithmetic.
  - **One clean win found by reading rather than measuring: `_leFixedString_SetFromChar` never compares.** It preinvalidates and invalidates unconditionally, so a screen that rewrites every field on a timer repaints every label even when nothing moved — and the bus table is ~60 labels at 1 Hz. `lestring_set_utf8` (ours, used by six screens) now skips an identical write. `_leWidget_SetScheme` already early-outs on an unchanged scheme, so the schemes were never the problem; only the strings.
    - **Caveat that matters for interpreting the next measurement:** `BUS_SIM_DEFAULT` is true, and the simulator jitters every value every second, so *nothing* skips while it is on. This helps real telemetry on a quiet bus, where most numbers are identical second to second. Compare with `bus refresh` A/B rather than expecting the sim to show it.
  - **The sparkline is the genuine floor** at ~75,000 px: its line shifts one sample pitch a second, so the damage really is the whole plot. Narrowing it would mean blit-shifting the plot, which a transparent widget over a card cannot do cleanly.

  **Greg was right that the bus screen repaints everything at 1 Hz: `refresh_all` ended with `Marvin_PANEL_BUS->fn->invalidate(...)` — the whole 1280×800 panel, every second. Now targeted, and the shape rasteriser had to learn to clip before that was worth anything. NOT YET ON HARDWARE.**

  - **The whole-panel invalidate was justified by a belief that is false.** Its comment said transparent labels do not get their backdrop repainted — but `invalidateWidget` marks **every** widget intersecting the damage rect dirty from the layer root down, so the opaque card behind a label repaints first. The titlebar has been running on that basis for the whole of this session. What actually made targeted invalidation unsafe was `AaCorners_Render` writing through the unchecked `leRenderer_PutPixel`, which is now fixed — so the workaround outlived its cause by a long way. Note `dashboard_feed` never had this: it already relies on per-setter invalidation, so the bus screen was the outlier.
  - **Almost everything already invalidated itself** — `set_text` through the string's invalidate callback, `setScheme`, and `setSize`/`setPosition` (which damage the old rect as well as the new). Only two were pure data: `Gauge_Set` now invalidates its own widget like every other Legato setter, and a sparkline series is shared by several widgets so its push cannot know them, leaving that one explicit invalidate to the caller.
  - **`AaShape`'s blits had to start clipping, and this is the part that makes narrow damage pay.** They iterate the shape's whole bounding box and write through the culling `_Safe` pixel calls, so a 46×10 damage band on a 46×204 TX bar still computed coverage for all 9,384 pixels and threw away 95% of it. `clip_to_draw` now culls and clips the iteration up front, for `rrect_blit` and `ArcRing` alike. Output is provably unchanged: the writes outside the draw rect were already being culled, so only discarded work was removed.
  - **Narrowed the geometry updates the setters could not.** A TX bar's value moving it two pixels damaged its full height, because `setSize` and `setPosition` each damage old *and* new and the union is the taller bar; likewise an error pill's whole track. `bar_set_height` and `pill_set_width` write the rect directly and damage only the band that can have changed — the same idiom `ui_manager`'s `panel_set_pickable` uses to change a flag without invalidating, and safe for these particular widgets because they are plain leWidgets with no border, no resize handler and no picking.
  - **The gauge damages only the sector its leading edge sweeps**, like `widget_tilt`'s `damage_between`. Its span can reach 180°, where cos and sin are *not* monotonic across it, so the bounding box of the two endpoints alone would miss the top of the arc whenever the sector crosses 90° — the box is the union of the two endpoint caps plus any axis extremum strictly inside the range. Verified by brute force: across 69 angle pairs including 0→180, 90→91 and every axis crossing, **zero pixels that actually change fall outside the damage box**, median box 16% of the widget (and far less for a real per-second utilisation delta).
  - **Net, per refresh: 1,024,000 px → ~160,000, about 6× less** — and what remains is content that genuinely changed. **The sparkline is now the floor at ~75,000 px**: its line shifts one sample pitch every second, so every pixel of it really does move. `bus refresh <full|targeted>` toggles the old behaviour at runtime so the saving can be measured rather than asserted, and `bus probe` now also times the panel so the old cost has a number.
  - Left alone deliberately: `screen_system`'s detail-panel invalidate runs once per node tap and re-points the entire tree at a different node, so a full repaint is what it actually needs.

  **"Let's fix everything." Swept the rest of the graphics tree: the vector rasterizer is now gone from the UI entirely, and no repaint path does per-pixel float. NOT YET ON HARDWARE.**

  - **The bus screen was probably worse than the wiimotes screen ever was, and nothing had measured it** — every custom paint there runs on the **1 Hz refresh**, so cost is continuous rather than only-while-touched. Estimated before converting: the gauge's two `ArcStroke` over a ~200 px semicircle (and per `legato_vector_review.md`'s own correction 1, an arc scans the *full circle* regardless of span), plus **seven TX bars up to 46×204 through `VectorRectFill` — about 197 ms per refresh on the bars alone**. `bus probe [iters]` now measures the gauge, the sparkline and a TX bar, so this stops being an estimate.
  - **`AaShape_ArcRing`**: integer annulus, row-scanned from `u² ∈ [r_in² − v², r_out² − v²]`, two-toned by an angular wedge tested with **two cross products against a Q12 sine table** instead of the rasterizer's per-supersample `Atan2`. One pass colours both track and fill, so their seam is never blended twice — which stroking two arcs inherently does. Both the gauge and tilt use it; tilt's inline copy and its duplicate sine table are gone.
  - **`AaShape_RRect` gained per-corner radii**, which is what `panel_aa`'s round-top variant needs (a bottom-anchored bar rounds only its top). A square corner falls out of the same distance form as the straight edges.
  - **`video_frame` is now integer and byte-identical.** It fills its whole surface — 630×420 for each of two callers — with a float rounded-box SDF per pixel, at setup. That is pure boot time. Verified: **0/15 nibble difference** on the emitted ARGB4444 words.
  - **The sparkline's last `sqrtf` went too**, so no repaint path anywhere does per-pixel float. The rim's root is now `AaShape_Isqrt` on a 64ths ratio; max alpha difference **2/255** over 128,536 (numerator, denominator) combinations.
  - **A real bug the verification caught, and only the gauge could have caught it.** `ArcRing`'s row scan emits two x-intervals, left and right of centre — but when a row passes inside the inner radius they merge, and I emitted only the left arm, **silently dropping the entire right-hand half of the ring**. Tilt could not expose it: its quadrant is all on one side, so the missing pixels were angularly rejected anyway. Verifying against *both* callers' geometries is what found it. After the fix, six geometries (gauge at 0/62/100%, tilt at 0/45/90°) all agree with an exact float reference to **≤0.0149** with **zero wedge misclassifications**.
  - **Three float uses deliberately left, each with a reason:** `aa_corners`'s `accent_corner` solves a per-ray *ellipse*, so there is no fixed threshold to compare a squared distance against — and it costs ~15 ms once per System Info view change, not per repaint. `video_frame`'s float is only its signature. `song_list`'s is **inertial scroll physics** (px/ms velocity and a per-ms decay), a few operations per touch event; converting it would risk changing how the scroll feels for no measurable gain. Recorded so the next audit does not treat them as oversights.

  **All three wiimotes widgets measured after conversion — fret 21.8 → 9.0 ms, and the screen is done.**

  | widget | before | after | ratio |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | tilt 157×157 | 203 ms warm / 349 cold | **9.6 ms** | 21–36× |
  | whammy 273×64 | 116 ms | **16.0 ms** | 7.2× |
  | fret 112×64 | 21.8 ms | **9.0 ms** | 2.4× |

  - whammy is now the ceiling at ~735 cycles/px, which is four rounded-rect coverage queries plus blends. Added one early-out that helps exactly that shape of work: **reject on a single axis before squaring** — past the radius on x alone already puts d² beyond `r_out`, so two compares replace a multiply-add-compare. The whammy's thumb covers 16×40 of a 273×64 track, so that is the answer for the overwhelming majority of its pixels, and every widget compositing several shapes benefits. Re-verified: still **0 differences** against the dedicated capsule form and **0.0077** against the float SDF.
  - Worth recording what the whole arc was, because the first three attempts all missed: the cost was never the damaged area, never Legato's frame pipeline, and never the cache walk that looked so convincing. It was **per-pixel arithmetic on a core with neither an FPU nor a hardware integer divide** — first Legato's `Atan2`-per-supersample arc rasterizer, then soft float when I replaced it with analytic maths in `float`. Only integer coverage with a bit-by-bit `isqrt` and saturated cases resolved by comparison actually moved the numbers. Every conversion was verified against a float reference of the intended geometry before it went near hardware, and that harness caught two real bugs of mine (a truncated thumb position worth 27 RGB565 steps, and a half-pixel coverage offset in the tilt annulus) that no amount of code reading had.

  **Measured on hardware: tilt 203–349 → 8.8 ms (23–40×), whammy 116 → 16.6 ms. `fret` was then the worst at 21.8 ms, so it went too — and converting it collapsed all four shapes into one.**

  | widget | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | tilt 157×157 | 203 ms warm / 349 cold | **8.8 ms** |
  | whammy 273×64 | 116 ms | **16.6 ms** |
  | fret 112×64 | 21.8 ms | converted, unmeasured |

  - **The generalisation that fell out: every shape here is a rounded rectangle.** A capsule is one whose radius equals its shorter half-extent; a disc is one with no straight part. The skeleton is the inner rect inset by the radius, so distance-to-skeleton — and therefore coverage — is a single expression covering all three, with the round corners and the straight edges emerging from the same `max(q, 0)`. `AaRRect` + `AaShape_RRectSet/RRectCov` is now the core, `AaCapsule` is a typedef of it, and `AaShape_Capsule` / `_Disc` / `_RRect` are three ways to fill one rasteriser. That is a net *simplification* of aa_shape rather than another special case.
  - **Verified the refactor could not regress what already worked:** the unified form reproduces the dedicated capsule maths **exactly — 0 differences across 100,652 points** over ten shapes including both pill orientations and degenerate 2×2. Since whammy, the titlebar LED, the sparkline marker and tilt's caps all go through that path, this proves them unaffected rather than arguing it. The rounded rect itself matches an exact float SDF to **0.0077** across 44,074 pixels at six sizes, radius 0 to 12.
  - **What is left on the vector rasterizer, and it is no longer the UI's problem:** `widget_gauge` (two `ArcStroke`, bus screen, 1 Hz) and `panel_aa`'s `round_top_paint` (`RectFill` for the bus chart bars). Both are cold compared with anything on the wiimotes screen, and both now have a straightforward conversion available — the gauge is the same annulus-plus-wedge as tilt, the bars are `AaShape_RRect` with two corners.

  **whammy 116 → 16.4 ms on hardware ("wicked responsive"), and tilt converted — the last of the vector arc rasterizer on an interactive surface.**

  - **The tilt figure moving 203 → 349 ms is not a regression, it is the probe's new honesty.** Budgeting total lock time means tilt runs *one* iteration where it used to average twenty; that single sample is cold-cache, where the old mean was nineteen warm runs behind one cold one. True range ~203–349 ms.
  - **tilt rewritten as an integer annulus.** Row by row, the band's x-interval comes straight from `u² ∈ [r_in² − v², r_out² − v²]` with two `isqrt` per row, so it visits **26% of the widget** instead of scanning the whole clipped rect at 8 supersamples a pixel. Coverage is the distance from the mid-radius against the stroke half-width — one `isqrt` per pixel, no trigonometry at all.
  - **The angular test is the interesting part, and it needs no `atan2`.** A point is inside the fill wedge iff it is at or below the boundary ray, i.e. `cos(d)·v ≤ sin(d)·u` — one cross product against a 91-entry Q12 sine table (182 bytes of flash). That replaces the `leVector2_AngleOf` → `Normalize` + `Atan2` chain the rasterizer runs **per supersample**. The same monotonic test, binary-searched over 0..90 in seven comparisons, also replaced the `atan2f` in `value_from_point`, so the widget now holds no float whatsoever — table, `isqrt`, and integer compares.
  - Round caps and the thumb are discs, which needed a new primitive: `AaShape_Disc(cx, cy, r, …)` in half-pixel units, because the thumb is a 13 px rim around a 10.5 px body sharing one centre and **a `leRect` cannot express two radii of different parity about the same point**. `AaShape_Capsule` and it now share one rasteriser, and `AaShape_Isqrt` is public since three widgets need it.
  - **Verification caught a real bug of mine.** Against an exact float reference of the intended geometry the band coverage was out by **0.5010** — a half pixel. Cause: I wrote coverage as `hw − |d − r|` where every other analytic path in this tree uses the standard `hw + 0.5 − |d − r|`, so the band read half a pixel narrow on *both* edges. Fixed, and the annulus rejection widened by a half-pixel so the fringe is not clipped away. After: **max coverage difference 0.0145** (the 64ths quantisation) and **zero fill/track misclassifications across 59,535 pixels at nine angles** — the cross-product wedge test is exact.

  **`wiimotes probe` found the worst paints in the whole UI, by a wide margin. Whammy converted; tilt was next.**

  | widget | rect | full repaint | per pixel |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | whammy | 273×64 | **116 ms** | ~5300 cycles |
  | tilt | 157×157 | **203 ms** | ~6600 cycles |
  | fret[0] | 112×64 | 22 ms | ~2400 cycles |

  Against a plain panel fill at ~24 cycles/px. Greg: *"cpu utilization also goes to 90+% when I'm actively wiggling whammy or tilt"* — of course; a single tilt repaint is 203 ms of CPU, so a handful of drag events a second saturates the core.

  - **The probe locking the system up was my bug, now fixed.** Each iteration holds the render lock for one whole paint, so 20 iterations × 200 ms froze the UI for seconds. `RenderProbe_WidgetUs` now budgets the *total* lock time (250 ms) rather than the iteration count: cheap widgets still get 20 samples averaged, expensive ones degrade to a single measurement, which is where averaging matters least anyway.
  - **whammy converted to integer coverage, and the shared capsule maths is now a reusable API.** It was evaluating four soft-float rounded-rect SDFs plus three blends *per pixel* over the whole widget. `ui/gfx/aa_shape.h` gained `AaCapsule` + `AaShape_CapsuleSet/CapsuleCov/ColumnCov` — half-pixel integer units, coverage 0..`AA_COV_ONE`, composable with `(a·b)/AA_COV_ONE` — because whammy composites four overlapping capsules per pixel and so cannot use the blit form. Saturated coverage now writes opaquely instead of read-modify-lerping, which covers the interior of every shape. The two float helpers outside the paint (`damage_between`, `value_from_x`) went integer too, so `math.h` is gone from the file.
  - **Verification caught a real bug, and the first result was the useful one.** Comparing composited RGB565 against the float original gave a **27-step** difference — far too large for rounding. Cause: `thumbx = cx + (span·value)/100` truncates toward zero, losing up to half a pixel of thumb position, enough to land the rim edge a pixel out. Now rounded, which puts it on the nearest half pixel — finer than the 1.28 half-pixels the thumb moves per value step, so the quantisation sits below the control's own resolution. Re-verified with the float reference given the *same* quantised geometry, which isolates the coverage maths from that deliberate approximation: **1 RGB565 step across 183,436 pixels and 11 values**, and rendered side by side the two are indistinguishable.
  - **Still to do: tilt at 203 ms**, which is `leDraw_VectorArcStroke` ×2 + `leDraw_VectorArcFill` ×2 — the known-bad arc path, made worse by that file's own correction 1 (an arc scans the full circle at radius+halfwidth regardless of span). It needs an integer annulus with an angular test, and the angular test is the part that wants care: the span is a quadrant, so it can be done with cross-product sign checks against the two boundary rays rather than the `Atan2` the rasterizer uses per sample. Its two round caps and its thumb are just discs and can reuse `AaShape_Capsule`.

  **Three follow-ups done: corners now clip to the draw rect (a correctness fix), the tiles invalidate only what changed, and the wiimotes widgets are measurable.**

  1. **`AaCorners_Render` and `AaCorners_RenderRoundImage` cull and clip to the draw rect.** This closes the latent out-of-bounds write — the boxes went through the *unchecked* `leRenderer_PutPixel`, so any damage rect smaller than the widget sent them writing past the scratch buffer. A box entirely outside the rect now returns immediately, and a partially overlapping one iterates the clipped region only. The backdrop sample moved to the *clipped* box's outer pixel so that read stays in bounds too; when the clip excludes the arc's outside entirely, every surviving pixel saturates and bg is never used. `AaCorners_RenderSurface565` is left alone deliberately — it writes a caller-owned surface, not the render buffer, so the draw rect does not apply.
  2. **`tiles_refresh` invalidates the value label and the plot, not the whole card.** The caption and the unit are static strings. Checked rather than assumed: the value spans card-x 12..44 and the plot 66..145, while the radius-12 corner boxes occupy 0..11 and 146..157 — so neither rect, **nor their merged bounding box** (Legato may coalesce them), touches a corner. A tile refresh therefore skips four of the six glyphs, all four antialiased corners, and drops fill from 6636 px to 2966 (44%). Only safe because of (1).
  3. **`wiimotes probe [iters]`** times the whammy, tilt and a fret, so the last per-pixel float paints on an interactive surface stop being guesswork. The timing loop moved out of `titlebar.c` into `ui/gfx/render_probe.c` (`RenderProbe_WidgetUs`) so any screen can use it; the wiimotes side follows the `HealthMonitor_Report` line-sink shape the codebase already uses, and gates on `UiManager_BaseCanvas()` since that screen keeps no shown flag. `render_probe.h` documents how to read a result — subtract a contentless rect of the same size — and the two caveats that matter: **the numbers move with DDR contention** (the render lock suspends Legato and input but not the capture DMA; the fixed cost has read 82 µs and 165 µs on the same build), and a data-dependent widget costs a different amount every run.

  **Result of the corner rewrite, and the answer to "are the soft floats all gone": no, and the audit is worth keeping.** plot 3764 → **2498 µs**, card 7593 → **6330 µs**. Grepping our own graphics tree for float:
  - **`ui/widgets/whammy/widget_whammy.c` — 50 float references inside a per-pixel scan loop, on a widget the wiimotes screen drags interactively.** Untouched, unmeasured, and the most suspicious thing left in the UI. `widget_tilt` and `widget_gauge` are still on the arc rasterizer besides.
  - **`ui/gfx/aa_shape.c` was still fully float per pixel** — my own inconsistency, converted in this pass with the same half-pixel/`isqrt32` treatment. Verified against the float version over 25,870 pixel/alpha combinations across discs, both pill orientations and five alpha levels: **maximum alpha difference 2/255**, which in RGB565 usually cannot change the output value at all.
  - Cold and deliberately left: `video_frame.c` (per-pixel, but filled once at setup), `song_list` (per item), `aa_corners`'s `accent_corner` (needs the per-ray ellipse), the sparkline's one `sqrtf` per rim pixel.

  **What the numbers now say the card is made of, and it is not what I would have guessed: `card − plot` = 3832 µs is essentially the three text labels.** The extra fill area accounts for ~132 µs at the measured 30 ns/px, leaving ~3700 µs for six glyphs — about 600 µs each, inside Legato's string renderer rather than our code. Text is now 58% of a tile refresh.

  **On prerendering (Greg's suggestion) — right idea, but the corners are no longer where it pays.** Caching finished corner pixels keyed on (radius, borderWidth, fill, border, bg) would work well (every card shares those five values, 1152 B per entry) and remove the remaining per-pixel work, but that is only a few hundred µs now. **The high-value form of the same idea is to stop repainting what did not change:** the caption and the unit are *static* strings, yet all six glyphs, all four corners and 6636 px of fill repaint every second because `tiles_refresh` invalidates the whole card, when only the value text and the plot actually move. Invalidating just those two rects would drop four of the six glyphs, all four corners and ~4000 px of fill from the per-second cost.
  - **The blocker is a real bug, and fixing it is worth doing regardless of speed:** `AaCorners_Render` writes through `leRenderer_PutPixel`, the variant with **no bounds check**, so a damage rect smaller than the widget makes the corner boxes write outside the scratch buffer. That is very likely why every screen in this codebase invalidates whole cards. The rewrite above already localises the geometry, so adding a draw-rect test is small — and it unlocks partial invalidation everywhere, not just here.

  **`aa_corners` was hiding inside both figures, which is why narrowing the polyline's rows changed nothing.** Greg: *"not much difference. I want to fix aa_corners."* Right on both counts, and the second explains the first — **`AaCorners_Render` writes through `leRenderer_PutPixel`, the *unculled* variant, so it repaints all four corner boxes regardless of the damage rect.** Damaging the plot marks the card dirty (the card intersects it), so every `plot` probe was also paying the card's 576 px of soft-float corners. That is why halving the polyline's visited pixels moved nothing, and it means the corner cost sat in the `plot`, the `card`, and every card and button on every screen.
  - **Rewritten in integers with the saturated cases resolved by comparison.** Half-pixel units make the `r - m - 0.5` offsets exact odd integers, so a squared distance is integer and the two saturated coverages are comparisons against `(2·ra ∓ 1)²`. Three cases then need no arithmetic at all: **outside the outer arc the answer is `bg`, which the pixel already holds, so the write is skipped entirely**; inside the inner arc it is `fill`; between the arcs it is `border`. Only the two ~1 px bands take a square root, and that is now a bit-by-bit `isqrt32` rather than a soft-float `sqrtf`. Per radius-12 bordered corner: **144 `sqrtf` + 288 `leColorLerp` → 38 `isqrt` + 76 lerps, with 23 pixels not written at all.**
  - **Verified by comparing resulting RGB565 pixels, not coverage weights**, across radii 2–45 and border widths 0–2 with the real card colours — 13,958 pixels, **maximum per-channel difference 1 RGB565 step** (R/B are 1/31 of range). That residue is the coverage quantisation to 1/256 before `leColorLerp`'s integer percent, i.e. the same rounding the float version already did; rendered side by side the two are indistinguishable. An int32-overflow assert was armed throughout, which is what bounds the radius at 45.
  - `AaCorners_RenderRoundImage` (bus badges, album-art overlay) and `AaCorners_RenderSurface565` (the modal corner cut) got the same treatment. **`AaCorners_RenderLeftEdge` deliberately keeps the float path**: its inner boundary is an ellipse whose radius is solved per ray, so there is no fixed threshold to compare against — and it is the coldest of them, one call per node card on System Info.

  **Integer polyline measured: 7074 → 3764 µs (1.9×), and I had predicted 10×. Third wrong prediction, same root cause — I keep modelling per-pixel cost and the model keeps being ~10× optimistic.** 3764 µs over 1628 pixels visited is ~1850 cycles a pixel for what is ~10 integer operations plus an occasional write, so something in the per-pixel path still dominates and I cannot name it from reading. **Stopped predicting and went structural instead:** the row range is now narrowed per column to the rows the candidate segments can actually reach, which halves the pixels visited (816 vs 1628, measured offline) and is correct regardless of what the per-pixel cost turns out to be — a pixel further than `t_out` from every candidate's y-extent is further than the stroke in y alone. Re-verified bit-exact against the float brute force over 300 random plus 4 edge series: **0.000000**.

  **Where this leaves the titlebar: 13.7% → ~1.5%, and it is now diminishing returns.** Per part, the two remaining items are the plot and `card − plot` (~3829 µs: `AaCorners_Render`, three labels, the fills), and the second is now the bigger half. Two things worth more than further sparkline work:
  1. **`aa_corners.c`, which is the whole-UI item.** It runs `sqrtf` plus float coverage maths on *every* pixel of *every* corner box — 576 px per radius-12 card — and it is on every card and button on every screen. Same fix shape as the polyline: integer squared-distance tests against the outer and inner arcs, `sqrtf` only on the ~1 px band. Riskier than the polyline (an inner arc, an outer arc, and a border colour to blend between), so it wants the same brute-force verification.
  2. **`SAMPLE_MS` 1000 → 2000**, which halves the tile cost outright for the price of a 48 s window instead of 24 s. One constant, no visual loss, available any time.

  **And the analytic rewrite in `float` bought almost nothing — 7894 → 7074 µs — because this core has no FPU. Redone in integers. THE REAL LESSON OF THE WHOLE SESSION.** I predicted 10–20× and got 10%, for a reason that is obvious in hindsight and that invalidates a premise I had been carrying since the `AaShape_Capsule` fix: **`float` is not cheap here.** Every operation is a libgcc soft-float call at ~80 cycles, so ~16 float ops per (pixel, segment) × 2.5 segments × 1628 pixels ≈ 6.5 ms — which is what was measured. Swapping the vector rasterizer's fixed-point supersampling for soft-float was close to a wash.
  - It also explains the capsule: the dot came out at **339 µs where I had estimated ~35**, and 144 px × 16 float ops × 80 cycles = 230 µs accounts for it. That fix was still a 17× win, but for the wrong reason — the gain was dropping 8 supersamples per pixel, not "analytic is cheap".
  - **`paint_polyline` is now entirely integer.** Half-pixel units keep pixel centres on integers; comparing squared distances *pre-multiplied by* |d|² removes the per-pixel projection divide entirely; the interior and exterior tests are integer comparisons against per-segment thresholds. The only float left is one `sqrtf` per rim pixel — about 120 of the plot's 1628.
  - Range analysis, since this is where an integer rewrite goes wrong silently: in half-pixel plot-local units |w| ≤ 160 and |d| ≤ 90, so |w|²·len2 ≤ 6.7e7 and cross² ≤ 8.3e8, both inside int32. Only the rim's min-across-segments comparison needs int64, and only for those ~120 pixels. **Verified against a float brute-force reference over 300 random series plus flat/zero/full/alternating edge cases: maximum coverage difference 0.000000, with an int32-overflow assert armed throughout.**
  - **Carry this forward:** `aa_corners.c` is float per pixel too (`sqrtf` plus float coverage maths on every pixel of every corner box), which is very likely most of the remaining `card − plot` cost, and it is on every card and button in the UI. The fix there is the same shape — integer squared-distance tests, `sqrtf` only on the arc band.

  **Attribution measured, and the polyline was 67% of it. Replaced with analytic coverage (first in float — see above).** `titlebar probe` with per-part timing:

  | part | total | drawing (total − same-size empty rect) |
  |---|---|---|
  | dot 12×12 | 427 µs | **339 µs** (was 5740 — 17× better) |
  | plot 80×28 | 8047 µs | **7894 µs** ← the target |
  | card 158×42 | 12026 µs | 11737 µs |

  So `card − plot` = 3979 µs is the fill, AA corners and three labels; the sparkline alone was 7894 µs. Per segment that is 343 µs ≈ 1760 cycles/px over a ~156 px bounding box — 18× cheaper per pixel than the arc path, as predicted, but there are 23 of them and their boxes overlap, so the rasterizer shaded ~1.6× the plot's area at 8 supersamples each.

  - **`paint_polyline` now shades the plot rect once, each pixel from its distance to the *nearest* segment.** Taking the minimum over segments rather than stroking them one at a time is what makes the joins correct — the union of round-capped strokes *is* the set of points within the half-width of the polyline — and it means each pixel is blended exactly once, where per-segment drawing double-blends the rim wherever two boxes overlap (the old code relied on the caps overlapping and accepted that).
  - **The candidate-segment window is provably free.** Vertices are x-monotonic and evenly spaced, so the segments that can reach a pixel column are a short contiguous run found by two forward-only indices. Verified offline against brute force — every segment for every pixel — over **200 random series: maximum coverage difference 0.000000**. Worth doing, because a windowing bug here would show as an occasional missing pixel that no static reading would catch.
  - Only ~350 of the plot's 1628 pixels are shaded at all, at 2–3 distance evaluations each with a `sqrtf` only in the ~1 px rim, against the rasterizer's ~28,700 supersample evaluations. `LE_ANTIALIASING_8X` and `leDraw_Vector*` are now gone from the sparkline entirely.
  - The 800-byte segment table is `static`, not stack: LEGATO_Tasks has 4 KB, and Legato paints one widget at a time on one task while the only other caller of the painter holds the render lock that suspends it — so the two can never overlap.

  **What was left before that change, and why the next step was a measurement rather than an optimisation.** All the remaining titlebar cost is the 1 Hz tile refresh (2 cards × ~15–17 ms ≈ 3.4% of the CPU). Adding up everything I can account for in one card — 84 µs fixed, 411 µs of fill at the measured 31 ns/px, ~580 µs of AA corners, ~2.1 ms of vector polyline, ~750 µs of end marker (now removed), ~300 µs of glyphs — reaches **4.2 ms against 15–17 ms measured**. A 3–4× gap means the estimates are not to be trusted, which is the same mistake twice already this session. So `titlebar probe` now also times the real parts (dot / plot / card) and subtracts a contentless rect of the same size, giving each one's drawing cost directly. **Run that before changing anything.**
  - Candidates, in the order I would take them once the numbers say which matters:
    1. **The AA-corner early-out**, and this one is a whole-UI win rather than a titlebar one. `AaCorners_Render` calls `sqrtf` for *every* pixel of every corner box (576 px per radius-12 card), but most are unambiguously inside or outside the arc — the same squared-distance test that `AaShape_Capsule` now uses would cut it to the ~1 px band. Every card on every screen goes through this (`PanelAA_Enable`, `ButtonAA_Enable`).
    2. **The sparkline polyline.** 23 `leDraw_VectorLine` calls at `LE_ANTIALIASING_8X`. The line evaluator is much cheaper than the arc one — `leVector2_Rotate` uses only `LE_REAL_I16_MULTIPLY`, which is a 64-bit multiply and a *shift*, where the arc's `Atan2`/`Normalize` use `LE_REAL_I16_DIVIDE`, a real 64-bit division — so this is not the same bug. Either drop `aaMode` to 4X/2X (the review's own suggested lever) or go analytic distance-to-segment as the capsule did.
    3. **Halve the sample rate.** `SAMPLE_MS` 1000 → 2000 halves the tile cost outright, and costs only that the 24-sample window covers 48 s instead of 24 s. One constant, no visual loss, and the mockup's 800–1100 ms was never a requirement.
  - Not available: **partial invalidation of a card.** Only the plot and the value change, but `AaCorners_Render` writes through `leRenderer_PutPixel`, which does *not* cull to the draw rect (unlike `GetPixel`), so a damage rect smaller than the widget would let the corner boxes write outside the scratch buffer. That is likely why the screens all invalidate whole cards. Worth a separate look — it is a latent out-of-bounds write, not just a missed optimisation.

  **CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE: `LEGATO_Tasks` 13% → bouncing 1–6%.** The probe reads identically before and after (`84 µs` fixed, `31 ns/px`), which is the right answer and a check on the instrument — it damages a widget that paints nothing, so a fix to the *dot* could not possibly move it.
  - **The 1–6% spread is a sampling artifact, not instability.** The tiles repaint once a second in a single burst; `health` averages over 500 ms, so the reading depends on whether that burst lands inside the window. The pulse, at 10 Hz × ~120 µs, is now noise.
  - **Follow-through: the analytic capsule is now shared, and the last `ArcFill` in the titlebar path is gone.** The sparkline's end marker was still calling `leDraw_VectorArcFill` — the identical pathological path, just smaller. Both callers now use `ui/gfx/aa_shape.c` (`AaShape_Capsule`), which is where the coverage maths and the whole explanation live; `panel_aa`'s `dot_paint` is nine lines again. The marker's diameter went from 5 px (r=2.5) to `2×(thickness+1)` = 6 px, deliberately even so it centres exactly on the vertex — vertices sit on integer coordinates, which are pixel *edges*, so an odd diameter is inherently half a pixel off.
  - **Still on the arc-fill path and still unmeasured: `widget_tilt` (two `ArcStroke` + two `ArcFill`) and `widget_gauge` (two `ArcStroke`).** Both were migrated ahead of the dot on `legato_vector_review.md` §6's advice, both are arc-based, and tilt scans 254×254 per that file's own correction 1. The wiimotes screen drags the tilt thumb interactively, which is exactly where 32,000 cycles/px would be felt — and the 2026-07 drag-latency entry measured lag on that screen and attributed it to "paint cost per frame". Worth pointing the same probe at them.

  **RESOLVED — it was never Legato's frame pipeline. It was our own antialiased circle: `leDraw_VectorArcFill` costs ~32,000 cycles per pixel on this core.** With the fixed probe:

  | damage | painted content | cost |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1×1 | panel fill | **86 µs** |
  | 12×12 | panel fill (probe widget) | **88 µs** |
  | 12×12 | panel fill + the status LED's AA disc | **5828 µs** |
  | 260×40 | panel fill | 412 µs |

  Legato's per-frame overhead is **86 µs** and its fill is **31 ns/px** — both entirely reasonable, and both about 70× smaller than the 5.8 ms I had been attributing to them for two rounds. The 5.7 ms difference between the two 12×12 rows is one radius-6 disc.

  - **Mechanism.** `_arcTest` in `legato_vector_arc_fill.c` runs, **per supersample**, `leVector2_AngleOf` → `leVector2_Normalize` (software square root **plus** a 64-bit divide) and `leReal_i16_Atan2` (more 64-bit divides). `LE_REAL_I16_MULTIPLY`/`DIVIDE` are 64-bit multiply-then-divide, and the ARM926 has **neither an FPU nor a hardware integer divide**, so each is a libgcc call. At `LE_ANTIALIASING_8X` that is 8 samples × ~5 such operations per pixel: 144 px → 1,152 evaluations → 5.7 ms.
  - **Fix, in our tree: `dot_paint` reverted to analytic coverage** — distance to the capsule's spine, with the interior and the outside settled by squared-distance comparison so only the ~1 px rim pays for a `sqrtf`. A 12×12 dot goes from 1,152 supersample evaluations to **36 `sqrtf` calls**; expected ~35 µs against 5,740. One code path covers disc and both pill orientations (the spine degenerates to a point when the widget is square), verified offline against the analytic area — 112.82 vs π·6² = 113.10 for the dot, and both pill orientations within 0.5 px².
  - **This is not just the titlebar's LED. It is every `PanelAA_EnableDot` in the UI** — the bus screen's per-node status dots *and* its error pill tracks (≈20 shapes, repainted at 1 Hz), the system screen's bullets, the nav footer. That alone could have been double-digit CPU on the bus screen.
  - **The 2026-08-06 vector-rasterizer migration is the origin, and this document asked for exactly this measurement and did not get it.** `legato_vector_review.md` §6 recommended moving the `panel_aa` dot (and `tilt`, and `gauge`) onto `leDraw_Vector*`, noting the cost was "a plausible win, not a certain one" that "should be measured with `marvin-perf` before and after". It shipped unmeasured. Correction added at the top of that file. **`widget_tilt` and `widget_gauge` were migrated first, are arc-based, and scan far larger rects (tilt 254×254 per that file's own correction 1) — they are unmeasured and now suspect.** `widget_fret` uses `RectFill`, which has no `Atan2` in its inner loop, so it is not implicated.
  - **The model now closes properly:** `3.85% baseline + 10/s × (86 µs frame + 5740 µs disc + 1619 µs cache walk) + ~1.7% tiles` = **13.0% against 13.7% measured**, with the remainder being the tiles' own AA corners and sparkline segments.

  **Patch #14 landed and bought ~1.6 ms/frame, not the ~7.4 ms predicted — the prediction was wrong and the agreement that supported it was a coincidence.** On hardware: `dot 12x12` **7447 → 5828 µs**. So the whole-buffer cache walk was real but cost `1619 µs / 64,000 lines` = **25 ns ≈ 20 cycles per line**, not the ~100 cycles guessed. That ~100 was what made the estimate land on 8.0 ms next to a measured 7.45 ms, and the match was read as confirmation when it was two independent errors cancelling. The lesson is the one this journal keeps relearning: an order-of-magnitude estimate agreeing with a measurement is not a mechanism, and **~5.8 ms of fixed per-frame cost is still unexplained.** The patch stays — it is correct, it is a real saving on every repaint in the UI, and it is now the *smaller* half of the problem.

  **The probe itself was flawed, which is why the card number moved the wrong way** (15153 → 17685 µs). It damaged a metric card, whose paint cost depends on the *shape* of the sparkline that second — 23 antialiased segments whose bounding boxes change with the data, worth milliseconds of swing between runs. So the "1186 ns/px" figure measured the CPU load history, not the renderer. Replaced with an **invisible, resizable probe widget** parked in the clear span between the PIC logo and the first tile: transparent and contentless, so a damage rect on it paints only the parent panel's background fill and costs strictly `fixed + k·area`. `titlebar probe` now sweeps 1×1 / 12×12 / 64×32 / 260×40 and reports the intercept and the slope. The 1×1 point is the one that matters — it is as close to pure fixed overhead as this can get.

  **Where the remaining ~5.8 ms is not:** `updateWidgets` (the probe calls `leRenderer_Paint` directly, so the 100 Hz walk is excluded by construction), and any other whole-buffer cache maintenance (grepped — `drv_gfx2d.c` was the only site in the whole gfx and driver tree). What is left inside one `leRenderer_Paint` is the 9-layer walk with two driver ioctls each, `preRect`'s `invalidateWidget` pass over the damaged layer's **entire** widget tree (plus `_validateChildren` over every non-intersecting subtree), `findDirtyWidget` restarting from the layer root once per painted widget, `postRect`'s GFX2D copy and its `while(busy)` spin, and `postFrame`'s 9-layer rect-array management.
  - **Free next measurement, no reflash: run `titlebar probe` on different base views.** The tree-walk hypothesis predicts the fixed cost tracks the layer's widget count — the bus screen carries ~250 widgets against the dashboard's ~100 and System Info's ~130. If the intercept is flat across all three, it is the per-layer/ioctl path instead, and the widget tree is exonerated.
  - Noticed while reading and worth its own look: `plib_gfx2d.c`'s instruction-queue writer **drops the instruction and returns 1 if the GPU is busy**, and `DRV_GFX2D_Blit` returns `GFX_SUCCESS` regardless — so a contended blit can be silently skipped. Not a cost, a correctness smell.

  **Greg chose 3, and it is applied** as re-apply patch #14 (see the list at the bottom of this file) — narrowed to *both* rects, not just the destination, since the source clean is equally over-broad and equally load-bearing for the cached `leImage` blit path. Expected on hardware: `titlebar probe` dot falls from ~7447 µs to tens of µs, the pulse from ~7.85% to ~0.1%, and **every** repaint in the UI gets ~7.4 ms cheaper — dashboard telemetry, drawer slides, touch-drag frames, screen transitions. NOT YET CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE; the numbers above are the prediction the probe will check.
- **Unrelated but confirmed by the same run: bus util reads 0.0%**, so the T1S tile is a flat line on real traffic — the open question from the first entry is now answered, and it needs either a scale change or the honest acceptance that an idle bus looks idle.

**Same evening — `titlebar` wasn't in `help`, and the reason was a silent CLI cap that had already been eating three existing commands. Then the obvious fix turned out to be a trap: `configASSERT` is compiled out in this build.** Greg: *"did we run out of cli slots? titlebar not in commands"*. Yes — and worse than one slot.

- **`cfg->maxBindingCount` was hard-coded at 26 next to a table that had grown to 29.** `embeddedCliAddBinding` returns `false` when the pool is full and `register_commands` discarded it with `(void)`, so **`gamma`, `qspi` and `settings` have been unreachable at the prompt for as long as they have existed** — registered in code, absent from `help`, no diagnostic anywhere. `titlebar` was simply the fourth casualty. Worth noting the `gamma` command is cited in an earlier entry as the A/B tool for the HEO levels work; whatever was concluded there was not concluded with that command.
- **Fixed by deriving the pool from the table**, not by bumping the number: `bindings[]` moves to file scope and `maxBindingCount = CMD_COUNT`, so the two cannot drift again. The CLI buffer went 1024 → 2048 B in the same breath — the binding pool dominates it at ~20 B/command on this 32-bit target, and 1024 left only ~40 B of slack, i.e. the *next* command after this one would have overflowed it.
- **And overflowing it was not benign, which is the real find: `configASSERT` expands to nothing here.** `FreeRTOSConfig.h` never defines it, so FreeRTOS's `#ifndef configASSERT → #define configASSERT( x )` wins. `Console_Initialize`'s two guards — `configASSERT(embeddedCliRequiredSize(cfg) <= sizeof(s_cli_buf))` and `configASSERT(s_cli != NULL)` — were therefore both no-ops, and the path they were meant to guard ends in `s_cli->writeChar = …` on a NULL pointer: **a boot data abort with nothing logged**, which is precisely the failure shape the 2026-07 freeze hunt spent days on. Both are now real `if` statements that log at ERROR and leave the console disabled (recoverable) rather than dereferencing.
- **The trap: wrapping the fix in `configASSERT` deletes the code.** The first attempt was `configASSERT(embeddedCliAddBinding(s_cli, bindings[i]))`, which expands to nothing at all — **every command silently unregistered**. Caught by checking the object file for the symbol (`nm | grep AddBinding` → not referenced) rather than by trusting that it built clean, which it did. Failure is reported via `LOG_ERROR` instead, since that is always on.
- **Open, and Greg's call: there are 58 `configASSERT`s in our own tree and every one is dead.** Several are load-bearing in the sense that the journal describes them as catching pool exhaustion "at bring-up" — `configASSERT(s_nlbl < LBL_MAX)` in every screen's `add_label`, the widget pools, `s_rx_notify != NULL`. They do nothing, so an undersized pool is a silent out-of-bounds write into BSS, not a stop. Defining `configASSERT` would turn all 58 live at once, and a hard stop on a booth demo may be worse than the overrun — so this is a decision, not a cleanup. A middle option is a project macro that logs at ERROR and continues.

- **If it lands on mechanism A, the lever is already documented** in the 2026-07 drag-latency entry: the two `vTaskDelay(10)` loops in the generated `tasks.c`. That entry wanted them *halved* for input latency; the walk cost wants them *raised*. Worth knowing those two pull in opposite directions before either is touched.

---

**2026-08-07 (evening) — the titlebar gains the mockup's two sparkline tiles and a pulsing status LED, and the render lock had to become a real lock to allow it. BUILDS CLEAN; NOT ON HARDWARE YET.** From the `~/Downloads/Marvin` mockup's `App.tsx` header — `CpuWidget` + `BusWidget` (its `LatencyWidget` is defined but never rendered, so it was skipped) and the dot's `animate-pulse`. Everything else the mockup does differently was left alone, as asked.

- **The sparkline widget was single-instance with a global ring buffer; it is now a series plus a derived widget.** `SparklineSeries` holds the samples, `SparklineWidget` embeds an `leWidget` as its first member and points at a series — the shared paint override reaches its style by casting, the way Legato's own widgets derive, so there is no side table keyed by widget pointer and no instance cap. That split is what the titlebar needs: **one history plotted in five places at once** (there is a titlebar per base-view screen — dashboard, wiimotes, bus, system overview, system detail), where a per-widget buffer would leave the four off-screen copies stale and make every screen switch arrive with a half-drawn plot.
- **Only the on-screen instance is repainted, and the screens say which that is.** `Titlebar_SetShown(bar, shown)` is called from each screen's existing shown/hidden hook. Deriving it instead from `UiManager_BaseCanvas()` was the alternative and it loses one case: fullscreen video, where the dashboard's canvas is still the base canvas but is completely covered, and `screen_video.c` already routes that through `ScreenDashboard_SetShown(false)`. Hidden canvases are still *rendered* by Legato whether or not they are bound to a hardware layer, so repainting them costs real DDR bandwidth to write pixels nobody can see. `shown = false` is ignored unless the caller owns the current instance, so the incoming screen can't be switched off by the outgoing one whichever order the two land in.
- **`UiManager_RenderLock` is now serialized by a mutex, and this was a latent bug the new caller would have exposed.** It works by `vTaskSuspend`ing LEGATO_Tasks + SYS_INPUT_Tasks, and suspend/resume are **not counted** — with two holders inside at once, the first `Unlock` resumes the renderer while the second is still mid-edit, i.e. Legato paints a tree being mutated. It was safe with two callers because the dashboard feed and the bus screen are mutually exclusive (one base view is shown at a time); the titlebar tick runs alongside *whichever* screen owns the base view, so it overlaps both. Fixed in `ui_manager` rather than by giving the titlebar its own scheme, since the hazard belongs to the lock.
- **Colours come from existing schemes, so this needed no MGS Generate.** The tiles want the mockup's cyan `#22D3EE`, violet `#A78BFA`, orange `#FB923C` and red `#F87171`, and both the sparkline and the AA shapes fill from a scheme's **BASE** — a slot only the `SCHEME_NODE_*` set carries an accent in (the `SCHEME_TEXT_*` ones colour `text` and leave `base` grey). `NODE_MARVIN`, `NODE_FAUXMOTE`, `NODE_FRETBOARD` and `NODE_LIGHTSHOW` are those four colours exactly, so they are used as mono colour carriers — the same trick, and the same wart, as the bus screen's gauge. Value text uses `TEXT_CYAN_400` / `TEXT_VIOLET_400` / `TEXT_TIER_6` / `TEXT_TIER_7`, which already hold the matching four.
- **The LED pulse rides Legato's own per-widget alpha rather than a new widget.** `PanelAA_EnableDot`'s paint now draws its capsule at `getCumulativeAlphaAmount()` instead of a hard 255, so the tick is one `setAlphaAmount` per step — and `setAlphaAmount` only invalidates on a *change*, so a still frame costs nothing. Three things make it correct rather than lucky: the vector rasterizer multiplies coverage by the attr alpha, so the antialiased edge stays right at every level; the damage walk marks **every** widget intersecting the rect dirty from the layer root down, so the opaque panel behind repaints first and the fade never accumulates; and the renderer's own global-alpha state is not consulted by `leRenderer_BlendPixel`, so there is no double blend. Every existing dot is unaffected — alpha defaults to 255 and the flag is off.
- **Tailwind's `animate-pulse` in integers:** a triangle over a 2 s period, smoothstepped as `x²(3−2x)` in Q8 (`(x*x*(768-2*x)) >> 16`), mapped to alpha 255 → 128 → 255. 100 ms tick = 20 steps a period. The tick period is the *pulse's* step, not the sample's: metrics move once a second (the health supervisor's own cadence).
- **CPU% is real, published from the health supervisor rather than computed again.** `supervisor_sample` already derives the idle share every second for its runaway check, so `HealthMonitor_CpuPermille()` is `1000 − idle`, one word, no new sampling. ISR time counts as load, which is what a CPU meter should say. T1S is `T1SLink_GetBusStats().util_permille`. **Both tiles are live telemetry — no simulator**, deliberately: unlike the bus screen, which can label itself SIMULATED in its UPTIME tile, chrome that is on every screen has nowhere to put that caveat.
- **The plots autoscale to their own window with a floor, and plot 24 of the 40 samples.** Two decisions that only show up at 28 px tall: a fixed 0–100% scale flattens every real signal into a straight line, but pure autoscale amplifies an idle bus's noise into a mountain range — hence a 15-percentage-point minimum span, with a flat signal centred in it rather than pinned to an edge. And the *pitch* is what decides whether a plot reads as a line or a comb: 40 samples across the 74 px of plot is under 2 px each, so the widget grew a `window` (view) property and the tiles plot the newest 24, ~3.2 px each — the mockup's own proportion (24 samples / 72 px).
- **Geometry was checked by rendering the tile offline at 8× with the firmware's own integer maths** (`value_window` / `sample_to_x` / `value_to_y` / the area ramp, in Pillow) before touching the panel — the same reason the QR rasterizer has a host test. That is what confirmed the pitch problem was real and that the min-span floor makes an idle bus read as a calm low line. Numbers: tile 156×42 at y=5 (`12 + 40 + 12 + 80 + 12`), plot 80×28 at x+64, caption/value/unit boxes 12/22/16 px tall (their fonts' own heights, per the `fit_font` lesson), value and unit baselines both landing on card-relative y=36. The two tiles + `mx-2` divider occupy x 700..1028, leaving 291 px of clear bar between them and the PIC logo.
- **Open, and only hardware answers it:** whether CPU on this board sits pinned near 100% with capture + Legato running, in which case the 50/75% colour thresholds are decoration and want retuning — and whether an idle T1S bus reads low enough to look inert at a booth. The number would still be honest; a simulator behind a visible marker is the fallback if it looks dead.

**2026-08-07 (later still) — the project card gets its own shape: one full-height column-2 card about how the firmware was written. BUILDS CLEAN; NOT ON HARDWARE YET.** Greg wanted guitar-pic to look unlike the seven board pages, keeping the photo, the QR and the bus rail but replacing the middle with one large box, and to use it for the AI-assisted-development story.

- **The switch is a field, not a branch: an entry with `story` set gets one 680×620 card instead of the what-it-does + parts-list pair.** 19 body rows against the pair's 8, same font and pitch, so the wrap limit stays `PROSE_COLS`. Boards are described by what they do and what is on them; the project by how it was made, which is what visitors actually ask about.
- **Each of the three cards now owns its caption and body as children, in card-relative coordinates.** They were siblings of the panel, so hiding a card would have left its text floating over the replacement — the switch has to be one `setVisible` on the card. The QR slot already worked this way (`hiding the card hides the whole slot in one call`), so this follows the pattern rather than inventing one. Both text sets are written on every entry and the unused one lands in hidden labels: a few string copies, and no second branch through `show_detail`.
- **`LBL_MAX` 96 → 128.** The story card adds 20 labels to about 79 already in use, so 96 would have tripped `add_label`'s `configASSERT` at bring-up — i.e. a boot-time assert on the demo, not a compile error. Counted rather than guessed: 41 in the overview (eight cards × ≤5, plus the headline) and 58 in the detail tree. Widgets stay at 46 of 64, buttons 9 of 12, images 1 of 3.
- **Copy is deliberately concrete and carries no metric.** Four paragraphs: the agent beside MPLAB reading datasheets and explaining changes; per-subproject journals as durable memory so a session resumes rather than restarts; the human hardware loop, with offline proof in Python where the maths allows; and two real bugs found by reading rather than guessing — the white pressed button from an unset scheme slot, and the boot hang from code landing in uncached memory. A "written N% by AI" line was considered and dropped: unverifiable, and weaker at a booth than a specific bug.
  - Greg's call on one wording: it opens "**Every** board in this rig was programmed with an AI coding agent". Flagged first that `beatbox` arrived from `dspicguitarhero` and `fauxmote` predates this work, so a hedge was available; he confirmed the stronger claim.
- Validated the way the rest of this screen's content is: 18 lines of 19, longest 57 of 57 columns, all ASCII, and the 19th row's bottom at y=584 inside a 620-tall card.
- Also in this change, from Greg: **fretboard's parts list drops `MCP1804 LDO + MCP16301 buck`** — not that board's power.
