**2026-08-07 (later) — the QR slot is wired, and the codes are generated on the panel rather than pre-rendered anywhere. NOT ON HARDWARE YET.** All eight cards carry one now, so the `product` flag is gone: the QR slot is gated on `qr_url != NULL`, and the URL lives beside the part number in the `NODE` table.

- **Generated at setup, not baked, even though there are only five distinct images.** Five unique URLs cover the eight cards (four PIC32CM nodes share one), which looks like an argument for shipping five PNGs. It loses anyway: baking them means either an MGS Generate in the loop or a `tools/` script plus SD-card files plus a size contract — an asset pipeline for something that is a pure function of a string. And *this morning's* entry is the argument: `process_node_photos.py` silently dropped `guitar-pic` because its list didn't mirror the firmware table. A QR has the worse version of that failure, because a stale or mismatched tile still *looks* like a valid QR. Encoding costs ~12 KB of flash and sub-millisecond per tile on an 800 MHz core, so there was nothing to buy back.
- **The version is pinned to 4 (33 modules), and that is load-bearing.** Given a version *range* qrcodegen picks the smallest that fits, so a shortened URL would silently produce a 29-module code — which, dropped into a tile whose size the layout constants are derived from, is a layout bug you find on hardware. Pinning min == max instead makes an over-long URL fail the encode: verified at the boundary, 62 bytes encodes and 63 returns false. `boostEcl` then lifts the ECC level for the URLs with room to spare, so the shorter ones get more error correction at the same module count for free. The longest URL in the table is 58 bytes.
- **164 px at 4 px/module, not fitted to the old 192 px box — and the box was wrong anyway.** Resampling a QR is how you make one that won't scan, so the card holds the tile at 1:1 and the layout is derived from the rasterizer (`QR_IMG` ← `QR_RASTER_W`) rather than the reverse. That also fixed a latent overlap: the reserved 192 px slot ran to y=212 against a caption at y=200, invisible only because the slot was empty. About 22 mm on this panel, so a 0.68 mm module — scans at arm's length, not across the room. Trading the quiet zone down for a bigger code was rejected; growing the card would have eaten into the bus rail.
- **`tools/qr-verify` decodes what the rasterizer actually emits, and links the firmware's own `qr_raster.c`.** That is why the rasterizer has no Legato or Harmony dependency — it compiles for the host unchanged, so the code under test is the code that runs on the panel, with `ui/qr_art.c` (the Legato/DDR cache around it) left out since it has no pixel logic. All eight decode back to their URL character for character. It also checks the things a decoder won't: exact tile size, pure black/white pixels, a genuinely blank quiet zone. The URL list is **scraped from `screen_system.c`** rather than copied, and the scrape hard-fails if the `.qr_url` count doesn't match the `.name` count — the direct lesson from this morning.
- **qrcodegen is vendored as a copy, not a submodule.** Two files, frozen upstream, one consumer. `third_party/oa-tc6-lib` is a submodule because it's a shared Microchip library every node's firmware tracks; a submodule here would add a `git submodule update --init` step to every clone for a library we will never pull from again. Provenance (upstream URL, pinned commit, MIT terms) is recorded in the vendored directory, which is the one thing the submodule would otherwise have given us.
- **Open: nothing here has been seen on hardware.** The host test proves the bitmap is a correct QR; it cannot prove the 2D engine blits a RAW RGB565 `leImage` into the detail canvas at the right place, or that the panel's contrast and the frame's dark surround leave it scannable by a phone. That is the first thing to check at the panel.

**2026-08-07 — all eight photo slots filled, and the tool was silently ignoring one of them. NOT ON HARDWARE YET.** Greg supplied the remaining six renders plus the whole-rig shot; no firmware change was needed, since `PHOTO_N` derives from the table.

- **`process_node_photos.py`'s `NODES` list never gained `guitar-pic`**, so the project card's photo was dropped on the floor with no diagnostic — it isn't a node, and the tool's list was written before the card existed. The `PHOTO` table in `ui/node_art.c` is the contract and the tool now mirrors all eight entries. Worth noting the failure shape: an unlisted name is not reported as "no source for", because the loop only walks names it knows, so the card would just have read blank on hardware with nothing anywhere saying why.
- **`--fit cover` for every source, and `contain` would have been visibly wrong.** All the new renders arrived pre-framed portrait at 1408×3040 — aspect 0.4632 against the column's 0.4645 — so `contain` scales to **287** px wide and leaves a 1 px zinc-900 stripe down one edge of the frame; `cover` crops ~4 px of height instead and fills the rect exactly. This is the case the README's "already framed portrait" note is for.
- **`marvin.png` came in RGBA, which the pipeline flattens rather than composites.** Its alpha was uniformly 255 so nothing was lost, but that was checked, not assumed: `render()` does `convert("RGB")`, which **discards** an alpha channel without compositing it over anything, so a source with real transparency would land with its transparent regions showing whatever garbage RGB sat under the alpha. If a future source has a non-opaque alpha it needs an explicit composite onto `ZINC_900` first.
- **The "keep the originals in the repo" rule from the 2026-08-06 (night) entry is now bounded to 2× the slot, not full resolution.** Six full-res renders are 42 MB and `.git` is already 596 MB, so the committed sources are `576×1240` (5.5 MB total) — headroom for a re-crop or a fit-mode change, which is what that rule was actually for, without the repo carrying a photo archive. The full-res files are **not** in history; they exist only on Greg's disk. `lemmy.png` (288×620) was left as-is, being already small and already committed — note it is now exactly slot-sized, so it has **no** re-fit headroom at all. `guitar` arrived at 704×1520 and was likewise left alone, then replaced mid-session with a full-res render and brought onto the 576×1240 convention with the rest.
- **Outputs were regenerated from the downscaled sources**, not kept from the full-res pass, so the committed inputs actually reproduce the committed outputs. The quality difference is nil — a 2× LANCZOS box down to 288×620 versus 4.9×.
- **Idempotence over untouched sources was confirmed twice**, which is what makes a single-photo swap safe: `guitar` was then replaced with a new render and reprocessed, and the other seven outputs came back byte-identical. That swap also **retired the asset we had been watching** — `guitar.png` was 322,163 B, 61% of `decode_one`'s 512 KiB `SCRATCH_BYTES`, and the new one is 285 KiB. `lightshow` is now the largest at 302 KiB, so the whole set sits at ~59% of the ceiling or below. Overshooting it is a silent blank frame behind a `NODEART:` warning, so it stays worth tracking.

**2026-08-06 (night, tenth) — System Info gains an eighth card for the project itself, and the grid goes 4+4. NOT BUILT YET; needs the design Generate already done.** Greg: *"add one more card for the overall project guitar-pic to top left (so 4 in top row)"*.

- **The card is not a node, and the code says so.** `NODE_BUS_N` (7) is the boards on the bus, held in bus order for the BUS POSITION rail; `NODE_N` (8) adds the project at index `PROJECT`. It has no PLCA id, so it never appears on the rail, and its photo key is `ID_PROJECT` = `0xFF` — deliberately outside the id space rather than the next id up, since "8" would read like a ninth node.
- **Grid is now four wide in both rows**, so one `CARD_W` (303) replaces `R1_W`/`R2_W` and the two build loops collapse into one. The top row reads parent-then-brain: `guitar-pic / marvin / fretboard / beatbox`; the bottom row is unchanged. marvin's card loses its 408 px hero width, which costs nothing — every string already fit the 303 px row-2 cards.
- **`SCHEME_NODE_PROJECT` = Microchip red `#E4002B`**, added to `default_design.zip` (73 → 74 schemes) by cloning `SCHEME_NODE_MARVIN`'s member text verbatim and recolouring `base`/`foreground`/`text` — the three slots a node accent has to carry, since the card's left edge and bullet dots fill from `base` while the name reads `text`. No existing scheme was shaped that way (the spare `SCHEME_TEXT_*` colour the text but fill grey), which is why this needed a new one rather than a reuse. Verified: deltas exactly `[base, foreground, id, name, text]`, all 73 pre-existing schemes byte-identical, `audit_refs.py` clean. Greg's Generate has landed it — `le_gen_scheme.c` carries `#e60029` in RGB565, the exact nearest representable form of `#E4002B` in 5/6/5.

Two consequences, both handled rather than fudged: a new `product` flag drives the QR slot (it was keyed off `part != NULL`, which would have promised a product page the project hasn't got), and the callout **drops Bold_18 → Bold_16 when it doesn't fit the card** — 25 glyphs × 11 px is 275 against a 269 px column, so it would have clipped. The detail header's callout label also grew 320 → `D_PART_W` (364) to hold it at Bold_24.
- **The project's detail lights the whole rail** in each node's own colour, which turns BUS POSITION into the legend for the accents the other seven cards use. Its parts list is the system-level one: all three MCU families, the PHY, the Click board, the support silicon, both toolchains.
- Photo slot added to `node_art.c` keyed `0xFF` (`system/nodes/guitar-pic.png`) — `PHOTO_N` is derived from the table, so the pixel pool, the loader and the splash's artwork progress count all scale on their own. Costs 714 KB of a 222 MB `.region_ram`, and with no file on the card it degrades to the empty frame like any other missing photo.
- Re-verified mechanically at the new card width: every tag ≤ 26 ch, prose ≤ 57, parts row ≤ 61, chip ≤ 36, and the detail name still lands on screen after the longest callout. Pools 78/96 labels, 45/64 widgets, 9/12 buttons.

**2026-08-06 (night, ninth) — System Info's content rewritten from the hardware up: real part numbers, a real Microchip parts list, bigger body text. NOT BUILT YET.** The design is settled, so this pass was only content — except that a real parts list does not fit in three bullets, so column 2 was rebalanced to make room.

- **What was wrong with the placeholder copy.** Three nodes named a part that doesn't exist as an orderable number (`PIC32CM6408`, `PIC32CM PL10` — the silicon is `PIC32CM6408PL10048`); beatbox named a part it doesn't run; every follower's BUILT WITH was the same three generic bullets; **MPLAB X IDE** appeared on all seven cards when everything is built in **VS Code with the MPLAB extensions**; and MCC content type was unstated where it differs per family. No card named a board, a support part, or a toolchain.
- **Facts, and where they came from.** Silicon and kit numbers verified against the Microchip product API: `SAM9X75D2G` (800 MHz Arm926, 2 Gbit DDR3L in package) on the SAM9X75 Curiosity Development Board **EV31H43A**; `PIC32CM6408PL10048` on **PIC32CM PL10 Curiosity Nano EV10P22A** — which is what guitar, fretboard, lemmy and lightshow actually run on, per Greg, *not* the ATE_2026 board their specs still name; `dsPIC33AK512MPS512` as **GP DIM EV80L65A** on the **Curiosity Platform Development Board EV74H48A**. Every node's T1S PHY is a `LAN8651B1` on a **MIKROE Two-Wire ETH 3 Click** — fauxmote included, so the shared-PHY bullet on all seven cards is now literally true rather than aspirational. MCC content type read from the manifests rather than the prose: `guitar/config.mcc/mcc/mcc-manifest-generated-success.yml` declares `com.microchip.mcc.harmony.Harmony3Library`, beatbox's declares `com.microchip.mcc.melody.Library` — so **MCC Harmony** on the PIC32 nodes, **MCC Melody + XC-DSC** on beatbox alone.
- **fauxmote stays deliberately quiet about its silicon** (Greg's call): the card names the **Adafruit Feather** form factor and "Bluetooth Classic radio" and stops — no vendor, no SDK. It keeps its LAN8651 row, because that part is shared with everyone else.
- **`BUILT WITH` → `MICROCHIP INSIDE`, 3 rows → 8, font 14 → 16.** The section is a parts list now: silicon, board, support parts, then tools. fretboard is the fullest at 8 rows (it is the only node with custom hardware — the phototransistors reach the Nano's ADC through a signal-conditioning board carrying MCP6002 op-amps, MCP1804 LDO and MCP16301 buck); fauxmote is the thinnest at 3.
- **The room came from `WHAT IT DOES`, which was oversized, not from anywhere structural.** It was 380 px for content needing 276, so it is now sized to its content (`SEC_BODY_Y + PROSE_MAX × PROSE_PITCH` + pad = 296) and the surplus went to the parts card (228 → 312). Column 2's total is unchanged at 620, and columns 1 and 3 — photo, QR, bus rail — were not touched.
- **Body text up one step**, which is the point of the rebalance: prose 16 → 18 (`PROSE_COLS` 62 → 57) and card taglines 14 → 16 (26 chars/line in the narrow cards, `C_TAG_PITCH` 20 → 22 so 20 px glyphs don't touch). Advances decoded from `le_gen_fonts.c` rather than guessed — 14→8 px, 16→10, 18→11, 24→14 — and every string checked against the resulting budget by script (widest: prose 54/57, parts row 56/61, card tag 23/26). Pools unchanged and re-counted: labels 73/96, widgets 43/64, buttons 8/12.
- **Doc drift left standing, on purpose:** `guitar`/`lemmy`/`lightshow` SPEC.md §2 still name the ATE_2026 board and marvin's spec §3.1 still describes the Curiosity Hybrid. That is a separate doc pass — noted here so it isn't lost.
- **Bug found by Greg on the first look, and it was never a System Info bug: you cannot empty a `leFixedString` by assigning `""`.** Symptom was fauxmote's detail view keeping the previously-selected node's text in the rows fauxmote doesn't fill. `_leFixedString_SetFromChar` returns `LE_SUCCESS` early on `size == 0` **without touching `length`**, so `lestring_set_utf8(str, "")` was a silent no-op and the stale glyphs repainted from the old buffer contents. `clear()` is the only path that zeroes the length (and it preinvalidates the old rect). Fixed centrally in **our** `util/legato_utf8.c` — `lestring_set_utf8` now routes an empty decode through `str->fn->clear(str)` — rather than per-screen, since all five screens that use the helper were exposed. **Latent before this session**, not introduced by it: any node with a shorter list than the seeded node 0 would have shown the difference. This session made it obvious because the new prose uses a blank line as a paragraph break in every node and the list lengths vary (3–8 parts rows).
  - `screen_keyboard.c`'s `(s_text[0] != '\0') ? s_text : " "` is **not** this bug and was left alone: that one guards an *opaque* label, which skips its paint on an empty string and so never erases its own background. System Info's labels are transparent over a panel-wide invalidate, so a cleared string correctly draws nothing and the card fill repaints beneath it.
- **Second bug from Greg, same look: a clipped descender — the 'g' in "lightshow".** A label box shorter than its font loses a pixel row top *and* bottom, and the bottom row is where a descender lives. `DejaVuSansMonoBold_20` is **25 px** tall and the detail header's name box was **24**, so every node name with a descender lost its tail. Fonts' real heights were decoded from `le_gen_fonts.c` rather than inferred from the point size — 12→16, 14→18, 16→20, 18→22, 20→**25**, 24→29 — and they are not size+2 uniformly, which is exactly how a 24 px box for a "20 px" font looked reasonable.
  - **Fix is a guard in each screen's own `add_label`, not a hand-tuned number:** a new `fit_font()` grows a short box to the font's height *and* shifts `y` by the same halved amount, which leaves the text on precisely the rows it was already drawn on — because Legato centres at `y + h/2 - fontH/2` (`leUtils_ArrangeRectangleRelative`), so adding the same term to both cancels. Nothing moves; the clipped rows simply appear. Verified by simulating the arrangement for every affected site.
  - System Info's header additionally got explicit `D_NAME_Y`/`D_NAME_H` constants, because that box wants to be deliberately taller than the guard's minimum and its `y` compensates so the name's baseline still lands on the part number's (both 105). The guard is now a no-op on this screen — belt and braces.
  - **Audited every other screen the same way** (script: for each label helper, resolve the `h` argument through the file's macros and compare against the font's decoded height). **`screen_bus.c` had 7 real ones** — KPI value 28 px around a 29 px font, and six 14 px boxes around a 16 px font: the KPI sub-caption, the "40s ago" axis labels, and the per-node energy rows, where "Li**g**htshow" and "Lemm**y**" are exactly the clipped case. Same `fit_font` guard there fixes all seven at once. `song_select`, `dashboard` (dynamic values), `keyboard`, `navigation` and `titlebar` came back clean.
  - **Not statically verified:** the 11 `add_cap` boxes in dashboard/song_select (16/18 px) take their font from the MGS string binding, and the design zip's `fontconfig.json` carries no font name, so the box-vs-font comparison can't be closed from the design DB. Those captions are uppercase, where there is no descender to lose, and 16/18 px matches the mockup's text-xs/text-sm → Mono_12/Mono_14 (16/18 px) mapping exactly. If we want it airtight, `leString` exposes `getFont()`, so `add_cap` could run the same guard after attaching the string.

**2026-08-06 (night, ninth) — `ram_nocache` at 64 MB stalls the boot, because `.text` lives at a hard-coded address inside DDR. CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE (64 MB fails, 34 MB boots).** Greg tried 64 MB first — generous headroom for the ninth surface — and got a splash, a progress bar filling *jerkily*, then a complete stall. Asked whether we had run out of other RAM.

**We had not: the code segment fell inside the non-cached window.** `ddram.ld` places `.text` at a literal `0x23f00000` — DDR base + 63 MB — and that address does **not** move when the regions are resized, while `MMU_Initialize` maps the whole non-cached window `TTB_SECT_STRONGLY_ORDERED`, which is non-cacheable *and* unbuffered. At 34 MB the window ends at `0x22200000` and `.text` sits safely above it; at 64 MB it ends at `0x24000000` and swallows **1,048,576 of `.text`'s 1,420,932 bytes — 74% of the firmware — executing uncached**, every instruction fetch its own DDR transaction.

- **The "jerky" progress bar is the diagnostic, not a side note.** `.text` *straddles* the boundary, so the first 1 MB of code crawls and the remaining 372 KB runs at full speed — progress lurches according to which functions are live. Uniform slowness would have looked like a uniformly slow bar; the jerkiness is what says "some code is cached and some isn't". The stall follows from the artwork stage already being 18 s of a 23 s boot: at a 10–50× penalty it becomes minutes, and the health monitor has a timeout.
- **Nothing warns, and that is worth knowing.** `.text` uses an explicit address rather than `>ram`, so the linker never region-checks it against `ram_nocache`; and because `.region_nocache`'s *used* size was 32.79 MB, no bytes actually collided. A silent, correct-but-unusable build.
- **So the region's real ceiling is 63 MB, and going near it is worse than overshooting elsewhere:** had usage grown past 63 MB it would have overlapped `.text` outright. 34 MB is the right setting — 32.79 MB used, ~28 MB of clearance below the code. Anything beyond ~60 MB needs `.text` moved too, which is an MCC change since `ddram.ld` is generated. The splash's 4 MB RGBA8888 buffer is now the *preferred* source of extra non-cached memory rather than growing the region.
- Recorded in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §0, whose "bounded by `ram_nocache`" note previously implied the region could simply be grown as needed.

---

**2026-08-06 (night, eighth) — Phase B shipped broken; two bugs, and the second corrects a wrong assumption in the design. BUILDS CLEAN; NOT ON HARDWARE YET.** Greg: System Info did not come up on the first selection after boot — the video blanked but the dashboard stayed — and it worked after visiting any other screen first.

- **Bug 1, the reported one: `s_view` was tracking the wrong thing.** `Setup()` ended with `bind_view(VIEW_OVERVIEW)`, which set `s_view = VIEW_OVERVIEW` while nothing was bound (the bind no-ops when the base view is not System Info, which at build time it is not). So the first real entry hit the `v == s_view` guard and returned without binding. `s_view` says which view is *current*; whether its canvas is on BASE is a different fact, and those diverge every time another base view takes the panel. **The guard is gone** — `bind_view` is unconditional, since re-binding is a handful of register writes — and `Setup` now leaves `s_view = VIEW_COUNT` rather than claiming a view. Greg's workaround is the tell: leaving System Info was the only thing that reset the guard, and the failed entry had already set `s_base_view`, so the *next* departure did it.
- **Bug 2, worse and not yet seen: the detail canvas was bound without being repainted.** The overview→detail path bound the canvas and never invalidated it, and these labels are written straight into their `leFixedString`s, which does not damage the widget — that is exactly why the old code carried an explicit invalidate. Every node would have shown **node 0's text** (the only content ever painted there, from boot) with just the photo correct.
- **The assumption that was wrong: "each view owns a canvas, so both are pre-painted and static".** True of the grid; false of the detail view, which is one tree re-pointed at whichever node was tapped. So a canvas is only safe to bind immediately if its content is already correct, and the detail canvas has to be **repainted before it is bound** — otherwise the bind, which is instant, shows the previous node.
  - So the frame-count wait is **back, but guarding the bind rather than the photo** — which is strictly better than what it did before: it fixes the stale-content bug, and the photo comes along for free because it is enabled in the same call. `UiManager_FrameCount` / `UiManager_WaitFrameAfter` restored; `view_task` replaces `photo_task`.
  - The asymmetry is the point, and it is not arbitrary: **detail→overview binds immediately** (static content, so it is genuinely instant — the original complaint), **overview→detail waits** (content just changed, and waiting keeps the grid on screen until the detail view is complete, which is the arrival Greg liked). Phase B still bought the fix; it just does not remove the wait in the direction where content changes.
- **A race closed while tracing the state machine, not on hardware:** `bind_view` bumps the generation counter, so a view that actually reaches the panel invalidates a deferred bind still in flight. Tap a card, leave the screen, come back to the grid — `s_shown` is true again by then, so that check alone would have let the pending detail bind land on top of the grid.

**2026-08-06 (night, seventh) — Two glitches from hardware layers racing canvas repaints. One was ours and got the structural fix; the other was never ours. BUILDS CLEAN; NOT ON HARDWARE YET.**

**Phase B is done: System Info's two views are now two layer-screens (7 and 8), each with its own canvas.** Greg reported that the deferred photo reveal was right going *into* a node but wrong coming back — "the image clearly disappears first and then the screen is redrawn". That is the same root cause the reveal was working around, just in the other direction: a hardware layer changes instantly, a canvas repaint does not, so any design where the two must agree *and* a repaint is involved can only ever be papered over. `show_view` is now `UiManager_BindSystemView` — a layer bind, no drawing — with the photo layer flipped in the same call, so the page and the photo arrive in one frame.
- **The right architecture removed code.** `photo_task`, the `leRenderer_GetDrawCount()` wait (`UiManager_FrameCount` / `UiManager_WaitFrameAfter`) and the `s_photo_gen` generation counter are all deleted. Worth keeping the finding that produced them, though: the draw counter *is* the exact "has this repaint finished" signal, moving in the renderer's `postFrame` only once every damaged rect on every layer is drawn, where `leRenderer_IsIdle()` is also true between `leUpdate` calls and needs a tuned window. `UiManager_WaitRenderIdle` stays for boot, which drains damage from everything at once and so has no single repaint to key on.
- **Input had to move from visibility to `LE_WIDGET_ENABLED`.** Both roots are attached to their Legato layers for life and `leInput` walks *every attached layer*, so the off-screen view would have kept answering taps — the old full-screen containers hid each other, but separate layers do not. The pick walk requires `ENABLED`, so `ScreenSystem_SetInput` now puts it on the current view alone. This is the trap in splitting one canvas into two.
- **`ScreenSystem_SetShown(false)` resets `s_view` to `VIEW_COUNT`.** Without it, leaving from the overview and coming back would hit `show_view`'s same-view guard and never re-bind — the canvas is off BASE by then, so "already showing that view" is false even though the variable says otherwise.
- Cost: `.region_nocache` 30.84 → 32.79 MiB (+2,048,000 B — exactly one full-screen RGB565 surface, as predicted). Greg grew the region 32 → 64 MiB and `CONFIG_CANVAS_NUM_OBJ` to 40, so there is now ~31 MiB of headroom rather than the 1.16 MiB that made this a decision in the first place. Each view carries its own titlebar (`TITLEBAR_MAX` 8, five in use). **This closes the "Reopen if" on the 2026-08-06 Phase A decision** — reopened for the layer-ordering artifact rather than the ~150 ms draw time it named.

**The dashboard's test-pattern flash was never ours.** Greg saw SMPTE bars briefly before the video appeared and guessed it should be gated on there being no stream — right on both counts. The bars are a *permanently painted* static widget group in the dashboard canvas, sitting under HEO by design (`build_video`'s own comment: "what the panel shows wherever the live video is not covering"). Leaving for any full-screen view calls `UiManager_VideoHide()`, which unbinds HEO; coming back, `ScreenVideo_ShowWindowed()` only sets intent and `heo_reconcile` rebinds on its **next video-task tick**, and only once `source_valid`. The bars are simply what is underneath for that window — so the bus and wiimotes screens have always done this too, and System Info only added another route.
- The group is now one transparent container toggled by a single flag, published from `heo_reconcile` through the existing dashboard feed queue (video task posts, UI task applies under the render lock) — no new task, no polling.
- **The gate is deliberately asymmetric.** "Video up" publishes immediately, so the pattern is gone before HEO covers it; "video gone" waits 400 ms, because HEO unbinds for reasons that are about to be undone (a view switch, a geometry rebind, a source re-lock) and publishing those straight through would restore the bars for exactly the gap being removed. A genuinely absent source still gets NO SIGNAL.
- **Built hidden**, which also removes the same flash at boot reveal.

**2026-08-06 (night, sixth) — Pressed state was flashing WHITE on every button in the design, not just the node cards. Fixed in the design zip + a pressed-border variant. CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE** — Greg: *"tested. looks good."* Greg spotted it on the marvin card.

- **Root cause is a scheme slot nobody had reason to set.** Legato's classic button skin fills from `LE_SCHM_BACKGROUND` while pressed and `LE_SCHM_BASE` while up (`drawBackground`), and a plain panel *never* reads `background` at all (`leWidget_SkinClassic_DrawStandardBackground` uses `base` only). So every `SCHEME_FILL_ZINC_*` had a correct `base` and `background` still at Legato's default **#FFFFFF** — invisible for the dozens of panels on those schemes, and white-on-touch for the two buttons: the node cards (zinc-900) and the song-select close button. `ButtonAA` mirrored the same slot, so body and AA corners agreed with each other and were both wrong.
- **Fixed in `default_design.zip`, one step lighter than each scheme's own base:** 900→#27272A (the mockup's `hover:bg-zinc-800`), 800→#3F3F47, 700→#52525C, 600→#71717A. Set on all four rather than only the two in use, so the next button placed on a zinc fill doesn't rediscover this.
- **The hover *border* needed an overdraw, because the stock line border has no state branch** — `DrawStandardLineBorder` is hard-wired to `SHADOWDARK`. New `ButtonAA_EnablePressedBorder` redraws the four straight runs plus the corner boxes in `LE_SCHM_HIGHLIGHT` while pressed; `HIGHLIGHT` is free for a LINE-bordered button (the classic skin reads only BASE/BACKGROUND/TEXT plus bevel-only slots) so it carries "pressed border" by convention, set to #71717A on `SCHEME_FILL_ZINC_900`. Opt-in per button, since a scheme without a deliberate HIGHLIGHT would flash Legato's default there.
  - **Two traps, both hit rather than predicted.** (1) `leWidget_SkinClassic_DrawLineBorder{Left,Top,Right,Bottom}` are **declared in the header but not defined in this build** — a link error, not a compile error, which is why the plan looked cheaper than it was. Used `leRenderer_HorzLine`/`VertLine` instead. `DrawRoundCornerLineBorder` *is* defined but deliberately unexported, and hand-declaring a vendor symbol would not survive a regen. (2) The variant must chain to the **stock** paint, but `add_button` has already applied plain `ButtonAA_Enable`, so `btn->fn->_paint` is `aa_paint` by then — chaining to that would run the corner pass twice, the first time with the un-pressed border. It now detects that and reuses the stock paint the plain variant captured.
  - Run geometry checked by arithmetic: the straight runs are inset by the radius and meet the corner boxes exactly (top run ends x+286, right box starts x+287) at both card widths, so there is no nick where they join.
- **The back button lost its border**, which the mockup never had (`bg-zinc-800 hover:bg-zinc-700`, no `border` class) — `add_button` gives every button `BORDER_LINE`. The cards are the only bordered buttons in the design.

**The accent edge does survive a press, which was the open risk.** The pressed border draws down the left edge too, exactly where the accent band sits, so it depended on Legato's damage pass repainting the later sibling over it — the same overlay-repaints-after-content property `add_image_frame` relies on. Confirmed on the panel rather than assumed; had it not held, the left edge would have read zinc-500 while held instead of the node colour.

**Process note worth keeping: a design-zip edit and an MGS Generate are not atomic, and the gap is silent.** The first Generate ran between the background edit and the highlight edit, so the pressed *fills* appeared in `le_gen_scheme.c` while the pressed *border* did not — a half-applied palette that builds clean and looks almost right. Nothing warns. Compare the generated values against the zip before believing a Generate landed (`awk '/leScheme SCHEME_X =/,/^};/' le_gen_scheme.c`), and finish all zip edits before asking for one. Related hazard, avoided by luck here: MGS holding the design open will write its in-memory copy over a tool edit on its next save.

---

**2026-08-06 (night, fifth) — The node cards' accent edge now curves around the corners, as the mockup's CSS border does. BUILDS CLEAN; NOT ON HARDWARE YET.** The loose end left by the radius change: at radius 4 a bar inset by the radius was near enough, at 16 it ends square with a grey arc above it.

- **The mockup needed no screenshot to decode — it is a CSS border, and CSS does two things a bar cannot.** `rounded-2xl border border-zinc-700` with `borderLeftWidth: 6px; borderLeftColor: <accent>`. (1) The corner band's **inner edge is an ellipse**, radii `R-6` horizontally and `R-1` vertically, so the band is 6px thick at the straight edge and tapers to 1px where it meets the top border. (2) The colour **hands over along the mitre**, the line from the outer corner toward `(6, 1)` — shallow at 6:1, so the accent owns most of the arc and the top border keeps a short sliver.
- **New `AaCorners_RenderLeftEdge` + `PanelAA_EnableLeftAccent`.** The ellipse is handled by its polar form along each ray, which keeps the AA band measured radially exactly as the existing circular path measures it, so the two entry points anti-alias identically. The mitre is a signed distance softened over one pixel.
- **It blends over the surface rather than over a sampled backdrop, which is the one structural difference from the other `AaCorners` entry points.** The card has already painted its fill, its 1px border and its own AA corners by then, so the band's outer edge lands on real backdrop and its inner edge on real fill — no second backdrop sample, and nothing to get wrong about what is behind a given corner. The consequence is that the accent must be a **transparent later sibling covering the whole card**, not a bar: sibling order is load-bearing, and the old 6px inset panel is gone.
- **Verified offline before hardware, by porting the C loop verbatim to Python and rendering it** — the harness idiom the vector-conversion work established, and the only feedback available for a purely visual change. Checks that passed: exactly 6 accent pixels across the straight run, the outer corner pixel still page-black (nothing leaks outside the rounded rect), thickness ramping 0→5→6 down the arc, the bottom corner mirroring the top (the `flip_y` path), and rows 0–1 of the arc carrying **no** accent at all — which is correct, not a bug: that part of the arc is above the mitre and belongs to the top border. A reference render of the CSS geometry was made first, so the port was compared against the target rather than against expectation.

---

**2026-08-06 (night, fourth) — System Info re-reviewed against a refreshed `SystemScreen.tsx`: corner radii were wrong everywhere, plus a text pass. CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE** — Greg: *"looks good on hardware."* Greg re-downloaded `~/Downloads/Marvin` (20:13) and flagged the radius.

- **The radius is three values, not one, and the mockup's Tailwind classes cannot be read at face value.** Its `theme.css` overrides `--radius-sm/md/lg/xl` off a `0.625rem` base but leaves `--radius-2xl` at Tailwind's default — so `rounded-2xl` is **16** while `rounded-xl` is **14**, i.e. they are *not* one step apart on a scale. Resolved: `CARD_R 16` (node cards, the four section cards, the photo frame), `BTN_R 14` (back button), `BOX_R 4` (the bus-position id boxes, the only `rounded` in the design). `CARD_R` had been 4 for all of it. `add_button` now takes a radius because it serves both the 16 cards and the 14 button. Free to do: `AaCorners_Render` computes coverage per pixel with no table and no cap.
  - **The status pill was radius-4 and should have been `rounded-full`** — moot now (pills dropped, below), but worth recording as the same class of misreading.
  - The card's 6px accent edge is inset by the radius so it doesn't square off the corners, which at 16 leaves a 16px notch at each end; kept, since the mockup gets a curved edge free from `borderLeftWidth` on a rounded box and reproducing that is a bespoke draw.
  - **The photo asset's baked frame had to be regenerated at 16** — `tools/node-photos/` now carries `CARD_R = 16` with a note that it tracks the screen's constant. This is the coupling the 2026-08-06 (night, third) entry predicted: the frame lives in the asset, so a radius change is a re-export plus a card copy, not just a rebuild.
- **The `.md` content specs have not changed** — the bundle's `-1` files are the *newer* Figma import and are byte-identical to `docs/screens/`. Every text delta is in the `.tsx`, and they all tighten: fretboard's tagline became "The eyes - senses every note the instant it appears", and five nodes' prose lost a trailing clause each (marvin "and it's" → "and the"; fauxmote dropped "not the primary path this project takes"; guitar "A small, dedicated board with one job, done fast and reliably."; fretboard "Real-time sensing and on-device decision-making, without waiting on anything else."; lemmy dropped "It doesn't sense or decide anything itself"). beatbox and lightshow unchanged.
- **BUILT WITH is now three short items per node** instead of two combined ones — `MPLAB X IDE` / `MCC (MPLAB Code Configurator)` / `Microchip LAN8651 networking` for the four follower MCUs. `BUILT_MAX` was already 3, so no pool change.
- **Three mockup changes Greg had not called out were confirmed with him rather than assumed**, all three "match the mockup": the **subhead is gone** (the grid takes that space — `GRID_Y` 134→118, cards 319→327, and row 2 still bottoms out exactly on `BASE_H - MARGIN`), the **status pills are gone from all seven cards** (`add_pill` and `PILL_*` deleted, and `status`/`status_scheme` dropped from `node_info_t` since nothing else read them), and **fauxmote now names LAN8651** — its MCU is the one non-Microchip part, but its T1S PHY is the same LAN8651 as everyone else's, which is on-message for the headline rather than against the "keep it quieter" note.
- Wrap widths were re-verified by script over the new table rather than by eye, as before: longest prose line **61 of 62**, longest card tag **30** (33 fit in the narrow row-2 column), longest BUILT WITH item **52 of 54**, all ASCII, and every card's two tag lines still reassemble into exactly its detail tagline. `CAP` is documented against its real driver, which is marvin's tagline plus the `· THIS DEVICE` suffix at 65 *bytes* (the `·` costs two) — not a prose line.

Confirmed on the panel, including the 16px corners and the accent-edge notch the inset leaves at each end — that was the detail flagged as most likely to want revisiting, and it reads fine, so the bespoke curved-edge draw stays unnecessary.

**Second node photo landed: `guitar`** — a Les Paul with the dev board mounted on it, source 704×1520 at aspect 0.463 against the column's 0.465, so `cover` cropped a few pixels of height and nothing at the sides. 322,163 B, i.e. **61% of `decode_one`'s 512 KiB `SCRATCH_BYTES`** ceiling and the largest asset so far — worth watching, because exceeding it is a silent blank frame with only a `NODEART:` warning behind it. Re-running the tool left `lemmy.png` byte-identical, confirming the pass is idempotent over sources already approved. Five slots still empty (marvin, fauxmote, fretboard, beatbox, lightshow) and reading as an empty frame by design.

**2026-08-06 (night, third) — Full-colour photo CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE** — Greg: *"the image itself looks great."* Two follow-ups from that look, one of them not a bug.

- **"Border is missing" was a stale card file.** The frame bake landed at 19:16:12, after the first unframed export had already been copied. The installed asset carries a complete 1px `#404040` ring on all four edges (verified per-edge; md5 `7c457c1bf2e0b63e176bba6cc3a428db`). Nothing to fix — but worth knowing this failure mode exists now that the frame lives in the asset rather than in the canvas: a stale photo silently loses its frame, where before the canvas always drew one.
- **The photo arrived visibly ahead of the rest of the screen**, because enabling a hardware layer is instant while the view is still being painted into the canvas. The reveal is now deferred: `photo_task` (static, prio 2, own task because the tap is dispatched from inside `leUpdate`) waits for the repaint, then enables the layer. Takedown stays immediate.
- **That wait is not a timer, which was Greg's question, and the first cut deserved the challenge.** It began as `leRenderer_IsIdle()` held continuously for 40 ms — a real signal, but the window was there to paper over the fact that `IsIdle()` is `frameState == LE_FRAME_READY`, which is *also* true between `leUpdate` ticks. **`leRenderer_GetDrawCount()` is the exact signal and was sitting right there**: it advances in the renderer's `postFrame`, which runs only once every damaged rect on every layer has been drawn. So the test became "has the frame count moved past what it was when we queued the repaint", with no tuned constant left except the 5 ms poll and the 8 s timeout. The baseline is sampled in `photo_apply` rather than in the task, because that is where the damage is queued *and* where the renderer is known idle (Legato dispatches widget events between frames — the same property `UiManager_RenderLock` already depends on), which is what makes "the count moved" mean "our damage was drawn" rather than "some frame finished".
- New reusable primitives in `ui_manager`: `UiManager_FrameCount()` + `UiManager_WaitFrameAfter()`. The boot loader keeps the old `wait_render_idle` (now `UiManager_WaitRenderIdle(stable_ms)`) because it drains damage from everything at once and has no single baseline frame to sample.

**The structural fix was deliberately not taken:** give overview and detail their own canvases, which per the §13 property (Legato paints a canvas whether or not it is bound) makes the switch a pure layer bind with **no repaint to outrun** — page and photo land in the same frame, no wait at all. That is the same Phase B the 2026-08-06 evening entry already flagged for the transition cost, and it would now fix two things at once. It needs a second full-screen RGB565 surface (~2.0 MB) against the 1.16 MB `.region_nocache` has free, so it wants the region grown — which Greg has already offered. Left for a decision rather than assumed.

---

**2026-08-06 (night, later) — The board photo moved off the RGB565 canvas onto its own opaque RGBA8888 OVR1 layer, so it displays full-colour. BUILDS CLEAN, ZERO NEW WARNINGS; CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE (see the entry above).** Greg asked whether the album-art RGB888 layer mechanism could be reused, offering more canvases and a bigger `.region_nocache`. It turned out to need **neither**. Design in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §19.

- **`.region_nocache` is byte-for-byte unchanged at 32,336,480 B**, because the photo never becomes a canvas. Nothing on that layer needs Legato — it is one static raster at a fixed rect — so `UiManager_NodePhotoShow/Hide` drive OVR1 straight through the PLIB, exactly as the §16 video frame overlay does. **No new Legato layer, no `add_layer.py`, no MGS Generate, no `CONFIG_CANVAS_NUM_OBJ` bump.** The album-art strip is a canvas only because it carries labels beside the cover; this doesn't. Cost is `.region_ram` +2,499,840 B for the 7 slots at 4 B/px instead of 2.
- **OVR1 is free by construction here, which is the fact the whole design rests on.** `UiManager_ShowSystem` already calls `UiManager_VideoHide()` + `UiManager_VideoOverlayHide()`, and the other two OVR1 users are reachable only from the dashboard (`UiManager_OpenKeyboard` is called from `screen_dashboard.c` alone; the song-select dialog likewise). The nav drawer is the one thing that can appear over this screen and it rides **OVR2, above** the photo — so it covers the photo's left edge, which is the wanted behaviour, not a conflict.
- **RGBA8888, not the RGB888 the question assumed** — and not `RGB888_PACKED` either, which the XLCDC does support and which would have been 25% cheaper in both RAM and bandwidth. The photo arrives through Legato's PNG decoder, and spec §4.8.7 records `gfx2dFormats[]` mapping RGB888 to `-1`; RGBA8888 is the mode the decoder and the XLCDC already agree on, proven by the album-art tier. Carried `game_art.c`'s alpha fix across verbatim: a PNG-without-alpha decode into RGBA8888 leaves the alpha byte **zero**, which the LCDC reads as a fully transparent layer.
- **That alpha finding killed the nicer version of the frame.** The plan had been to alpha-round the corners in the asset — better AA than the runtime trick, free in Python. But alpha cannot survive a decode that has to force it opaque, so the corners are instead **baked as opaque page-black** at radius 4 with a 1px `#404040` border, reproducing exactly what `PanelAA_EnableRoundImage` did at runtime (`SCHEME_BACKGROUND`'s base is black, its shadowDark `#404040`). Verified on the output: corner (0,0) is black, the border reads 64, and the AA ramp across the radius is 0→4→40→60→64.
- **Being opaque everywhere is not cosmetic — it is what makes the BASE discard valid.** The photo rect is added to `base_discard_reconcile` as a third case below video and modals, saving **21.4 MB/s** of BASE reads at 60 Hz against the layer's own 42.9 MB/s. Had the corners stayed transparent, the discard would have had to be inset to keep BASE readable underneath them.
- **One bug caught by reading the boot order rather than by running it:** `photo_apply()` is gated on the screen being shown, because `show_view(VIEW_OVERVIEW)` runs at *build* time inside `init_screens` — while the splash still owns OVR1. Ungated, the first thing the new code would have done is disable the splash mid-boot.
- The canvas keeps an **empty** frame widget for nodes with no photo (the six that have none), so the never-fail-boot contract still reads as an empty frame rather than nothing; the widget and the overlay are exclusive. `NodeArt_Photo` → `NodeArt_Pixels` (the `leImage` was only ever there to be handed to `setImage`, and nothing draws this through Legato now).

**Still unverified, and only checkable on the panel:** whether the XLCDC blender treats an alpha-255 RGBA8888 overlay as *fully* opaque — if not, the discarded BASE shows through and the discard needs insetting or dropping — and whether direct LCDC scanout of cached `.region_ram` is as safe in practice as the write-once argument says (CPU decodes, `decode_one` cleans, nothing writes again). Both fail visibly, and both fall back to moving the slots into `.region_nocache`.

**2026-08-06 (night) — First real node photo installed: `lemmy`. NOT ON HARDWARE YET.** The Lemmy puppet render (816×1276, portrait) run through `tools/node-photos/` into `firmware/marvin/data/system/nodes/lemmy.png`; the other six slots still read blank by design.

- **`--fit cover`, not the default `contain`.** The source is 0.64 aspect against the column's 0.46, so `contain` letterboxes ~85 px of zinc top and bottom and leaves the puppet at about half the frame height. `cover` centre-crops 112 px off each side — which costs only the outer amp stacks, and the puppet is centred well enough that both hands survive at the edges.
- **Verified against the firmware's own gates rather than assumed:** IHDR reads `288×620`, depth 8, colour type 2, non-interlaced (`png_dims` only checks the signature and dims, and lodepng takes it from there), and the 300 KiB file sits inside `decode_one`'s 512 KiB `SCRATCH_BYTES` ceiling with ~40% headroom. Worth knowing that ceiling exists: a photo over 512 KiB is rejected with a `NODEART:` warning and reads blank, which is a plausible trap for a less-compressible source.
- **`tools/node-photos/` still pointed at `game/node_art.{c,h}`** in its README and module docstring — corrected to `ui/`, which the move in `d54fd21` had missed.

**Source photos are kept in the repo** alongside the processed output — `tools/node-photos/data/lemmy.png` (2 MB, the original 816×1276 render) gets committed, so a re-crop or a fit-mode change never needs the original hunted down again.

**The photo is RGB565 end to end, and that is measurably fine on this image** — asked because 16bpp sounds thin for a photograph. The PNG is 8-bit RGB on the card; `decode_one` converts once at boot into an RGB565 slot, `CANVAS_SYSTEM` is `GFX_COLOR_MODE_RGB_565` (§8: "RGB565 to match the panel"), so the blit is a native copy and no second conversion happens. Legato's `rgb888_rgb565` is a pure mask-and-shift (`0xF80000`/`0xFC00`/`0xF8`) — **truncation, no rounding and no dithering** — which puts the error at max 7 and mean 2.81 per channel, and collapses the image's 45,678 distinct colours to 2,327. In principle this render is the worst case for 565: median luma 25/255 with 61.7% of pixels below luma 32, and dark smooth gradients are exactly where 5-bit R/B bands. It survives because the render is finely textured (fabric, grain, specular noise), which self-dithers. **If a future photo does band, the fix costs no firmware change:** dither to the 565 grid inside `process_node_photos.py`, since the quantization is a plain truncation the export can pre-compensate for.

**2026-08-06 (evening) — System Info screen: layer 7, a seven-card node showcase, and a data-driven detail view. BUILDS AND LINKS CLEAN; NOT ON HARDWARE YET.** Asked for against `SystemScreen.tsx` in `~/Downloads/Marvin` plus the eight content specs in [`screens/`](screens/) (previously untracked; they are the content source of truth and are now committed).

What is proven, and what isn't: Greg ran MGS Generate mid-session (`LE_LAYER_COUNT` 7→8, `Marvin_PANEL_SYSTEM` emitted onto `root7`), and the full ninja build now links with **zero new warnings** across all six touched TUs — the only two that appear are a pre-existing `/*`-within-comment in `video/video.h`. Budgets came out exactly as predicted: `.region_nocache` **32,336,480 B of 33,554,432 → 1,217,952 B (1.16 MiB) free**, i.e. the one spare full-screen RGB565 surface is now spent, and `.region_ram` grew ~3.02 MB for the photo slots + decode scratch. Before Generate the un-aliased compile failed on **exactly** `Marvin_PANEL_SYSTEM` and nothing else, which is what established that Generate was the only thing missing. **Nothing has been seen on the panel.** The prose wrap column — the one number only the panel can settle — is verified arithmetically instead (below).

- **The screen is one canvas holding two views behind a single seam.** `build_overview(parent)` / `build_detail(parent)` never name their panel, and `show_view()` is the only thing that knows how a view becomes visible. Today that is a `LE_WIDGET_VISIBLE` toggle on two full-screen container panels plus one panel invalidate. **Greg's question was how hard a later move to pre-rendered layers would be, and the answer turned out to be "the architecture already does that for screens":** `ui_manager.c`'s `paint_all_screens_once` comment already states it outright — Legato renders into a canvas surface whether or not that canvas is shown or bound, so *"showing a screen is a pure layer bind with no repaint"*. `bind_canvas` is four register/canvas calls; `ScreenBus_SetShown` only gates a refresh task. So drawer → System Info costs **zero** drawing already, and the new panel joins `paint_all_screens_once` to inherit it. The only repaint in question is the internal overview↔detail switch, and there is a shipped upper bound on that: `screen_bus.c` invalidates its **entire** 1280×800 panel with ~200 labels **once a second, continuously**. A single repaint on a discrete tap is strictly cheaper than what already runs at 1 Hz. If it ever isn't, Phase B is a second canvas and a rewrite of `show_view` alone — the node table, layout constants, label pool and both builders are untouched by it, and it would additionally let the detail surface be painted *while hidden* and revealed complete.
- **`CONFIG_CANVAS_NUM_OBJ` is not a real ceiling, and neither is `LE_LAYER_COUNT`** — the first only sizes a static `GFXC_CANVAS` array whose unused slots `GFXC_Initialize` marks `CANVAS_ID_INVALID`/`GFXC_FX_IDLE` (~150 B BSS each, inert), the second is MGS-derived, and `leState.layerList` is a dynamic `leList`. The real ceilings are the 4 XLCDC hardware layers (which cap *simultaneously visible* canvases, irrelevant here — System Info lands on BASE like bus and wiimotes) and `ram_nocache`. Layer 7 fits the existing pool of 8 **exactly**, so this change needed no pool or linker edit at all.
- **New `add_layer.py` in the MGS skill, because adding a layer-screen had no recipe.** It clones a sibling layer + its root panel under fresh uuids (`--like BUS` + `SYSTEM` → `SCREEN_SYSTEM`/`PANEL_SYSTEM` → `Marvin_PANEL_SYSTEM`). Cloning rather than synthesizing is the point: a layer carries ~25 properties whose enum ordinals appear nowhere in the zip, and a sibling the firmware already renders correctly is the only trustworthy source for all of them at once. Template chosen by *role* — `BUS`/`WIIMOTES` are `100×100` `sizeLocked` with the canvas window set at runtime, while the modal layers carry real design sizes. Layer and panel uuids turned out to live in `screen.json` alone, so the edit is one member; it is a **text splice**, so the diff is **one hunk, +367/−0**, and the script asserts the pre-existing layers and everything outside `layers[]` are byte-identical afterwards. Verified against `git show HEAD:` member-by-member: one member's sha1 changed, nothing else.
- **Widget-type pins are confirmed real in this design, which is what made the layer add safe.** The design instantiates **only `PanelWidget`, ×7** — yet `LE_BUTTON_/IMAGE_/LABEL_/CIRCULARGAUGE_WIDGET_ENABLED` are all `1`. Per the skill's own test (a flag at `1` with no design instance can only be a pin), pins are in effect, so nothing the firmware uses is on loan from a design widget.
- **Two traps caught by reading rather than by debugging.** (1) **A Legato button cannot host children**, and `leUtils_PickFromWidget` keeps the **last** child whose rect contains the point — so a card's text, drawn as siblings over the card button, would have swallowed every tap. Every label/dot/bar/frame on this screen is therefore `LE_WIDGET_IGNOREPICK`, set inside the helpers so it cannot be forgotten; the two view containers are IGNOREPICK too, which is also what stops a tap on empty page being captured instead of falling through to the titlebar. Confirmed against the pick walk: it requires **both** `ENABLED` and `VISIBLE`, and a child returning an IGNOREPICK result leaves the previous candidate standing. (2) **A runtime JPEG decode lands as noise on this device** — `process_gh3_cover_art.py` records that Legato's JPEG decoder gates its block writes on the renderer clip rect, which is stale during an offscreen boot decode, which is why *both* album-art tiers are PNG. `node_art` was written JPEG-first and switched to PNG before it ever ran.
- **Content is hand-wrapped ASCII line arrays, one string per rendered line, because Legato labels do not wrap and the fonts are Latin-1 only.** Verified by script over the table rather than by eye: no non-ASCII anywhere in it, longest prose line **62** chars against the 63 that fit (632 px box ÷ 10 px advance at `DejaVuSansMono_16`), longest tagline line 30 of 33, built-with 54 of 75, headline 728 px and subhead 686 px of 1248. The advances came out of each font's own `glyphs.json` — every DejaVu Mono here is genuinely monospace (one advance across all 191 glyphs) and regular/bold match at every size. `«`, `»` and `·` are used as glyphs and **are** inside the declared 160–255 range, so they are properties of the fonts rather than on loan from someone else's caption; the mockup's `›` (U+203A) is **not** present and was replaced.
- **`TITLEBAR_MAX` was exactly full at 4** (dashboard + wiimotes + bus + this) and is now 8. Worth knowing why that mattered: `Titlebar_Add` returns NULL on exhaustion rather than asserting, so overflowing it costs a screen its entire chrome — including its only way back to the drawer — with no build error and no assert.
- **Board photos load from the card, not the design** (`game/node_art.c`, 288×620 RGB565 slots in `.region_ram`, keyed by PLCA id), following `game_art.c`'s decode-into-fixed-slots pattern including its never-fail-boot contract: a missing, wrong-size or corrupt file leaves the slot empty and the card reads blank. So a photo swap needs no rebuild and no Generate. `tools/node-photos/` prepares them, and its output was checked against a re-implementation of the firmware's own `png_dims` — the header parser accepts both fit modes' output. **Recorded debt:** the PNG-header + decode-into-slot logic now exists in both `game_art.c` and `node_art.c`; extracting a shared helper was deliberately not done here to keep a working album-art path untouched, and is worth doing if a third consumer appears.
- **The QR column is reserved, not wired.** The card exists with its caption and an empty image widget, both as children of the card so one `setVisible` hides the whole slot for the nodes without a product page. Dropping the real images in later changes nothing else.

**Pool utilisation was computed rather than left to a boot `configASSERT`**, which caught two pools sized *exactly* full (8 buttons of 8, 2 images of 2) — correct but with no slack, so both were given room. Final: 45/64 widgets, 75/96 labels, 8/12 buttons, 2/4 images. The Legato allocator is untouched by this screen (every widget is static BSS); the only pool draw is MGS's own `leWidget_New()` for the 8th root panel, against a 2 MB variable heap.

**Still unverified and only checkable on the panel:** whether the prose column *looks* right at 62 columns, whether four drawer rows still clear the footer (they land y 113..369 against a footer at 715, so this is arithmetic, but the eyeball is free), and the overview↔detail transition cost — instrument `show_view` with the `screen_splash.c` tick-delta idiom around `leRenderer_IsIdle()` and compare against a `screen_bus.c` `refresh_all()` before deciding Phase A is enough. Board photos are optional for a first look: without them the photo column reads blank by design.

**2026-08-06 (later) — The bus screen's gauge and charts now carry the mockup's rounded details: round arc caps on the gauge, `radius={[2,2,0,0]}` tops on the TX bars, and a stadium error-bar fill. Rendered and measured on a host harness, then CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE first try** — Greg: *"looks good on hardware."* Asked for against `BusStatsScreen.tsx` in the Figma bundle. +368 B flash, +304 B bss (one more `leWidgetVTable` copy).

Three deltas against the mockup, all of them "the shape is right, the corners aren't":
- **`widget_gauge` → `LE_CAPSTYLE_ROUND`.** The mockup's two `<path>`s are `strokeLinecap="round"` on *both* the track and the fill, so the ring's ends and the fill's leading edge are semicircles. It had been `SQUARE` since the vector conversion — [`legato_vector_review.md`](legato_vector_review.md) §4.2 wrote "a 180° annulus with square caps", which described the float code it was replacing rather than the mockup, and nobody re-checked the source. No geometry change was needed: the ends sit on the horizontal diameter, so the caps grow into the empty pixels between the arc and the widget's bottom corners.
- **TX-rate bars → new `PanelAA_EnableRoundTop`.** A single `leDraw_VectorRectFill` with radii on the top two corners only. **This is the case `add_rect`'s old comment said couldn't be done** — "bars that are resized every refresh are left square … an AA pass keyed to the old size would smear". True of `AaCorners_*`, which *eats* corners back toward a backdrop; false of a vector fill, which **redraws the whole body from the current rect on every paint** and so carries nothing over from the previous size. The radius rides in `cornerRadius`, which is inert because the variant clears the background type (the stock skin only consults `cornerRadius` when `backgroundType == FILL`) — same trick, same reason, as `PanelAA_EnableDot`.
- **Error-bar fill → a pill**, i.e. the same `PanelAA_EnableDot` the *track* already used; the mockup puts `rounded-full` on both. One consequence worth knowing: `dot_paint` rounds by `min(w,h)/2`, so a fill narrower than the 8 px track reads as a **vertical** blob, not a rounded end. `refresh_all` therefore floors any non-zero count at `EBAR_H`; zero falls below `dot_paint`'s 2 px minimum and draws nothing, which is what the mockup does at 0%.

**The host A/B harness from the vector conversion was rebuilt and is worth rebuilding again** — for a purely visual change it is the only feedback available without the panel. It is ~15 vendor `.c` files (`vector/` + `core/legato_real_*` + `common/legato_math|rect`) plus **two stub headers**: an empty `device.h` and a `renderer/legato_renderer.h` declaring just the five entry points the vector tree touches (`BlendPixel` is the only one the kernel actually calls). `BlendPixel` writes an RGB888 canvas → PPM → PNG. Renders confirmed all three shapes and the graceful degradations: a bar shorter than `4 × radius` rounds by less and a 1 px bar comes out flat (the rasterizer clamps each radius to a quarter of the smaller side), and the pill fill is invisible below 2 px.
- **Measured, not eyeballed, because it is the one thing that could damage a neighbour:** the round-capped gauge's ink spans **x 1..74, y 1..41 of its 76×44 rect** at every value from 0 to 1000‰ (square caps reached y 37). Inside the rect, and clear of the `%` label's glyph band — which matters because `refresh_all` invalidates the *whole panel*, so the clip rect during paint would not have caught an overshoot.

**One rounded difference left, deliberately not taken: the utilization history is `type="monotone"` in the mockup** — a monotone cubic spline through the 40 samples, not the polyline `widget_sparkline` draws. Its round caps already round the *joins*, so the remaining gap is curvature between samples, not corners. Doing it means a spline evaluator emitting short vector segments (the rasterizer has no curve primitive), which is a bigger change than anything above and reads as smoothing that the data does not contain. Raise it if the panel makes the polyline look angular.

**2026-08-06 — The modal scrim is DONE and VERIFIED ON HARDWARE: a full-screen 75% dim with no framebuffer, no DMA and no bandwidth. Also confirmed BASE DMA *is* discarded behind the dialog.** Greg: *"that's cool. you even caught the need to dim the corners of the dialog. working as expected."*

Greg asked to try the DMA-disable idea from yesterday's research, and it works out exactly as the registers promised. `HEOCFG12.DMA = 0` makes HEO take its pixel from the **default-colour register**, and HEO's `HEOCFG9` carries a real **alpha** there (`ADEF`) — so the layer emits one constant ARGB pixel with no buffer at all. MCC had *already* configured HEO's blender as straight src-over on source alpha (`SFACTC = A0*As`, `DFACTC = 1-(A0*As)`, `A0 = 255`), so the composite is exactly `out = black·ADEF/255 + dst·(1 − ADEF/255)` and `ADEF = 191` is `bg-black/75` to the letter. Nothing had to be reconfigured to make the maths come out — the blend we needed was the blend already there for video.
- **`UiManager_ScrimShow(pct)` / `_ScrimHide()` are intent only**, applied by the video task's `heo_reconcile`, so HEO stays single-writer as it always has. That reconcile is now three-state — **video / scrim / off** in priority order — and acts only when leaving a video bind or when the level changes, because every layer `update` busy-waits a vsync. Latency is one 20 ms video tick, i.e. the same tick that hides the video, so the dim and the video's disappearance land together.
- **The trap this change had to defuse: `heo_bind` never touched `HEOCFG12`.** Video worked only because `DRV_XLCDC_Initialize` leaves HEO's `DMA` set and nothing had ever cleared it. The scrim clears it — so without a fix, closing the dialog would have brought the video back as a **solid black wash**, with the capture pipeline healthy and nothing obviously wrong. `heo_bind` now re-asserts `DMA = 1` itself. This is the sort of latent coupling that only shows up when a second user of the layer appears.
- **Second coupling, caught by reasoning rather than by looking:** the dialog's rounded corners hold a *copy* of the base view (§18), but what the panel shows around them is the base view *dimmed* — so an undimmed copy would have read as four bright notches in the corners. `scrim_dim565` applies the same factor the blender does. The corner trick and the scrim had to learn about each other.
- **OVR1/OVR2 cannot do this**: their `ADEF` is documented "only for post-processing usage". So the scrim is HEO's, and the nav drawer (which does *not* hide the video) can never have one.
- **The on-screen keyboard adopted it the same day** (Greg), so both modals now dim identically off the shared `MODAL_SCRIM_PCT`. Nothing else was needed: the keyboard's corners are square, so unlike song-select it holds no copy of the base view that would have to be dimmed to match, and its rounded key buttons and text-field box sample backdrops *inside* its own canvas — which the scrim, being a layer below, cannot touch. Also free in the degenerate case: closing song-select and immediately opening the keyboard (the 2P START path) resolves to hide-then-show within one 20 ms tick, and since `heo_reconcile` acts on level *change*, the net result is zero register writes.

**The black 2px column right of each modal's border — one cause, two bugs, and Greg spotted it on both modals at once.** `gfx_canvas.c:342` aligns a **non-32bpp** canvas window's X **down to a multiple of 4** (`setPositionParm.x &= ~0x3`) and does *not* widen or shift the window to compensate — while `gfxcGetWindowPosition` keeps returning the **unaligned** value that was requested. So the canvas renders somewhere the rest of the system does not believe it is:
- **Song-select** asked for x=90, landed at **88**, covering 88..1187 — but the BASE discard rect, derived from the stored 90, covered 90..1189. Columns **1188..1189 were told to skip BASE's fetch with nothing drawn over them**: black. Keyboard identically at 1168..1169 (110 → 108). Exactly the reported symptom, and *pre-existing for song-select* — the discard rect has been 2px right of the dialog since it was introduced; today's keyboard discard inherited it.
- **Second bug from the same root, found while tracing the first: the album-art strip was 2px out of its slot.** `ScreenSongSelect_ArtOrigin` returned `SONGSEL_X + ART_X` = 90+343 = 433, but the dialog was rendering at 88, so the art sat 2px right of its rect inside the dialog. Note yesterday I "verified" 433 against the figma export — which was true of the *export* and false of the *hardware*, because the export assumed the dialog at 90. A derivation checked only against another document is not checked.
- **Fix: align the request instead of the result.** New `CANVAS_X_ALIGN(x)` in `ui_manager.h`; `SONGSEL_X` → 88, `KBD_X` → 108. Nothing moves visually (the hardware was already using those), but every derived value becomes truthful at once — discard rect, corner-cut sample offset, and `ArtOrigin` (now 431, and the art canvas is RGBA8888 so it is exempt from the alignment and lands exactly there). The corner cut had also been sampling the base view from 2px right of the true corner; invisible on flat card backdrop, correct now.
- **Swept the other canvases:** dashboard / wiimotes / bus are at x=0, album art is RGBA8888 (exempt), and `NAVIGATION_CLOSED_X` was *already* deliberately aligned — that local `CANVAS_WIN_X_ALIGN` in `screen_navigation.c` is the same fact discovered independently for the drawer's slide, which is the clue I should have followed sooner. **Still unaligned by nature:** the drawer's Move FX steps `xpos` by 6, so intermediate positions get floored and the slide can jitter up to 3px. Cosmetic, in motion only, not chased.

**Confirmed for Greg: yes, BASE DMA is discarded behind the song-select dialog.** `UiManager_OpenSongSelect` sets `s_dialog_discard`, and `base_discard_reconcile` programs the LCDC's single BASE discard window (`BASECFG4.DISCEN` + `BASECFG5/6`) to the 1100×660 dialog rect, priority-ordered video rect → dialog rect → none, all from the video task so BASE stays single-writer. The scrim does **not** disturb it: BASE is still read outside the dialog — that is what gets dimmed — and still skipped behind the opaque dialog.

**Same session, bandwidth sweep off the back of Greg asking whether BASE is disabled when the video goes fullscreen.** It is not *disabled* — it stays enabled with the **discard window covering the whole panel** (`DISCXPOS/YPOS = 0`, `DISCXSIZE = 1279`, `DISCYSIZE = 799`, `DISCEN = 1`), which is the documented mechanism for exactly this and already skips ~123 MB/s of BASE reads. The size fields are 11-bit, so full-screen fits with room to spare. Everything else is quiet in that state too: the OVR1 video frame is hidden on fullscreen enter, OVR2 is unbound unless the drawer or album art is up, so HEO is the only reader.
- **Gap found and closed: the keyboard modal was not discarding BASE**, despite being an opaque 1060×560 canvas over the dashboard — the same situation as the song-select dialog, which does. The old comment justified it as "the dialog does no bandwidth-heavy work", which is a reason it wasn't *needed*, not a reason it wouldn't help. **~71 MB/s** recovered.
- **The discard rect now comes from the modal's own canvas window** (`modal_discard_set(canvas)` via `gfxcGetWindowPosition`/`Size`) instead of `DIALOG_W/H/X/Y` constants duplicated in `ui_manager.c` — which literally carried a *"keep the 1100x660 size in sync"* comment. They happened to still be right, so no bug; but the hazard was real, and yesterday's border-inset work touched that very layout.
- **`ScreenDashboard_SetShown()` added**, the peer of `ScreenBus_SetShown`/`ScreenWiimotes_SetShown` the dashboard never had. While fullscreen video covers it, or another base view has replaced it, telemetry was still repainting into a 2 MB surface nobody scans out — wasted CPU *and* DDR **writes**, which the discard does nothing about (that only stops reads). The gate lives in `dashboard_feed.c`, because the dashboard has no periodic work of its own and the feed is its sole writer; `ScreenDashboard_SetShown` is a one-line face on it for call-site symmetry.
  - **Deferred, not dropped** — the interesting part. The feed blocks on its queue, so simply skipping applies would leave stale widgets until the next event *of each type*, which outside a run could be a very long time. So `s_have[]`/`s_latest[]` became persistent across drains: while hidden, events keep coalescing; the show flushes exactly the set that changed. A show also has to **post a wake event** (an out-of-range type the drain ignores), because the consumer is parked on `portMAX_DELAY` and would otherwise not notice. `SetShown` only wakes on a real false→true transition, so the several paths that legitimately re-assert "shown" cost nothing.
  - **The modals deliberately do NOT gate it:** the dashboard stays visible (and now dimmed) around song-select and the keyboard, so telemetry must keep updating behind them — that is the compositor's whole coexist premise (§5). Only *full* occlusion gates.
  - Checked every hide path has a matching show path: fullscreen exits via `ScreenVideo_ShowWindowed`, a base-view swap returns via `UiManager_ShowDashboard`, and both modal closes route through `ShowWindowed`. A modal cannot be opened *from* fullscreen (dashboard input is gated off there, so every tap exits first), so there is no state where the dashboard is gated off while partly visible.
- **Not measured, by construction only:** there is no bus monitor wired up, so all of these are "the registers say the fetch is skipped", not observed DDR traffic. Worth a real measurement some day.

**Verified on the panel first try** — the dim and the AA'd corners against the dimmed dashboard both landed as intended, which also confirms the blend equation and `ADEF` behaving as the datasheet describes for a DMA-disabled layer. **Still unknown (not a risk, an opportunity):** whether the window geometry (`HEOCFG2`/`3`) is honoured in this mode. We program it full-screen either way, so it cannot be told apart from here; if it *is* honoured, a partial-screen dim is available for free too.
