**2026-08-06 (last) — Tilt and whammy drag lag: the cause is that a 2-pixel thumb move repaints the whole widget. Both now damage only the band that can change — measured **9× less work for tilt**, **3.8× for whammy**, and the framebuffer comes out **bit-identical** to a full repaint. CONFIRMED ON HARDWARE: Greg — *"significantly improved!"*.** His report that started it: *"tilt is a bit sluggish (it was sluggish before too). Whammy is better but also a bit sluggish."*

Ruled out first, so it is on record: **the send path is not the problem.** `tilt_changed` → `Fauxmote_SendTilt` and `whammy_changed` → `send_guitar` both just latch state under a critical section and give a semaphore to the TX task — no blocking I/O in the Legato task's context.

The actual budget per drag step: `SYS_INPUT_Tasks` polls touch every 10 ms, `LEGATO_Tasks` ticks every 10 ms, and with `LE_PREEMPTION_LEVEL 0` `leRenderer_Paint` runs the **entire frame synchronously** inside that tick. So latency is ~20 ms of scheduling plus the paint — and the paint was repainting all 157×157 of the tilt (and all 273×64 of the whammy) because `value_set` called `invalidate`, which damages the widget's whole rect. Legato already coalesces touch-move events into one (`leInput_InjectTouchMoved` overwrites the pending event), so there is no queue backlog to blame: it is purely paint cost per frame.

- **`widget_tilt` — full repaint 44 476 pixels scanned / 355 808 point evaluations; a 1° step now 4 786 / 38 288.** The damage rect is the union of the round-cap box at the old and new angle. That box also contains the thumb (a smaller disc on the same centre), and because cos/sin are monotonic across the swept quadrant the two boxes bound the sector between the angles too — so it is provably everything that can change. **This only works because of yesterday's vector conversion:** the old float paint scanned its whole rect regardless of the clip rect (`leRenderer_PutPixel` culled the *writes*, after paying for the coverage maths), whereas the vector kernel clips its **scan area** up front. The review predicted "partial repaints become safe again"; they are also now the fast path.
- **`widget_whammy` — 17 472 px / 50 824 `rrect_sdf` (each a `sqrtf`) → 4 480 / 13 440 for a 1-step move.** Still the float paint, so it needed both halves: the loop is now bounded to `rect ∩ clip`, and `value_set` damages a full-height column band around the two thumb positions. Bounding the loop is invisible and free — those pixels were being computed and then culled.
- **Verified, not assumed:** the host harness renders angle/value A, damages only the computed rect, refills it with the parent background (what Legato's parent repaint does) and repaints — then diffs against a full repaint at B. **0 pixels differ, worst 0**, across tilt 44→45, 45→46, 40→50, 0→1, 89→90, 0→90 and whammy 0→1, 20→21, −1→1, −50→50, 99→100, 60→0, 0→−1. Two consecutive drag rects always overlap, and `addDamageRectToList` merges overlapping rects, so a burst of changes between paints cannot fragment the list.
- Cost: **+992 B** flash (`.text` 1 372 052 → 1 373 044).

**Raised and settled: the whammy's fill geometry is deliberate, keep it.** The fill capsule is padded by `hh` — half the widget height, 32 px — so its rounded ends land outside the span it nominally covers: at 25% the thumb centre is at `cx + 32` while the fill runs `cx − 32` to `cx + 64`, and at ±1 a 65-px lozenge appears at once rather than growing from nothing. I flagged that as a defect; it is not. The padding is what makes the fill's end match the track's own radius instead of a stubby cap, the code says so (`/* pad to a capsule end cap */`), and on the panel the thumb riding inside a swept bar reads well — Greg: *"whammy looks good. stick with what we have."* The "fill should end at the thumb" reading was inferred from a mockup I have not seen. Consequence for perf: the damage band stays 70 px wide, so the whammy's 3.8× stands and the further ~3× is off the table by choice.

**If ~20 ms of scheduling latency still reads as lag** after this, the remaining lever is the two `vTaskDelay(10)` loops in the MCC-generated `config/default/tasks.c` (`LEGATO_Tasks`, `SYS_INPUT_Tasks`). Halving them would halve the fixed part, at the cost of another MCC re-apply patch on a generated file — worth doing only if the panel still feels slow with the paint cost gone.

---

**2026-08-06 (earlier) — Follow-up 4 landed and is CLOSED: `tilt`, `gauge`, the `panel_aa` dot and `fret` now draw with Legato's vector rasterizer. Every per-pixel `sqrtf`/`cosf`/`sinf`/`atan2f` is gone from all four paint paths; +7 912 B flash. A/B'd against the old float code on a HOST harness, then run on the panel later the same day (the drag-lag work below was measured on it). All four signed off on the panel — the gauge's crisp ring accepted as intended. The `marvin-perf` A/B was dropped as not worth the cost (decision log).**

The four steps of the adoption order are done in order, one widget each, all four verified pixel-against-pixel (see below). Shared conventions live in a new header-only [`ui/gfx/vec_draw.h`](../default/src/ui/gfx/vec_draw.h): `UI_VEC_AA` (the project-wide 8X pin — one place to drop to 4X if profiling asks), `UI_VEC_DEG16`, a `leRectF` builder, and an arc-point helper. Header-only on purpose: a new `.c` would mean editing `user.cmake`, and the vector `.o` files were already being compiled and thrown away by `--gc-sections`.

- **`widget_tilt`** — the whole 157×157 rect scan with ~8 soft-float transcendentals per pixel is now four calls: `ArcStroke` for the track (90°..180°, round caps), `ArcStroke` for the fill, and two `ArcFill` discs for the thumb. The angle mapping is the thing to remember: the vector API is 0° right / counter-clockwise, so the widget's "tilt d degrees off the left ray" is vector angle **180 − d**, and both arcs therefore start at their high-tilt end. `value_from_point` keeps its `atan2f` (once per touch).
- **`widget_gauge`** — two `ArcStroke`s, and this one **changes how it looks**, deliberately. The old coverage was `1 − |d − r|/(t/2)`: a *feathered* ring, fully opaque only on the exact centre line and fading to nothing over 3.5 px each side. The vector stroke is crisp, so a `thickness = 7` gauge now reads as a solid 7 px ring instead of a soft smear. That is what the mockup's stroke width was asking for and I think it is the right reading — but it is the one change here that is a taste call rather than a fidelity fix, so look at it first. Making it soft again would mean `hardness < 1`, which is exactly the poisoned path (vendor bug (a)); dropping `thickness` to 5 is the cheap knob if it reads heavy.
- **`panel_aa` dot** — now draws a true **capsule**, which retires both the sampled-backdrop hack (`leRenderer_GetPixel(rect.x - 1, …)`) and its `rect.x > 0` guard, and covers the case the review missed: this vtable also serves `screen_bus`'s **pill tracks**, not just square dots. A single rounded `RectFill` cannot express either shape (see the clamp bug below), so it is a band `RectFill` plus one `ArcFill` per end, degenerating to a single disc when the widget is square. `PanelAA_EnableDot` now **clears the widget's background type** — the shape blends against what is behind it, so a skin fill underneath would leave square corners. Documented in the header; the call sites still ask for `BACKGROUND_FILL` and are simply superseded.
- **`widget_fret`** — one `RectFill` idle (`alpha = 191` replaces `cov * 0.75f`), or ring-then-inset-body when held. `rrect_sdf` and the last `sqrtf` are gone.

**Verified with a host-side A/B harness, since a screendump needs the panel.** The vendor vector tree compiles natively with one shim header standing in for `legato_renderer.h` (plus a stub `device.h`) — so old float path and new vector path can render the same widget into the same RGB888 canvas under stubs that mimic what `paintWidget` hands a paint (frame rect = canvas, **clip rect = the widget's damage rect**), and be differenced. Results, over tilt at 0/1/30/45/89/90°, gauge at 0/1/250/500/999/1000‰, fret idle+held, a 12×12 dot and a 100×8 pill: **tilt** ≤ 40/255 worst channel, mean 4–11, and only on 1-px edges; **fret idle** 7 122 pixels differ by ≤ **8** (pure alpha rounding, 0.75 → 191/255); **fret held** 84 px, worst 31; **dot** 38 px, worst 31; **pill** 28 px, worst 7. Gauge differs by design as described. **No case painted a single pixel outside the widget rect** — worth checking explicitly, because the tilt arc's *scan* box is the full circle (254×254, larger than the widget) and only the clip rect keeps it in bounds.

- **The harness earned its keep immediately: it caught a bug the compiler could not.** `LE_REAL_I16_FROM_FLOAT(f)` is `((int32_t)(f * 65536))` — **no parentheses around the argument** — so `LE_REAL_I16_FROM_FLOAT(THUMB_R + RIM_HALF)` expanded to `12.0f + 1.25f * 65536` and the thumb rim came out at radius **1.25 px instead of 13.25**. On screen that is a one-pixel dot where a 26-px disc belongs; in a diff it is unmissable. The parens at the call site are now load-bearing and commented. Legato's own uses of that macro are all `/` and `*`, which is why nothing in-tree ever tripped it.
- The harness is **ephemeral** (built in `$TMPDIR`), because it duplicates the old float paints that this commit deletes. The reusable part is the recipe: shim `gfx/legato/renderer/legato_renderer.h` down to `GetFrameRect`/`GetClipRect`/`BlendPixel`/`PutPixel`/`CurrentColorMode`, add an empty `device.h`, and compile the ten `gfx/legato/vector/*.c` plus `legato_real_i16/u8`, `legato_rect`, `legato_math`, `legato_color_*`. Worth landing as a tool if we do the remaining widgets — offered, not taken.

**Three more findings about the vendor vector code, on top of the two bugs §5 of the review already lists.** All three change what is *possible*, so they matter more than the two known ones:

- **`leDraw_VectorRectFill` clamps every corner radius to `min(w,h)/4`, not `min(w,h)/2`** — `_clampCorners` halves the `leRectF` extents, which are *already* half-extents. So a **capsule is not expressible** as a rounded rect fill at all. This kills the review's §4.5 plan for `bar` (capsule track + capsule fill) exactly as written, and it is why the dot is band-plus-discs. Our fret radius (8 on a 112×64 pad, clamp 16) is unaffected.
- **`widget_whammy` cannot move at all**, and not for a perf reason: every shape in it is a capsule (same clamp), *and* the fill, tick and thumb are all multiplied by the track's coverage (`* cov`) so they clip to the capsule. The vector API has no clip-rect setter — `leRenderer_GetClipRect` is read-only — so there is no way to express "inside the track only". It stays hand-rolled; it is the widget that would have benefited second-most.
- **The review's "shrinks the scan to each shape's own bbox" is wrong for arcs.** `_calculateScanArea` uses the **full circle** at `radius + halfWidth`, ignoring the span — the tilt arc scans 254×254 where the old loop scanned 157×157. It only comes out even because the renderer's clip rect is the widget's damage rect. Fine here, but it means an arc drawn small inside a large widget scans far more than it paints.

**Nothing is owed on this item any more** (both the on-panel look and the Legato-developer note closed 2026-08-06; the `marvin-perf` A/B was dropped — decision log). **`sparkline` was converted later the same day (Greg asked), which retracts the reason given for skipping it** — see the decision log: it is not the most repainted widget, the bus screen refreshes at 1 Hz.

**FOLLOW-UPS (opened 2026-08-05, Greg).** **ALL FOUR ITEMS ARE CLOSED (2026-08-06).** 1 — swept, nothing else was affected. 2 — tier ramp reordered, Generated, confirmed on the panel. 3 — research only, nothing implemented (that was the deliverable). 4 — four widgets converted and signed off on the panel, gauge's crisp ring accepted, Legato-developer note sent, `marvin-perf` A/B knowingly dropped (decision log). Item 3's free HEO scrim and item 4's unconverted widgets are the live leads out of this block.

**1. Sweep the rest of the firmware for the string-renderer alignment bug — DONE 2026-08-06, and the answer is that there was nothing else.** The cause is general: `leUStringRenderRequest.align` **only distributes multiple LINES within the string's own bounding box** — `drawUString` draws every glyph at `req->x + lineX` with `lineX` starting at that box's origin — so for a **single-line** string `LE_HALIGN_RIGHT` and `LE_HALIGN_CENTER` behave exactly like `LEFT` and the text runs rightward off the anchor. But it only bites *direct* renderer callers, and there is exactly one in the whole firmware: `widget_song_list.c`, fixed in `af5825b`.
- **`screen_bus.c` was the prime suspect and is innocent.** Its right-aligned table numbers — and the dashboard's, the keyboard's, the wiimotes' — are all `leLabelWidget`s, and the stock label skin resolves alignment *correctly*: `leUtils_ArrangeRectangleRelative` positions the kerning rect inside the label's bounds by halign, and only then passes the **already-resolved** `kerningRect.x` as `req.x`, leaving `req.align` to do its intended multi-line job. Worth knowing precisely, because "the renderer's align is broken" is the wrong lesson — the renderer's align means something else, and every stock widget uses it correctly.
- Also checked: **no custom-paint widget draws text at all** (gauge, sparkline, bar, whammy, tilt, fret) — the bus gauge's centred `%`, for instance, is a child label. `grep -rn "leStringRenderer_\|leStringUtils_"` over hand source returns three hits, all in the song list plus one comment.
- And the fix is **exact, not approximate**: `leStringUtils_GetLineRect` sums `glyph.advance`, the same quantity `drawUString` advances `lineX` by, so the measured width is precisely what the draw consumes and the right edge lands on the anchor rather than off by a side bearing.
- **The rule for next time:** passing `LE_HALIGN_*` to a *widget* is fine; passing it to `leStringRenderer_Draw*` is not, and needs the `draw_str` pattern (measure, resolve to a left edge, draw `LEFT`).

**2. Revisit the tier colour ramp — DONE 2026-08-06, Generated and confirmed on the panel (see decision log + session log).** New ramp is `blue-400 #60A5FA · cyan-400 #22D3EE · green-400 #4ADE80 · lime-400 #A3E635 · yellow-400 #FACC15 · orange-400 #FB923C · red-400 #F87171 · red-600 #DC2626`, bonus stays `SCHEME_TEXT_ZINC_300`. Greg's objection was that the old ramp *"doesn't read as ordered"* and that he wants the mockup's progression — cooler for easy, hot for harder — fitted to eight steps. Two measurements settled it: the sawtooth is structural (see decision log), and the old ramp's ΔE claim was CIE76 (min ΔE2000 is really **10.2**, not 27).

- **The mockup's five anchors were already in the repo and nobody had connected them to the labels.** [`TIER_PALETTE`](../../../tools/gh3-cover-art/process_gh3_cover_art.py) in `tools/gh3-cover-art/process_gh3_cover_art.py` — tiers 1/3/5/6/7 are green-400 / blue-400 / yellow-400 / orange-400 / red-400, the mockup's five, with teal (2), violet (4) and red-500 (8) inserted to reach eight. Its comment even says *"set the matching MGS `SCHEME_TEXT_TIER_n` text color to this"*. **Look there first for the mockup palette — no `.tsx` is checked into this repo**, so the offline tooling is the only in-repo record of the mockup's colours.
- **The baked art and the labels had disagreed since the band ramp landed on 2026-08-05** — `TIER_PALETTE`'s accents were still the *rejected spectral sweep* (green · teal · blue · violet · yellow · orange · red · deep red), baked into every large cover strip. Adopting that art palette as-is was offered as the zero-asset-cost option and rejected: it carries the violet reversal and the weak T7→T8 pair. So the fix was **two** changes plus a re-bake, and the two are now cross-checked programmatically (`TIER_PALETTE[t][0]` == scheme `text` for all eight).
- **What landed:** the eight `SCHEME_TEXT_TIER_1..8` `text` roles in `default_design.zip`, `TIER_PALETTE`'s accents and `<hue>-900` tints, and a re-bake of all 70 covers. Only **24 larges** changed — tiers 5–8 already carried yellow/orange/red-900 and red-950, so only tiers 1–4's tints moved. No C changes: `SongDetail_TierScheme` maps tier → scheme and `SongDetail_TierColor` derives the badge colour from it, so the switch is entirely design DB + art tool. MGS Generate has been run and `le_gen_scheme.c` carries the new colours (`SCHEME_TEXT_TIER_1` = `0x60A5FAFF`, RGBA).
- **Two `mgs-legato-design` gotchas found doing it** (both now in the skill): MGS's serializer is `json.dumps(indent=4, sort_keys=True)` *except* empty containers render `[\n]`, so a naive json round-trip rewrites ~1200 unrelated lines — edit the member as text instead; and `mgs_zip.repack()` only backs up `if not os.path.exists(bak)`, so a stale `.bak` from an earlier session silently means **no backup of the current state**.
- **Consumers to re-check on hardware:** the **song-list tier badge** (`SongDetail_TierColor`, bold 12px on zinc-900), the song-select detail strip's `★★★ TIER 3` line (over baked art), and the dashboard TIER cell. The badge is deliberately **not** tier-coloured on the selected row (`SL_SEL_BADGE`, zinc-500) because the ramp is tuned for zinc-900 and fails against the zinc-100 selection fill — that rule still holds for the new ramp (every stop measures 1.2–2.3:1 on zinc-100), so don't be tempted to re-enable it.
- **Tier 8 is knowingly sub-AA** at 3.67:1 on zinc-900. If it reads too dim on the panel, the fallback is `red-500 #EF4444` (4.71:1) at the cost of dropping T7→T8 separation from ΔE 18.2 to ~10.6 — i.e. back to the problem we just fixed. Judge it on hardware before changing it.

**3. Dimming the base layer for a modal scrim — the CLUT idea, and something better in the datasheet.** Greg asked whether the HEO CLUT could dim BASE, and whether a 2bpp layer could get us the mockup's `bg-black/75`. Read the SAM9X75 LCDC register docs; both work, and one is free:

- **The cheap answer: HEO with DMA OFF emits a solid ARGB colour with NO framebuffer at all.** [`HEOCFG9`](https://onlinedocs.microchip.com/oxy/GUID-51CE727C-126C-4670-A2BA-189781F25924-en-US-2/GUID-4965633A-8D35-4848-A113-565B06B19321.html) §5.2.7.89 carries `ADEF[7:0]` / `RDEF` / `GDEF` / `BDEF` — "Default **Alpha** value when the high-end overlay DMA channel is disabled". And `xxxCFG9.DMA = 0` is documented as "the pixel for the current layer is retrieved from the default color register". So `ADEF = 191, RDEF/GDEF/BDEF = 0` + DMA off = a full-screen 75% black wash for **zero memory and zero DDR bandwidth** — no 2 MB surface, no 123 MB/s read, nothing for the CSI-2 contention budget to worry about. HEO already sits **below OVR1 and above BASE** in our z-order, which is exactly where a scrim belongs, and it is free during song-select because the video is hidden anyway.
  - **Note OVR1/OVR2 are NOT equivalent here:** their `ADEF` (§5.2.7.47 / §5.2.7.64) is annotated "*only for post-processing usage*". HEO's has no such caveat. So this trick appears to be HEO-specific — worth confirming, since it decides whether the keyboard modal can have its own scrim while HEO is busy.
  - **To verify on hardware:** that the window geometry (`HEOCFG2`/`3`) is still honoured with DMA off; and which blender factors consume `ADEF` as *source alpha* — `SFACTC = A0_MUL_AS` with `A0 = 255` and `DFACTC = M_A0_MUL_AS` is the standard src-over pair (factor tables in §5.2.7.50). Also that it cooperates with `heo_reconcile`'s single-writer discipline over HEO.
- **The CLUT answer, if a scrim ever needs to be non-uniform** (a hole, a gradient, rounded corners): **CLUT entries carry 8-bit alpha** — `ACLUT[7:0]` in [`HEOCLUT`](https://onlinedocs.microchip.com/oxy/GUID-51CE727C-126C-4670-A2BA-189781F25924-en-US-2/GUID-CE4A34A5-1CE1-4CA6-83CB-6A24737500CD.html) §5.2.7.119 (and the OVR/BASE equivalents), and `CLUTMODE` offers **1/2/4/8 bpp** (§5.2.7.81). So Greg's instinct was right and even cheaper than guessed: **1 bpp** is enough for a binary scrim mask — 1280×800 = **125 KB**, ~7.7 MB/s, with two arbitrary ARGB colours available per pixel. 2 bpp buys four.
  - Caveat for that path: `CLUTEN` and `GAM` are mutually exclusive on the same layer ("When GAM = 1, writing in LCDC_HEOCLUT has no effect", and CLUTEN blocks it too) — and we currently use HEO's **gamma CLUT** for the video levels expansion (ui_compositor §15.1). A CLUT-mode scrim on HEO would therefore have to be swapped in and out around the video, not layered with it. The DMA-off default-colour route has no such conflict.
- Either way this is **research only — nothing is implemented and none of it has run on hardware.** It supersedes ui_compositor.md §18's "needs a full-screen surface plus a spare hardware layer" as the reason the scrim was skipped; the real answer is that it may cost almost nothing.

**4. Move the custom widgets onto Legato's vector rasterizer (`gfx/legato/vector/`) — Greg, 2026-08-06. The four-widget conversion is DONE the same day; see the entry above.** Background is [`legato_vector_review.md`](legato_vector_review.md) (2026-08-05) — what the API is, why it wins, the per-widget mapping in §4, the adoption order in §6 — with a corrections block now at its top. What is left of the item:
- ~~**On hardware:** look at all four, then `marvin-perf` before/after on tilt and gauge~~ — **SIGNED OFF (Greg, 2026-08-06).** All four look right on the panel; the gauge's crisp ring is **accepted** as the intended reading, so no `thickness` drop to 5. The `marvin-perf` A/B was **consciously dropped, not forgotten** — see the decision log. `UI_VEC_AA` in `ui/gfx/vec_draw.h` remains the one-line 8X→4X knob if a repaint cost ever shows up.
- ~~**To the Legato developer**, now five items~~ — **REPORTED (Greg, 2026-08-06).** All five went across: §5's two bugs (sticky gradient shader, `arc_stroke` convexity), the `arc_fill` `ranges[1]` nit, `leDraw_VectorRectFill` clamping corner radii to `min(w,h)/4` (so capsules are inexpressible), and `LE_REAL_I16_FROM_FLOAT` not parenthesising its argument. Two of them shape our code until they are fixed upstream, so they stay documented rather than closed: `hardness` is pinned to `LE_REAL_I16_ONE` at every call site to dodge the sticky shader, and the radius clamp is what keeps `ui/widgets/bar` and `widget_whammy` off the vector path (see "not converting" below). Re-test those two when a new Legato drops.
- **Not converting, with reasons:** `widget_whammy` (capsule shapes *and* it clips everything to the track's coverage, which needs a clip-rect setter the API does not have), `ui/widgets/bar` (the review's §4.5 target after `progressbar_aa` was deleted — blocked by the same radius clamp, and its dithered gradient has no vector equivalent), and `aa_corners`/`panel_aa`/`button_aa` (the corner-box footprint beats anything the API can express — the review's own conclusion). `widget_sparkline` **was** on this list and came off it the same day — five widgets converted, not four.

**2026-08-05 (last) — Song-select taken over from MGS: 35 widgets across TWO layer-screens stripped, both rebuilt in C and re-styled to `SongSelectModal.tsx`. The design now holds ZERO authored widgets — seven layers, seven bare panels, assets only. Plus: rounded dialog corners on an opaque RGB565 overlay, faked by copying the layer below. Code done, builds + links; PENDING Greg's MGS Generate and a hardware look.**

The last MGS-authored tree, and the only one spanning **two** layer-screens: 30 widgets under `PANEL_SONG_SELECT` (layer 2, the 1100×660 dialog on OVR1) and 5 under `PANEL_SONG_SELECT_ALBUM_ART` (layer 3, the 508×208 RGBA8888 cover strip on OVR2). That split is the feature, not an accident — everything that changes with the selection (cover, tier line, title, artist) lives on the small second surface, so switching songs repaints 0.42 MB instead of the whole dialog. Kept exactly as-is; `screen_song_select.c` builds the widgets on both layers, `screen_album_art.c` keeps owning layer 3's surface and window.

**§17.1's trap was armed and this is the strip that would have fired it.** Song-select held the design's last `leButtonWidget`, `leLabelWidget` *and* `leImageWidget` — the three types the whole code-built UI needs. They are pinned (Greg did it after the dashboard strip), and the tell that a pin is real rather than design-derived is `LE_CIRCULARGAUGE_WIDGET_ENABLED = 1` with no circular gauge anywhere in the design. Worth re-checking after Generate, because from now on **every** widget type the firmware uses is on loan from a pin — the design has no widgets left to keep any of them alive.

**Rounded corners without per-pixel alpha — Greg's idea, and it's the cheap one.** The mockup's dialog is `rounded-xl`; the dialog canvas is opaque RGB565, so its corners can't reveal the dashboard the way §16's ARGB_4444 video frame reveals HEO. Greg: *"is there any way we can effectively fake this? content behind dialog corners is fixed. could we just pull through the base layer in the corners and cheat the transparency?"* Yes — and it beat both alternatives I'd costed (RGBA8888 dialog: 2.9 MB + ~174 MB/s OVR1 read while open, in the bandwidth region that has knocked CSI-2 out of lock; fixed-colour corner cut: four black notches over live dashboard content). The corner boxes are filled with `base_surface[(SONGSEL_Y+y)*stride + SONGSEL_X+x]` and AA'd against the dialog's own fill + 1px border: **4 × 12 × 12 px, no format change, no extra layer.** New `AaCorners_RenderSurface565` takes a per-pixel backdrop *sampler* and writes the surface directly instead of through `leRenderer`, so it runs outside a paint pass; `ui_manager` runs it at **open**, keyed off `UiManager_BaseCanvas()` so it tracks whichever base view is behind. Written up as ui_compositor.md **§18**. What makes it possible at all: both canvases are CPU-readable RGB565 statics and `UiSurface_Get` already records every canvas's base pointer — an overlay can read what's under it even across hardware layers.
- **The one real consequence, found by doing the layout arithmetic rather than on hardware:** anything that repaints over a corner undoes it. The song list spanned y 98..660 and its row separators + selected-row fill span its full width, so **every scroll would have wiped the bottom-left corner.** Fixed by ending the list `DLG_R` short of the dialog's bottom edge — which is also what the mockup's `rounded-xl overflow-hidden` does to its last row, so the faithful layout and the correct one agree.
- Composes with the existing BASE-discard optimisation: the dialog still discards BASE DMA behind itself, and that stays safe *because* the corner pixels were copied rather than composited.
- Snapshot semantics are the limit: live content moving under a corner would go stale until the next open. Those four spots are page/card background today.

**Mockup deltas applied.** Row height 44 → **57** (`px-4 py-3` + two `text-xs` lines + `border-b`) and the tier badge the `SongList` widget has always supported but the screen never filled — `T3`, coloured from the same scheme as the detail strip's tier text via new `SongDetail_TierColor` (no second copy of the palette). The widget's nine hand-picked greys → Tailwind zinc, and its selected row is now **full-bleed** zinc-100 with the `border-b` drawn *over* it for every row, rather than an inset rect with the rule only on unselected rows — matching how CSS actually paints a `border-b` on a `w-full` button. Also `SL_PAD_X` 20 → 16, `SL_BADGE_COL` 50 → 28 (badge `w-4` + `gap-3`), art radius 14 → **12** (`rounded-xl`), SELECT radius 10 → **8** (`rounded-lg`), and the tier line reads `★★★  TIER 3` (new `SongDetail_TierTextLong`) — the dashboard's TIER cell keeps stars-only because its column header already says TIER.

**A whole class of height was one pixel-pair short, caught by Greg asking to check the mode buttons.** Tailwind's `border` adds to an **auto-height** element — content + padding + border is the rendered height regardless of `box-sizing`, which only bites when an explicit height *is* set. So every bordered element here was under-derived by 2: difficulty buttons 40 → **42**, mode buttons 32 → 34, list rows 56 → **57** (`border-b`, one edge). The 42 corroborates independently — exactly what the figma import carried for those buttons. **Rule for the next Tailwind→px port: check each element for a `border-*` class and add it; a bordered element is 2px (1 for a single edge) taller than padding + line-height suggests.**

**Then Greg said the buttons still read short and re-exported the mockup — and they were, for a second, unrelated reason: the mode buttons are `py-3`, not `py-2`.** So mode is **42** as well, and the earlier local copy of `SongSelectModal.tsx` was simply stale. The re-export also drops `1p-human` from `MODES`, so **the mockup is now 2 modes and agrees with the firmware** — that stops being a deliberate delta. Two lessons: the mockup is a moving target, so re-read the component at the start of a styling pass rather than trusting a copy read earlier in the session; and *"it looks shorter than the mockup"* was right twice over while my arithmetic was self-consistent both times, because it was arithmetic over the wrong source.
- **Re-deriving every element against the fresh export also caught a miss that had nothing to do with either:** the selected row's tier badge is `text-zinc-500`, not the tier hue — `TIER_COLOR[s.tier]` is the *unselected* branch of that ternary. The tier colours are tuned for contrast on zinc-900 and several fail against the zinc-100 selection fill, so this is a real legibility rule, not decoration. Fixed in the widget (`SL_SEL_BADGE`), since `songlist_row_t.selected` is stamped by the widget *after* the row provider returns and the provider therefore cannot know.
- Everything else in the export re-derives to what is already built: dialog 1100×660/r12, header 65, setlist strip 33, row 57 with text at x=44, art 208/r12 with its three lines at 122/142/172, metric pitch 48, right column 224 at p-4, SELECT 56/r8 bottom-anchored. BPM is still in the mockup and still deliberately dropped.

**Every other layout number is derived**, and the derivation reproduced the imported design's own values where those were right — metric grid at y=313, SELECT at y=588, art labels at 122/142/172 — which is a good sign the Tailwind→px resolution is faithful. Where it disagreed it was by 1px, and the album-art canvas position moved with it: `screen_album_art.c` now asks `ScreenSongSelect_ArtOrigin()` instead of carrying `ART_X 433 / ART_Y 160`. Its *size* stays local, because 508×208 is an **asset** property (the slot covers are decoded into) — so the centre column is sized around the art, not the reverse.

**No design-side additions at all.** Just the two strips: all 23 orphaned strings are kept and driven from C via `stringID_*`, and the mockup's **BPM cell is dropped** — Greg: *"lose the bpm we don't have this data"*, matching the same call on the dashboard song card yesterday. So the metric grid stays a 2×2 (ALBUM|YEAR / GENRE|DURATION). Two images (`BUTTON_ICON_CLOSE`, `BUTTON_ICON_CHECK`) stop being widget-referenced and become code-referenced — `prune_unused_images.py` greps hand source, so they're safe, but that's now the only thing keeping them.

**The setlist line is a composed runtime string** (`"SETLIST · %d TRACKS"` from `GameCatalog_Count()`), which is a deliberate localization debt: the mockup's caption embeds a count, and the count is data. The design string keeps the same words so its glyphs — including the `·` — stay declared. Verified rather than assumed: `DejaVuSansMono_12`'s `ranges.json` declares Latin-1, so U+00B7 is a property of the font, not on loan from that caption. **Second sighting of `audit_glyph_coverage.py`'s blind spot** — I wrote the dot as `\xC2\xB7`, so the audit reports "every rendered literal is pure ASCII", exactly as it does for the byte-assembled ★.

**Verified:** design zip self-consistent after both strips (`audit_refs.py` exit 0, 26 images, 115 strings, manifests == asset dirs, 0 dangling refs); glyph coverage clean; all six touched TUs compile clean under `-Wall -Wextra`; **full `ninja` links** — and it links *both* before and after Generate, because the new code names only the two panels that survive. Layout arithmetic checked by evaluating every macro: all content inside the dialog, no overlap, 9 rows visible. **Nothing has been on the panel yet.**

**Then, deliberately AWAY from the mockup (Greg): the difficulty and mode buttons go to `py-4`** — 42/42 becomes **54 / 50** (they differ because the mockup's captions are text-sm and text-xs, and that distinction is kept). Same call as the dashboard's 60px gameplay buttons: a real panel wants a bigger touch target than a browser mockup. Derived from one `PAD_BTN_Y`, so the padding is the only number to change. **No spacing adjustment was needed, which is worth recording because it is counter-intuitive:** the taller stack ends at y=497 leaving 91px above the bottom-pinned SELECT, and the *mockup* leaves **105px** there (its three 42px mode buttons end at 483) — so the column is now tighter than the original, not looser, and the `mt-auto` rhythm carries over untouched. Resulting size hierarchy reads correctly too: SELECT 56 (the primary action) > difficulty 54 > mode 50.

**Remaining deliberate deltas from the mockup**, now that the mode count has stopped being one: no BPM cell (no data), no `bg-black/75` scrim (needs a full-screen surface + a spare HW layer — §18), 8 tiers rather than the mockup's 5, and 64 SD-catalog tracks rather than its 15. No letter-spacing anywhere either — Legato has no `tracking-widest`.

**Post-Generate checks:** `le_gen_screen_Marvin.h` should declare only `Marvin_PANEL_SONG_SELECT` + `Marvin_PANEL_SONG_SELECT_ALBUM_ART` for layers 2–3 (the other 32 `Marvin_*SONG_SELECT*` / `*ALBUM_ART*` externs gone); the three `LE_BUTTON_/LABEL_/IMAGE_WIDGET_ENABLED` still `1`; 115 `stringID_*` and 26 `leImage` unchanged. **On hardware:** the corners against the dashboard, the column hairlines, badges + selected row, the art strip's three lines, difficulty/mode radios (mode's selected chip drops its border, since the mockup's `border-zinc-200` is its own fill), SELECT commits + closes, X closes. And touch everywhere — the art's corner overlay carries `IGNOREPICK`, but that was the dashboard's first-run bug, so it's the thing to check first.

**2026-08-05 — Dashboard taken over from MGS: 124-widget figma subtree stripped, dashboard now built in C and re-styled to the current mockup. New tier palette (two-shade difficulty bands). Code + design edits done; PENDING Greg's MGS Generate.**

The dashboard was the last and largest imported tree — **124 widgets**, of which hand code touched **34**. Everything on the human card, plus ACCURACY / STAR POWER / the strum chip / DETECTOR / ACTUATORS on the robot card, was a static placeholder no code had ever written. Meanwhile the mockup (`~/Downloads/Marvin`, reorganised into `components/screens/DashboardScreen.tsx` composing `PlayerPerformance` / `VideoStream` / `NowPlaying`) had moved on.

**Decisions (Greg, 2026-08-05):**
- **Header: keep the GUITAR + PIC logos, add the mockup's status dot.** The mockup replaced the logos with a text title; we keep branding. So `titlebar.c` stays 53px and the content origin stays y=77 — the whole re-layout lands inside the existing content rect.
- **Drop ACCURACY, ★ STAR POWER (both cards) and the human CONTROLLER block.** The last one is Greg's call and the right one: *"we don't actually have any connection to the human wiimote — no telemetry"*. Dropping accuracy + star power frees the 76px that pays for the mockup's taller (42px) option rows.
- **DETECTOR rows are now wired**: `Computer Vision` → `DETECTOR_CV_MARVIN_V1`, `Neural Network` → `DETECTOR_FRETBOARD`. Greg: *"cv is computer vision, fretboard is a nn implementation — keep labeling as neural network"*, and the bus module keeps the name `fretboard`. First real function on that card.
- **ACTUATORS is now one enable toggle per actuator node** — GUITAR / LEMMY / LIGHTSHOW, in the mockup's `grid-cols-2` (so LIGHTSHOW sits alone on the second row), each label-left with a state dot at the right edge. Replaces the single inert "GUITAR ACTUATORS" row after a mockup update mid-session. Still **not connected to the nodes**: the toggle latches the operator's intent and shows it, and wiring each index to its node (guitar output enable, lemmy motion, lightshow output) is one call per entry in `actuator_on_release`. Three new strings (`ACTUATOR_GUITAR/_LEMMY/_LIGHTSHOW`); `PLAYER_ROBOT_GUITAR_ACTUATORS` and `PLAYER_STATE_ENABLED/_DISABLED` fall out of use and are kept.
  - A Legato button paints its own caption and cannot host children, so the dot is a **sibling drawn over the button with `IGNOREPICK`** — the same idiom the card frames now use.
- **Tier colours → two-shade difficulty bands, amber mid-band.** Greg rejected the spectral sweep on the grounds that the easiest→hardest progression read wrong, and asked about two shades per band.

**The tier palette, chosen from measurements rather than taste.** Today's ramp had two defects: T7 `#F87171` / T8 `#EF4444` were both red (ΔE **21** — the pair most needing separation), and T4 violet → T5 yellow reversed direction mid-ramp. New ramp: green-300/500 · amber-300/500 · orange-300/500 · red-300/500, lighter = lower tier of the pair.
- **Every adjacent tier is now ΔE ≥ 27**, contrast 4.7–12.6:1 on zinc-900, and luminance steps **down** band over band (0.70/0.41 · 0.68/0.44 · 0.57/0.32 · 0.50/0.23) — "deeper and hotter = harder", sawtooth within each band.
- **Amber rather than yellow for the MEDIUM band** because yellow-400 is the most luminous hue available: with yellow, T3 became the brightest swatch on the screen (15:1) and outshouted T8. Amber flattens that hump (0.68 ≤ T1's 0.70) at a cost of only 2 ΔE.
- Band hues are the EASY/MEDIUM/HARD/EXPERT pill colours, so tier colour finally means the same thing as difficulty colour elsewhere. Star count still carries the exact tier, so the shading only has to say "upper or lower half of the band" — which is why 4 hues suffice for 8 tiers. A **one-step-apart** variant was measured and rejected (within-pair ΔE 11–21, which RGB565 largely erases — the same failure as the old red pair).

**The big structural find: MGS enables Legato WIDGET TYPES from what the DESIGN instantiates.** Stripping the dashboard removed the design's last `ProgressBarWidget` and its only `GradientWidget`, so the next Generate set `LE_PROGRESSBAR_WIDGET_ENABLED` / `LE_GRADIENT_WIDGET_ENABLED` to **0** and `leProgressBarWidget` / `leGradientWidget` ceased to exist — the build broke on code that had compiled minutes earlier, with nothing in the C changed. Greg spotted it: *"I think mgs disabled some widgets because no longer used in MGS design?"*
- **A widget type used only by hand-built code is a latent break every Generate.** The firmware uses five types (`leButtonWidget` ×60, `leLabelWidget` ×33, `leProgressBarWidget` ×13, `leImageWidget` ×10, `leGradientWidget` ×2). Of those, **Button / Label / Image survive only because song-select is still MGS-authored** — every other layer is now a bare panel. Take song-select over too and the entire code-built UI stops compiling.
- **Resolution (Greg): drop the two, force the three.** The progress bar and gradient were removable at no cost, which is the interesting part: `ProgressBarAA` already drew its own fill at pixel resolution, so the stock widget contributed *only* a square track and a vtable to hijack. Both are now `ui/widgets/bar` on a plain `leWidget` (`Bar_Enable` / `Bar_SetPermille`), and `progressbar_aa` is deleted. **Still to do: force `LE_BUTTON_/LABEL_/IMAGE_WIDGET_ENABLED` on in the Legato component** before song-select is rebuilt — that is not optional, just not urgent.
- A bar left at 100% with two colours *is* a gradient rect, which is how the test pattern's black→white ramp is drawn now. One widget covers both.

**Gradients are dithered, because RGB565 banding is not subtle.** Greg: *"could you implement a dithered gradient? otherwise gradient on RGB565 tends to be disappointing."* Correct — 5/6/5 bits give ~32 neutral grey levels, so the test pattern's 718px ramp would show ~22px bands. `ui/gfx/gradient.c` interpolates in 24-bit RGB and quantizes through an **ordered Bayer 8×8** threshold, per channel at that channel's real bit depth; the endpoints are plain `0xRRGGBB` because converting to `leColor` first would throw away the precision the dither needs. Modes with 8 bits per channel skip it. Cost is one `PutPixel` per pixel, so it is for rarely-painted fills — the static ramp, and a bar on change — not per frame.

**Two more defects found while rebuilding, both fixed:**
- **`widget_progressbar_aa` kept its fill width, radius and backdrop in MODULE statics.** Correct only while exactly one AA bar existed (the playtime bar). The new dashboard has three (playtime + two streak bars), and a shared `s_fillw` would have painted all three to whichever was set last. The replacement keeps a per-widget registry keyed by pointer.
- **The old playtime bar was filling WHITE.** It used `SCHEME_FILL_ZINC_800`, whose BACKGROUND (the colour the old ProgressBarAA drew the fill with) is `#FFFFFF`. Moot now — `Bar_Enable` takes the fill colours as arguments, since a gradient needs two and a scheme has one BACKGROUND slot. `SCHEME_PILL_HUMAN` was still needed though: the human multiplier pills were on `SCHEME_FILL_YELLOW_400`, i.e. yellow in *both* states. (The three `SCHEME_BAR_*` schemes added mid-session were removed again once the bars took explicit colours — recorded in `design_dashboard_schemes.py` rather than left to rot.)

**Card corners: the old `round_card()` used the wrong AA primitive.** It called `PanelAA_EnableRoundImage` on filled cards, which eats the corners back to the panel's own BASE (zinc-900) instead of the page black — so the radius never actually showed. The rebuilt cards use a **frame overlay added last**: a full-card panel, `SCHEME_BACKGROUND`, `BORDER_LINE` + radius, `PanelAA_EnableRoundImage`. The classic skin does draw a *rounded* line border when cornerRadius > 0 (verified in `legato_widget_skin_classic_common.c` — `DrawStandardLineBorder` branches to `DrawRoundCornerLineBorder`), then the AA pass eats the corners to black. Drawing it last is what makes the player art, which is flush to the card's top edge, read as clipped to the radius — the mockup's `overflow-hidden`. **Verify on hardware.**

**Runtime glyphs: the tier stars were one prune away from vanishing.** `SongDetail_TierText` assembles ★ (U+2605) as raw UTF-8 bytes at runtime, so MGS cannot see it; the glyph survived in `DejaVuSansMonoBold_12` only because *another screen's* caption (`SONG_SELECT_SongTier` = '★ Song Tier') happened to be bound to that font. Fixed properly by **declaring U+2605 in the font's `ranges.json`** (new skill script `add_font_range.py`) — the glyph is now a property of the font, not a side effect of someone else's string. Note `audit_glyph_coverage.py` reports "every rendered literal is pure ASCII" here: a byte-assembled character is invisible to a source-literal grep, which is a real blind spot in that audit.

**Mockup deltas** beyond the drops: metric row is now GENRE · **BPM** · DURATION · TIER (BPM comes from the catalog, which has always parsed it and never shown it); DIFFICULTY → **DIFF**; SELECT SONG / START are **60px tall** (the mockup's `flex-1`) instead of 36 — a real touch-target win; START becomes **STOP** while a run is in flight (`GameController_IsBusy` / `_Stop`); the state chips and strum chip switch on the same signal; **new streak bar** under each streak value (`streak/99`); album art radius 10 → 8 (`rounded-lg`); the test pattern gains the mockup's missing middle bar row and a centre crosshair; and the video frame goes 1px/r6 → **2px/r4** (`border-2 rounded-sm`), which is the follow-up the wiimotes work deliberately left open, so both screens now match. **PLEASE STAND BY** is drawn as the mockup has it — white on a black box over the ramp, centred on baseline `VIDEO_H - 65` — with one compromise: the mockup specifies **bold 32px** and the design carries bold **24** and **40**, nothing between. Measured widths for the 15-glyph message: 210 px at 24, 289 px at the mockup's 32, 360 px at 40. Chose **40**, because the on-screen keyboard already ships it (so it costs no flash) and a stand-by slate reading 25% larger than the mockup is defensible; the box hugs the measured advance rather than the mockup's literal pixel numbers. An exact Bold_32 would be a new font asset — offered, not taken. **Skipped** deliberately: the test pattern's "1000 Hz" / "720x480" corner captions, its TEST PATTERN chip (Greg — the bars say it already; `VIDEO_TEST_PATTERN` is kept unused), and the mockup's `vs MARVIN` delta row (no human score source exists, and it is `active`-only in the mockup).

**The test-pattern bars were full intensity; the mockup's are 75%.** Greg spotted it — the mockup's canvas draws the bars at **191** (`0xBF`), the standard SMPTE 75% level, with `#131313` rather than black for the middle row's separators. Ours were `#FF0000`/`#00FF00`/… i.e. 100%, which on the panel reads as a much harsher card than intended. All seven `SCHEME_TEST_PATTERN_*` recoloured to the 75% level, plus `SCHEME_TEST_PATTERN_DARK` (#131313) for the separators. Two elements in that card must *not* dim, so they stopped borrowing bar colours: the centre crosshair (new `SCHEME_FILL_WHITE`) and the NO SIGNAL dot, which the mockup draws `bg-red-500` (new `SCHEME_FILL_RED_500`) — it had been using the test-pattern red, and would have gone dull with it.

**Two-state captions are swapped design strings, not C literals** (PLAYING/IDLE, ACTIVE/IDLE, START/STOP): a `leTableString` per state, with the label re-pointed. Keeps them translatable — the one case where the strip's "drive strings from C" rule needs care, because the *text* changes rather than just its content. Placeholder strings whose value was always overwritten (`SONG_INFO_Title` = 'Title', `PLAYER_ROBOT_Score` = '000000', …) are genuinely dead now and show in the unused list; they are runtime data, not translatable captions.

**The human card's name is display-only** (Greg): the mockup's tap-to-edit affordance was built and then removed — the name is entered on the keyboard START already raises for a 2-player match, so a second entry point is redundant. `player_name_committed` now refreshes the card's label as well as `Results_SetPlayer`. `screen_video.c` gated fullscreen input through `Marvin_PANEL_DASHBOARD_BOTTOM`, a design widget this strip deletes — it now asks `ScreenDashboard_Content()`, the same handle pattern as `_Titlebar()`.

**Verified:** design zip self-consistent (26 images, 111 strings, 70 schemes, 0 dangling refs, glyph coverage clean); the 10 unused images are exactly the 5 reserved nav icon pairs, unchanged by this work; **`ninja` links** after Greg's Generate, and all six touched TUs compile clean under `-Wall -Wextra`. **Not yet eyeballed on hardware.**

**First hardware run: no touch anywhere on the dashboard except the titlebar hamburger.** Cause: the card **frame overlays**. `leUtils_PickFromWidget` keeps the *last* child whose rect contains the point, so the full-card overlay that has to be added last (to paint on top of the art, which is the whole reason it exists) also won every touch inside its card. The hamburger worked because the titlebar has no overlay. Fixed with `LE_WIDGET_IGNOREPICK` on all four card frames + the album-art corner-cut overlay: the pick walk then discards them and keeps the real target. Video tap-to-fullscreen was never affected — it works by *bubbling* to `Marvin_PANEL_DASHBOARD`, not by being the picked widget. **Rule, now in ui_compositor.md §17: paints-on-top and wins-the-touch are the same property in Legato — any decorative widget stacked over interactive ones needs `IGNOREPICK`.**

Everything the design side needs is generated: 70 schemes, `0x2605` present in `le_gen_fonts.c`, and Greg pinned `LE_BUTTON_/LABEL_/IMAGE_WIDGET_ENABLED` (plus progressbar/gradient/circulargauge, which cost nothing — `--gc-sections` drops them and `nm` finds none of their symbols in the ELF).

**Open / to check on hardware:** the robot card's bottom ~84px is empty, because the mockup's own column is top-aligned with fixed gaps and simply leaves the slack there. Faithful for now; if it reads as unfinished, distribute it across the section gaps.

**2026-08-05 — Nav drawer taken over from MGS: figma subtree stripped, drawer now built in C from a 3-row entry table (Dashboard / Wiimotes / Bus Statistics), styled to the mockup. Code + design edits done; PENDING Greg's MGS Generate (the build cannot link until then).**

The drawer was the last figma-imported widget tree still authored in MGS. It is also the panel with the most reason to be code-owned: **its row set has to track which screens actually exist**, which is a code fact. The design shipped seven rows, four going nowhere, and the Diagnostics row was wired to the *bus* screen.

*Why hand-code (same reasoning as bus / wiimotes / keyboard).* One `NAV_ENTRY[]` line per row — caption `stringID`, icon pair, base-view verb — so adding a screen adds a row, and the four unbuilt rows can't drift into a shipping menu. The pattern is proven: [`screen_bus.c`](../default/src/ui/screens/bus/screen_bus.c) built a whole screen into an empty MGS panel, [`titlebar.c`](../default/src/ui/titlebar.c) is the shared-component flavour. Recorded as ui_compositor.md **§17** (the general "panels built in C" rule), which also retires two stale claims there: the drawer is not "its own `Navigation` MGS Screen" any more, and ButtonAA is no longer single-radius (`AaCorners_Render` computes coverage analytically, so radius 8 works).

**Design zip (5 scripted edits, all idempotent + dry-run first).** `strip_subtree.py PANEL_NAVIGATION` removed 19 widgets and kept the root panel; new `design_nav_panel_retarget.py` made that panel a plain opaque backdrop (`SCHEME_FILL_ZINC_900`, FILL, border NONE — it had been carrying a *row's* scheme plus a full `BORDER_LINE` box) and recoloured `SCHEME_NAV_BUTTON_UNSELECTED.text` white → `#D4D4D8`; `add_image.py` added the `NAV_ICON_BUS` pair; new skill script `add_string.py` added `NAV_BUTTON_Bus_Statistics`.
- **The unselected label was white, so only the fill and icon ever changed on selection.** The mockup styles the whole row `text-zinc-300` → `text-white`. Now the label tracks the icon. Safe to edit that scheme in place: it was referenced by the nav widgets only, and they are gone.
- **The Bus icon is `network.svg`** — the lucide original `assets/README.md` had already reserved "for a Bus Statistics nav row the drawer doesn't have yet". Rasterized zinc-300 + white at 24×24; verified both members have **identical opaque-pixel counts (174)**, i.e. alpha-identical, which is the property the pair relies on.
- **New reusable skill script `add_string.py`.** Adding a string means **two** lists: `strings[]` (identity + per-language value) *and* `bindings[]` (`{string, language, font}`). An unbound string generates a `leTableString` with no font — and the binding is also what makes MGS include the value's glyphs, so the font choice belongs in the add, not later. It refuses a duplicate name (the generated C symbol *is* the name).
- **Kept deliberately: 5 icon pairs + their captions** (Activity Logs, Performance, System Info, Settings, Diagnostics). They are now referenced by neither a widget nor C, so `prune_unused_images.py` will report them — that is expected, noted in `assets/README.md` so a future prune doesn't quietly delete them.

**Mockup deltas, all from `NavigationDrawer.tsx`** (Tailwind v4 → px): row width 287 → **288** (`w-full` inside `p-4`), corner radius 12 → **8** (`rounded-lg`), subtitle + `STATUS` zinc-200 → **zinc-500** (`text-zinc-500`), the status dot gets AA via `PanelAA_EnableDot` instead of being a 12×12 square, and the three `border-*` hairlines are single 1px zinc-700 rules (right edge, under the header, over the footer) instead of figma's full `BORDER_LINE` boxes on three nested transparent panels. Every layout number is now derived — `HDR_H` from title+subtitle+`p-6`, `FTR_H` from the footer's content row + padding — rather than copied from the export.

**Kept verbatim from the old module** because it is proven and design-independent: the nocache surface, `NAVIGATION_CLOSED_X` window-clip derivation, the whole Move-FX slide path (stop-before-restart, jump path owning the close ending), the base-view pickability gating, and `navigation_highlight`'s repaint-only-the-two-changed-rows + first-call-paints-all + per-row `invalidate()` (the fix from earlier today).

**`nav icon <row>` was hard-coding the drawer's geometry** (`16+16`, `113+64*row+16`, rows 0–6) — a copy of the layout it exists to inspect, and it would have gone stale silently. Now `ScreenNavigation_GetIconRect(row, …)` supplies the rect and the row bound, so the console tracks `NAV_ENTRY` and the dump adapts to the icon size.

**Verified so far:** design zip is self-consistent (26 images, 102 strings, manifest == asset dirs, **0 dangling widget asset refs**, no unbound string, no binding to an unknown font/string); `screen_navigation.c` + `console.c` both compile clean under `-Wall -Wextra` with the three not-yet-generated symbols stubbed. **Not yet verified on hardware.**

**Handoff — this will not link until MGS → Generate**, because the code names `stringID_NAV_BUTTON_Bus_Statistics`, `NAV_ICON_BUS`, `NAV_ICON_BUS_SELECTED` first (the same handoff as this morning's icon-pair work; confirmed the failure is exactly those three identifiers and nothing else). Post-Generate sanity check: `le_gen_assets.h` should declare **26** `leImage` (24 today) and the `Marvin_BUTTON_NAV_*` / `Marvin_PANEL_NAVIGATION_TOP` externs should be **gone** — if they are still there, MGS didn't read the edited zip (close and reopen the design; see the Generate-lag note below).

**2026-08-05 — Two root causes, one bug: a missing `invalidate()` in the nav drawer produced BOTH a visual artifact on the icons AND killed the USB CDC link. Fixed and verified on hardware. Along the way: a canvas-dump facility, USB sink hardening, and a nocache corruption detector.**

**Symptom 1 — a small block of wrong pixels at the top-left of every nav icon,** which "changed colour but not shape" (pink, later blue) and appeared on icons that had never been selected.

**Symptom 2 — opening the drawer and selecting a row killed the whole perf-log USB stream.** Console (FLEXCOM2 UART) unaffected, drain task healthy, only a reboot recovered. Song-select — which does *more* graphics work, including album-art decode and OVR2 binding — never triggered it.

**Both were `navigation_highlight()` not invalidating.** The image setters raise no damage, and the damage `setScheme` raises does not cover every pixel the icon draw touches. That left a small block at each icon's top-left holding its previous paint:
- **stale blue** where the row had been selected (the `#195DFF` fill, AA-blended with icon ink), or
- **uninitialized DDR** where it had not — and this is the second root cause: **`.region_nocache` is `NOLOAD` and is never zeroed.** `_szero`/`_ezero` bracket **`.bss` only**, so every canvas surface starts as whatever DDR held at power-on (or the previous session's framebuffer after a warm reset). That garbage is what read as "pink".
- Hence the artifact tracked the row's *previous* state and needed no selection to appear. Chasing it through the image data — RLE encoding, PNG `bKGD` chunks, channel order — found nothing, because **those pixels were never written**.

**The USB death is the same bug reaching further.** The map explains why:

| address | size | object |
|---|---|---|
| `0x20000000` | 0x90 | **UDPHS endpoint objects** |
| `0x20000090` | 0x30 | linker fill |
| `0x200000C0` | 4 MB | `LE_SCRATCH` (Legato renderer) |
| `0x204000C0` | … | capture pool, then every canvas surface |

The USB driver's endpoint/transfer state is the **first object in the region**, 48 bytes below Legato's scratch, and nothing bounds-checks any of it. A paint driven by a malformed damage rect underruns the scratch and lands on it. Fixing the invalidate cured both symptoms — Greg confirmed screen capture then survived drawer open/close, which is what tied them together. *The underrun mechanism remains inference: the addresses and the correlation are measured, the write itself was never caught in the act.*

**Fix:** `navigation_highlight()` invalidates each button it touches, and repaints only the two rows whose state changes rather than all seven (first call still paints all seven, so it does not depend on MGS's build-time defaults). Per-state icon pairs (`NAV_ICON_X` zinc-300 + `_SELECTED` white, same SVG, alpha-identical) were the feature that exposed it — a scheme cannot recolour an image, and the figma export had frozen Dashboard in its selected state, so its icon was permanently white.

**Detection added, because this took far too long to find.** `health/nocache_guard.c` seeds patterns in the region and re-checks them from the drain task. Its limit is real and documented: link order decides placement, so it sits just after `LE_SCRATCH` (catching an overrun past it) and **cannot** guard the USB objects — the linker emits `config/default` objects before any of ours, so nothing we write lands in that 48-byte gap; only a change to the MCC-generated `ddram.ld` could. The sink's **submitted-vs-completed write divergence** covers that blind spot by watching the effect instead of the memory, and is the check that would have named this in minutes.

**Debugging lessons worth keeping.**
- **Instrument before theorising.** Five hypotheses died to measurements: drain-task stack overflow (302 of 512 words free), a TX credit leak (`credits 3, reclaims 0`), HEO scaling (Bus Stats hides video and still died), RLE encoding (off changed nothing), and unchecked RX arming (`queued, failures 0`). Each was plausible from reading code and wrong.
- **A classifier hid the answer.** The first `nav icon` dump bucketed pixels as ink/backdrop/red and reported "0 red-dominant" — I concluded the framebuffer was clean. It was not: the icon's *shape* had a hole, which Greg spotted in the ASCII art. Printing a legend of the actual RGB565 values is what exposed the stale blue.
- **The decisive test was Greg's:** song-select doing more graphics work without dying ruled out "too much graphics" and pointed at the drawer specifically.

**Two real latent defects found on the way, fixed, neither the cause.** TX credits are returned only on `WRITE_COMPLETE`, so a host closing the port mid-burst leaked them permanently (3 losses = permanent stall); and `prime_rx_read()` discarded its result while only being called from `CONFIGURED`/`READ_COMPLETE`, so one silent arming failure left the device deaf to commands for the session while TX kept working.

**Still open:** the USB link appeared to die for host sessions *after the first* even with the link flags reading healthy (`cfg 1`, `cls 1` never incrementing). That may simply have been this same corruption, but it was never isolated on its own — worth re-testing now the invalidate is fixed, and treating as its own investigation if it persists.

**2026-08-05 — `assets/` now holds real originals (not design-zip exports), and the nav drawer got per-state icons. Generated + built.**

**Rule (Greg): never source archived assets from `default_design.zip` unless there is no other source.** The zip's `sourceData` is a *derived* copy — icons rasterized to the pixel size the UI needs, artwork cropped and scaled. So `firmware/marvin/assets/` is now the upstream material, documented in [`assets/README.md`](../assets/README.md):
- `icon/lucide/` — **13 lucide SVGs** (Greg downloaded them), lucide's own filenames so a fresh download diffs cleanly. The mockup used `lucide-react@0.487.0`. Includes `network.svg`, unused, for the Bus Statistics row the drawer still lacks.
- `image/source/` — the **upstream masters**, all higher-res than anything in the design: the two AI renders (`VitruvianGuitarist.png` 1024², `LemmyOnStage.png` 816×1276), `LemmyPuppet.jpg`, and the 361×84 Microchip lockup. The repo's existing `HumanPlayer*.png` / `LemmyOnStagePlayerImage*.png` are all already 254×208, i.e. derived — this establishes the chain.
- `font/` — the two real TTFs (Greg dropped them in). The design carries 14 font assets but only **two distinct TTFs**; the Noto pair is kept, marked superseded.
- **Verified the archive is faithful rather than assuming it:** rasterized all 12 icon SVGs at each asset's size and sampled colour and compared side by side — every one reproduces its design asset.
- **Trap found and documented: `play.svg` ships as an open outline** (`fill="none"`), but the shipped asset is a *solid* triangle because the mockup rendered it `<Play className="… fill-current" />`. It is the only icon needing `fill`; re-rasterizing it naively yields an outline. Also `Home` is now a lucide alias — the file is `house.svg` (Greg caught this).

**Why the nav icons had inconsistent colours — and the fix (option 2, per Greg).** `NAV_ICON_DASHBOARD` was `#FFFFFF` while its six siblings were `#D4D4D8`. Cause: in the mockup the icon inherits `currentColor` from its button, which is `text-white` when active and `text-zinc-300` otherwise, and `useState('dashboard')` makes Dashboard the default active row — **so the figma export froze one row in its selected state**. Not an import error, a stateful design captured in one frame.
- marvin could not reproduce that: `navigation_highlight()` swapped `SCHEME_NAV_BUTTON_SELECTED`/`_UNSELECTED`, but a scheme changes a button's *fill* and **cannot recolour an image** — `setPressedImage`/`setReleasedImage` are press states with fixed pixels. Net effect on hardware: Dashboard's icon was permanently white and the rest permanently zinc-300 regardless of selection.
- **Fixed with per-state icon assets.** All 7 rows now ship a matched pair (`NAV_ICON_X` zinc-300 + `NAV_ICON_X_SELECTED` white), both rendered from the *same* SVG so only colour differs — verified alpha channels are pixel-identical. `navigation_highlight()` now sets the image alongside the scheme. Design went 17 → 24 images; the design still binds the rest variants as the widget default.
- Note both nav schemes have `text=#FFFFFF`, so the **label** never changed colour on selection either — only the background did. Now the icon tracks it too.
- **This was the one genuinely build-breaking handoff of the session** (hand source referenced `NAV_ICON_*_SELECTED` before Generate emitted them); confirmed the failure, then Greg generated and `ninja` links with 24 `leImage` / 7 `_SELECTED` present.

**New reusable skill script: `set_image_source.py`** — swap an existing asset's `sourceData` while keeping its uuid, so every widget binding survives (contrast `add_image.py`, which creates a new asset needing rebinding). Updates the blob plus the dimensions in `imageconfig.json` and `colorCount` in `rawconfig.json`, and warns when pixel dimensions change since that moves layout. Also `export_assets.py`, written earlier in the session and now framed in the skill as a **recovery** path for when the zip is the only copy — not the way to build an asset archive.

**2026-08-05 — Unused-image prune: 44 → 16 images, ~604 KiB of flash to be reclaimed on the next Generate.** Rule (Greg): delete anything referenced by neither the Composer design nor hand code. New reusable skill script `prune_unused_images.py` does the analysis and the removal; zip 3.71 → 3.21 MB, manifest and asset dirs agree, no widget references a missing image, strings/fonts untouched, `ninja` still links.

- **Flash impact, measured from the generated arrays rather than guessed:** the 28 doomed `*_data[]` arrays in `le_gen_images.c` total **618,472 B (604.0 KiB)**; the 16 survivors total 288,639 B (281.9 KiB). Bigger than the font cleanup's 443 KiB. **Pending Greg's MGS Generate** — unlike the strings/fonts pass, `ninja` does *not* regenerate, so `le_gen_images.c` is unchanged until then (still 44 arrays). Harmless meanwhile: nothing in hand source names a doomed image.
- **The analysis has to test both halves, and a naive grep gets it wrong.** "Unused by widget" is not unused (logos + the hamburger are drawn from C), but a bare `\bMarvin\b` grep over hand source *keeps* the 114 KB `Marvin` image purely on prose hits — the master screen is also called Marvin, so comments and `Marvin_*` symbols create false positives. Fix: strip comments before matching. With that, `Marvin` is correctly dead (the splash loads raw RGBA8888 from QSPI, not from the design). `game_art.c` was also checked for indirection — it builds its `leImage`s at runtime via `leImage_Create`, so album art holds no design asset.
- **Superseded originals:** `HumanPlayer` / `LemmyOnStagePlayerImage` were dead while their `*_gradient` variants are the ones widgets use — 241 KB of source blobs. Confirmed different sha1s, so genuinely superseded rather than duplicates.
- **Byte-identical triplet:** `BUTTON_ICON_HAMBURGER`, `figmaImg_Icon`, `figmaImg_Icon_18` share one sha1; the two figma copies were dead and went.

**Naming — 9 of the 16 survivors still carry meaningless `figmaImg_*` names.** Identified by walking the design to the widget that uses each, then *rendering the PNGs* to confirm. Every one is a button `pressedImage`+`releasedImage` icon, not baked text — the nav rows carry real `NAV_BUTTON_*` strings, so localization is unaffected. All 9 are widget-only with **no** hand-source reference, so renaming is zero-risk (same uuid, no C patch).

**The mockup's icons are [lucide](https://lucide.dev) SVGs rasterized to small PNGs** — not font characters. Rendering the assets confirmed the set exactly matches `NavigationDrawer.tsx`'s imports: Home / FileText / Gauge / Info / Activity / Gamepad2 / Network / Settings.

| current | used by | size | is | proposed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `figmaImg_Icon_0_0` | dashboard `..._SELECT_SONG` | 14×14 | list+note | `BUTTON_FACE_SELECT_SONG` |
| `figmaImg_Icon_1_0` | dashboard `..._START` | 14×14 | play ▶ | `BUTTON_FACE_START` |
| `figmaImg_Icon_11` | `BUTTON_NAV_DASHBOARD` | 24×24 | Home | `NAV_ICON_DASHBOARD` |
| `figmaImg_Icon_12` | `BUTTON_NAV_LOGS` | 24×24 | FileText | `NAV_ICON_LOGS` |
| `figmaImg_Icon_13` | `BUTTON_NAV_PERFORMANCE` | 24×24 | Gauge | `NAV_ICON_PERFORMANCE` |
| `figmaImg_Icon_14` | `BUTTON_NAV_SYSTEM_INFO` | 24×24 | Info | `NAV_ICON_SYSTEM_INFO` |
| `figmaImg_Icon_15` | `BUTTON_NAV_DIAGNOSTICS` | 24×24 | Activity | `NAV_ICON_DIAGNOSTICS` |
| `figmaImg_Icon_16` | `BUTTON_NAV_SETTINGS` | 24×24 | gear | `NAV_ICON_SETTINGS` |
| `figmaImg_Icon_17` | `BUTTON_SONG_SELECT_CLOSE` | 20×20 | X | `BUTTON_ICON_CLOSE` (matches `BUTTON_ICON_CHECK`/`_HAMBURGER`) |

*Correction to an earlier note in this entry's first draft: these are 24×24 / 20×20 / 14×14 icons. The "287×56" figures were the **button widget** sizes, not the images.*

**Design bug found while naming, and FIXED: the Wiimotes nav row was showing the Activity-Logs icon.** The drawer had 7 rows but only 6 distinct icons — `figmaImg_Icon_12` (visually confirmed as lucide `FileText`, a document) was bound to **both** `BUTTON_NAV_WIIMOTES` and `BUTTON_NAV_LOGS`. Reads as a figma-import artifact (the Wiimotes row copied from Logs, inheriting its icon). No gamepad existed anywhere in the design — checked all 44 images including the 28 just pruned, whose only 24×24 members were hamburger duplicates.

Fixed by **adding lucide `gamepad-2` as a new image asset programmatically** — no Composer import. Greg supplied the authentic SVG (rather than me reconstructing the path from memory); `currentColor` substituted for `#D4D4D8` — sampled from the existing nav icons' opaque pixels so the stroke matches — then `rsvg-convert -w 24 -h 24`. Result is 24×24 RGBA, 675 B, stroke `(212,212,216)` identical to its siblings. Added as `NAV_ICON_WIIMOTES` and bound to `BUTTON_NAV_WIIMOTES`'s pressed+released images; `figmaImg_Icon_12` is now Logs-only, so every row has its own icon. Zip 17 images, manifest == asset dirs, no widget→missing-image ref, `ninja` links.

**Decision (Greg, 2026-08-05): button icons stay IMAGES; glyph-vs-image consistency is judged per control group, not globally.** File size is not a constraint here, so the deciding factor is consistency *within a set of buttons* — and for the five `BUTTON_*` images, all-glyphs is impossible: lucide `ListMusic` (SELECT SONG) and `Menu` (hamburger) have no reasonable Unicode equivalent (♪ U+266A is a note, not a playlist; ☰ U+2630 is in none of the 14 fonts). Since only all-images is achievable for that set, that is what we keep. **Two conversions were considered and rejected on those grounds**, despite both being cheap and available: `BUTTON_ICON_CHECK` → `'✓ SELECT'` (U+2713, which the keyboard *already* draws as a glyph via `GLYPH_CHECK`) and `BUTTON_FACE_START` → `'▶ START'` (U+25B6, already in `DejaVuSansMono_18` via `WIIMOTE_DPAD_RIGHT`).
- **The resulting rule, which the firmware already follows:** a control group goes all one way, and the group's *least-representable* icon decides which way. Song-select + dashboard-gameplay buttons ⇒ images (forced by ListMusic/Menu). Keyboard (`✓ OK`, `⌫`) and the wiimotes d-pad/strum (`▲ ◀ ▶ ▼`, `−`) ⇒ glyphs (no image assets exist and MGS auto-includes them). So the mixed picture across screens is not an inconsistency — it is this rule applied per group.
- **None of the five is a character in the mockup**: all are lucide SVG components (`Menu`, `X`, `Check`, `ListMusic`, `Play` with `fill-current`), rasterized to 14–24 px PNGs.
- **Open, needs a look on hardware:** the two dashboard gameplay buttons disagree about icon/text spacing. `GAMEPLAY_SELECT_SONG` is `' SELECT SONG'` with a **leading space** and no `setImageMargin`; `GAMEPLAY_START` is `'START'` with neither. Only `SONG_SELECT_SELECT` declares one (`setImageMargin(9)`). So on SELECT_SONG that leading space may be load-bearing — an earlier note in this journal called it a figma artifact to clean up "regardless", which was overconfident. The tidy fix is an explicit `imageMargin` on both and no leading space, making spacing a declared property rather than an invisible character; verify visually before doing it.

**Renames applied: zero `figmaImg_*` names left.** All 9 renamed per the table above (`_12`→`NAV_ICON_LOGS` now that it is Logs-only), verified `outputName` == manifest `name` for all 17 images, build links. New skill script `rename_images.py`: a rename must move **both** `imageconfig.json`'s `outputName` *and* the `images.json` entry's `name`; widgets key off the uuid so bindings are safe, but the generated C symbol *is* the name, so it refuses to rename a code-referenced image unless forced and prints the `&OLD`→`&NEW` patch. All 9 were widget-only.

**Unexplained Generate lag (cause NOT established).** Greg ran Generate several times while these zip edits were landing, and at one point the generated tree reflected the *strip* and the *orphan sweep* but **not** the image prune — `le_gen_assets.h` still declared all 44 `leImage` while the zip had 16. It resolved on a later Generate and everything renders correctly now. No root cause proven. Two things noticed that may or may not be related, recorded only as leads: `.legato_generate_cache.zip` shares a timestamp with `default_design.zip` after an external edit, and MGS re-serializes the design on save — so an edit made while Composer holds the project open is at least plausibly missable or overwritable. **The cheap diagnostic if this recurs: the design should list 17 images; 44 means MGS is not reading the zip on disk.** Closing and reopening the design before Generate is the low-cost precaution.

**New reusable skill capability: `add_image.py`** — adding an image needs four writes (`imageconfig.json`, `rawconfig.json`, `sourceData`, plus the `images.json` manifest entry) and it works by **cloning a sibling's config** rather than synthesizing one, so every field this MGS version expects is present including `memoryLocation`. Two non-obvious details: `rawconfig.json`'s `maskColor.image` is **self-referential** and must be repointed to the new uuid, and `mgs_zip.repack` only *replaces* existing members, so the three new asset members have to be appended in a second `zipfile` open. `--bind WIDGET:prop,…` repoints widgets in the same pass.
