**2026-08-05 — Wiimotes / manual-override screen re-layout to the `ManualOverrideScreen` mockup (`~/Downloads/Marvin`). Decision: hand-code the screen in C (bus-screen model), strip the figma-imported subtree out of the design zip. IN PROGRESS.**

*Why hand-code, not MGS Composer.* The mockup's component pixel sizes are already **identical** to the design's (71×71 buttons, 180×71 strum, 273×148 whammy, 157×157 tilt, 5 frets ≈107×64), so this is a re-parent + reposition of ~30 widgets — exactly the tedium Composer is worst at, and the resulting zip delta is unreviewable. Meanwhile three mockup elements **cannot** be expressed in MGS at all and need custom-paint widgets regardless (tilt is a static PNG today; whammy is four dead nested panels; frets need held-state feedback). [`screen_bus.c`](../default/src/ui/screens/bus/screen_bus.c) is the proven precedent: a whole screen built programmatically into an empty MGS panel, ported from a mockup TSX with Tailwind units resolved to `#define`s.

*Layout fits 1280×800 at the mockup's native sizes.* Two 618-wide cards side by side (margins 16, gap 12) against needs of 572 (guitar: strum 180 + whammy 297 + ± 71 + padding) and 588 (wiimote: dpad 225 + 16 + actions 150 + 16 + tilt 157 + padding); bottom row ≈274 tall; 800 − 274 − 16 − titlebar 65 = 445 left for the 420-tall video. Dropped vs today: `PANEL_WIIMOTES_HUMAN` (620×716, **empty**), the `RobotLemmy` header, and the ENABLED pill — the mockup has none of them.

**Decisions (Greg, 2026-08-05):**
- **Tilt maps to the accelerometer, not a star-power bit.** Star power isn't a thing we control — we only need to *simulate tilt*. Rest pose = gravity on **−X**; tilting up rotates it toward **+Y**. So `gx = −32·cos θ`, `gy = +32·sin θ`, `gz = 0` in wire units over θ∈[0°,90°]. `MF_AUX_STARPOWER` stays unused (fauxmote already ignores it and its header comment already says star power belongs in the ACCEL slice).
  - **`MF_MSG_ACCEL` turns out to be fully implemented on the fauxmote side already** — `mf_link.c` dispatches it to `Wiimote_SetAccel`, and `wiimote.c` converts signed 1/32-g units to raw report bytes on its advertised calibration (`0x85` = 0 g, 27 counts/g) and folds them into report modes `0x31/0x33/0x35/0x37`. Only marvin's sender is missing.
  - **The three `mf_proto.h` copies have drifted** — fauxmote's has the `MF_ACCEL_LSB_PER_G` / `MF_ACCEL_LEVEL_{X,Y,Z}` block and an updated `MF_AUX_STARPOWER` comment; marvin's and fretboard's don't, despite the header's "keep the three byte-for-byte in sync". Resync as part of this work.
- **Whammy spring-returns to center** on release. Maps to the wire for free: `MF_WHAMMY_MASK` is 5-bit 0..31 with `MF_WHAMMY_REST = 0x10` (center), so the mockup's bipolar −100…+100 *is* the wire format. No proto change.
- **Video gets the rounded frame, 2 px stroke.** Confirmed against the mockup: `VideoStream` is `border-2 border-zinc-700 rounded-sm` → 2 px, `#3F3F47`, and Tailwind **v4** (`tailwindcss@4.1.12`) `rounded-sm` = 0.25 rem = **4 px** radius. Border-box, so the stroke eats the outer 2 px of the 630×420 rect — same convention as the dashboard's existing overlay. Parameterize `screen_video.c`'s `border_fill` instead of copying it.
  - `BORDER_C4 = 4` already renders `#444444` ≈ zinc-700, so the colour needs no change.
  - The dashboard uses the *same* `VideoStream` component in the mockup but ships 1 px / r6 today. Left alone for now — one-line follow-up if we want it to match.
- **Tap-on-video will drive the IR pointer** on this screen (not fullscreen-toggle like the dashboard). Deferred: get the video up first, logic later.

**Plan — all code landed, builds clean, awaiting MGS Generate + hardware check:**
- [x] Resync `mf_proto.h` (marvin + fretboard ← fauxmote), drop the stale "(planned)" on `MF_MSG_ACCEL`. All three copies md5-identical again.
- [x] `Fauxmote_SendAccel()` + `Fauxmote_SendTilt()` in marvin — latched + dirty-flag, the `SendPointer` pattern. Tilt→gravity uses a **91-byte `32·sin(θ)` table** (`cos θ = sin(90−θ)`, one table for both axes) rather than `sinf/cosf`: the ARM926EJ-S has **no FPU**, so trig would be soft-float.
- [x] Extract the AA rounded frame fill out of `screen_video.c` into a shared `ui/gfx/video_frame.{c,h}` taking (buf, w, h, radius, stroke, c4). Dashboard keeps 720×480 / r6 / 1 px; wiimotes gets 630×420 / **r4 / 2 px** per the mockup.
- [x] `ui_manager`: show the video on the wiimotes base view instead of `VideoHide()`, via a `ScreenWiimotes_VideoRect()` / `_VideoFrameSurface()` pair so the layout stays the screen's business.
- [x] New `ui/widgets/whammy` — bipolar centre-detented slider, capsule track + centre tick + centre-out fill + capsule thumb, drag & spring-return. Maps to the wire as `16 + value·16/100`.
- [x] New `ui/widgets/tilt` — quarter-arc, AA annulus with round caps, drag to aim. Geometry derived from the widget rect so the mockup's 157×157 reproduces its exact numbers (centre 137,137 / r108 / 38 px track).
- [x] New `ui/widgets/fret` — AA rounded pad, dim -600/-500 at 75% at rest, bright -400/-300 + ring when held, momentary touch. **Custom-painted rather than schemed** because Tailwind's `ring-2` is a *second* colour the 16-field `leScheme` can't carry; also avoids adding 10 schemes (~7 KB of baked tables) to the design.
- [x] Design zip stripped: 39 widgets deleted under `PANEL_WIIMOTES`, layer + root panel intact, zip integrity OK, 0 dangling scheme refs. Script kept at `docs/design_wiimotes_strip.py` (idempotent, dry-run by default).
- [x] `screen_wiimotes.c` rewritten as a programmatic builder + accel/whammy/fret wiring. Static widget pools constructed in place — no allocator.
- [x] Builds: all new/changed files clean under `-Wall -Wextra`, and the full `ninja` links.

**Two findings worth keeping:**
- **Text comes from the DESIGN string table (`leTableString` + `stringID_*`), not C literals** — the opposite of what screen_bus.c does, and deliberately. Five of these labels are non-ASCII: the d-pad arrows **▲ ◀ ▶ ▼** (U+25B2/25C0/25B6/25BC) and the true minus **−** (U+2212). MGS only auto-includes glyphs for strings it can see in the design, so C literals would have rendered blanks after the next Generate with **no build error** — exactly the trap the 2026-08-05 glyph rule warns about. Using table strings also means each label keeps the design's font binding, and the string table stays at 0 unused.
- **`PANEL_WIIMOTES` has the same `background=FILL` + scheme uuid as `PANEL_BUS`/`PANEL_DASHBOARD`**, so `PanelAA_Enable`'s "opaque parent" precondition for the cards' AA corners holds unchanged.

**Deliberately dropped from the mockup:** the 🎸 emoji that counter-rotates inside the tilt arc (no glyph; adding one at 58 px is not worth it) and the star-power threshold pip (star power is out per Greg — tilt is just simulated tilt now).

**Orphan sweep DONE** (`docs/design_wiimotes_orphan_sweep.py`, idempotent, dry-run by default): deleted the string `WIIMOTES_ENABLED` (the pill the mockup drops — its font `DejaVuSansMonoBold_12` keeps 17 other bindings, so no font orphaned) and the **3 images** the deleted widgets referenced (`figmaImg_TiltControl` = the old static tilt PNG, plus `figmaImg_Icon_0_1`/`_1_1`; 18.3 KB of zip members). Final zip: 208→199 members, 102→101 strings/bindings, images 47→44, integrity OK, manifest and asset dirs agree, no widget→missing-asset refs, other layers untouched. Strum labels now read **`▲ UP` / `▼ DOWN`** (Greg: spell DOWN out; the arrows already exist in the design via the d-pad strings, and D/O/W/N are ASCII, so no glyph cost).

**Two traps hit during the sweep, both now in the skill:**
- **The image assets are listed in a manifest *and* stored per-uuid.** `assets/images/images.json` carries an `images[{id,name,type}]` list beside each `assets/images/{uuid}/` directory — dropping only the directory leaves a manifest entry pointing at nothing. My first attempt did exactly that. Worse, the manifest lives **under `assets/`**, so the "scan design JSON for live references" loop skipped it. Fixed, and the validation now asserts `manifest ids == asset dirs`. Fonts have the same shape (`fonts.json` + `assets/fonts/{uuid}/`).
- **`mgs_zip.repack`'s `.bak` is the *pristine* original, not the previous state** — it only copies when no `.bak` exists, so the backup here was two cleanups old (229 strings). Restoring it to "undo the last run" reverted the whole earlier string/font cleanup. Recovered with `git checkout -- <zip>` and replayed both scripts from HEAD. **Use git, not `.bak`, to roll back a step**; the transform scripts no longer print a misleading "backup at …" line.

**Also worth knowing for the later image cleanup:** "unused by widget" is **not** unused — hand source draws assets by generated symbol name. 35 of 47 images had no widget reference, but 5 of those (`Marvin`, `LOGO_GUITAR`, `LOGO_MICROCHIP`, `LOGO_PIC`, `BUTTON_ICON_HAMBURGER`) are live from C. The remaining ~26 unreferenced images are ~350 KB of source blobs — including a **byte-identical twin of the tilt PNG** (`figmaImg_TiltControl_0`, same sha1) and semantically-named assets that look like planned features (`HumanPlayer`, `LemmyOnStagePlayerImage`, `STAND_BY`, `WII_CTRL_LED_*`, `LED_SYSTEM_*`, `VIDEO_LED_NO_SIGNAL`, `LOGO_MGS`). Left alone deliberately — that is its own step, and each needs a "planned feature or dead?" call, not a bulk delete.

**Generated + verified (Greg ran MGS Generate).** `le_gen_screen_Marvin.c/.h` lost the whole imported subtree (**−379 / −39 lines**, all 26 `Marvin_*_WIIMOTES_ROBOT_*` globals gone, `Marvin_PANEL_WIIMOTES` retained as the builder's root). `le_gen_stringtable.c` and `le_gen_fonts.c` moved with the two retargeted strum labels: both bind to `DejaVuSansMonoBold_14`, which gained the two arrows for **+208 bytes total** (`_data` 3824→3864, `_glyphs` 14500→14668) — the predicted auto-inclusion, at a trivial cost. All 17 `stringID_*` the builder uses survive. The full `ninja` build then links against the regenerated sources.

**`le_gen_*` now also reflects the orphan sweep**, verified directly: `figmaImg_TiltControl_data` is gone from `le_gen_images.c` (7040 bytes of blob, plus the two icons), `stringID_WIIMOTES_ENABLED` is gone from `le_gen_assets.h`, and `le_gen_stringtable.c` carries `DOWN`. `stringID_*` indices above the deleted string shift down by one — harmless, the builder uses the macros. `ninja` links clean. Worth confirming in MGS before flashing, since the generated tree and the zip must not drift.

**On table strings vs C literals, generally (Greg, 2026-08-05).** Glyph auto-inclusion is the reason that bit us here, but the *stronger* reason to prefer `leTableString` + `stringID_*` in a hand-built screen is **localization**: a design string has a per-language value, and a C literal can never be translated. So table strings are the default for any user-facing caption. **`screen_bus.c`'s C literals are therefore a known debt, deliberately not paid** — the bus screen is a one-off demo view whose text is mostly column headers and formatted numbers, so importing ~40 strings into the design to make it translatable isn't worth it. Worth remembering if the bus screen ever stops being a demo, and worth *not* "fixing" on sight.

> **Next:** hardware check. Nothing in this pass has run on the panel yet — the layout arithmetic is verified only by evaluating the macros (all controls land inside their cards, video at 325,78 630×420 with a 14 px gap above the cards) and the three new widgets' paint math has never been seen on a display.

**2026-08-05 — MGS string + font cleanup DONE in the zip (pending Greg's MGS Generate + build). Headline: every non-DejaVu font in the design was the *same* TTF — Noto Sans Regular — mislabelled; 127 of 229 strings were dead figma leftovers. Result: 229→102 strings, 47→13 fonts, all text now DejaVu Sans Mono, ~443 KiB of the 769 KiB font data reclaimed.** Transform script kept at `docs/design_string_font_cleanup.py` (idempotent, dry-run by default, validates before writing); new reusable scripts in the `mgs-legato-design` skill: `audit_strings_fonts.py`, `audit_widget_strings.py`.
- **Applied (backup at `default_design.zip.bak`):** stage A retargeted 28 bindings → DejaVu; stage B deleted 127 unused strings; stage C deleted 34 fonts; stage D renamed the 32 live `figmaStr_*` to semantic names. Post-transform audit: **0 unused strings, 0 unused fonts, 0 bindings on a non-DejaVu font**, zip integrity OK, 13 font dirs × 4 members with no orphans, images untouched.
- **Generated + verified (Greg ran MGS Generate).** `le_gen_fonts.c` is now **13 fonts / 325.8 KiB, down from 46 / 768.9 KiB — 443.1 KiB of flash reclaimed**, matching the prediction to the byte. `le_gen_assets.h` carries 102 `stringID_*` and 13 `leRasterFont`. The one predicted dependency held: **`DejaVuSansMono_12` went 193→194 glyphs (+28 bytes), MGS auto-adding U+2014 (—)** for the retargeted em-dash placeholders, so it now carries — ■ ★.
- **The glyph rule (Greg, 2026-08-05), now in the skill:** characters used by **MGS-defined strings are auto-included** in the bound font on Generate; characters that only ever reach a label at **runtime** (`setString` from a C literal, the QSPI CSV, a formatted device name) are **invisible to MGS and must be added to the font's character set by hand** — otherwise they render as a missing glyph with no build error. New `audit_glyph_coverage.py` checks both sides. **Current state is clean:** every design string is covered; every rendered literal in hand source is pure ASCII; the song catalog's four non-ASCII characters (é ä ß ç) are Latin-1 and the fonts that draw song text (`DejaVuSansMono_12/14/20`, `DejaVuSansMonoBold_12/24`) all carry Latin-1. **Range decisions on the two thin fonts (Greg, 2026-08-05):** `DejaVuSansMono_9` **got Latin-1 added** (95→191 glyphs, landed with the final Generate) so the bus screen is safe to draw catalog text. **`DejaVuSansMonoBold_40` is deliberately left Basic-Latin-only** (+ the ✓/⌫ extras) — Latin-1 at 40px would cost roughly **+48 KB** (that font runs ~509 bytes/glyph vs ~227 at 24px) for glyphs its ASCII QWERTY cannot produce. *One reachable path if that ever matters:* `Results_SetPlayer` (`results/results.c`) filters only `,` `"` `\n` `\r`, not non-ASCII, so a name set through the console `player` command is prefilled into the keyboard entry field and would show a missing glyph. The cheap guard is to restrict `Results_SetPlayer` to printable ASCII — the character set the keyboard itself can produce — rather than paying the 48 KB.
- **Naming:** the freed `GUITAR_*` names went to the live labels that earned them (`GUITAR_PLUS`, `GUITAR_MINUS`, `GUITAR_STRUM_UP/DOWN`, plus new `GUITAR_EXTENSION`/`GUITAR_WHAMMY`), wiimote card → `WIIMOTE_*` (`WIIMOTE_DPAD_UP/LEFT/RIGHT/DOWN`, `WIIMOTE_A/B/ONE/TWO/HOME/TILT/HEADING`), dashboard human card → `PLAYER_HUMAN_*`, shared player values → `PLAYER_Streak/Accuracy/StarPower`, plus `PLAYER_STRUM_BAR_Status`, `VIDEO_NO_SIGNAL`, `NAV_STATUS`/`NAV_ConnectionStatus`. **Renamed rather than repointed** the two widgets whose text matched an orphan (`'+'`, `' UP'`): repointing would have inherited the orphan's font (`GUITAR_PLUS` was on the doomed `DejaVuSansMono_28`) and, for strum-up, would have made the button pair read `UP` / ` DN` — the rename lands the same semantic name with zero visual change.
- **Deliberately did NOT merge the duplicate string values, against the original stage-D sketch.** On inspection the "duplicates" are not redundancy: 54 of the 102 live strings belong to code-referenced widgets, so their text is a *placeholder overwritten at runtime*, and the pairs that share text are independent fields (`SONG_INFO_START_TIME`/`STOP_TIME` both `'0:00'`; the two dashboard `'Connected'` labels are the guitar and the remote; the three `'—'` are accuracy, star power, and the guitar minus button). Merging would couple unrelated fields through one asset for ~100 bytes. The remaining candidates were static headings duplicated *across screens* (`GENRE`, `DURATION`, `MODE`, `DIFFICULTY`, `EASY`, `SELECT SONG`) — merging those would mean editing the song-select heading silently changes the dashboard's. Left separate on purpose.
- **How strings/fonts actually bind here.** `stringtable.json` holds `strings[]` (name + per-language value) and a separate `bindings[]` list of `{string, language, font}` — **a font is chosen per string, not per widget** (widgets carry `{"type":"string","value":"{uuid}"}` and no font ref at all; `screen.json` contains zero `"type":"font"` refs). Containment is tight: font uuids appear only in `assets/fonts/fonts.json`, each font's own `fontconfig.json`, and `stringtable.json`; string uuids only in `stringtable.json` + `screen.json`. `state.json`, `memorylocations.json`, and `styles.json` reference neither, so both cleanups are confined to those members.
- **No hand source references any design string.** Nothing outside `config/default/` mentions `stringID_*` — every runtime text update goes through `setString` with a dynamic/fixed string, so deleting or renaming a *string* asset can never break the C build. **Fonts are the opposite:** 8 are referenced by symbol from hand code (`DejaVuSansMono_9/12/14/20`, `DejaVuSansMonoBold_12/14/24/40`, in `screen_bus.c` / `screen_keyboard.c` / `screen_dashboard.c` / `screen_song_select.c`) and must survive any prune regardless of bindings.
- **The big finding: 31 of 47 fonts are the same typeface, and it isn't the one their name claims.** Hashing each font's `sourceData` blob and parsing the TTF `name` table: `figmaFont_Menlo_*` (22), `figmaFont_Cousine_13`, `figmaFont_Inter_13`, and `NotoSans_Regular_*` (7) **all share one identical 455188-byte blob = "Noto Sans Regular"** — a *proportional* face. So the Figma import never carried Menlo/Cousine/Inter across; MGS substituted Noto Sans and kept the requested name. That is why `figmaFont_Menlo_12`'s `M` advance is 11px while `DejaVuSansMono_12`'s is 7px — those labels are not monospace today. Only 4 real faces exist in the design: DejaVu Sans Mono Book (8 sizes), DejaVu Sans Mono Bold (6), Noto Sans Regular (31), Noto Mono Regular (1), DejaVu Sans Mono Bold Oblique (1, a 0-glyph stub that MGS doesn't even emit).
- **Strings: 102 live / 127 dead.** Live = referenced by a widget in the one master screen (7 layers). The 127 dead are 121 `figmaStr_*` mockup leftovers (`figmaStr_46_3_C` = '46.3°C', `figmaStr_135_416`, …) plus 6 deliberately-named ones (`GAMEPLAY_SETTINGS`, `GUITAR_DISABLED`, `GUITAR_MINUS`, `GUITAR_PLUS`, `GUITAR_STRUM_DOWN`, `GUITAR_STRUM_UP`) that read as authored-then-orphaned — the wiimotes buttons that *should* use them still point at figma twins (`GUITAR_PLUS` '+' vs live `figmaStr___3` '+', `GUITAR_STRUM_UP` 'UP' vs live `figmaStr__UP` ' UP'). `SCREEN_KEYBOARD` and `SCREEN_BUS` hold **zero** design strings (built entirely in code), and 13 widgets have a null string uuid (runtime-set).
- **Only 28 live strings sit on a non-DejaVu font**, all retargetable to a DejaVu size that already exists — `figmaFont_Menlo_{12,12_1,12_2}`→`DejaVuSansMono_12`, `_14/_14_2`→`_14`, `_18_2`→`_18`, `_24_2`→`_24`, and the two 13px ones (`Cousine_13` WHAMMY, `Inter_13` TILT) →`_12`. **So no MGS font *additions* are needed** — the whole job is a JSON edit plus deletions.
- **Retarget is layout-safe (checked, not assumed).** Computed each label's rendered width from the source and target fonts' own glyph `advance` tables and compared against the widget rect: **0 of 31 retargeted labels overflow**, worst deltas are `Wii guitar` +17px (73px rect) and `WHAMMY` −19px, and **6 labels that overflow their rect today stop overflowing** (`figmaStr__` '—', `label_STATUS`, `label_ENABLED_0`, `label_WHAMMY_1`). 13px→12 (not 14) specifically because DejaVu 14 would push `TILT` to 32px in a 28px rect.
- **MGS auto-adds out-of-range glyphs from bindings.** Each font's `ranges.json` lists only declared ranges (DejaVu = Basic Latin + Latin-1; the Noto-Sans ones = Basic Latin only) yet `glyphs.json` contains extras that exactly match the special characters used by strings bound to that font — `DejaVuSansMono_12` carries U+25A0 ■ + U+2605 ★, `_18` carries the four ▲▶▼◀, `figmaFont_Menlo_12_1` carries U+2014 —. So retargeting a string whose text needs a glyph the target lacks (only `—` here) should self-heal on Generate; verify by re-running the audit after regen rather than trusting it.
- **Proposed cleanup, staged so each step is verifiable.** (A) retarget the 28 bindings to DejaVu; (B) prune the 127 dead strings — repointing the wiimotes widgets onto `GUITAR_PLUS`/`GUITAR_STRUM_UP` first where intent is recoverable; (C) delete the then-unused fonts — 32 non-DejaVu + the Oblique_40 stub + `DejaVuSansMono_28` (its only two bindings are the dead `GUITAR_PLUS`/`GUITAR_MINUS`, and it is the single largest font at 53,940 bytes), leaving **13 fonts, all DejaVu**, 769 KiB → ~326 KiB; (D) optional — rename the 32 live strings still called `figmaStr_*` (widget-safe, zero source impact) and merge duplicate values ('—' ×3, 'Connected' ×3, 'GENRE'/'DURATION'/'MODE'/'DIFFICULTY'/'EASY'/'SELECT SONG' ×2 each, −8 strings). Then MGS Generate → build → eyeball.
- **The substituted face is *proportional*, so stage A restores the mockup rather than departing from it.** Measured from the glyph `advance` tables: in every Noto-Sans-backed font `i`=3px while `M`=11px (9–14 distinct widths per font), vs DejaVu Sans Mono where every glyph is one width (7px at 12pt). The mockup named **Menlo** and **Cousine** — both genuinely monospace — so the import silently turned mono text proportional; only `Inter` (the `TILT` label) was ever meant to be proportional. Today **28 of the 102 live strings (~27% of visible text) render proportional mixed in beside DejaVu mono on the same screen** — the dashboard's `Streak`/`Accuracy`/`StarPower` values are proportional while the `STREAK`/`ACCURACY` headings directly above them are mono. Ironically `NotoMono_Regular` *is* monospace and is the one Noto font nothing binds to.
- **RESOLVED (2026-08-05, measured against the mockup at `~/Downloads/Marvin`): keep DejaVu Sans Mono — it is a metric clone of Menlo, not an approximation.** Compared the embedded DejaVu blob against the system Menlo and SF Mono with fontTools: DejaVu Sans Mono vs **Menlo differs by 0.00% on advance/em (0.6021), x-height (0.5469), cap-height (0.7290), ascender (0.7598) and descender (−0.2080)**, same 2048 upm. That is because Menlo is derived from Bitstream Vera Sans Mono and DejaVu Sans Mono is the other descendant of the same face (Menlo also borrows DejaVu glyphs for extended coverage) — they share a skeleton. So no re-import is wanted; DejaVu is simultaneously the licensing-clean choice (Bitstream Vera + Arev, permissive/commercial/embeddable) and the closest possible match. Of everything in Tailwind v4's `font-mono` stack (`ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, Monaco, Consolas, "Liberation Mono", "Courier New"`), only Liberation Mono is even free — the rest are Apple/Microsoft/Monotype and non-redistributable.
- **The mockup is a single-typeface design — there is no second font to match.** `src/styles/fonts.css` is empty (no `@font-face`, no custom family), so every text element resolves through Tailwind `font-mono` or an inline `fontFamily: monospace` — including `TILT` and `WHAMMY`, which the Figma export mislabelled `figmaFont_Inter_13`/`figmaFont_Cousine_13`. Those were Figma's own substitutions, confirming the retarget of both to DejaVu was correct. The only non-mono glyph anywhere in the mockup is one 🎸 emoji (`ManualOverrideScreen`). Note the browser preview and the Figma export disagreed: on macOS `ui-monospace` resolves to **SF Mono**, which Figma couldn't use so it fell through to **Menlo** — DejaVu matches Menlo exactly and runs ~4% larger x-height/cap than SF Mono.
- **Two gaps found, both weight rather than typeface — BOTH NOW CLOSED (2026-08-05).** `DejaVuSansMonoBold_20` was missing (the mockup's `text-xl font-bold` h1 + NowPlaying song title, 14 uses) — Greg added it in Composer, the only hole in the mockup's ladder. And the MGS-authored labels were under-bolded at 12 of 102 bold (12%) vs the mockup's ~31%.
- **Typography matched to the mockup per label (`docs/design_font_match_mockup.py`, 24 bindings changed).** Built `docs/mockup_typography.py` — a JSX scanner that resolves each mockup text node's Tailwind classes *with inheritance* (and captures `{expr}` interpolations, which are exactly the elements marvin's runtime-updated strings correspond to) to px + bold. Matching by text value alone proved useless (the same word appears at several sizes in different components), so every entry was read off the specific mockup element the widget came from, component by component: `PlayerPerformance` → robot/human cards, `NowPlaying` → song/gameplay card, `NavigationDrawer` → drawer, `SongSelectModal` → dialog, `ManualOverrideScreen` → wiimotes. **Design-string weight is now 31% bold, matching the mockup's ~31% exactly.**
  - **21 of the 24 changes are weight-only and provably width-neutral** — DejaVu Sans Mono Bold has the *same advance* as Regular at every size (7/8/10/11/12/14px at 12/14/16/18/20/24), verified from the glyph tables, so no label can start overflowing. Bolded: player names, robot/strum status, the 1x–4x multipliers, the big score (24), detector buttons, START / SELECT SONG, the difficulty pill, WIIMOTES_ENABLED, strum UP/DN, WHAMMY, TILT, and `SONG_INFO_Title` onto the new Bold_20.
  - **3 changes move size**, all checked against the widget rect: the song-select **MODE** buttons 14→12 bold (the mockup has DIFFICULTY at 14/bold but MODE at 12/bold — 56px text in a 192px rect), and **`GUITAR_MINUS` 12→24** because marvin had the −/+ pair mismatched at 12 and 24 while the mockup renders both at 24 (14px in a 71px rect).
  - **`GUITAR_MINUS`'s character was also wrong — U+2014 EM DASH where the mockup has U+2212 MINUS SIGN** (confirmed on hardware: at 24px the em dash read visibly wide next to the `+`). Measuring the ink extents explains why and settles it objectively: in DejaVu Sans Mono at 24px, `+` and `−` are both **12.4px wide with their bar at +7.5px**, drawn as a matched pair, while the em dash is **14.4px (16% wider) and sits 0.9px lower**. Fixed the character, not the size. The accuracy / star-power placeholders keep U+2014 — the mockup uses an em dash for those, they are standalone "no value" dashes rather than half of a ± pair. **Lesson: when a symbol looks wrong next to a sibling, compare glyph ink extents before touching sizes or padding.**
  - The mockup's 13px one-offs (`WHAMMY`, `TILT`) map to 12 — there is no Bold_13 and 12 is what the neighbouring labels use.
- **Post-Generate expectations for this change (one automatic, one manual — the rule Greg stated):**
  1. `DejaVuSansMono_24` will **auto-gain U+2014 (—)** because `GUITAR_MINUS` moved onto it and that is a *design* string. No action; `audit_glyph_coverage.py` flags it only in the pre-Generate state.
  2. **`DejaVuSansMonoBold_20` needs range 160–255 added by hand in MGS.** It came in ASCII-only (32–126), unlike every other DejaVu size, and `SONG_INFO_Title` is set at runtime from the QSPI catalog — which contains **`Mauvais Garçon`** (U+00E7), plus `Héroes del Silencio` / `Doppelgänger` / `Ein kleines bißchen Horrorschau` in the artist/album fields. MGS will *not* auto-add ç: it appears only in runtime data, never in a design string. Without the range that one title renders with a missing glyph.
- **Placeholder vs static text (matters for merge safety).** Of the 102 live strings, **54 belong to widgets that hand source references** (so their design text is a placeholder overwritten at runtime) and **48 are the actual static displayed text**. All the figma-named `label_*` widgets (`label_Connected_1`, `label_IDLE_0_0`, `label_WHAMMY_1`, `LABEL_DASHBOARD_NO_SIGNAL`, …) are *not* code-referenced — that text is what ships.
- **Tooling gap found:** `mgs_zip.repack` can only *replace* members, so deleting a font (which owns 4 zip members: `fontconfig.json`, `glyphs.json`, `ranges.json`, `sourceData`) needs a `drop` set added to the helper before stage C. **Added.**

**2026-08-04 (last) — bus screen finished to the mockup: layout on cards, then all four charts (`c59c8fe`, `e251ca0`).**
- **Layout rebuilt from the mockup** (`BusStatsScreen.tsx`) with Tailwind units resolved to px (gap-3=12, rounded=4, px-4=16, text-xs/sm/xl=12/14/20). Every section is a bordered AA card — and usefully, `SCHEME_FILL_ZINC_900`'s `shadowDark` is already #404040 ≈ zinc-700, so `PanelAA_Enable` + `BORDER_LINE` gives the mockup's card border with **no new scheme and no regen**. Table is one card with a zinc-700 header rule + per-row zinc-800 rules; role is a rounded pill (fill widget + text label, so no bespoke 2-tone scheme).
- **Dropped the reserved id-2 fauxmote** from the coordinator node table (never built, not planned) and renamed the controller to plain `fauxmote`. That removes a permanently-absent row from both `nodes` and the bus screen, leaving marvin + 6 followers = the mockup's 7 rows / `NODES 7/7`, so the table is the mockup's 334px. `docs/t1s-podl-link.md` §7.1 updated.
- **Two new reusable widgets** (`ui/widgets/gauge/`, `ui/widgets/sparkline/`), both on the PanelAA vtable-override pattern and drawing **only** with `PutPixel`/`GetPixel`/`VertLine` — deliberately avoiding Legato's stock arc/curve paints, since the stock rounded-rect paint is what hung earlier. Gauge = semicircle, ring coverage `1-|d-r|/(t/2)` for AA edges + `atan2` sweep fraction; sparkline = 40-sample ring buffer painted as a connected 2px polyline via per-column vertical runs. Both bounded by the widget rect.
- **Charts.** Gauge in the util card with the % centred inside it (paint order matters: card → arc → text); history sparkline + y-ticks + 40s/now footers; TX-rate bars (bottom-anchored, rescaled to the running max, full node names); error bars (pill track + node-coloured fill + total + CRC/SYM split). **Reminder that bit us:** pill/stadium rounding must go through `PanelAA_EnableDot` (rounds by `min(w,h)/2` *without* setting `cornerRadius`) — a `cornerRadius` of `h/2` is exactly the degenerate value that hangs the stock paint.
- **Simulated data feed (`ace0706`)** so the screen can be developed without hardware. All values now read through a data-source layer (`bus_stats`/`bus_self`/`bus_node`/`bus_node_count`) that returns either live `T1SLink` telemetry or a simulator — needed because a healthy bus is near-idle and error-free, so thresholds/ramps/gauge/chart scaling never exercise on real data (and followers read zeros until reflashed). Seeded from the mockup's values, advanced per tick with xorshift32 jitter; seeds tuned so per-node CRC/SYM span all three tiers instead of saturating red, and **beatbox is marked absent** so OFFLINE + `NODES 6/7` are covered. `BUS_SIM_DEFAULT` (**currently `true` — flip to `false` before any demo of real data**) plus public `ScreenBus_SetSimulated/Simulated` for a future console toggle. While simulated the UPTIME sub-line reads **SIMULATED** in yellow, so the screen never implies the numbers are live.
- **Added `DejaVuSansMono_9`** in MGS for axis/detail text (range 32–126) so full node names fit the ~54px bar slots; small but legible on the panel. Pools now 96 widgets / 200 labels (using 63 / 144). Colour note: the gauge/sparkline read colour from a scheme's `BASE`, so they use the mono `SCHEME_NODE_MARVIN`/`SCHEME_NODE_LIGHTSHOW` as carriers (their hex is the mockup's exact cyan/red); dedicated `SCHEME_FILL_CYAN_400`/`RED_400` would be tidier at the next regen.

**2026-08-04 (later still) — shared titlebar component + fixed a boot render hang traced to Legato's stock rounded-rect paint. Bus screen now has chrome + round AA status/node dots.**
- **Reusable titlebar (`ui/titlebar.{c,h}`).** `Titlebar_Add(parent)` builds the hamburger + GUITAR/PIC/Microchip logo images (statically allocated) into any base-view panel and wires the hamburger to the drawer via a new public `ScreenNavigation_ToggleDrawer()`. Bus screen uses it (content shifted below the 65px bar). **Migration done (`b63fa47`):** Greg deleted the MGS titlebars from dashboard + wiimotes and both now call `Titlebar_Add` in their Setup — all three base views share one code-built titlebar (net −85 lines), and the two `Marvin_BUTTON_*_TITLEBAR_NAVIGATION` wirings + `hamburger_on_press` are gone from `ScreenNavigation_Setup`. **Non-obvious dependency:** `screen_video.c` gated `Marvin_PANEL_DASHBOARD_TITLEBAR` out of picking during fullscreen video (so a tap exits fullscreen rather than hitting the hamburger); `Titlebar_Add` now *returns* the bar widget, the dashboard keeps the handle, and `ScreenDashboard_Titlebar()` exposes it for that gate. Any future screen wanting chrome = one `Titlebar_Add(panel)` call.
- **Legato gotcha — stock rounded-rect paint hangs at `cornerRadius >= min(w,h)/2`.** The bus screen froze at boot (`LEGATO_Tasks` 99%, `UI: render-idle wait timed out`) once its content painted. Bisected (grid vs KPI vs table vs dots vs AA) to the **status/node dots**: a `leWidget` with `cornerRadius == size/2` (a full circle, 8/12px) sends the classic-skin paint into a spin. Big cards (radius 6 ≪ 149) never hit it. Our `AaCorners` was innocent (bounded `r×r` loop). **Fix: `PanelAA_EnableDot`** — fill a plain square (`cornerRadius` left 0 → no stock rounding) then AA-round the corners to the backdrop (sampled just outside the dot) via `AaCorners_RenderRoundImage`. True AA circle, no stock rounding. Use this for any small round widget; never set `cornerRadius` at/above half the size.
- **Debugging aid:** bisected with temporary `#if BUS_DIAG_*` gates in `screen_bus.c` (grid/table/rows/dots/dot-AA), now stripped. Static-widget allocation made the "empty label → paint-never-idle" theory easy to rule out.
- **Reachable + working on hardware (`b4b38e0`).** `UiManager_ShowStats()` added and the BASE swap generalized to three views via `hide_current_base()` (dashboard / wiimotes / bus; `SetBaseViewPickable` routes to the active one). Showing the bus view hides the live video (it owns the panel) and `ScreenBus_SetShown(true)` starts the ~1 Hz refresh. **Nav drawer → Diagnostics** opens it; the bus titlebar hamburger reopens the drawer to leave. **The T1S bus-stats feature is now complete end-to-end** (telemetry backend → follower v2 heartbeats → screen → navigation). Remaining polish, all deferred: the util sparkline + TX/error bar charts + SVG gauge, true RTT-ping latency, PHY-MMD accurate CRC/sym counters, and migrating dashboard/wiimotes onto the shared `Titlebar_Add`.

Palette is Tailwind **v4**; convention = palette schemes named by color, component/semantic schemes keep function names.

**2026-08-04 — T1S bus-statistics UI: Phase 1 (coordinator telemetry backend) landed; extended (v2) heartbeat contract defined. Phases 2 (followers) + 3 (screen) pending.** New Diagnostics screen showing per-node bus stats (mockup: "GUITAR HERO ROBOT CONTROLLER"). Backend built first so the data is real, not simulated. Plan file: `~/.claude/plans/purrfect-mixing-valley.md`.
- **Data reality (from exploration).** The T1S link only had global `s_tx_count`/`s_rx_count`/`s_ctrl_*` + a presence table. Per-node TX/RX/CRC/symbol don't exist. CRC/symbol errors are **inherently local** to each node's receiver (LAN8651 checks FCS in HW; the driver surfaces only aggregate STATUS0 events via `TC6Regs_CB_OnEvent`, no per-source attribution and no counter registers in the repo) → each node must count its own and **report them in the heartbeat**. Decisions: build the backend; CRC/sym = event tally of STATUS0 FCS / loss-of-framing (real, approximate, no datasheet needed); latency = heartbeat age for now (RTT ping deferred); first screen cut = header tiles + per-node table (sparkline + bar charts deferred); reached via the existing screenless **Diagnostics** nav entry; MGS authors panel+titlebar only, grid built in code (keyboard-screen precedent).
- **Extended heartbeat (v2), the Phase-2 contract** (`docs/t1s-podl-link.md` §7.2 updated). `0x88B6` payload grows 8→**20 bytes**: after the legacy `ver/type/id/flags/seq`, append LE `tx_count(u32) rx_count(u32) crc_err(u16) sym_err(u16)`; bump `version` 1→2. Coordinator gates on `payload_len >= 20`, so v1 followers still parse fine and report zeros — **rolls out node-by-node, no flag day**. 12 extra bytes are free (frames pad to the 60-byte min).
- **Phase 1 — coordinator (`net/t1s/t1s_link.{c,h}`, done, not yet built).** Grew `s_node_rt[]` with `tx/rx_count`, `crc/sym_err`, derived `tx/rx_rate` (+ prev snapshots); HB RX branch parses the v2 fields; `TC6Regs_CB_OnEvent` now tallies marvin's own FCS→`s_self_crc_err` and loss-of-framing→`s_self_sym_err` (reinit behaviour preserved); `recompute_rates()` runs once/sec in `t1s_task` turning cumulative deltas into frames/s (marvin's own + each node's). New API: `T1SLink_GetNodeStats(idx,*)`, `T1SLink_GetSelfStats(*)` (marvin's row: id 0, "marvin", tx/rx = data+ctrl, own crc/sym), `T1SLink_GetBusStats(*)` (aggregate tiles: util estimate = Σrate·64B·8 vs 10 Mbps clamped to 1000‰, tx/rx totals, crc/sym totals, err ppm, uptime, nodes online/total). Console `t1s`/`nodes` extended to dump it all — the headless verification path before any UI.
- **Phase 3 scaffold landed (boot fix).** Greg's MGS regen added Legato **layer 6** = full-screen `Marvin_PANEL_BUS` (RGB565, 1280×800) and bumped `LE_LAYER_COUNT` 6→7 — but with no firmware canvas for layer 6, boot stalled in the paint/reveal (the GFX-canvas renderer maps layer *i*→canvas *i*; canvas 6 had no pixel buffer). Fix = minimal `ui/screens/bus/screen_bus.{c,h}` peer base view (`CANVAS_BUS`=6): `InitSurface` (pixel buffer) + `Setup` (window, fill panel, placeholder "10BASE-T1S BUS" label, gate picking off) + `SetInput`/`SetShown` (placeholder). Wired into `ui_manager` `UiManager_Initialize` (InitSurface before `GFX_CANVAS_Task`), `init_screens` (Setup), `paint_all_screens_once` (invalidate), and added to `user.cmake`. Built + painted at boot but **not bound to a HW layer / not reachable yet** — the `MGS` `BUS` panel has **no titlebar/hamburger authored**, so `UiManager_ShowStats` + the Diagnostics-nav swap wait on Greg adding a titlebar (else the view would trap with no way back). Actual MGS name is `Marvin_PANEL_BUS` (not the assumed `PANEL_STATS`).
- **Phase 2 done (2026-08-04).** The v2 heartbeat + real tx/rx + crc/sym event tally landed on all 6 follower nodes, committed one-per-node (`guitar`/`lemmy`/`lightshow`/`beatbox` `t1s_follower.c`, `fretboard` `t1s_detector.c`, `fauxmote` `mf_t1s.c`) — see each node's journal. Reflash each **with marvin** to see its row populate on the coordinator's `nodes`/`t1s` console + the bus screen. All app-logic-only; no MCC/peripheral change.
- **Phase 3 remaining.** `screen_bus.c` scaffold is in (canvas 6, painted at boot — see the boot-fix note above); still to build: the header stat tiles + per-node table (backed by `T1SLink_GetBusStats`/`GetNodeStats`/`GetSelfStats`), a ~1 Hz updater task, and `UiManager_ShowStats` + the Diagnostics nav swap. **Blocked on Greg adding a titlebar/hamburger to the MGS `Marvin_PANEL_BUS`** — without a way back, wiring the nav swap would trap the view.

**2026-08-03 (later still) — on-screen touch keyboard modal for player-name entry (new layer-screen `KEYBOARD`, layer 5 / `CANVAS_KEYBOARD`); 1P-Human game mode dropped. Pending Greg's MPLAB build.** Prompts for the human competitor's name at the start of a 2P match, then starts the run.
- **New module `ui/screens/keyboard/screen_keyboard.{c,h}`.** The whole keyboard is built **programmatically** into the empty MGS `Marvin_PANEL_KEYBOARD` (a 1060×560 centered layer-5 dialog, scheme `SCHEME_PANEL_GRAY_18181B`) — no per-key composer authoring. Chrome (title, rounded text-field box via `PanelAA_Enable`, entry/counter labels) + a QWERTY grid of `leButtonWidget`s laid out from code (three letter rows + CLEAR/SPACE/⌫/OK bottom row + an X close). Keys reuse `ButtonAA_Enable` for rounded AA corners and `SCHEME_BUTTON_MODE` (dark) / `SCHEME_BUTTON_EASY` (green OK). Owns a `KBD_MAX`=16 edit buffer; a single shared `key_on_release` dispatches by looking the pressed widget up in a `{btn,kind,ch}` table, mutating the buffer and `setString`-ing the entry + counter labels.
  - **Why per-key widgets, not one custom widget (Greg's repaint concern):** a single full-screen custom widget would repaint the whole 1060×560 surface on every keypress. Separate `leButtonWidget`s give Legato's damage tracking for free — a keypress repaints only that key's rect + the two text labels (transparent over the opaque box), never the full surface. The QWERTY layout is generated in a loop from a table, so this costs no composer work (that was the only reason to avoid many buttons). ~35 runtime widgets from the Legato heap (like `SongList_New`), built once at boot.
  - **Fonts:** `DejaVuSansMonoBold_40` (added by Greg with U+2713 ✓ + U+232B ⌫ in the subset) for letters, ⌫, and the "✓ OK" cap + the entry text; `DejaVuSansMonoBold_24` for CLEAR/SPACE/X; `DejaVuSansMono_20` for title/counter. Stays DejaVu-Sans-Mono-only (§4.5 licensing).
- **`ui_manager` modal verbs** `UiManager_OpenKeyboard(title, initial, maxlen, commit)` / `UiManager_CloseKeyboard()` mirror the song-select dialog: seed the session (`ScreenKeyboard_Prepare`), gate the base view out of picking, drop the OVR1 video-frame overlay, bind `CANVAS_KEYBOARD`→OVR1 (RGB565) + show, and hide the live video for a clean backdrop. No BASE discard (the dialog does no bandwidth-heavy work, unlike song-select's album-art path). Close hides the canvas and `ScreenVideo_ShowWindowed()` restores HEO + the frame. `CANVAS_KEYBOARD`=5; `ScreenKeyboard_Init/Setup` wired into `init_surfaces`/`init_screens`/`paint_all_screens_once`; the dialog is input-gated off at boot so the always-attached layer-5 panel doesn't swallow dashboard touches.
- **Trigger = 2P game start.** Dashboard START (`screen_dashboard.c` `start_on_release`): if the committed `GameSelection` mode is `GAME_MODE_2P`, open the keyboard prefilled with `Results_GetPlayer()`; OK → `Results_SetPlayer(name)` + `GameController_Start()`, X → close without starting. 1P-robot starts immediately (unchanged). Console `play` left as a direct-start bypass.
- **1P-Human mode dropped** (Greg): song-select `MODE_COUNT` 3→2, mode radio is now {1P-robot, 2P-robot-vs-human}; `mode_value()` maps radio index→`game_mode_t` (index 1 → `GAME_MODE_2P`); the retired `GAME_MODE_1P_HUMAN` enum value is left defined so any persisted selection keeps its meaning. The MGS `BUTTON_SONG_SELECT_1P_HUMAN` widget is hidden in code (`setVisible(FALSE)`) — Greg can delete it in the composer to reclaim the layout gap.
- Files: `ui/screens/keyboard/screen_keyboard.{c,h}` (new), `ui/ui_manager.{c,h}`, `ui/screens/dashboard/screen_dashboard.c`, `ui/screens/song_select/screen_song_select.c`, `cmake/marvin/default/user.cmake`. Composer side (done by Greg): blank `KEYBOARD` layer-screen (layer 5, `LE_LAYER_COUNT` 5→6, panel 1060×560@(0,0)) + `DejaVuSansMonoBold_40` with ✓/⌫. No MCC/Legato hand-edits. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build + hardware test** (2P START opens the keyboard prefilled; typing/backspace/clear/space track; OK sets player + starts; X cancels; only the pressed key + text labels repaint). Relates to spec Q12 (player identity).

**2026-08-03 (later still) — marv-perf connect froze marvin; root-caused to the UDPHS/AIC boot-storm fix (`8d55533`). Reverted that fix and re-did it as the real driver-level Fix 3 (init finishes detached), which keeps the connect path untouched.** Symptom: on marv-perf connect, marvin printed `PerfLog: mask=0x00000042` (the host's SET_TYPE_MASK = SESSION+DROP, so RX + dispatch worked) then wedged — no session/data records ever flowed back. Greg's bisect: it worked before the boot-storm fix. Confirmed by reverting `8d55533` (AIC masks UDPHS source 23 at boot + `SYS_INT_SourceEnable` in the CDC sink after attach) → connect works again, but the power-on-with-cable boot storm returns.
- **Why the AIC-mask fix broke connect is not fully derived** — `SYS_INT_SourceEnable` is IRQ-safe and nets the same enabled state as the boot-time AIC enable, so on paper the connect-time AIC state is identical either way. Empirically it isn't. Rather than chase it, the fix now lives entirely *outside* the connect path.
- **Correction (after Fix 3 + Fix 2 both failed on hardware): the storm is PRE-driver-init.** The JTAG evidence in the bug writeup (`gDrvUSBUDPHSObj[0].status == UNINITIALIZED` with the CPU pinned in the UDPHS ISR) says the storm fires inside `AIC_INT_Initialize()`'s `__enable_irq()` at `initialization.c:442` — long before `DRV_USB_UDPHS_Initialize` (line 513). `CLK_Initialize()` (line 430) has already clocked UDPHS, so a pending ENDRESET (host attached at power-on, or UDPHS state persisted across a warm reset — `nSRST` doesn't reset the block) storms the moment the AIC unmasks source 23. **Fix 2 (device ISR) and Fix 3 (driver init) both target windows that haven't happened yet at storm time, so neither did anything** — confirmed on hardware (boot-with-cable still froze with both applied).
- **Actual fix (applied): quiesce UDPHS immediately before `AIC_INT_Initialize()`** (`initialization.c`, re-apply patch #13). Set `DETACH`, `UDPHS_IEN = 0`, `UDPHS_CLRINT = all` — with IEN masked the controller can't assert its line, so the AIC unmask is safe. `DRV_USB_UDPHS_Initialize`'s Reset-IP toggle restores IEN=0x10 later, so enumeration is normal. Crucially this does **not** mask the AIC source or move the interrupt enable, so the connect path stays the working reverted one (unlike `8d55533`).
- **Fix 2 + Fix 3 (`drv_usb_udphs_device.c`) were tried first and REMOVED — insufficient alone and unproven-necessary once #13 landed.** They target a *second*, narrower window (host attached across the gap between driver-init and the app opening the device). With the full set (revert + #13 + Fix 2 + Fix 3) marvin booted 5/5 with the cable attached; we're now testing **#13 alone** (Fix 2/3 reverted to pristine) to confirm it's the sole necessary new patch. The Fix 2/Fix 3 detail below is kept as record — if boot-with-cable ever storms in the narrow post-init window, the cleanest backstop to re-add is **Fix 2** (no-client ISR ack: `DETACH` + clear all pending, `IEN` left intact — do NOT zero IEN, nothing re-arms ENDRESET so that would break enumeration).
  - **Fix 3** — init sets `DETACH` *after* the Reset-IP `EN_UDPHS` toggle (the toggle clears DETACH), so the controller comes up disconnected until the app's `USB_DEVICE_Attach`. Shrinks the post-init attach window but can't close it alone (brief attach windows remain in init).
  - **Fix 2** — merges the UDPHS ISR's two no-client guards into one branch that DETACHes + clears all pending interrupts (instead of just `SYS_DEBUG_MESSAGE`), so a level-sensitive ENDRESET latched with no client can't self-sustain. `IEN` left intact (see above).
- **Resolved (confirmed on hardware 2026-08-03): #13 alone is the fix.** `8d55533` reverted (both `plib_aic.c` and the `perf_log_sink_cdc.c` `SYS_INT_SourceEnable`); the pre-AIC UDPHS quiesce in `initialization.c` (patch #13) is the sole new patch. Fix 2/Fix 3 reverted (not needed). Verified: power-on with USB attached boots clean **and** marv-perf connects + streams. So the observed storm was entirely the pre-driver-init window at `AIC_INT_Initialize`; the narrow post-init no-client window never bit in practice (Fix 2 remains the documented backstop if it ever does).

**2026-08-03 (later) — decoupled the fretboard teacher label from live play: marvin keeps holding frets back-to-back on the wire, but the `commanded_mask` teacher label it sends is the *released-style* per-note command.** The atomic teacher label shipped earlier today (opcode `0x04`) sent the *driven* mask `v` as the label — but that mask carries the timing pipeline's back-to-back fret hold (a fret held across consecutive same-fret notes because the note queue shows it re-needed), which is exactly the legato artifact edge-ai training must *not* learn (see edge-ai journal: the confirmed fret-accuracy ceiling). Greg's call: the fretboard already re-adds the physical hold (lazy actuator), so marvin should keep playing held live (no release/repress churn on the wire) while logging the clean released-style command as the training target.
- **`game/game_timing.c`.** `process_releases` now returns the `lookahead_held` mask — the frets it kept past their scheduled release *solely* because of the back-to-back lookahead (`note_q_needs_by || strum_q_needs_by` at the old lines 269-270). That set is precisely how the live (held) and released-style masks differ each frame. `advance()` builds both: `mask = s_frets_active (+strum)` drives the wire unchanged; `teach = s_frets_active & ~lookahead_held (+ the same strum bits)` is the released-style label. `publish_mask(mask, teach)` forwards both.
- **`actuator/fretboard_link.{c,h}`.** New `FretboardLink_SendWithTeacher(mask, teacher_mask, producer)`; `FretboardLink_Send(mask, producer)` is now a thin forwarder passing `teacher==mask`. The `0x88B9` teacher opcode `0x04` edge-tracks `teacher_mask` (not the driven `v`). The guitar/fauxmote wire, dashboard, and `PERF_REC_ACTUATOR` all still use the driven `v`. Only the timing pipeline distinguishes held vs released; manual/gameplay producers have no lookahead so `teacher==mask` is correct for them.
- Behaviour on the wire (guitar + fauxmote) is unchanged — marvin still plays back-to-back holds live. Only the fretboard's `commanded_mask` label changed, so a `--labels=commanded-fb` capture now yields released-style training data. **Not yet built.** Confined to two app files (no MCC/Legato). Next: build marvin + fretboard, capture the 5-song corpus, export `commanded-fb`, retrain.

**2026-08-02 (later) — arming the fretboard is now gated on the *gameplay window*, not on the `active` selection. Fixes: the fretboard started driving fauxmote the instant `active fretboard` was set, even in menus.** `active fretboard` only *selects* the detector now; the node arms only while marvin is actually inside a song, and disarms when the song ends or `active cv` is chosen. This mirrors how CV control is already gated (marvin owns the controller for menu nav / manual control; the automated pipeline drives only in-song).
- **New shared predicate `Detector_FretboardDriving()`** (`detector/detector.c`) = `s_game_active && active==DETECTOR_FRETBOARD`. `s_game_active` is set by `Detector_SetGameActive(bool)`, driven from `GameTiming_SetEnabled()` — marvin's single gameplay-window choke point (opened by `game_controller` `play_until_done()` on `GP_SCREEN_in_song`, closed at `finish()`; manual control forces it off while it owns the wire). This one predicate is simultaneously the fretboard's arm bit *and* the gate that makes marvin cede fauxmote + the guitar node.
- **`actuator/fretboard_link.c`.** Reverted the blanket `active!=cv → return` gate in `FretboardLink_Send` (it wrongly blocked menu navigation, so with the fretboard selected marvin couldn't drive the menus to *start* a song). Instead the guitar-node output is suppressed in `fretboard_link_task` when `Detector_FretboardDriving()` (release the wire once on the transition, then silent). New `FretboardLink_UpdateArm()` pushes the arm bit over T1S (`T1SLink_SendFretboardCtrl(ARM,…)`) edge-triggered, retried on TX failure; no-op on the UART transport.
- **`game/game_timing.c`.** `GameTiming_SetEnabled()` now also calls `Detector_SetGameActive(enabled)` + `FretboardLink_UpdateArm()`, so the fretboard arms as a song starts and disarms as it ends.
- **`net/fauxmote/fauxmote_link.c`.** `fx_tx_task` gate changed from `active==cv` to `!Detector_FretboardDriving()` — marvin drives the GUITAR slice everywhere except inside a fretboard-owned song (so menu nav via guitar masks still flows to fauxmote when the fretboard is *selected* but no song is running). Same release-once-on-handoff.
- **`console/console.c`.** `cmd_active` replaces the direct arm-send with `Detector_SetActive(id)` + `FretboardLink_UpdateArm()`; echoes `active = fretboard (armed — song active)` or `(armed when a song starts)` so the deferred arm is visible. The `fretboard arm|stream|model` console commands still send arm directly (manual override, last-writer-wins).
- **Built** (marvin links clean). On-hardware handoff test still pending. The earlier 2026-08-02 entry below described arm-on-`active`; this supersedes its gating (the direct-drive-to-fauxmote wire path it added is unchanged).

**2026-08-02 — `active <cv|fretboard>` is now the real detector arbiter: it arms/disarms the fretboard over T1S and gates marvin's own CV output, so exactly one detector drives fauxmote (the game).** Before, `active` set only marvin's internal `s_active_id` (`detector.c`) and sent no bus frame, so it changed nothing on the fretboard node; and the fretboard's mask reached fauxmote only because marvin re-mirrored it (`FretboardLink_Send` → `Fauxmote_SendGuitarMask`). Now the fretboard drives fauxmote **directly** peer-to-peer (controller channel `0x88B7`, done on the fretboard side this session), and marvin steps out of the way when it's the active detector.
- **`console/console.c`.** `cmd_active`: after `Detector_SetActive(id)` it calls `T1SLink_SendFretboardCtrl(T1S_DET_CTRL_ARM, id==DETECTOR_FRETBOARD ? 1 : 0)` and echoes `active = <name>` (`+ " (fretboard link down)"` if the ctrl send failed). `cmd_detect` trimmed to `cv` only — `detect fretboard` now returns usage (`detect cv <on|off>`); the `fretboard` variant was a dead no-op (`s_enabled_mask` bit nothing read). `cmd_status` dropped the `detect fretboard` line; help strings updated (`active <cv|fretboard>`, `detect cv <on|off>`).
- **`actuator/fretboard_link.c`.** `#include "detector/detector.h"` made unconditional; `FretboardLink_Send` early-returns unless `Detector_GetActive()==DETECTOR_CV_MARVIN_V1` — so while the fretboard is active, marvin stops driving the guitar node, the dashboard, and new fauxmote masks.
- **`net/fauxmote/fauxmote_link.c`.** `fx_tx_task` sends `MF_MSG_GUITAR` only while `Detector_GetActive()==DETECTOR_CV_MARVIN_V1`; a local `driving` flag emits **one all-release GUITAR frame** on the cv→fretboard handoff so no held note sticks (the 50 ms floor-refresh made this gate necessary — it re-sends independently of `FretboardLink_Send`). Nav / pointer / link_cmd stay marvin's job regardless (menu nav is always marvin's).
- Default `active=cv` preserves today's behavior exactly (fretboard boots disarmed/silent, marvin CV drives). **Manual-override hazard** (fretboard's local `arm`/SW0 last-writer-wins with `active`) is documented on the fretboard side, not solved here. **Not yet built** — MPLAB build of marvin + fretboard and on-hardware handoff test are next. See `firmware/fretboard/docs/journal.md` (2026-08-02) for the node-side half + wire format.

**2026-07-28 — fixed the T1S fauxmote channel: min-frame padding was rejected by exact-length checks (both directions dead, presence fine).** On hardware the controller node showed present (heartbeat `0x88B6`), but `fauxmote status` reported no STATUS and marvin→fauxmote commands did nothing. Fauxmote's own counters confirmed the bus was healthy both ways (`synced=1`, `rx≈12k`/`tx≈7.6k` of `0x88B7` frames). Root cause was symmetric: the T1S MAC-PHY pads short frames to the 60-byte Ethernet minimum, so a received mf_proto payload carries trailing pad (STATUS arrives as ~45 bytes, not 4; GUITAR as ~45, not 3). Both message dispatchers used **exact** `!=` length checks and dropped every padded frame — while presence survived because its check is a tolerant `>=`. The documented contract ("the per-type length check ignores the trailing pad") was never actually implemented. Fix = make the per-type checks `>=`: marvin `latch_status` (`net/fauxmote/fauxmote_link.c`) accepts `len >= MF_LEN_STATUS`; the fauxmote-side mirror (`firmware/fauxmote/main/mf_link.c` `MfLink_HandleMessage`) accepts `len >= MF_LEN_*` for GUITAR/WIIMOTE/POINTER/ACCEL/LINK_CMD. UART transport is unaffected (its LEN framing already delivers exact lengths, which satisfy `>=`). No wire/protocol change. **Pending Greg's build on both marvin and fauxmote.**

---

**2026-07-28 — fauxmote link routed over 10BASE-T1S on the coordinator side (default); FLEXCOM5 UART kept as the opt-in fallback.** The ESP32/fauxmote end already rides the shared PLCA bus (its own LAN8651, ethertype `0x88B7`, controller node id 1, heartbeat node_type 3 — done in a prior session). This brings the marvin end onto the same channel, mirroring how the guitar/fretboard nodes were staged before their hardware existed. **marvin has a single LAN8651 MAC-PHY**, so the fauxmote channel is *not* a second interface — it rides the existing `net/t1s` TX/RX path alongside the guitar command (`0x88B5`) and presence heartbeats (`0x88B6`), on its own ethertype `0x88B7`.
- **`net/t1s` gained a generic "controller" channel.** New `T1S_NODE_CONTROLLER` (node table row id 1, gated on the fauxmote-T1S build so a UART build shows no phantom "controller absent" row), ethertype `T1S_ETHERTYPE_CTRL 0x88B7`, a static TX FIFO (depth 8, item = `type + len + ≤8B payload`) drained one-per-service-wake in `t1s_task` (interleaves with the latest-wins guitar command), and an RX demux that hands `0x88B7` frames to a registered `T1SLink_ControllerHandler`. Public API: `T1SLink_SendToController(type,payload,len)` + `T1SLink_SetControllerHandler(...)` + ctrl tx/rx counters. `send_to_node` was generalized to take an ethertype. `T1SLink_Initialize` is now idempotent (static `s_initialized`, first wins) so both the fretboard-T1S and fauxmote-T1S paths can call it.
- **`fauxmote_link` keeps its whole producer/latching/STATUS layer; only the transport seam swaps** (same shape as `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT`). New `MARVIN_FAUXMOTE_TRANSPORT` flag **defaults to T1S**; `=0` selects the FLEXCOM5 UART point-to-point fallback. On T1S: `send_frame` → `T1SLink_SendToController` (MAC-PHY supplies FCS, so the SOF/LEN/CRC8 wrapper is dropped), STATUS uplink arrives via a registered `ctrl_rx` handler, no dedicated peripheral / no RX task. The STATUS latch was factored into a shared `latch_status()` used by both transports. Frame layout `[dst=ctrl][src=coord][0x88B7][TYPE][mf payload]`.
- Files: `net/t1s/t1s_link.{c,h}`, `net/fauxmote/fauxmote_link.{c,h}`, `cmake/marvin/default/user.cmake` (documented `MARVIN_FAUXMOTE_TRANSPORT=1`), `console/console.c` (`fauxmote status` shows transport + ctrl tx/rx; `nodes` lists the controller row automatically). `mf_proto.h` wire content unchanged (still byte-identical to the fauxmote copy). No MCC/Legato edits. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.** On-bus verification waits on the fauxmote LAN8651 bring-up (later session).

---

**2026-07-23 — fauxmote link moved to a dedicated FLEXCOM5 USART; fauxmote is now transport-independent (runs on UART *and* T1S guitar builds).** fauxmote previously borrowed FLEXCOM1 — the fretboard/guitar UART-transport peripheral — which was only free in the T1S guitar build, so the whole fauxmote path was compile-guarded to `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT == T1S`. A new FLEXCOM5 USART (added in MCC) gives fauxmote its own peripheral, dissolving the conflict.
- **New peripheral (MCC).** FLEXCOM5 USART on `PA16`/`FLEXCOM5_IO0` (`FAUXMOTE_TX`) and `PA15`/`FLEXCOM5_IO1` (`FAUXMOTE_RX`), AIC source 14, ring-buffer mode. Static config (`FLEX_US_MR`/`FLEX_US_BRGR`, 8-N-1, MCK, OVER=1, CD=66/FP=5) is byte-identical to FLEXCOM1; fauxmote overrides baud to 1 Mbaud at runtime via `SerialSetup`, so the static CD/FP is non-binding. Commit `a36ccdd` (re-applied patch #11 after the regen).
- **App migration.** `fauxmote_link.c/.h` is a mechanical `FLEXCOM1_USART_*` → `FLEXCOM5_USART_*` rename (transport comes via `definitions.h`; no include change). With FLEXCOM1 no longer contended, every `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT`-guard that existed *only* to keep fauxmote off UART-guitar builds was dropped: `app.c` `Fauxmote_Initialize()`, the `Fauxmote_SendGuitarMask` mirror tap in `fretboard_link.c`, the `fauxmote` console command, and `game_controller.c`'s `ensure_wii_connected()` pre-flight (every build drives a Wii through fauxmote, so the Wii-connect check now always runs — the old `#else return true` stub is gone). The remaining `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT` guards (all in `fretboard_link.c`) are the genuine guitar-actuator UART-vs-T1S split and are unchanged. Commit `1872a9c`; verified on hardware.

**2026-07-23 — T1S host SPI moved FLEXCOM4 → FLEXCOM3 (MCC regen + app rename); verified driving the guitar over T1S.** Board rework relocated the T1S link off FLEXCOM4. Regenerated MCC and renamed the app-side wrapper; the chip-select moved from CS0 to CS1 in the process.
- **Pin map (new → old).** SPI: MOSI `PC22`/`FLEXCOM3_IO0` (was PA10), MISO `PC23`/`FLEXCOM3_IO1` (was PA9), nCS `PC24`/`FLEXCOM3_IO4` (was PA12), SCK `PC26`/`FLEXCOM3_IO2` (was PA11). GPIO: `T1S_IRQ_N` `PB18` (was PB25; falling-edge, internal pull-up carried over), `T1S_RST` `PA17` (was PB3; output, idle high). AIC source 13 (FLEXCOM4) → 8 (FLEXCOM3).
- **Chip-select CS0 → CS1, transparent to app code.** `FLEXCOM3_SPI_Initialize` sets `FLEX_SPI_MR.PCS = NPCS1` and configures `CSR[1]`; nCS/PC24 is the peripheral-muxed `FLEXCOM3_IO4` line and `FLEXCOM3_SPI_WriteRead()` takes no CS argument, so hardware drives CS. `t1s_link.c` never references a CS index or the nCS pin → the CS0→CS1 move needed **zero** app change. App diff was a pure `FLEXCOM4_SPI_*` → `FLEXCOM3_SPI_*` rename (`TransferSetup`/`CallbackRegister`/`WriteRead`); the `T1S_RST`/`T1S_IRQ_N` GPIO macros resolve to the new pins through `definitions.h`.
- **MISO pull-down re-created on PC23.** The absent-chip-determinism pull-down on MISO (added in the "absent-chip handling" work in the decision log) did not survive the FLEXCOM3 regen; re-added on `PC23` via MCC (`5faa2b7`) so a tristated/unpopulated LAN8651 reads a deterministic 0x00 rather than noise.
- **Re-apply patch #11 (`plib_aic.c` UDPHS boot-storm mask) was reverted by the regen and re-applied** before commit (as always after MCC regen). Commits: `70b1fc7` (regen + patch #11), `9ce4fcf` (app rename), `5faa2b7` (MISO pull-down). Verified on hardware: marvin drives the guitar follower over T1S.

**2026-07-22 — marvin froze on boot whenever the USB-CDC (UDPHS) cable was attached at power-on; root cause = `AIC_INT_Initialize` unmasks CPU IRQs with UDPHS already AIC-enabled but its driver uninitialized → ENDRESET interrupt storm. Fixed on hardware (deterministic first-load boot across many power cycles). Local fix minimized to a single generated file.** Symptom: cable attached at boot → totally dead (no splash, no DBGU past the `bkptnone` bootstrap banner; marvin's own `printf` also targets DBGU, so "only the banner" = hang before marvin's first log); attach *after* boot → fine; a variable number of `load-ram.sh` (nSRST) reloads would eventually boot. Diagnosed by JTAG-halting the stalled core and reading PC + AIC/UDPHS registers.
- **Root cause chain.** `AIC_INT_Initialize()` ([plib_aic.c](../default/src/config/default/peripheral/aic/plib_aic.c)) configures every tabled source, writes `AIC_IECR` to **enable** each (incl. UDPHS, source 23, level-sensitive), then calls `__enable_irq()` *before returning* — while `DRV_USB_UDPHS_Initialize` hasn't run (`gDrvUSBUDPHSObj[0].status == UNINITIALIZED`). `UDPHS_IEN` reset default is `0x10` (**ENDRESET enabled**); with the controller attached (D+ pull-up) a connected host drives bus resets → ENDRESET asserts. The instant `__enable_irq()` runs, the ISR fires with no client, can't clear the level-sensitive source → 100% CPU in the UDPHS ISR → `SYS_Initialize` never finishes, scheduler never starts. The variable "N loads" = a race on whether the host is mid-reset when `__enable_irq()` executes. Confirmed at the halt: `AIC_ISR`=23, `IPR0` bit23 pending, `IEN`=0x10, `CTRL`=0x100 (attached), `isOpened`=false, all PC samples in `FreeRTOS_IRQ_Handler`↔`DRV_USB_UDPHS_Tasks_ISR`. (`nSRST` does **not** reset UDPHS, so the attached/enabled state persists across the JTAG dev-loop's warm resets.)
- **Fix (local, minimal footprint — one generated file).** In `AIC_INT_Initialize`, after the configure loop and **before `__enable_irq()`**, leave UDPHS (source 23) MASKED (`AIC_SSR`←23, `AIC_IDCR`) — handler/priority still configured. The perf-log CDC sink enables it via `SYS_INT_SourceEnable(UDPHS_IRQn)` in `PerfLogSinkCdc_Initialize` (our tree) *after* open+attach. A masked AIC source can't vector the CPU regardless of controller/host state, so boot always completes; enumeration works once the sink arms it. **Masking must be inside `AIC_INT_Initialize`** — any mask after it returns loses the race (an earlier `SYS_INT_SourceDisable` in `initialization.c` right after the call never executed). Verified on hardware: first-load boot every time, cable attached, across many cold power cycles; CDC enumerates after boot.
- **Scope decision (w/ Greg): applied only the AIC mask locally.** Two *additional* real driver defects were found and are documented for upstream but **not applied locally** — once source 23 is masked until a client exists, neither can fire: (1) `F_DRV_USB_UDPHS_DEVICE_Tasks_ISR` no-client paths return without acknowledging the interrupt (storm on a level-sensitive controller); (2) `F_DRV_USB_UDPHS_DEVICE_Initialize` sets DETACH *before* a core-reset toggle that clears it, so init finishes *attached*. Keeping the local change to the single `plib_aic.c` patch minimizes the MCC re-apply surface. The full three-fix set + evidence lives on branch `fix/udphs-aic-boot-storm` and in [`udphs_aic_boot_storm_bug.md`](udphs_aic_boot_storm_bug.md) (for filing with Microchip).
- **Filing framing.** Headline defect is the AIC PLIB pattern: `AIC_INT_Initialize` blanket-enables all sources and unmasks CPU IRQs while drivers are uninitialized, so any source that can assert from reset (UDPHS/ENDRESET with a host present) storms before `SYS_Initialize` completes. Sources shouldn't be AIC-enabled until their driver has a client. Driver is Harmony USB v3.16.0; AIC PLIB in `csp`.
- **Re-apply after MCC regen: patch #11 (`plib_aic.c`) only.** The sink `SYS_INT_SourceEnable` is in our tree (no re-apply).

**2026-07-10 — streak: cleaned the training set + units-wrap tens carry (host + regen header; pending build).** Two linked changes after Greg reviewed the corpus. (1) **Audit:** the auto-selected units exemplars had let mid-roll samples in (a rolling wheel half-transitioned reads as a digit and smears the template); removed them / relabelled the rolling ones to units-`x`, corpus 76→65. Future policy: a digit is usable only if identical across neighbouring frames. (2) **Units-wrap tens carry** (`gp_streak_track`): cleaner data made units-9 read better, exposing the tens carry lag (49→40→50). Now a confident units read that drops ≥`GP_STREAK_WRAP_MIN`(5) steps the current (debounced) tens by one — applying the carry the instant the ones rolls (49→50). Works off the debounced digits and fires only on a genuine wrap, so it can't re-inflate a stale value: a wrong-high tens corrected downward stays corrected (guards the "stuck 30-high, can't recover" failure Greg flagged; regression-tested). Capture: carry-lag dips 30→0, anchors exact, 76 tests. Only `game/gameplay_score.c` (tracker) + regenerated `gameplay_metadata.h` (cleaned banks + WRAP_MIN) change. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.** See gameplay journal 2026-07-10.

**2026-07-10 — score + streak readers rewritten to integer math; fixed a coverage out-of-bounds bug (pending build).** Converted the shared coverage pipeline (luma → ink threshold → grid resize → per-cell coverage) in `gameplay_score.c` to pure integer so the firmware reproduces the host prototype **bit-for-bit**. This directly cuts hardware misreads: the device was extracting coverage in float32 while templates were built/validated in float64 (plus different rounding modes), so its reads diverged from what was validated. Now luma = `B*29+G*150+R*77` (no /256), ink threshold = rational `GP_{SCORE,STREAK}_INK_NUM/DEN` of the min..max range (`DEN*(luma-lo) ≷ NUM*(hi-lo)`), integer `gp_edge`, integer coverage; no float in the readers (ARM926 has no FPU). **Also fixed a real OOB bug**: when an ink bbox is smaller than the glyph grid, the last cell's block ran past the bbox and read the unused tail of the scratch buffer (stale data) → wrong coverage on short glyphs; clamped `yb→rh`/`xb→rw` to match the host (numpy) slice. Cross-check is now strict bit-exact (presence + every digit + every confidence flag). Digit banks in `gameplay_metadata.h` rebuilt with integer coverage. Files: `game/gameplay_score.c`, `game/gameplay_metadata.h` (regen). The multiplier (colour counts) was already integer. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.** See gameplay journal 2026-07-10.

**2026-07-10 — dashboard streak label wasn't clearing at song start / after a miss (fixed).** The streak label kept showing the pre-reset count. `ScreenDashboard_ApplyStreak` set an *empty* string for streak 0, and `lestring_set_utf8("")` doesn't repaint/clear the prior glyphs (the score/status labels never hit this — they always render non-empty). Fix: always render the number, so 0 shows "0" and repaints. Both reset paths already posted 0 (song start `run()` → `PostStreak(0)`; miss → tracker resets to 0 when the odometer isn't locked); only the label paint was the bug. File: `ui/screens/dashboard/screen_dashboard.c`. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.**

**2026-07-10 — streak reader: grew the units corpus to fix 9/0/6 detection (host-only; regenerated header).** The dark-on-light units wheel has a fat distance tail, so a correct digit from an under-sampled template exceeded the confidence gate and was dropped — most visibly units-9 (4 exemplars → real 9s at dist ~12300 > gate 9500, "rarely detects 9"). Mined the 6549-frame capture for 30 more **units-only** exemplars (10 each of 9/0/6, `streak__xx<u>__cap…` so they feed only the dark-on-light bank and leave the white-on-dark bank the clean original). units-9 median distance 12300→~5700 (clears the gate); capture confident units-9 reads 0→256; in-sample 154/154; tracker unchanged (0 big jumps, max 304, anchors exact). Only `gameplay_metadata.h` (regenerated digit banks) changes on the firmware side — no logic change. See gameplay journal 2026-07-10. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.**

**2026-07-10 — gameplay_engine: streak tracker debounce + plausibility clamp (pending build).** Fixed a reported streak **lock** and two failures it surfaced, all in `gp_streak_track` (host-proven, see gameplay journal 2026-07-10). The old monotonic forward window couldn't represent a value below `last`, so a tens misread locked the value high (11x→14x…); "confident read trumps" fixed that but unmasked a systematic **tens 0↔8 flicker** (the slashed zero's top loop closes under aliasing → reads 8 confidently, 100↔180) and a **carry-roll 900 blip** (mid-roll hundreds reads 9 for ~2 frames at 99→100). Fix, both symmetric so they never lock: **per-place debounce** (`GP_STREAK_DEBOUNCE {2,2,1}` — a changed digit commits after N consecutive confident reads; slow wheels 2, units immediate) + a **plausibility clamp** (`GP_STREAK_MAX_STEP 50` — reject a committed per-frame value jump a streak can't make; seed bypasses it). `gp_streak_state_t` gains `pend[3]`/`pend_n[3]`. Over the capture: 0 implausible jumps, max 304, anchors exact; host suite 74. Still open: units-9 detection (needs more units exemplars — corpus-growth pass next). Files: `game/gameplay_score.{c,h}`, `game/gameplay_metadata.h` (regen). **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.**

**2026-07-10 — gameplay_engine: note-streak reader (odometer OCR + confident-read tracker) → dashboard STREAK label (pending build).** Last scoring-block value after score + multiplier. The streak is a 3-tumbler odometer (note icon + hundreds/tens/units) that only appears at ~25+ and whose wheels *roll* — the units constantly, tens/hundreds at carries — so any frame can show unreadable wheels. Proven host-side (`tools/gameplay`, see its journal 2026-07-10) then ported.
- **Stateless read (`gp_read_streak`, `gameplay_score.c`, pure/cross-checked).** **Presence = the odometer is *locked at its final position*, detected from the note icon — not the digits** (Greg): the counter slides in from the bottom, overshoots, and bounces down to settle (`score-0335.png` = reference locked frame), and mid-slide the cells are misaligned → garbage. The fixed gold note glyph is a position fiducial — its coverage matches the locked template (L1 < `GP_STREAK_NOTE_MAX_SAD`) only when settled, cleanly rejecting both absent and slide/overshoot frames. When locked, three fixed cells (units dark-on-light, tens/hundreds white-on-dark) → 16×10 coverage → integer-L1 argmin over a **per-polarity 0-9 bank**; a wheel failing the gate is flagged unreadable. Fills `{present, digit[3], known[3]}`. Data frozen into `gameplay_metadata.h` (`GP_STREAK_*`: note template + two digit banks + ROIs + thresholds) by `gameplay export-c`. ROIs are absolute (fixed rig; chrome registration exists but is unused in v0).
- **Stateful tracker (`gp_streak_track`/`gp_streak_reset`, state owned by the caller).** **Presence is binary — not present ⇒ streak reset ⇒ 0** (immediate; the sole reset path). While present, **a confident wheel read is authoritative for its place** (Greg: a clear digit read trumps the rollover logic) — it wins even if the value drops, so a misread is a self-correcting one-frame blip; an unreadable (rolling) wheel is filled (holds last, or resets to 0 when a higher place carried). ≥2 confident wheels seed. This replaced a monotonic forward window `[last, last+30]` whose forward-only search couldn't represent a value below `last` → a tens misread locked the value high (11x read as 14x…); confident-trumps fixes it. Host validation over the 6549-frame capture: hand-read anchors exact (100 % in-sample confident-digit accuracy with the refined 8×15 cells), 0 non-reset decreases, correct slide-gating, max 304.
- **Engine + dashboard.** `game_state_t.streak` (new `uint16_t`); the observer holds one tracker (`s_streak`), advances it on `in_song` reads and resets it when gameplay is left; log adds `streak<s>`. Feed applies `DASH_EVT_STREAK` → `ScreenDashboard_ApplyStreak` → `Marvin_LABEL_DASHBOARD_ROBOT_Streak` via a fixed `leFixedString` (shows "0" at reset — see the 2026-07-10 label-clear fix).
- **Reset behaviour (w/ Greg).** Score/multiplier/streak zero out **the instant a run is requested** (top of `run()`, before nav/connect), not on reaching gameplay. During the song a **broken streak** (odometer no longer locked) blanks immediately (→ 0). **After the song ends everything stays** (score/multiplier/streak persist on the end screen) — the next run's request is the only reset. `play_until_done` tracks **peak streak** (persists across misses) + **final score** and logs `GC: result score N, peak streak M` — the values `Results_Append` (spec §4.8.6) will persist once that path is wired.
- **Known follow-ups (deferred, this commit lands the lock fix first):** (1) **Tens 0↔8 flicker** — the odometer's zero has a center slash; A2D aliasing closes its top loop on ~half the frames, so a tens "0" genuinely matches the **8** template better (4590 vs 6885) and reads 8 *confidently*, alternating frame-to-frame (100↔180, etc.; a hundreds variant flashes 900). Real per-frame ambiguity, not a template bug → fix with a **per-place debounce** (a changed confident digit must persist ≥2 reads before committing; can't lock since it follows reads up or down). (2) **Integer-coverage rewrite** — coverage is still float, so the per-wheel `known` flag can differ C-vs-Python on a borderline wheel (Python `np.round` half-to-even vs C half-up), benign for the tracker; going integer (midpoint threshold + integer grid edges) removes all FP (ARM926 has no FPU) and gives bit-exact parity.
- Host cross-check (`test_firmware_classify` `streak`): C == Python on presence + confidence flags + every confident digit, all 47 corpus frames; `test_streak.py` covers the tracker logic. Full gameplay suite **71 passed**; `gameplay_score.c` compiles clean under `cc -Wall -Wextra` via the cross-check driver. Files: `game/gameplay_score.{h,c}`, `game/gameplay_metadata.h` (regen), `game/gameplay_engine.{c,h}`, `game/game_controller.c`, `ui/dashboard_feed.c`, `ui/screens/dashboard/screen_dashboard.{c,h}`. No MCC/Legato hand-edits. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build + hardware validation** (streak label tracks the odometer, holds through single rolls, jumps at carries, blanks at song start / on a miss). Career-font score mode is the only deferred scoring-block item.

**2026-07-14 — Wiimotes screen buttons wired to fauxmote (manual override).** The MGS regen that named the wiimotes control buttons (`BUTTON_WIIMOTES_ROBOT_{FRET_*,STRUM_UP/DOWN,GUITAR_PLUS/MINUS,UP/DOWN/LEFT/RIGHT,A/B/ONE/TWO,HOME}` — promoted from anonymous `panel_Button_N_0`/label placeholders) is now hooked up to drive the ESP32 Wiimote directly over the fauxmote link.
- **Direct-to-fauxmote, momentary (w/ Greg).** `screen_wiimotes.c` runtime-registers press/release callbacks on every control button via a `{widget-slot, field, bit}` table. Frets/strum/± build the GUITAR slice → `Fauxmote_SendGuitar(mask, MF_WHAMMY_REST, aux)` (whammy fixed at rest — the `WhammySlider`/`TiltControl` panels were **not** named/converted to interactive widgets by the regen, so they're not wired this pass); D-pad + A/B/1/2/Home build the WIIMOTE nav slice → `Fauxmote_SendNav(core, dpad, MF_STICK_CENTER, MF_STICK_CENTER)`. A bit is held only while its button is pressed.
- **Priority override.** New `Fauxmote_SetOverride(bool)` in `net/fauxmote/fauxmote_link.{c,h}`: while on, `Fauxmote_SendGuitarMask()` (the gameplay-mirror hook the timing pipeline calls via `FretboardLink_Send`) is a no-op, so the screen's direct sends own fauxmote's GUITAR slice. The real T1S guitar keeps playing — only fauxmote's feed is commandeered.
- **Show/hide seam.** New `ScreenWiimotes_SetShown(bool)`, called from `UiManager_ShowWiimotes`/`ShowDashboard` (distinct from `SetInput`, which the nav drawer also toggles for its modal). On show: zero state + `SetOverride(true)` + push neutral. On hide: push neutral guitar/nav *then* `SetOverride(false)` (never leave a note held; hand fauxmote back to the mirror).
- `Fauxmote_*` are compiled unconditionally (not behind the `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT` T1S guard that wraps the mirror tap + `Fauxmote_Initialize`), so the screen's calls always link. Files: `ui/screens/wiimotes/screen_wiimotes.{c,h}`, `net/fauxmote/fauxmote_link.{c,h}`, `ui/ui_manager.c`. No MCC/Legato hand-edits (MGS regen staged separately). **Verified working with Greg** on the wiimotes screen driving fauxmote; whammy/tilt deferred until those widgets are named in MGS.
