**2026-07-08 — SONG card playtime bar wired; song-info widget rename sync.** An MGS regen renamed the SONG-info section and replaced the hand-built progress-bar hack (`panel_Container_63..66_0` / `panel_Text_15/16_0` / `label__0_00_0` / `label__7_22_0_0`) with three named widgets: `LABEL_DASHBOARD_SONG_START_TIME` (the "0:00" origin marker), `LABEL_DASHBOARD_SONG_STOP_TIME`, and a real `leProgressBarWidget` `PROGRESSBAR_DASHBOARD_SONG_PLAYTIME`. The renamed detail labels (Title/Artist/Album/Genre/Tier/Duration/Status) already used stable `LABEL_DASHBOARD_SONG_*` names, so only the new widgets needed wiring — dashboard code had no references to the removed decorative names.
- **Both time markers are runtime `leFixedString`s** (set up in `song_detail_init` alongside the Status label). `STOP_TIME` tracks the committed selection: `ScreenDashboard_ApplySelection` rewrites it with the song's `m:ss` (same `SongDetail_Duration` string shown in the DURATION field), "-" when unknown. `START_TIME` counts up with elapsed play time (see below); it resets to "0:00" on a fresh selection.
- **Bar fill + elapsed marker = playthrough time.** New feed event `DASH_EVT_PLAYTIME` (`u.play_ms`) + `DashboardFeed_PostPlaytime`. `game_controller.c` `play_until_done` posts `0` at play start (resets bar + marker) and elapsed ms each `GC_PLAY_POLL_MS` (~300 ms) poll. The dashboard consumer calls `ScreenDashboard_ApplyPlaytime`, which clamps elapsed to `s_song_len_s` (captured in ApplySelection), rewrites `START_TIME` as `m:ss` (formatted directly so 0 reads "0:00" — `SongDetail_Duration(0)` gives "-"), and drives the bar at **pixel resolution** via `ProgressBarAA_SetPermille` (0-1000) rather than the stock 0-100 `setValue` (unknown duration → empty bar, un-clamped marker). The 0-100 value quantized the 349px-wide bar to ~3.5px steps — for a long song it held still several seconds then hopped; permille advances it by single pixels.
- **Rounded (pill) bar ends.** New custom widget `ui/widgets/progressbar_aa/` (`ProgressBarAA_EnableRoundImage`) mirrors `panel_aa`: copies the *full progressbar vtable* (so `setValue` etc. survive), overrides `_paint` to run `AaCorners_RenderRoundImage` after the normal draw — eating the four corners back to the card's `SCHEME_PANEL_GRAY_18181B` BASE (resolved at paint time in the active mode). Radius 4 = half the 8px height → full capsule; the fill's leading cap rounds with it (interior pixels preserved). **The radius is carried by the AA pass, not the widget `cornerRadius`:** the stock classic skin's `drawFill` is a plain square `RectFill`, but its `drawBackground` switches to `FillRoundCornerRect` (ArcFills that overshoot the rect — a track visibly taller than the 8px fill) as soon as the widget `cornerRadius` is nonzero. So the widget keeps `cornerRadius` 0 (stock draws a plain square 8px track) and the AA pass owns the rounding. `ScreenDashboard_Setup` also pins the height to 8 (`setSize`, width preserved via `getWidth`). Added to `cmake/marvin/default/user.cmake`.
  - **Two-vtable gotcha (why the override first did nothing):** unlike a plain panel (where `panel->fn` *is* the base `widget.fn`), `leProgressBarWidget` holds two pointers to its vtable — its typed `bar->fn` and the base `bar->widget.fn`. The renderer dispatches paint via the *base* `widget.fn->_paint` (it works on a `leWidget*`), so `ProgressBarAA_EnableRoundImage` must repoint **both**; repointing only `bar->fn` leaves the stock paint running and the corners square.
  - **Partial-repaint gotcha (fill-end artifacts):** `AaCorners_RenderRoundImage` re-blends existing pixels toward the backdrop and writes at full-rect coordinates via `_Unsafe` pixel ops (relative to the current frame/damage rect, no bounds check). `panel_aa` is safe because a panel repaints its whole rect. But the stock `setValue` damages only the delta strip between old/new value, so a mid-bar update leaves the frame rect a sliver — the corner writes land outside that tile (garbage) and, over many updates, erode the AA bands into the fill (visible in the first/last ~10%). Fix + smoothness in one: the bar is no longer driven by the stock value at all. `ProgressBarAA_SetPermille` stores a 0-1000 fill fraction and invalidates the *whole* widget; `aa_paint` lets the stock skin draw only the (square) track — the widget value stays 0 so the stock fill is skipped — then draws the fill itself as a `RectFill` of the exact pixel width, then rounds the corners. Full-widget repaint every change keeps the corner writes in-bounds and idempotent, and the pixel-width fill removes the 100-step quantization.
- **Tier string is stars-only** (separate ask): `SongDetail_TierText` drops the " TIER n" suffix — main-setlist songs show just ★×tier, bonus still "BONUS". Shared helper, so song-select dialog and dashboard both change.
- Rides the existing single-consumer/render-lock feed flow — no off-thread widget touch. Files: `ui/screens/dashboard/screen_dashboard.{c,h}`, `ui/dashboard_feed.{c,h}`, `game/game_controller.c`, `ui/widgets/progressbar_aa/*` (new), `ui/song_detail.{c,h}`, `cmake/marvin/default/user.cmake`. No MCC/Legato hand-edits (MGS regen staged separately). **Pending Greg's MPLAB build + hardware validation** (stop time matches the selected song; bar fills smoothly over a playthrough and resets on the next run/selection; rounded ends clean with no fill artifacts).

**2026-07-07 — Wiimotes screen is now a nav-selectable base view; nav widget rename sync.** An MGS regen renamed the navigation widgets (dropped the `_0` suffix; `BUTTON_NAV_HAMBURGER` → `BUTTON_DASHBOARD_TITLEBAR_NAVIGATION`), gave the wiimotes screen proper names (`Marvin_panel_Marvin` → `Marvin_PANEL_WIIMOTES`, + `PANEL_WIIMOTES_*`/`BUTTON_WIIMOTES_*`), and added a **Wiimotes** nav entry + a titlebar hamburger on the wiimotes screen. Updated code to match and wired the entry.
- **Base-view swap (new compositor verb, first real use):** `UiManager_ShowDashboard()`/`UiManager_ShowWiimotes()` swap the BASE hw layer between `CANVAS_DASH` (layer 0) and `CANVAS_WIIMOTES` (layer 4). The outgoing canvas is **hidden first** (`gfxcSetLayer` requires the canvas inactive, and an active canvas drives its layer — two on BASE would fight), then the incoming one is bound. Showing wiimotes hides live video (HEO + OVR1 frame); returning restores it via `ScreenVideo_ShowWindowed()`. Mirrors the song-select open/close video handling.
- **Picking across a swap:** Legato picks across *all* attached layers regardless of canvas visibility, so the hidden view's panel must be gated off or it still intercepts touches. `UiManager_SetBaseViewPickable(on)` routes to the active view (`ScreenWiimotes_SetInput` / `UiManager_SetDashboardPickable`). The nav drawer now gates the **active base view** (was dashboard-only) — and because wiimotes is layer 4 (above the drawer's layer 1), gating it off is also what lets the open drawer receive touches over the wiimotes view.
- **Both titlebar hamburgers** (dashboard layer 0, wiimotes layer 4) wire to the same drawer toggle; the wiimotes one only picks while its view is shown (its subtree is gated otherwise).
- **Wiimotes fret buttons are inputs, not indicators (decided w/ Greg):** the wiimotes screen is for manual / override control, so its `BUTTON_WIIMOTES_ROBOT_FRET_*` are *not* fed by the dashboard feed — they'll drive manual actuation, not mirror activity. Files: `ui/screens/navigation/screen_navigation.c`, `ui/screens/wiimotes/screen_wiimotes.{c,h}`, `ui/ui_manager.{c,h}`. No MCC/Legato hand-edits (MGS regen staged separately). **Pending Greg's MPLAB build + hardware validation** (nav → Wiimotes shows the screen; its hamburger opens the drawer; Dashboard returns; video returns with the dashboard; touch routes correctly in both views and with the drawer open).

**2026-07-05 — `detect_active` no longer burns CPU while the source is dark.** Follow-on to the `cpu%` work below: VideoTask's active-area detector re-scanned the frame border **every 20 ms tick** while unlatched, and each *failed* scan (dark border) walked the whole band on uncached DDR (~2.6 ms) → ~13% whenever the source was dark (notably: booting while the Wii is asleep). Two fixes, and crucially **it still never gives up** — abandoning detection would permanently mis-frame a source that starts dark and later brightens:
- **Rate-limit:** `detect_active` self-caps to `VIDEO_ACTIVE_SCAN_MS` (100 ms, ~10 Hz) while unlatched instead of every tick. Trade-off: 16-frame accumulation now takes ~1.6 s after the source brightens (was ~0.3 s) — full-frame fallback shows briefly on wake, then snaps to the crop.
- **Dark-gate:** a cheap `frame_has_light()` (coarse grid, stride 64, returns on first bright sample) runs before the heavy border scan; an all-dark frame skips it → ~0 cost. So an asleep/dimmed Wii = near-idle, and detection resumes automatically the instant it brightens. Files: `video/video.c`. Build clean.

**2026-07-05 — Backed off the health monitor to a quiet safety net (freeze hunt done).** The supervisor + fault dump found & fixed all three freeze modes (T1S unbounded `service_pump`; uninitialized `SYS_FS_FSTAT.lfname` data abort; GameEngine song_select CPU peg — that last one since superseded by the synchronous-observe rework). Reverted the monitor out of investigation mode: heartbeat cadence 10 s → 60 s; all periodic telemetry (heartbeat line, `SUP:` summary, stack table, startup) → `LOG_DEBUG` (silent at the default INFO level); **`RUNAWAY` stays `LOG_ERROR`** as the always-on watch. The on-card log (`health/heartbeat.csv` + `stacks.txt`) is now behind a compile switch **`HM_SD_LOG_ENABLED` (default 0/off)**; build `-DHM_SD_LOG_ENABLED=1` to restore the crash-timing record (SD-only helpers gated too, so it's warning-clean either way). Re-investigate via `log_set_level(LOG_LEVEL_DEBUG)` and/or the `-D`. Build clean in both switch states. File: `health/health_monitor.c`.
- **Follow-up — `health` now shows a recent-window `cpu%`, not cumulative-since-boot.** The old table used `ulRunTimeCounter / total_since_boot`, a lifetime average with no decay — it buried *current* load under startup (e.g. VideoTask's boot-time active-area scan reading as a steady "13%" long after it had latched). `HealthMonitor_Report` now snapshots, waits `HM_REPORT_WINDOW_MS` (500 ms), snapshots again, and reports each task's share of *that* window (the supervisor's delta math, on demand). `health` takes ~½ s to answer and shows what tasks are doing *now*. (Separately noted: `detect_active` has a silent, no-give-up failure path — `frame_edges`-false re-scans the border every tick forever with no log; a bounded/observable fix is still pending.)

**2026-07-05 — Dashboard live-telemetry feed: fret activity → dashboard, single-consumer event flow.** The dashboard ROBOT fret buttons were static; wired them to track the actual guitar mask, structured as the general flow that score/multiplier/streak will also ride. New `ui/dashboard_feed.{c,h}`: a **single tagged event queue** (`dashboard_evt_t` = {type, union}) with **one consumer task** (prio 2, UI band) that blocks on the queue, coalesces last-per-type on drain, and applies to widgets. Design decided w/ Greg:
- **Hard constraint honored:** the actuation path (timing/gameplay/fretboard link) must never be delayed. Producers post with `xQueueSend(…,0)` (non-blocking, drop-on-full) — `FretboardLink_Send` (the convergence point for all producers) gets one `DashboardFeed_PostFret(v)` line, mirroring its existing `Fauxmote_SendGuitarMask` fan-out. One store-class op, no lock, no Legato touch.
- **Consumer is the sole dashboard widget writer, under a render lock.** Legato here is **single-threaded** — no `LEGATO_USE_OSAL`, no renderer lock, no `leTimer`; `_LEGATO_Tasks` runs `leUpdate` (events + one-pass paint) then `vTaskDelay(10ms)`. So *any* widget/damage edit from another task races the renderer. **First cut got this wrong** (consumer mutated widgets from its own prio-2 task): frets never showed and the SONG card updated one selection behind — the classic dropped-damage signature — and it likely contributed to a hard freeze. Fix: `UiManager_RenderLock()/Unlock()` — suspend `LEGATO_Tasks`+`SYS_INPUT_Tasks` and gate on `leRenderer_IsIdle()` (reliable now that scratch is sized to one full-screen pass, §13), so the edit can't corrupt an in-flight paint. The consumer wraps its coalesced apply in it. Held ~microseconds; never touches the actuation tasks (prio 4–5). Note this corrected a wrong assumption in an earlier draft of this entry that the old off-thread path was safe — the old `dash_selection_changed` actually ran **in-context** (from the SELECT button callback inside `leUpdate`), which is why it worked.
- **Same-class pre-existing race also fixed:** `dash_game_status` wrote the Status label from the game-controller task (prio 4) via `lestring_set_utf8` → `setFromChar`, which **does** invalidate — an off-thread invalidate that fires during gameplay (NAVIGATING→PLAYING…) alongside the fret traffic. Routed it through the feed too (`DASH_EVT_STATUS`, string copied into the event; `ScreenDashboard_ApplyStatus`). No off-thread invalidate remains.
- **Fret visual:** the classic skin fills a button with `LE_SCHM_BACKGROUND` when pressed (state ≠ UP) and `LE_SCHM_BASE` when up, so each fret scheme already encodes lit/unlit — no new schemes. Two non-obvious requirements to make it show (both needed): (1) the buttons must be **toggleable**, else `setPressed(TRUE)` just fires a click and leaves state UP; (2) **explicit `invalidate`** after `setPressed`, because `setPressed` only self-invalidates for image/bevel/pressed-offset buttons — for a plain scheme-filled button the UP↔TOGGLED colour swap wouldn't repaint. `ScreenDashboard_ApplyFret(mask)` does both, per bit (`TIMING_BIT_*`), touching only buttons whose state changed (cached `s_last_mask`); buttons also set `IGNOREEVENTS` (status display, not input).
- **Lifecycle:** `DashboardFeed_Init()` (queue) pre-scheduler in `UiManager_Initialize` so producers can post immediately; `DashboardFeed_Start()` (task) at end of `ui_boot_task` (post-reveal), so the consumer never touches the widget tree during build/paint/reveal. Buffered events apply on first run.
- **Future:** SCORE/MULTIPLIER/STREAK event types + post fns exist; the gameplay engine will post them once it produces the values (dashboard widgets already present). Files: `ui/dashboard_feed.{c,h}` (new), `ui/screens/dashboard/screen_dashboard.{c,h}`, `actuator/fretboard_link.c`, `ui/ui_manager.c`, `cmake/.../user.cmake`. No MCC/Legato edits. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build + hardware validation** (frets track under timing + manual producers; selection still updates; idle consumer ~0 CPU).

---

**2026-07-04 — Navigation is now fully closed-loop; observation made synchronous; timing pipeline no longer observes screens.** A real misfire (after the Wii remote woke, the exit from `practice_end_menu` strummed down 1 + GREEN → RESTART instead of down 4 → QUIT) traced to two gaps: (1) `observe()` was `RequestObservation()` + a fixed 140 ms delay + `GetLatest()`, which returned the last *retained* classification whether or not a fresh one had completed — so right after the remote woke, the "check" read stale screen/selection; (2) `select_to()` read the cursor once then strummed the delta and pressed GREEN with no re-check, so a stale/wrong read fired GREEN on the wrong row. The retained-`latest`/`GetLatest` design (and the dormant `SetObserveEnabled` master gate) were vestiges of the removed background scan.
- **Fix (decided w/ Greg):**
  - **Observation is synchronous request/response.** `GameplayEngine_Observe(out, timeout)` posts a request and blocks until the task classifies a frame *captured after the request* (the task keeps draining every frame at ~0 CPU, and skips the in-hand pre-request frame for a freshness guarantee), then returns the result via a depth-1 response queue. Deleted `RequestObservation`, `GetLatest`, `s_have_latest`/`s_latest`, `s_force_observe`, and the dead `SetObserveEnabled`/`ObserveEnabled`/`s_observe_enabled` master gate. A read can no longer return stale state; there is no ambient "current screen" to poll.
  - **Confirm-before-GREEN, bail-to-recover.** `select_to` → `select_and_confirm(expect_screen, target)`: blind-strum the delta (allowed within a screen), then **re-observe**; only press GREEN once the observed cursor == target on the still-expected screen. If the screen isn't expected / selection unreadable / cursor won't settle in `GC_MAX_CONFIRM` tries, return false without pressing — the run loop re-observes and retries, then FAILs after `GC_MAX_CONFIRM_FAIL`. Same contract for `select_song` (fixed-slot ordinal). `nav_to_main_menu`'s overlay QUITs go through the same path — the most exposed spot no longer fires on one stale read. Never navigate *between* screens without a fresh check first.
  - **`saturate_top` (FULL SONG / FULL SPEED = top item):** where the row is readable (`speed_select`) strum up until the selection reports index 0, then GREEN (up-strum can't overshoot — no wrap). Where it isn't readable (`section_select`), GREEN through, **assuming FULL SONG is already selected** — we only ever reach `section_select` with FULL SONG at the top. This is a deliberate stopgap: caught the controller stuck FAIL-looping on `section_select` (screen 8, no row reader → selection always -1 → could never confirm). **Missing piece to revisit (decided w/ Greg): a real `section_select` FULL SONG detector** — a top-slot highlight match (mirrors the `song_select` slot match) so we *confirm* rather than assume. Blocked on corpus data: only one `section_select` frame exists (FULL SONG selected); need a "non-top section selected" negative to build/validate the threshold (gameplay journal open question). Until then, assume + GREEN.
  - **`CurrentScreen()` deleted; timing pipeline stops observing.** `timing_pipeline` had *pulled* `GameplayEngine_CurrentScreen()` to gate actuation on `in_song` — a layering inversion (a getter that also lied about freshness). Removed the gate, `s_gate_on_gameplay`, `SetGateOnGameplay`/`GateOnGameplay`, the `timing gate` console subcommand, and the gameplay_engine include. The actuation window is owned solely by `TimingPipeline_SetEnabled`, which the controller already brackets to the verified `in_song` window (the gate could never react faster than the controller's own poll anyway). Bench test = `timing 1` actuates immediately (former `gate off`).
- Files: `game/gameplay_engine.{c,h}`, `game/game_controller.c`, `actuator/timing_pipeline.{c,h}`, `console/console.c`, `app.c`. No MCC/Legato edits. Firmware C written + syntax-reviewed; **pending Greg's MPLAB build + hardware validation** (START from an end-screen right after remote-wake; sticky-default landing; unmodeled-screen recovery; full run to `in_song`; idle ~0 CPU).

**2026-07-03 — Third freeze mode: GameEngine pegged on song_select → observation is now purely request-triggered.** The supervisor caught `RUNAWAY 'GameEngine' cpu=99% isr=0%` right after landing on `song_select`. Root cause: the game-state observer ran a **free-running background scan** (was 5 Hz), and one `gp_read_song` on song_select is ~150–190 ms of soft-float (64 templates × 15 offsets × 192, no FPU on the ARM926). Since a single classify ≈ the 200 ms scan period, background observation of song_select alone pegged prio-4 GameEngine and starved everything at prio<4 (console/touch/UI) — same "task with no CPU bound monopolizes the core" shape as the T1S spin. (A first pass added a duty-cap to bound it to ~1/3 CPU, but that still bursts ~190 ms and re-matches an unchanged list — laggy and wasteful.)
- **Fix (decided w/ Greg): no continuous scan at all — observation is purely request-triggered.** `game_task` now classifies *only* when `GameplayEngine_RequestObservation()` was called; otherwise it just drains the frame queue at ~0 CPU. Grounded in the code: the **only** consumer of the CV screen state is the game controller (menu nav + song-end polling) — the dashboard uses the touch `Selection`, and `xGameStateQueue` has no subscriber. So an idle marvin needs no screen scanning.
- Removed the 5 Hz period + duty-cap machinery (`gameplay_engine.c`); master gate enabled once in `Initialize` (does nothing without a request). Dropped the boot `SetObserveEnabled(true)` (`app.c`) and the per-session enable (`game_controller.c`). `play_until_done` was *passive* (`GetLatest`, relying on the background scan); it now `observe()`s — forces its own cheap in_song classify each poll — to detect song-end and keep the timing pipeline's in_song gate (`GameplayEngine_CurrentScreen`) fresh. Nav already forced via `observe()`, unchanged. Result: idle (incl. sitting on song_select) = ~0 CV CPU, no lag; nav/play force reads as needed. Build clean (XC32 v5.10). Pending hardware validation of a full START→play→end run.

**2026-07-03 — Investigating a several-hour freeze; built a health monitor + runaway-task supervisor.** Symptom: after a while (minutes to hours) marvin goes unresponsive — touch + FLEXCOM2 console dead — but the LVDS panel keeps showing *live* video. New `health/health_monitor.{c,h}` (in `user.cmake`, init last in `App_StartServices`, `health` console command) to characterize it:

- **Heartbeat** (10 s debug cadence): one line to `/mnt/marvin/health/heartbeat.csv` (open→write→close each beat so the last line survives a hang) + mirrored to DBGU: `timestamp,uptime_s,tick,frames,free_heap,worst_task,worst_stk_free`. The final timestamp bounds when it died; `frames` shows if capture had already stalled.
- **Stack dump** (10 min): full per-task table (state/prio/stack-HWM/run%) overwritten in `health/stacks.txt` + DBGU.
- **Supervisor** (prio 6 — above every app task, which top out at 5): samples the run-time-stats counters every 1 s and logs `RUNAWAY '<task>'` when one task eats the CPU while idle ~0%. It runs at higher priority than any spinner and uses its **own lock-free snapshot buffer** (never shares the heartbeat's mutex — a starved health task holding that mutex would otherwise deadlock the reporter exactly when needed), so it can name the culprit *during* the wedge. Also emits a `SUP: idle/top/isr%` summary every 10 s.

**First-run findings (froze at 140 s with a video signal present):**
- **Not a stack overflow / malloc-fail / assert.** No such DBGU message, all stacks healthy (the alarming-looking `worststk=IDLE:424` is fine — IDLE only has a 128-word stack, 424 B is 83% free). Heap flat at 22312 B across beats → no leak. Capture healthy at ~60 fps right up to the freeze.
- **Interrupts are still firing during the freeze** — decisive clue. The HEO framebuffer pointer is re-latched in the *ISC frame-done IRQ* (`ui_manager.c` `heo_frame_latch`), not a task, so "video still live" ⇒ the CPU is still taking IRQs. That **rules out** the FreeRTOS fault hooks (all `taskDISABLE_INTERRUPTS(); for(;;);`, which would freeze the image) and an IRQ-masking abort handler. Signature = **a task stuck in a non-blocking loop (or an all-task deadlock) while ISRs keep the panel alive** — console/touch (prio 2) starved, video (ISR-driven) survives.
- **Correlation:** an earlier run with *no* video signal survived 2+ h without freezing → suspicion falls on the frame-driven tasks (CvMarvinV1 / GameEngine / timing) or per-frame ISR load. The supervisor's `RUNAWAY`/`isr%` output is meant to settle which.
- **Fixed a self-inflicted hazard:** the monitor originally called `Storage_Mount()` itself, racing the boot mount (`STG: mount failed (fs err 20)` = `NOT_ENOUGH_FREE_VOLUME`, a stale/misleading latched error) — unsafe given `FF_FS_REENTRANT=0`. Now gated on `Storage_IsMounted()`, never self-mounts.

Related prior work: the 2026-07-01 "video-collapse recovery watchdog" entry below.

**Same day — the supervisor's boot-window activity wedged boot; gated it (and the health task) to post-reveal.** First flash of the supervisor build got stuck on the splash — the UI never revealed, and *even the prio-6 supervisor stopped printing*, which means the scheduler itself wedged (a prio-6 task only stops if the kernel stops, not merely if a lower task spins). Two findings:
- **Supervisor false-positive:** it fired `RUNAWAY 'UiBoot' 98%` repeatedly during startup — but that's the boot task legitimately decoding 128 cover-art images (CPU-bound), exactly the threshold caveat. Not a real runaway.
- **Regression cause:** dropping the heartbeat to 10 s + adding the prio-6 supervisor put health-monitor activity — SD I/O (`heartbeat_write`, FatFs is `FF_FS_REENTRANT=0`) and `uxTaskGetSystemState` (which `vTaskSuspendAll()`s the scheduler) — *inside the capture/reveal boot window* (first heartbeat now at ~17 s vs. 80 s before). `ui_boot_task` arms the CSI-2 D-PHY (`Video_CaptureEnable`) there, which `app.c`/journal flag as timing-critical. The prior working run's monitor only touched the card/kernel at 80 s, well after reveal.
- **Fix:** added `s_ready` + `HealthMonitor_NotifyReady()`, called at the very end of `ui_boot_task` (after `Video_CaptureEnable`). Both HM tasks stay fully idle until then — no card I/O, no task-list walks — so the boot window is byte-for-byte back to the working run's behavior. The monitor still covers the whole *running* phase, which is when the target freeze occurs. Also require **two consecutive** starved samples before logging `RUNAWAY` to cut transient false positives. Build clean (XC32 v5.10).

**Next:** flash + confirm it boots to dashboard again (validates the boot-window-perturbation cause), then watch DBGU for `RUNAWAY`/`SUP:` through a freeze to name the task; then decide fix + whether to enable the WDT (currently disabled at `initialization.c:445`; `WDT_MR` may be write-once → needs MCC config, not a runtime write).

**Same day — the supervisor caught it: freeze root cause is `T1SLink` busy-spinning at 99% in an unbounded `service_pump`.** Running without a T1S peer connected, the supervisor printed `RUNAWAY 'T1SLink' cpu=99% idle=0% isr=0%` (repeating — prio-6 supervisor stays alive above the prio-5 spinner). `isr=0%` is the tell: it's a **task-context busy-loop**, not an interrupt storm. Root cause in `net/t1s/t1s_link.c` `service_pump()`:
```
do { s_need_service = false; ... TC6_Service(...); } while (s_need_service);
```
`TC6_CB_OnNeedService` just sets `s_need_service=true`, and the TC6 lib re-flags it every `TC6_Service` call while the LAN8651 has a condition it can't clear (with no PLCA follower on the bus, a status latches after minutes → persistent `IRQ_N`). The `do/while` then never terminates → the prio-5 T1S task spins at 100%, starving everything ≤ prio-4 (console, touch, Legato, detector, health, idle). The ISC frame-latch runs in the ISR, so the panel stays live → exactly the reported symptom (console+touch dead, video up). **This is the multi-hour/random freeze.**
- **Fix:** bounded the pump — `while (s_need_service && ++iters < T1S_SERVICE_MAX_ITERS)` (cap 8), and when it bails with work still pending the task loop does `vTaskDelay(1)` to force a yield, so the T1S task can *never* monopolize the CPU regardless of the MAC-PHY state. Added `s_service_overruns` counter, surfaced as `svc ovr` in the `t1s` console command — a climbing count flags the stuck-MAC-PHY condition for root-causing (likely a TX/RX buffer or PLCA status with no follower; that's a separate, non-fatal investigation now).
- **Note there appear to be two freeze modes:** this T1S spin (prio-6 stays alive, `RUNAWAY` fires) and an earlier *silent* stop where even prio-6 died with no output (the up=627s run) — the latter is consistent with a CPU data-abort, which the new `fault/fault_report.c` handlers will now print. Fixing the T1S spin removes the dominant mode; keep the fault dump in to catch the other if it recurs. Build clean (XC32 v5.10).

**Same day — the fault dump caught the *silent* freeze mode: an uninitialized `SYS_FS_FSTAT.lfname` → data abort in `FATFS_stat`. Fixed.** The new handler printed `*** DATA ABORT pc=0x23f3b680 far=0x5de27022 dfsr=0x00000005 cpsr=0x2000001f ***`. Decoded: `dfsr=5` = section translation fault (unmapped address); `cpsr` mode `0x1f` = a FreeRTOS task (SYS mode); `pc` → `FATFS_stat` (`sys_fs_fat_interface.c:308`); `far=0x5de27022` = the wild address written. Line 308 is `if (fileStat->lfname != NULL) fileStat->lfname[0]='\0';` — and `far` *is* that `lfname` value. Root cause: `heartbeat_write()` (and `results.c` `Results_Append`) declared `SYS_FS_FSTAT st;` **uninitialized** before `SYS_FS_FileStat`. Per the SYS_FS contract (`sys_fs.h:1374`) the caller must set `lfname = NULL`; uninitialized, `st.lfname` held stack garbage → non-NULL → `FATFS_stat` wrote through it → abort. Explains the **random timing** exactly: crash only when that stack slot happened to hold an unmapped value (otherwise a silent harmless-looking write). `art.c`/`storage.c` already `memset`+`lfname=NULL` before `SYS_FS_DirRead` (the codebase knew the contract; these two sites missed it). **Fix:** `memset(&st,0,sizeof st)` before `SYS_FS_FileStat` in both `health_monitor.c` and `results.c`. Build clean.
- **So both freeze modes are now root-caused and fixed:** (1) T1S unbounded `service_pump` busy-spin (bounded), (2) this uninitialized-FSTAT data abort (the health monitor's own periodic `FileStat` is what made mode 2 fire regularly and exposed it — and the fault handler it shipped with is what caught it). The `fault/fault_report.c` dump + the supervisor together turned two silent multi-hour hangs into named, located bugs in one session.
- **Absent-chip handling (done).** Root of the no-peer/no-chip servicing: the LAN8651 wasn't populated, so SPI MISO floated and `TC6_Service` read garbage (IRQ_N has a 100k pull-up on the schematic, so it reads deasserted — not the driver). Two changes: (1) **MCC pin config** — added an internal pull-up on IRQ_N (PB25) and a **pull-down on MISO** so absent/tristated reads are deterministic (0x00) instead of noise. (2) **Absent-chip guard** in `t1s_link.c` — factored bring-up into `t1s_try_bringup()` (reset pulse + `TC6Regs_Init` accept, bounded; + `GetInitDone` wait, bounded) called from the task loop: if it fails, warn once and `vTaskDelay(T1S_ABSENT_RETRY_MS=1s)` then retry, instead of falling into the perpetual service loop. Result: with no LAN8651 the T1S task sits at ~0% CPU and self-heals if a chip is attached later; the chip-present bring-up path is behavior-identical (up on first attempt). The bounded `service_pump` remains the backstop.

**Same day — freeze is a silent CPU abort, not a task spin; made the fault handlers speak.** Post-gating the board boots fine, then froze again ~10 min in. The supervisor data is decisive: the last samples before the freeze read **idle=79–88%, 60 fps, heap flat, all stacks healthy** — i.e. the system was *idle* and then stopped dead. Not a runaway (idle would be ~0). (The lone 79% sample is self-induced — the 10-min stack dump, `top=Health:10%`.) A sudden hard stop from idle + panel still lit + console/touch/**prio-6 supervisor** all dead + **nothing on DBGU** = a **CPU data/prefetch abort landing in a silent `while(true){}`**.
- Ruled out along the way (all by reading code, after wrong guesses): D-PHY (video-only, never freezes); FatFs concurrency corruption (SYS_FS is internally mutexed, `gSysFsMutex`); silent `configASSERT` (it's a **no-op** — `configASSERT` undefined, `vAssertCalled` dead). The real silent-death path is `config/default/fault_handlers.c` — all four ARM exception handlers were `weak` `while(true){}` spins.
- **Built `fault/fault_report.c`** (in `user.cmake`): strong overrides for `data_abort`/`prefetch_abort`/`undefined_instruction`. Naked entries capture the banked LR (faulting PC: −8 dabt, −4 pabt/undef), SPSR, and CP15 FAR (`c6,c0,0`) + DFSR (`c5,c0,0`) for data aborts, then branch to a C reporter that dumps them via the **polled `DBGU_WriteByte`** (fault-safe — no RTOS/heap/IRQ, same primitive the MCC malloc/stack hooks use) and spins. cstartup enters aborts in Abort mode with a valid `_abtstack`, so C is safe there. objdump-confirmed the strong symbol won over the weak. Resolve the printed PC with `xc32-addr2line -e out/marvin/default.elf <pc>`.
- **Next freeze should now print** `*** DATA ABORT pc=.. far=.. dfsr=.. ***` — `far` is the bad address, `pc` the instruction. That converts the multi-hour/10-min silent hang into a located fault. (Follow-ups if wanted: stash pc/far to GPBR + reset for auto-recovery; enable `configASSERT` with a printing handler.)

**2026-07-02 — M10 game-state controller: START → auto-navigate GH3 → CV plays the song.** New `game/game_controller.{c,h}` — a FreeRTOS task that ports the offline `tools/gameplay` `NavController`. On `GameController_Start()` it reads the committed `Selection` (`game/selection.h`), RED-anchors to `main_menu`, then walks the Training/Practice path (TRAINING→PRACTICE→song→[LEAD]→difficulty→FULL SONG→FULL SPEED→loading→in_song) **closed-loop**: each iteration polls the gameplay engine's `{screen, selection}`, runs the plan step whose `expected_from` matches the observed screen, and recovers with RED on an off-plan screen (budgets: 60 iters / 12 recoveries, ported). Cursor moves are signed-delta strums from the *observed* position (sticky-safe); FULL SONG/FULL SPEED strum up to saturate; song_select toggles setlist (BLUE/YELLOW) then strums the ordinal delta. On `in_song` it calls `TimingPipeline_SetEnabled(true)` and the existing in-song gate arms CV actuation automatically; it holds until the song ends (`practice_end_menu`) / leaves gameplay / `Stop`, then releases + `TimingPipeline_SetEnabled(false)`.

- **Menu inputs are guitar fret/strum masks** (GREEN=bit0, RED=bit1, YELLOW=2, BLUE=3, strums=5/6) via `FretboardLink_Send(mask, PERF_ACTUATOR_PRODUCER_GAMEPLAY)` — a new producer id — so nav pulses drive **both** the T1S guitar and fauxmote→Wii (mirror-to-both). Pulses press→hold 40 ms→release (menus auto-repeat a held strum). It owns the wire by keeping timing/manual off during nav (sets timing off *after* `ManualControl_SetEnabled(false)` to beat that module's re-enable trap).
- **Observer hook added:** `GameplayEngine_GetLatest(game_state_t*)` — synchronous snapshot of the last classification, retained every classify (paired with `RequestObservation()` for a fresh read after each command). `game_state_t.selection` is the cursor index (static menus) / `gp_song_templates[]` index (song_select).
- **Triggers:** dashboard **START** button (`Marvin_BUTTON_DASHBOARD_GAMEPLAY_START`, wired in `ScreenDashboard_Setup`) + console **`play [stop|status]`**. Progress shows on the SONG-card **Status** label (`Marvin_LABEL_DASHBOARD_SONG_Status`, previously unused) via `GameController_SetStatusObserver` → NAVIGATING/PLAYING/READY/FAILED.
- **v1 limits (as planned):** robot-plays always (1P_ROBOT; respecting 1P_HUMAN is later); **start from menus, not mid-song** (no `+`/pause on the T1S mask path); relies on the nav doc's no-wrap + LEAD-at-index-0 assumptions — confirm on hardware. Status label is driven from the controller task (not the Legato/UI thread); acceptable for a text label, marshal later if it glitches.

**On hardware (2026-07-02):** first run navigated main_menu→in_song and handed off to CV end-to-end. Two fixes from that run: (1) **over-firing** — the loop settled a fixed 400 ms then re-matched on whatever screen it saw, so a slow menu transition made a step re-run (observed `GC: PRACTICE` ×3) and a stray confirm could leak onto the next screen (picked the wrong song). Replaced with `wait_screen_change(from)`: block until the screen actually leaves the step's origin before re-evaluating (applied to nav steps, RED recovery, and the anchor loop). (2) **pulses too fast** — GH3 dropped/merged inputs; slowed to 70 ms asserted / 180 ms gap so each strum is a discrete edge. Deliberately unhurried over fast. (3) **end-of-song handling** — the controller now *stays* on `practice_end_menu` after a song (release CV, status READY); it does **not** auto-exit (operator's choice — leave the results up). Cleanup happens only when the *next* run starts: `nav_to_main_menu()` QUITs out of the terminal overlays (practice-end idx4 / quit-confirm idx1 / pause idx6) and REDs back up the forward menus, so START works even when the game was left on an end/pause menu (RED alone is unreliable there). (4) **Wii pre-flight** — a run now checks `Fauxmote_GetStatus` first: if bonded-but-disconnected it issues `MF_CMD_RECONNECT` and waits ≤12 s for `CONNECTED` (status CONNECTING); if not bonded → `PAIR WII`; if it can't connect → `NO WII` and abort. No fauxmote STATUS at all (UART down / not running) → proceed anyway so a T1S guitar can still actuate. Guarded to the T1S build (no-op otherwise).

**2026-07-02 — Actuation gating: timing pipeline defaults OFF + only actuates in-song (first slice of the actuation-source arbiter).** Surfaced by the fauxmote link: with the detector-driven timing pipeline **enabled by default** and the CV detector watching a *menu* (not a note highway), it published spurious frets/strum every frame — harmless when they only reached an idle T1S guitar node, but the new mirror drove the **real Wii** (held buttons, menu stepping). Two changes: (1) `TimingPipeline` **defaults disabled** (`timing on` to arm it for a song); disabling now also sends one release so no frets stay held. (2) **Screen gate** (`s_gate_on_gameplay`, default on): `publish_mask` suppresses the mask to a release unless `GameplayEngine_CurrentScreen() == GP_SCREEN_in_song`, so the detector only actuates during actual play. Console: `timing <on|off|gate <on|off>>`, `status` shows timing+gate, and `fauxmote btn` warns when timing is on (it would overwrite the manual mask). **Direction (agreed w/ Greg):** the end-state is *explicit* control of who drives the controller — the game-state controller (M10) sending nav during menus, the CV detector (or another) during a song, mutually exclusive. This gate is the detector half; the menu-nav half waits on the M10 controller (gameplay engine is observe-only today). Tracked as the actuation-source arbiter open item. **Pending same MPLAB build as the link below.**

**2026-07-02 — fauxmote command link (marvin TX side): mirror the gameplay mask to the ESP32 Wiimote over FLEXCOM1.** New `net/fauxmote/fauxmote_link.{c,h}` transmits controller input to the fauxmote ESP32 over the **FLEXCOM1 USART (PA28/PA29)** — the fretboard UART-transport pins, which are **free while the guitar node rides T1S** (the current build, `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT=1`). Wire protocol is the shared `net/fauxmote/mf_proto.h` (byte-for-byte identical to `firmware/fauxmote/main/mf_proto.h`): `SOF/TYPE/LEN/CRC8` framing, `GUITAR`/`WIIMOTE`/`LINK_CMD` out + `STATUS` in. A TX task re-sends the latched `GUITAR` slice on change (low latency, via a notify) and at a 50 ms floor (self-heals dropped frames + feeds fauxmote's 200 ms watchdog); an RX task parses the `STATUS` uplink. **Routing = mirror-to-both:** `FretboardLink_Send()` (the single choke point both producers — timing pipeline + manual_control — already call) now also calls `Fauxmote_SendGuitarMask(mask)`, so every gameplay mask drives the T1S guitar node **and** fauxmote in lock-step (`GUITAR` byte 0 == the T1S 7-bit mask). Init in `App_StartServices` (guarded to the T1S build; before the producers). `SerialSetup` bumps FLEXCOM1 to the link's 1 Mbaud at init (MCC default was 500000 for the guitar UART). New `fauxmote` console command drives it for bring-up (`status`/`pair`/`stop`/`reconnect`/`unlink`/`ext`/`btn <mask>`). **Constraint:** owns FLEXCOM1, so it's mutually exclusive with a `TRANSPORT=UART` guitar build (compile-guarded). **Code-complete; pending MPLAB build + on-hardware bring-up** with the ESP32 wired (marvin TX PA28→ESP RX GPIO7, ESP TX GPIO8→marvin RX PA29, common GND). Protocol spec: [`../../../docs/marvin-fauxmote-link.md`](../../../docs/marvin-fauxmote-link.md).

**2026-07-02 — Root cause of "NAND boot produces a non-working app": marvin outgrew the bootstrap's copy window + a flasher erase bug.** After recovering the board (entry below), the real question remained — why does `program-nand.sh` yield a broken app while `load-ram.sh` runs marvin fine? Two coupled defects, both triggered by app growth:

1. **App exceeds the bootstrap's fixed copy window.** The NAND/QSPI at91bootstraps (`*_uboot` defconfigs) copy a compile-time `CONFIG_IMG_SIZE` bytes from `0x40000`→`0x23F00000` and jump — no size header. Stock is `0x100000` (1 MiB, sized for u-boot). `out/harmony.bin` is now **`0x13f948` (1.25 MiB)**, so the bootstrap copied only the first 1 MiB → truncated marvin. `load-ram.sh` is immune (loads the whole ELF over JTAG). This is *what changed*: marvin crossed 1 MiB.

2. **Flasher erase undershoots the app.** `nand_console.py` program mode hard-coded `nand erase 0x0 0x100000`, but writes the app across `0x40000 … 0x40000+APP_SZ` (=`0x180000` now). Pages past `0x100000` were written onto **un-erased** NAND → corrupt → `PMECC: failed to correct corrupted bits!` (the error from the recovery saga). This bit once the app passed `0x100000-0x40000 = 0xC0000` (768 KiB); it was intermittent because a prior chip-erase leaves those pages `0xff`.

**Fix (all three coupled pieces must cover the app):**
- Rebuilt the **NAND + QSPI** bootstraps with **`CONFIG_IMG_SIZE=0x00400000` (4 MiB)** — applied via the same `.config`-override technique as the blue-LED→PC18 repurpose (keeps the at91bootstrap clone vanilla; `oldconfig` for LED also carries the `-D` IMG_SIZE). objdump-verified: blue `mov r0,#82`/`r1,#0`, copy `#0x400000` from `#0x40000`→`0x23f00000`. Regenerated the PMECC-headed NAND bin (`make-pmecchead.sh`, word `0xc2605007`). **SD** bootstrap unchanged (loads by filename from FAT — no IMG_SIZE, immune); **bkptnone** unchanged (loads no app).
- `nand_console.py` program mode now **`nand erase.chip`** — guarantees every page the write touches *and* every page the 4 MiB copy window reads is clean `0xff`, regardless of app size or IMG_SIZE.
- `program-nand.sh` gained **`NAND_IMG_SIZE=0x400000`** + a guard that aborts if `harmony.bin` exceeds it (loud "rebuild the bootstrap" message instead of a silent truncated boot). Guard arithmetic tested (fits with 2.75 MiB margin; a 5 MiB app aborts).
- Coupling documented in `binaries/README.md` (new "app copy window" customization section + `marvin_cfg` recipe helper). Chose 4 MiB over 2/8 for headroom vs. boot-time erased-page reads.

**Not yet reflashed to hardware** — next step: `./program-nand.sh` with the new bins and confirm marvin boots standalone from NAND.

**2026-07-02 — NAND recovery from a partial flash: two bugs fixed in the OpenOCD flashing tooling.** A board with a *valid* boot-region at91bootstrap but a *corrupt* app (`harmony.bin` at NAND 0x40000, `PMECC: failed to correct corrupted bits!`) loops forever (RomBOOT → bootstrap → app-load fail → RomBOOT), and `erase-nand.sh` couldn't get u-boot up to erase it. Two distinct defects:

1. **DDR-cold branch never used adaptive clocking.** `erase-nand.cfg`'s bootstrap-none path resumed the init-and-stop bootstrap at a fixed TCK, so OpenOCD lost JTAG sync when `lowlevel_clock_init` switched the master clock — `wait_halt` caught a garbage state (`ThumbEE`, `pc 0xfffffff9`, MMU on) → u-boot load aborted at `0x23f00000`. `load-ram.cfg` documents `adapter speed 0` (RTCK) as mandatory here; the DDR-cold branch was under-tested (only the DDR-up/marvin-running path had been exercised). Fixed by mirroring load-ram's `adapter speed 0` + `reg cpsr 0x000000d3` before the bootstrap resume.

2. **Double DDR init corrupts the MPDDRC (halfword shift).** Even with (1) fixed, u-boot loaded but never ran — total console silence. A DDR write/read probe showed a systematic **2-byte (one 16-bit halfword) shift**: `deadbeef 12345678` read back as `5678dead …`; u-boot's `ea0000b8 e59ff014` vectors read back as `f014ea00 …`, so the core fetched garbage as its first instruction. Root cause: the looping NAND bootstrap **already initializes DDR3L** (it must, to copy the app to `0x23f00000`), and `erase-nand.cfg`'s PC-based heuristic mis-classified it as "DDR cold" (the bootstrap is caught in **SRAM**, `pc 0x301068`) and ran bootstrap-none, whose DDR init re-trained an already-live MPDDRC → mistrained. **Verified**: probing DDR right after `reset init` *without* bootstrap-none read back clean (`deadbeef 12345678`). Fixed by replacing the PC test with a direct DDR round-trip probe — reuse existing DDR when the probe passes, run bootstrap-none only when it fails. Erase then succeeded (`nand erase.chip`, boot region all-`0xff`).

Also **hardened `program-nand.cfg` + `program-qspi.cfg`**: their guard only bailed on PC-in-DDR (marvin running), so a looping partial bootstrap (caught in SRAM, DDR up) would slip past and hit the same double-init. Added a DDR probe (only when caught in SRAM/ROM, to avoid poking a running marvin's MMU-mapped memory) so they now bail with "run ./erase-nand.sh first" in that case too. These staging tools still require **cold** DDR (they stage into low DDR a live capture DMA would clobber) — the fix is a better guard, not DDR reuse. Cold path verified; the looping-bootstrap detection is inferred from the erase-nand proof, not independently run (NAND is now erased). `load-ram.cfg` left unguarded (its contract is "no bootable medium").

Open follow-ups: (a) if `reset init` ever catches the NAND bootstrap *mid*-DDR-init, the probe reads shifted → falls through to bootstrap-none → double-init again; the real fix there is an MPDDRC reset before bootstrap-none (not built — no repro yet). (b) `program-nand.md:57` still says "JP3 (NAND-CS) may be either way for programming"; a manually-booted u-boot reported `no devices` with JP3 OUT, so that note is suspect — needs a clean JP3-OUT test before correcting.

**2026-07-01 — Small covers → PNG (fixes dashboard album-art noise: JPEG offscreen decode is renderer-clip-gated).** On hardware the dashboard thumbnail rendered as **noise** while the song-select large strip was fine. Root cause: the small tier was JPEG, the large tier PNG, and the two Legato decoders differ in their offscreen `render` path. **PNG** `_render` decodes to a shared buffer then `lePixelBufferCopy(dst, …)` — a plain color-converting copy, no clip dependency. **JPEG** `_render` blits block-by-block, each block gated by `leRectIntersects(blockRect, clipRect)` where `clipRect = leRenderer_GetClipRect()` = `_rendererState.drawRect` (`legato_imagedecoder_jpeg_common.c:955`). `Art_LoadAll` decodes at boot **outside any paint frame**, so `drawRect` is stale/empty → JPEG blocks are skipped → the slot keeps its uninitialized `.region_ram` = noise. This **corrects** the 2026-06-30 note that the decode has "no dependence on the screen clip rect (verified by reading the decoder)" — true for PNG, **false for JPEG**; large was only ever validated on PNG, small/JPEG was "dashboard pending" so the wrong assumption never surfaced. No supported hook sets the clip for an offscreen JPEG decode (`leProcessImage`, the MGS/Legato pre-decode entry point, calls the same clip-gated `leImage_Render`; the renderer API exposes only `GetClipRect`, no setter). **Fix:** emit the small tier as **PNG** too (`process_gh3_cover_art.py` `make_small` → `.png`), routing it through the proven clip-independent path; regenerated all 64. `parse_name` already accepts `.png`; the RGB565 slot work stands (PNG `lePixelBufferCopy` color-converts into 565). Heap is fine — `LE_VARIABLEHEAP_SIZE` 2 MB vs a 144² PNG's ~166 KB lodepng peak. Small covers on the card grow (PNG photo > JPEG), SD-only. **Confirmed on hardware — the dashboard thumbnail now renders clean.**

**2026-07-01 — Small album-art tier → RGB565 (matches its display layer).** The dashboard now-playing thumbnail is shown on the **RGB565 BASE** layer, so decoding the small tier into RGBA8888 was pointless — the final pixels are 565 regardless. A 565-vs-888 comparison on the real 144×144 covers confirmed the depth is visually indistinguishable (banding only shows on synthetic smooth gradients, which photographic covers aren't; the *large* strip's baked difficulty fade is the only gradient asset and keeps its own RGBA8888 OVR2 layer). `game/art.c` now decodes each tier into its **layer-matching** mode: **small = RGB565 (2 B/px)**, large = RGBA8888 (4 B/px), threaded through `load_tier`/`decode_one` as a `leColorMode`. Wins: (1) native 565→565 copy blit for the thumbnail via the standard draw path (2D engine), which correctly positions/clips within the full-screen dashboard canvas — **no `LE_IMAGE_DIRECT_BLIT`** for small (kept for large, whose alpha-mode source the 2D engine declines); (2) the RGBA8888 `alpha |= 0xFF` opaque-fixup is skipped for 565 (no alpha channel — and a decode leaving alpha=0 would have rendered the thumbnail fully transparent, a real "art not showing" hazard now gone); (3) small pool RAM halves, ~5.1 MB → ~2.5 MB in `.region_ram` (64 songs). Builds clean (XC32 v5.10). **Confirmed on hardware** (via the PNG small tier — see the entry above; the JPEG offscreen decode this entry assumed turned out to be clip-gated, so the small covers moved to PNG).

**2026-07-01 — Selection seam: song-select SELECT → dashboard SONG card (gameplay-engine groundwork).** First step toward the gameplay engine: a committed-selection state that both the dashboard and the future engine read. New `game/selection.{c,h}` holds `{setlist, index, difficulty, mode}` keyed on the recognizer's stable `(setlist,index)` (matches `Art_*`/`Catalog_*`); pure static state, single observer, `valid=false` until the first `Selection_Set` (static zero-init, no init-call/`app.c` wiring). **SELECT commits, X cancels** — `screen_song_select.c` now retains the highlighted list index (`s_sel_index`), and SELECT calls `songsel_commit()` → `Selection_Set(...)`; the X button just closes (no commit). Setup seeds a boot default by committing the first-song selection once (after `song_detail_show(0)`), so the card isn't a figma placeholder at boot. `screen_dashboard.c` registers a `Selection` observer (`dash_selection_changed`) that mirrors the selection onto the MGS SONG-card widgets via the same per-label `leFixedString` mechanism the dialog uses: `AlbumArt`←`Art_Small`, `SongTitle/Artist/Genre`, `SongAlbum`=`"album - year"` (card has no separate year label), `SongDuration`, `SongTier` (stars+color), `GAMEPLAY_GameMode` (`1P ROBOT`/`1P HUMAN`/`2P R vs H`, ASCII — no `•` in the DejaVu mono subset), and the `GAMEPLAY_Difficulty` pill (text + fill recolored via `SCHEME_BUTTON_EASY/MEDIUM/HARD/EXPERT`). Naming rule confirmed: MGS **dynamic** labels end camelCase (`_SongTitle`, `_GameMode`), **fixed** labels ALL-CAPS (`_GENRE`, `_MODE`) and are left alone. Shared tier/duration formatting factored out of `screen_song_select.c` into new `ui/song_detail.{c,h}` (`SongDetail_Tier`/`TierText`/`TierScheme`/`Duration`), so the dialog and dashboard use one palette. `Status` label left as MGS "READY" (gameplay-driven later). Observer ordering relies on `ui_manager` calling `ScreenDashboard_Setup` (registers observer) before `ScreenSongSelect_Setup` (seeds default). Builds clean (XC32 v5.10, all four sources). **Confirmed on hardware — SELECT→dashboard mapping (art, metadata, tier, mode, difficulty) working.**

**2026-07-01 — Dashboard redesign: dedicated SELECT SONG button; top-bar tap retired.** MGS regenerated the Marvin screen with a reworked dashboard: the old `PANEL_BASE_TOP`/`PANEL_BASE_BOTTOM`/`PANEL_SYSTEM_*`/`PANEL_ROBOT_CONTROLS` are gone, replaced by `PANEL_DASHBOARD_TOP`/`_BOTTOM` (+ `_TOP_LEFT`/`_TOP_RIGHT`), and the guitar strum buttons were dropped. Three code effects: (1) **Song select** now opens from a real `BUTTON_DASHBOARD_GAMEPLAY_SELECT_SONG` button (released-callback → `UiManager_OpenSongSelect`), replacing the old header-panel vtable-tap hack in `screen_dashboard.c`. (2) `screen_video.c`'s fullscreen input-gate retargeted from `BASE_TOP/BASE_BOTTOM` to `DASHBOARD_TOP/DASHBOARD_BOTTOM`. (3) This regen dropped **all** MGS auto-event wiring, so the hamburger (renamed `BUTTON_NAV_HAMBURGER`, now under `DASHBOARD_TOP_LEFT`) is wired explicitly in `ScreenNavigation_Setup` via `setPressedEventCallback`; the orphaned `event_Marvin_BUTTON_SYSYEM_NAVIGATION_OnPressed` was removed. The guitar strum glue in `manual_input.c` is kept as-is (orphaned but preserved) — the `ManualControl` actuator logic stays even though the UI no longer drives it, so the strum buttons can be reattached later. **Pending hardware test / build.**

**2026-07-01 — HEO video levels expansion (gamma CLUT).** The captured source is mildly range-compressed vs the panel's full 0..255 — corpus measurement (active area, borders excluded): black cluster ~5 (dead border pedestal ~13, cropped out), white ceiling ~233, nothing ≥235. The HEO CBHS limited-range black-level block is **YCbCr-only** (datasheet §6.2.6.7 / SAM9X7 DS), so it can't touch our RGB888 HEO input; instead we use the HEO **per-component gamma CLUT** (works on true RGB, `HEOCFG1.GAM`), loaded once with a linear levels-expansion curve (`out = clamp((in−5)·255/228)`, `VIDEO_LEVELS_BLACK=5`, `WHITE=233`). Display-only — the DDR capture is untouched so the detector/gameplay see raw pixels — and free at scanout. Loaded in `UiManager_Initialize` while GAM/CLUTEN are clear (required to write `LCDC_HEOCLUT[256]`); `XLCDC_SetLayerRGBColorMode` rewrites `HEOCFG1` with `GAM(0)` on every bind, so `heo_bind` re-asserts `GAM` after it. Fixed curve (no runtime knob for the *values*), but a **`gamma <on|off>` console command** toggles the whole expansion on/off (`UiManager_SetVideoLevels`, gates `GAM` in `heo_bind` + requests a rebind) for A/B eyeballing on the panel. **Code-complete, pending hardware test** — the ~5/233 pedestal/ceiling may differ per Wii/converter, retune `VIDEO_LEVELS_*` if needed. Earlier "blacks already at 0" was wrong — conflated the rare true-0 outliers with the ~5 cluster; corrected here.

**2026-07-01 — Rounded AA frame around the windowed video + nav→OVR2.** The windowed video (HEO, a hard rectangle) now gets a 1px rounded frame with anti-aliased corners, matching the dashboard panels (`0x404040` = `SCHEME_PANEL` `LE_SCHM_SHADOWDARK`, radius 6). Since the video is on HEO (not a canvas), the `PanelAA` backdrop-blend trick can't reach it — the frame is a **direct-driven ARGB_4444 overlay on OVR1, above HEO**, carrying true per-pixel alpha so the LCDC composites it over the video. Z-order is `OVR2>OVR1>HEO>BASE` (`VIDPRI(0)`), and the open nav drawer covers the video's left edge, so the frame must sit *below* nav → **nav moved OVR1→OVR2**, frame took OVR1. The GFX canvas framework has no 4444 mode (`gfxColorMode` only 5551/8888), so the overlay is driven directly via the XLCDC PLIB (splash/HEO-style), buffer filled once from a rounded-rect SDF: corner-cut opaque black (dashboard backdrop), 1px stroke, transparent interior; alpha AA on the stroke→video edge. New `ui_manager` verbs: `UiManager_VideoOverlayShow/Hide` (OVR1) and `UiManager_ShowNavLayer/HideNavLayer` (nav on OVR2 via `bind_canvas`, which pokes RGB565 each open since album-art leaves OVR2 in RGBA8888). Reveal-time OVR1 RGB565 poke dropped (OVR1 users now self-poke). Frame lifecycle = "windowed video visible": shown by `ScreenVideo_ShowWindowed`, hidden on fullscreen enter and song-select open. Layer map now: BASE=dash, HEO=video, OVR1=video-frame/song-select-dialog, OVR2=nav/album-art (frame⇎dialog and nav⇎album-art are time-exclusive; frame hidden whenever video is). Design in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §16. **Code-complete, pending hardware test.** Watch for CSI-2 capture stalls — the overlay adds an always-on ~41 MB/s OVR1 read while windowed. **Future:** this OVR1 overlay is also where a **per-frame gameplay HUD** (note-highway markers, hit/miss, detected-note boxes) would be drawn over the video — direct buffer raster, no Legato (canvas framework can't own it and per-frame Legato repaint is too heavy), must be fast/minimal-overhead (incremental redraw), and double-buffered with a vsync address flip to avoid tearing. HUD coords map to the *displayed* window, so source-derived positions apply the §15 active-area crop+scale. See `ui_compositor.md` §16.1.

**2026-07-01 — Runtime active-picture detection + HEO source-crop.** The component→HDMI (Wii) source frames its active raster with dead black bars whose thickness varies by console/converter, so the active area is **detected at runtime**, not hardcoded. Offline analysis of the 101-frame `docs/gh3_screens` corpus (720×480 captures): active area ≈ **716×448 @ (0,15)** (top 15 / bottom 17 / right 4 / left 0), and — key finding — the bars are **not** black=0; they sit at a ~13 pedestal, the *same* level as in-video black (so a `>0` test can't separate them). What separates cleanly is brightness: border max ≤ ~14, content ≥ ~145, so a **fixed luma threshold (max of B,G,R > 40)** splits them with wide margin and no pedestal dependence (validated: recovers the exact box on 99/101 frames, all 101 pass a ≥700×420 gate). Bonus: 716×448 = 1.598 ≈ the panel's 1.600, so cropping to the active area makes fullscreen essentially aspect-correct. Implementation: `video.c` detector (`Video_GetActiveRect`) scans inward from each edge (bounded to the deepest border the min-size gate allows, so a dark loading frame is cheap), unions the bright bounds over 16 *bright* frames (dark frames find no content and are skipped), then locks if ≥700×420 — one-shot per capture arm, reset on re-arm/source-size change, full-frame fallback until locked. The compositor (`ui_manager` `heo_bind`) consumes the rect as the **HEO source crop**: offset the frame base by `y*stride + x*bpp`, size `HEOCFG4` to the active w/h, skip cropped columns via `XSTRIDE = (src_w-active_w)*bpp` (matches the GFX driver's own `FB_TYPE_SZ*(resx-sizex)`); `heo_frame_latch` adds the same offset per ring slot; `heo_reconcile` rebinds when the rect changes (detection flipping full→cropped). Applies to both windowed and fullscreen. **Code-complete, pending hardware test.**

**2026-07-01 — Tap-to-fullscreen live video (`screen_video`).** Tap the live video on the dashboard to blow it up to fullscreen (HEO stretch-to-fill across 1280×800); tap anywhere to shrink back to the windowed 720×480 rect. New `screens/video/screen_video.{c,h}` module — a screen module *without a canvas* (the video is on the HEO hardware layer, compositor-owned), so it owns only the interaction: a `touchDownEvent` re-pointed onto `Marvin_PANEL_DASHBOARD` (the full-screen layer-0 catch-all under the IGNOREPICK `root0`). Relies on Legato's touch **bubbling** (`legato_input.c` walks `targetWidget->parent` until a widget calls `leWidgetEvent_Accept`) — plain panels never accept, so a video-region tap bubbles up to the dashboard panel; windowed→tap-in-rect enters fullscreen via `UiManager_VideoShow(0,0,BASE_W,BASE_H)`. To exit from anywhere, fullscreen gates picking off on `Marvin_PANEL_BASE_TOP` + `Marvin_PANEL_BASE_BOTTOM` (the two subtrees holding every interactive dashboard widget) by clearing `LE_WIDGET_ENABLED` (flag-only, no repaint — same gate as `ui_manager`'s `panel_set_pickable`), so every tap then resolves to the dashboard panel and exits. Windowed placement is now owned by `SCREEN_VIDEO_WIN_*` in `screen_video.h` (moved out of `ui_manager`'s `VIDEO_WIN_*`); `ui_manager` calls `ScreenVideo_ShowWindowed()` at reveal and on song-select close. `base_discard_reconcile` follows the video rect, so fullscreen discards BASE DMA across the whole panel (free bandwidth win). Design in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §14. **Pending hardware test.** (The windowed HEO rect (280,76) lines up with the `VIDEO_STREAM` placeholder panel — abs (280,77) once its `BASE_BOTTOM`→`BASE_CENTER` parent offsets are summed. Fullscreen can't be entered with the nav drawer open: nav is modal (`UiManager_SetDashboardPickable(false)`), so the dashboard — and the video tap — is un-pickable while it's open.)

**UI architecture — nav drawer complete; song/mode-select dialog is the next UI step.** The canvas compositor + per-screen-module model is built and validated on hardware: `ui_manager` orchestrates screens (string table + `screenInit/Show`; the MGS screen state machine is disabled, worked around with the `compat/le_gen_init.h` stub), the dashboard is its own MGS Screen on BASE, and the nav drawer is its own MGS Screen (`Navigation`) re-hosted onto OVR1 — they coexist live (no active-screen gate; `leUpdate`/`leInput` cover all attached layers). `LE_LAYER_COUNT`=3 is pinned via the never-shown `LayerBudget` screen. **Nav drawer is feature-complete:** slides in/out via canvas Move FX (`NAV_CLOSED_X` dodges the window-clip row-wrap; mid-slide reversal cancels the in-flight move), single-active highlight (Dashboard closes, others switch), clean close, rounded buttons with anti-aliased corners (`ui/widgets/button_aa/widget_button_aa.c`, per-instance vtable re-point — see `ui_compositor.md` §10a). Authoritative design + the window-clip/alignment gotchas in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md); decisions 2026-06-25. **Next (decided 2026-06-29): migrate to MGS's single-master-screen / layer-screen model** — one master Legato screen named `Marvin` with one layer per panel (dashboard, nav, song-select, dialogs…), replacing the current "separate 1-layer MGS Screen per panel, manually re-hosted, `LE_LAYER_COUNT` pinned by the fake `LayerBudget` screen" approach. MGS then derives `LE_LAYER_COUNT` and auto-associates each layer-screen with its canvas, so the `LayerBudget` hack, the `compat/le_gen_init.h` stub, and the disconnect/re-host dance all go away; `ui_manager` shrinks to buffer assignment + `show_layer`/`hide_layer` verbs. **Splash stays separate/manual** (pre-Legato RGBA8888) on a canvas slot outside the layer-screen range. Authoritative model + direction now in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §0. SongSelect (MGS screen already generated, committed `1e16ac9`) becomes a Marvin layer-screen rather than a standalone Screen. The earlier "parked surfaces aren't pickable / reveal-then-promote" framing is **superseded** (a state-machine artifact).

**Song-select operator UI — scrollable list widget built & validated on hardware.** Custom Legato widget `ui/song_list.{h,c}` (a hand-written `leWidget` subclass) renders the SELECT SONG setlist (tier badge + bold title + dim artist + right-aligned duration), driven by a decoupled row-provider callback (no catalog dependency); drag-scroll with time-based kinetic inertia, tap-to-select. Bring-up via the `songlist` console command + a catalog-backed demo provider (`ui/song_list_demo.{c,h}`) — scroll/select/opaque-fill confirmed on hardware. **Bringing it up surfaced and fixed a real Legato/GFX2D cache-coherency bug** (see decision log 2026-06-24): the CPU-rendered scratch buffer was cacheable, so the 2D-engine blit leaked stale pixels through opaque widgets — fixed by placing the scratch in non-cached DDR. **Next:** author the full SELECT SONG screen in MGS (album art + catalog-fed metadata panel + difficulty/mode/Select), and the **tier / career-setlist data model** (the `tier` badge needs the deferred catalog tier column). Two upstream Microchip Legato/gfx bugs to file (decision log).

**Song catalog loader — built and committed; exercised on hardware via the `catalog`/`songlist` console commands.** New `game/catalog.{h,c}` maps the recognizer's `(setlist, index)` → human labels (title/artist/bpm/length) from `<card>/games/gh3-wii/songs.csv`, so the UI/high-score display shows names instead of `#4`. Flat **CSV** (decided over the spec's JSON for the same reasons `results` is CSV — no on-device tokenizer, `FF_FS_MAX_FILES=1`, static buffers, pandas/Excel-editable). The two CSV helpers were extracted out of `results.c` into a shared `util/csv.{h,c}` and the catalog reuses them. Lazy-loads once into a fixed `[GP_N_SONGS]` static cache (file closed after load), so lookups are O(n) in RAM and don't fight `results.csv` for the single FatFs handle. `catalog` console command (`reload` / `ls` / `<main|bonus> <index>`) validates the path against a checked-in seed catalog (`data/games/gh3-wii/songs.csv`). Columns: `setlist,index,title,artist,album,bpm,length_s,year,genre,difficulty`. `title`/`artist`/`album` are real, sourced from the `SONGS` table in `tools/fetch_gh3_cover_art.py` (incl. proper UTF-8 — *Héroes del Silencio*, *Mauvais Garçon*); that script gained a `--catalog` mode that also fills `year`/`genre` from MusicBrainz on a networked run. `bpm`/`length_s`/`difficulty` are reserved-but-blank (GH3 surfaces no per-song difficulty; the authentic signal is the career tier, deferred). Catalog is labels only: a missing/stale card degrades to "Unknown song" and never touches recognition (compile-time in flash). **Deferred:** the stale-catalog version warning — `export_c.py` doesn't emit `GP_METADATA_VERSION` yet (open item below). **Next SD consumer:** album-artwork pre-cache (spec §4.8.7) — directory structure is now locked (`art/{small,large}/<setlist>-<NN>.{jpg,png}`, one size-tier subdir per DDR cache). **Built 2026-06-30** (`game/art.{c,h}`, decision log): all covers decoded once into static RGB888 DDR caches during the splash, `(setlist,index)`→`leImage*` lookup, song-select strip wired; large tier is a 508×208 strip with a difficulty-colored fade baked offline. Dashboard small-thumbnail consumer still pending (no MGS slot yet).

**T1S inter-node link — built and working (marvin ↔ guitar).** marvin is the PLCA **coordinator** (node 0) on a 10BASE-T1S bus (LAN8651 over FLEXCOM4 SPI); hand-authored stack in `default/src/net/t1s/`. It sends the actuator command over T1S to the `guitar` follower node (id 3), which actuates the Wii GPIOs (guitar G1+G2 proven). Followers announce presence via a heartbeat (ethertype `0x88B6`); the `t1s` and `nodes` console commands report link/credits/per-node presence. Transport is behind `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT={UART,T1S}` (T1S default on the `t1s` branch). The `fretboard` node (id 4) firmware is now **written, T1S-only** (fretboard journal 2026-06-17): it streams its 17-byte frame to marvin **and** drives the `guitar` node directly — its on-device model infers the bitmask and sends it peer-to-peer to `02:..:03`. marvin's RX side needed no changes — `fretboard_link.c`'s T1S frame handler already parses the frame from node id 4 (`DETECTOR_ADC_FRETBOARD`) and the node-table + heartbeat-presence demux already cover it. **Next (marvin side):** **active-source arbitration** — both marvin (timing pipeline) and the fretboard now address the guitar (`02:..:03`), so marvin needs active-detector/active-guitar selection to ensure exactly one source drives at a time (the fretboard's SW0 is an interim manual arm; until then, don't run marvin's timing pipeline to the guitar while the fretboard is armed). Then fretboard hardware bring-up. PoDL is design-direction only. Detail in [`../../../docs/t1s-podl-link.md`](../../../docs/t1s-podl-link.md); decision log entry 2026-06-17.

**Board port: SAM9X75 Curiosity → Curiosity Hybrid (in progress).** USB Host is gone on the Hybrid; the fretboard link was rewired from USB CDC host to a direct FLEXCOM2 USART on 2026-06-08 (see decision log + session log). Code-complete and syntax-checked; pending a full MPLAB build and on-hardware bring-up. Display/MIPI I²C (incl. TC358743 control) also moved off FLEXCOM6/PA24-PA25 to FLEXCOM8 TWI/PB4-PB5 during the port — `spec.md` §3.1/§3.2 reconciled on 2026-06-08. Dated 2026-05-01/05-20 journal entries and `journal-archive.md` keep their FLEXCOM6 references as historical record (true on the original Curiosity board).

**M1 — Reference detector v0.** First CV detector running on captured frames, publishing `detector_state_t` records onto the detector-state bus. No actuation yet. See [`spec.md`](spec.md) §4.2 for subsystem detail and §8 for the milestone progression.

Scaffold is in place as of 2026-05-20: `detector/detector.{h,c}` owns the bus queue + a temporary drain task that logs records-per-second; `detector/cv_marvin_v1.{h,c}` subscribes to the video frame queue and publishes one `detector_state_t` per frame with `frame_epoch = Video_FrameInfo.frame_count` and all-zero fret state. Next: pick the v0 algorithm (Q8) and start populating `fret[]`.

**Capture/display pipeline is done as of 2026-05-02:** source → TC358743 → CSI-2 @ 972 Mbps/lane → SAM9X75 → ISC DMA → DDR (BGRX32) → XLCDC OVR1 → LVDSC → 10.1″ 1280×800 LVDS panel.

> **Deeper-dive references:**
> - [`spec.md`](spec.md) — system-level spec (read first).
> - [`capture_pipeline.md`](capture_pipeline.md) — HDMI → DDR capture stage (CSI2DC, ISC, register settings, datasheet citations).
> - [`display_path.md`](display_path.md) — DDR → LCD display stage (XLCDC OVR1 wiring, pillarbox, cache coherence).

> **Earlier entries** (capture/display bring-up, 2026-05-01 → 2026-05-15) are preserved in [`journal-archive.md`](journal-archive.md).

---

## Decision log

| Date | Decision | Rationale |
|------|----------|-----------|
| 2026-08-08 | **`LOG_*` from an exception context is enforced, not just documented: it writes the format string raw and reports the caller, instead of touching the mutex.** `log_emit` checks the CPSR mode once the scheduler is running — the port runs tasks in System mode and branches every C interrupt handler to Supervisor mode (`portASM.S` `FreeRTOS_IRQ_Handler`: `MSR CPSR_c, #SVC_MODE`), so any other mode is an exception context. On that path: no lock, no `vprintf`, no sink; polled-DBGU raw write, a one-shot `*** LOG FROM ISR site=0x… ***` marker, and `log_from_isr_count()`. The check is deliberately *inside* the scheduler-running branch, because `cstartup.S` enters `main()` in Supervisor mode and all pre-scheduler logging would otherwise be misread as an ISR. | The ISR path is what actually froze the board (see the same day's session log): in an ISR `xTaskGetCurrentTaskHandle()` returns the **interrupted** task, so `xSemaphoreTake` blocks *that* task on the log mutex, and if it already held the mutex nothing can ever break the deadlock. A comment saying "do NOT call from an ISR" had been in `log.h` from the start and was still violated exactly once — so the contract needed teeth, not better wording. Raw-writing rather than dropping keeps the message and its ordering, which is what a diagnostic is for; skipping the sink matters because `log_set_sink`'s callback runs on the caller and is no more ISR-safe than `vprintf`. Three instructions (`mrs`/`and`/`cmp`) on the hot path, and ARM926 has no `xPortIsInsideInterrupt` to use instead. |
| 2026-08-07 | **Titlebar logos shrank (`LOGO_PIC` 121×43 → 115×41, `LOGO_MICROCHIP` 194×45 → 177×41) and every image widget's rect is now exactly its image's size, so position *is* image position. Anchoring rule for the bar: the left group preserves left edges, the right group preserves right edges, and the status dot preserves its 20 px gap to the Microchip logo (`x` 1020 → 1037).** `PIC` → `(294,3) 115×41`, `MICROCHIP` → `(1069,3) 177×41`; both logos now share `y = 3` and a 41 px band with `LOGO_GUITAR`'s vertical centre at 23. | Greg found the two logos visually heavy and rescaled them (~95% and ~91%, uniform, and both PNGs are ink-tight — no transparent padding, so the image edge *is* the visual edge). The vertical half of the ask needed no arithmetic: `_leImageWidget_Constructor` never touches alignment, so the base widget's `LE_HALIGN_CENTER`/`LE_VALIGN_MIDDLE` defaults stand and `leUtils_ArrangeRectangle` re-centres the smaller image inside the old rect for free — which lands the requested down-1 and down-2 exactly, because the formula is `bounds/2 − sub/2` with **independent integer division** (44/2 − 43/2 = 1 → 44/2 − 41/2 = 2; 45/2 − 45/2 = 0 → 45/2 − 41/2 = 2). Shrinking the rects to match was still the right move: it makes the geometry in the source say what is actually on screen instead of leaving the true position implicit in centering slack, and it matches `LOGO_GUITAR`, whose 225×45 rect already equalled its image. It is also free of fill risk — a rect equal to an opaque image (the assets are RGB flattened onto black via `rawconfig.backgroundColor`) fully overdraws its own background, so `PIC`'s `SCHEME_BACKGROUND` FILL and `MICROCHIP`'s `BACKGROUND_NONE` become equivalent. Horizontal anchoring was the only real decision, since 17 px had to come from somewhere: right-edge for `MICROCHIP` keeps the bar's visible 10 px right gutter (centre-preserving would have grown it to 19), and the dot follows so it does not read as orphaned in a 37 px gap. |
| 2026-08-07 | **The log lock is recursive *and* bounded (250 ms), so logging can no longer deadlock the system.** `log.c` took a plain mutex with `portMAX_DELAY` and held it across the whole `vprintf → write → DBGU_Write` path. Now: `xSemaphoreCreateRecursiveMutexStatic` + `xSemaphoreTakeRecursive(…, LOG_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS)`; a nested `LOG_*` is survivable and *reported* to DBGU with the offending return address (`log_nested_count`); a line that times out is written **raw** — format string, no argument substitution — through the polled DBGU (`log_lock_timeout_count`). `configUSE_RECURSIVE_MUTEXES` is set in the MCC FreeRTOS component, so it is **not** a re-apply patch. | An unbounded wait on a shared diagnostic lock converts *any* stall in one task into a dead board: every logger queues behind the holder, all tasks end up blocked, and the CPU sits in idle with the panel frozen on the last frame the LCDC scanned. That is strictly worse than losing log lines, and it destroys the evidence you need — the log stops exactly when it becomes interesting. The timeout path deliberately does **not** call `vprintf`: `configUSE_NEWLIB_REENTRANT` is 0, so all tasks share one `_impure_ptr`, and the wedged holder is typically parked *inside* `vprintf` — calling it concurrently would trade a freeze for libc corruption, a worse failure mode. Writing `fmt` raw keeps the message identity and its ordering, which is what a diagnostic actually needs, and costs only the argument values. Recursion is reported rather than silently tolerated because a nested `LOG_*` is a defect in the caller even when it no longer kills the board. |
| 2026-08-07 | **System Info's QR codes are encoded on the panel from URLs in the `NODE` table, not pre-rendered into assets.** Vendored Nayuki qrcodegen (MIT, copy not submodule) + a Legato-free rasterizer pinned to QR version 4, cached one tile per distinct URL in DDR. `product` is replaced by `qr_url`; all eight cards carry a QR (six silicon product pages, LAN8651 for fauxmote, the GitHub repo for the project card). | A QR is a pure function of its URL, so the URL *is* the asset and belongs beside the part number in the table that already owns this screen's content. Pre-rendering only five images would still have bought a whole asset pipeline — MGS Generate, or a tool plus SD files plus a size contract — and the same morning's `process_node_photos.py` bug (a tool list that didn't mirror the firmware table, failing silently) is the argument against: a stale QR tile still looks like a valid QR. Cost is ~12 KB flash and 430 KB of DDR slots against 2 Gbit, and encoding needs no card mount, no decoder and no boot-progress step. Pinning the version min == max is what makes an over-long URL fail loudly instead of silently producing a smaller code in a fixed-size slot, and the layout is derived from the rasterizer's size rather than a QR being resampled to fit a box. |
| 2026-08-06 | **System Info carries an eighth card for the project itself (`guitar-pic`), which is a card and a detail page but *not* a node: no PLCA id, no rail row, no QR.** The grid goes 4+4 and a new `SCHEME_NODE_PROJECT` (Microchip red `#E4002B`) joins the design. | The screen's job is to sell the system, and the seven node cards only ever described parts of it — a visitor had nowhere to learn what the whole thing *is*. Modelling it as a NODE-table entry rather than a special case buys the card the whole grammar for free (tap target, detail page, prose, parts list) at the cost of three narrow special-cases: `NODE_BUS_N` vs `NODE_N` for the rail, a `product` flag for the QR slot (previously keyed off `part != NULL`, which would have promised a product page), and a photo key outside the T1S id space. A new scheme was unavoidable rather than lazy: a node accent needs `base`, `foreground` and `text` all carrying the colour, and no scheme in the design but the seven `SCHEME_NODE_*` is shaped that way — the spare `SCHEME_TEXT_*` fill grey, which would have put a grey edge and grey bullets on the card. Red also keeps the card legible as the *parent* rather than an eighth peer, since all seven node accents are pastels. |
| 2026-08-06 | **The `NODE[]` table in `screen_system.c` is the sole source of System Info's content; `docs/screens/*.md` is deleted. Supersedes the "markdown is the source of truth" half of the C-string-literals entry below.** | The markdown was a transcription target that had already drifted from the hardware it described — wrong part numbers on three nodes (`PIC32CM6408` / `PIC32CM PL10` where the silicon is `PIC32CM6408PL10048`), a part beatbox doesn't run, MPLAB X IDE on every card when everything is built in VS Code with the MPLAB extensions, and MCC Melody where the PIC32 nodes use MCC Harmony. A second copy of ~70 strings only helps if someone reconciles it, and nobody was: the content is now checked against the specs, the MCC manifests and the Microchip product API, and the table is the only place it lives. The C-string-literal decision itself stands — the wrap is still baked into the data (now a 57-column box, since the prose font went 16 → 18) — and the budget script in the verification notes replaces the markdown as the thing that catches an over-long line. |
| 2026-08-06 | **Two rules for anything drawn straight into a live scanout buffer, now in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §12.1: (1) never erase-then-draw — compose off-screen in CACHED memory and blit once; (2) count write TRANSACTIONS, not bytes, because `.region_nocache` is strongly-ordered.** Applied to the splash bar: `draw_field` composites art + glyphs in scratch and pushes one strip; `draw_fill` is incremental (~48 px/tick, was ~7200). | Both rules were paid for in hardware glitches the same day the bar landed. **(1)** Erase + draw as two passes over rows the beam is crossing means a caught frame shows the *intermediate* state — for text, a blank. At ~5% exposure per redraw × ~240 redraws that was ~10 blank frames a boot. Composing first makes every pixel go old-final → new-final, and since writes and the beam both run top-to-bottom the worst artifact is old-above/new-below, i.e. nothing for a digit change. **(2)** The whole-fill redraw was only 720 KB/s — *nothing* next to the LCDC's own ~360 MB/s — yet it tipped the controller into occasional underrun, because strongly-ordered writes are neither buffered nor coalesced: 180k separate serialized DDR transactions per second, arriving exactly when the 2D engine was blitting every canvas. Bytes were the wrong unit. Greg's observation that the whole-screen artifact only appeared "towards the end of the load" is what located it — that is the `RENDERING SCREENS` window and nothing else about the boot explains the timing. Kept in reserve rather than applied: showing BASE only at the reveal (−117 MB/s through the paint window, since the dashboard layer is invisible under the opaque splash but still being read) and a dithered RGB565 splash blob (−123 MB/s, −2 MB QSPI, −2 MB nocache; per-pixel alpha is unused because the fade rides the layer's global A0). |
