| 2026-08-06 | **The splash bar's milestones are derived from a per-stage boot profile stored in settings, not hand-apportioned — `settings_t.boot_ms` → `boot_stage_ms[4]`, record v2 → v3. The bar's last stage is treated as slack and deliberately excluded from the profile.** Marks are `cum_ms[i] × 990 / total`; within a stage the bar interpolates on `elapsed_in_stage / predicted_stage_ms`. The five permille constants demote to a compiled seed `{400, 3000, 700, 700}` used only until the first boot measures. `SetStage`/`Calibrate` log the measured-vs-predicted table. | Greg, after the first hardware run: *"how do we ground the various milestones?"* They weren't grounded — they were my guesses, and unverifiable, because `log.c` timestamps nothing. Storing per-stage durations rather than one total buys the thing a total cannot: **the bar's speed varies per stage**. Under a single `boot_ms` the bar crossed the 1–3 s artwork decode at the same rate as the 400 ms service spawn, so the marks had to be guessed *and* the motion inside each stage was wrong. **Excluding the slack stage is the load-bearing detail:** `READY` waits out `SPLASH_MIN_MS`, so its duration is anti-correlated with the work — store it as a cost and speeding the work up grows the idle wait, which trains the next boot to crawl through the stages that do something. Deriving it as `max(Σwork, min_hold) − Σwork` also removes the need to store the total, since that *is* the old `boot_ms`. Rejected level 0 alone (log the times, hardcode the marks from a few boots): grounded but frozen against one configuration — pull the SD card or change the cover count and it is guesswork again. Deferred level 2 (a per-cover callback through `GameArt_LoadAll`/`NodeArt_LoadAll` so the 450‰ artwork stage reports real fractional progress instead of interpolating) — it is the only remaining blind interpolation of any size, but it reaches outside the splash module. Version bumped twice in one session, which is free here: v2 never left Greg's board, so the cost is one more one-time backlight reset. |
| 2026-08-06 | **Boot splash gets a self-calibrating progress bar, drawn into the splash framebuffer with Legato's font *assets* but not its renderer; reveal becomes a 300 ms OVR1 alpha cross-dissolve; `settings_t` grows `boot_ms` and the record goes v1 → v2 (one-time reset of the saved backlight).** New `ui/gfx/glyph_blit.c` (ASCII 8bpp glyph → raw RGBA8888) + `ui/screens/splash/splash_progress.c` (stage table, 25 Hz priority-3 ticker task, calibration); `screen_splash` exposes its framebuffer and gains `ScreenSplash_FadeOut`. Design in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §12.1. | Greg asked for the mockup's loading bar and raised the two real questions himself: whether anything of Legato is usable pre-stack, and whether the bar could time itself by *"timing the boot and storing in settings."* **Answer to the first: the data layer, yes; the renderer, no.** `leFont_GetGlyphInfo` is a pure `const`-table lookup and an 8bpp glyph's coverage bitmap is directly addressable at `header.address + dataOffset`, so real `DejaVuSansMono_12` text needs no new font asset and no MGS change; `leFont_DrawGlyph` and the string renderer go through `leRenderer_BlendPixel`, which requires an active paint pass — the same wall that produced `AaCorners_RenderSurface565`. Drawing on a Legato overlay instead was rejected on **timing**, not feasibility: a layer-screen cannot paint until `init_screens()`, two thirds into boot, *after* the album-art decode — precisely the phase the bar exists for. **Answer to the second: calibrated time bounded by milestone floors/ceilings**, not either alone — pure milestones would sit still for the 1–3 s art decode, and pure time drifts into a pinned 99% or a jump. Clamping the time-driven value between the current stage's floor and the next one's makes it smooth, monotone, self-correcting, and honest, off a single stored number. The measured span deliberately *includes* the `SPLASH_MIN_MS` hold and excludes the 100% dwell + fade, so the stored figure is exactly what the next boot animates against and cannot drift upward each boot. `boot_ms` as a new field (Greg's call over reusing the spare `reserved0` byte as deciseconds): full ms resolution and room to grow, at the cost of moving the record's crc offset so every v1 record fails validation once and backlight returns to the 50% default. The fade is free — OVR1's blender already scales the source by A0 and the dashboard is fully painted underneath — but it must restore A0 to 255, or the video frame overlay inherits a transparent layer. |
| 2026-08-06 | **System Info is one canvas (layer 7) holding both views, switched by a single `show_view()` seam — not one pre-rendered canvas per view.** Canvas pool stays at 8 and `ddram.ld` is untouched. Greg asked how hard a later move to pre-rendered layers would be, given a worry about draw time. | Cross-screen switching is *already* pre-rendered-layer swapping — `paint_all_screens_once`'s own comment says Legato paints a canvas surface regardless of whether it is shown or bound, so "showing a screen is a pure layer bind with no repaint", and `bind_canvas` does no drawing. So the drawer→System Info transition costs nothing before this change and nothing after. The only repaint in question is overview↔detail, and `screen_bus.c` already invalidates a whole 1280×800 panel with ~200 labels **at 1 Hz continuously** and ships — a single repaint on a discrete tap is strictly cheaper, with no animation to stutter. Layer 7 also fits the existing pool of 8 exactly, so Phase A costs no config change at all, whereas a second canvas needs +1.95 MB of `ram_nocache` against 3.11 MB free (i.e. it forces the `ddram.ld` split to move) plus a `CONFIG_CANVAS_NUM_OBJ` bump and an extra `TITLEBAR_MAX` slot. Deferring it is therefore cheaper *and* reversible: the escalation is a rewrite of `show_view` plus the container parents, with the node table, layout constants, label pool and both builders untouched. **Reopen if** the measured transition exceeds ~150 ms; Phase B additionally buys painting the detail surface while hidden (no partial frame) and a free "back". |
| 2026-08-06 | **`CONFIG_CANVAS_NUM_OBJ` and `LE_LAYER_COUNT` recorded as non-constraints; `ram_nocache` is the real one.** | Asked directly ("can't we just increase the pool size?"). The pool only sizes a static `GFXC_CANVAS canvas[]` (`gfx_canvas.c:67`) whose unused slots `GFXC_Initialize` marks `CANVAS_ID_INVALID`/`GFXC_FX_IDLE` — inert, ~150 B BSS each — and raising it is an MCC regen and nothing more. `LE_LAYER_COUNT` is MGS-derived from the layer count, and `leState.layerList` is a dynamic `leList`, not a fixed array. What actually binds is (a) the 4 XLCDC hardware layers, which cap *simultaneously visible* canvases at 3 non-HEO and are irrelevant to how many screens exist, and (b) `ram_nocache`: 28.89 MB used of 32, so **one** more full-screen RGB565 surface (1.95 MB) fits. Worth writing down because the pool number looks like the ceiling and isn't. Reclaim target if ever needed: the splash's 4 MB RGBA8888 buffer. |
| 2026-08-06 | **System Info's content is C string literals — hand-wrapped ASCII line arrays in `screen_system.c` — not design strings driven by `leTableString`, which is the house default and the MGS skill's stated preference.** | Recorded as a deliberate exception, per the skill's own carve-out for a view that will never be translated. Two reasons specific to this screen: the wrap is **baked into the data** (one string per rendered line, sized to a 62-column box), so a translation would need re-wrapping anyway and the strings would not survive as strings; and it is ~60 strings of English marketing prose transcribed from `docs/screens/`, where the markdown is the source of truth and a design-table copy would be a second one to keep in sync. The glyph hazard the skill warns about does not apply here: the prose is pure ASCII (verified by script over the table), and the three non-ASCII glyphs used as ornaments (`«` `»` `·`) are inside the fonts' **declared** 160–255 ranges, so they are properties of the fonts rather than on loan from another caption. **Reopen if** marvin ever gains a second language, in which case this screen is the one that has to be rebuilt around a wrapping helper, not just retranslated. |
| 2026-08-06 | **Board photos live on the SD card as 288×620 PNGs decoded into fixed `.region_ram` slots (`game/node_art.c`), not baked into the design zip. PNG, not JPEG.** | Reuses `game_art.c`'s proven shape, including its never-fail-boot contract (a missing/wrong-size/corrupt file leaves the slot empty and the frame reads blank). Photos on the card mean a swap needs no rebuild, no MGS Generate, and no growth in `.text` — which matters because Greg is supplying real photos and will iterate on them. PNG is not a preference: `process_gh3_cover_art.py` records that Legato's JPEG decoder gates its block writes on the renderer clip rect, stale during an offscreen boot decode, so a runtime JPEG decodes to noise — which is why both album-art tiers are PNG too. **Accepted debt:** the PNG-header parse + decode-into-slot logic is now duplicated between `game_art.c` and `node_art.c`; a shared helper was not extracted here because it would mean refactoring a working album-art path for a screen that has not been on hardware yet. Extract it if a third consumer appears. |
| 2026-08-06 | **QSPI map remapped so nothing a host tool writes touches the SST26's non-uniform Block-Erase bands. Splash `0x000000` → `0x010000` (reservation `0x400000` → `0x3F0000`); assets keeps base `0x400000` but its top comes down `0x7FC000` → `0x7F0000` (`0x3FC000` → `0x3F0000`); the bottom 64 KiB and `0x7F0000–0x7FBFFF` are now named reserved bands; `[0x010000, 0x7F0000)` is declared as the host window (`QSPI_HOST_WINDOW_*`) and `program-qspi.sh` refuses to leave it or to take an offset that isn't 64 KiB-aligned. Settings unchanged at `0x7FC000` / 16 KiB. Supersedes the 2026-06-28 "partition map is locked" row.** | Reflashing the regenerated splash failed verify at offset `0x2001`: flash read `0x78` where the staged blob has `0x79` — a bit that went 1→0, which a page-program **cannot** do, so that region had never been erased. Cause: `SST26VF064B` Block-Erase (`D8h`) covers 8, 32 or 64 KiB **depending on address** — four 8 KiB blocks + one 32 KiB at each end of the array, 64 KiB only in the middle (DS20005119K §3.0) — while u-boot's spi-nor uses a flat 64 KiB `erase_size`. So `sf erase 0 0x3f0000` issued `D8h` at `0x0`, erased just `0x0–0x1FFF`, jumped to `0x10000`, and reported `Erased: OK`; `0x2000–0xFFFF` stayed programmed for the write to AND into (37,717 of 57,344 bytes altered, mean delta 24.6/255 — the visible band at rows 1.6–12.8 of 800). It hid until now because the first-ever splash write went onto a blank part, and assets never tripped it since `0x400000` is mid-array. Two alternatives were rejected: **chip-erase-first** (`sf erase 0 0x800000` does take u-boot's `C7h` whole-chip path, but it wipes assets and settings on every splash update — kept only as a documented recovery hatch), and **rebuilding the flasher u-boot with `CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_USE_4K_SECTORS`** (uniform `20h` would be correct everywhere, but it needs a u-boot clone + build, and whether the `sst26vf064b` ID entry even carries `SECT_4K` in that tree is unverified). Remapping needs no new u-boot and makes the normal `sf erase 0x10000 0x3f0000` correct by construction. Cost: 48 KiB off assets (read side still unbuilt) and splash slack 96 → 32 KiB against a fixed 4,096,000 B blob — the reservation now equals its own 64 KiB-rounded erase footprint, so the erase ends exactly at `0x400000` and cannot spill into assets. Incidental win: offset 0 is blank rather than pixel data, so there is nothing header-shaped for RomBOOT to inspect with JP4 IN. |
| 2026-08-06 | **`widget_sparkline` joins the vector path after all — the run chart's polyline is now AA `leDraw_VectorLine` segments, and the "most repainted widget" reason for skipping it is retracted as factually wrong.** Greg asked, then *"we have gobs of memory available. do it."* | The stated reason was that swapping N cheap `VertLine` fills for N supersampled OBB scans is the one place the vector API is plausibly slower, on the most frequently repainted widget. The second half is simply false: `screen_bus.c`'s `refresh_all()` runs behind `vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(1000))` — **1 Hz**, the slowest repaint in the UI, and only while the bus view is shown. The tilt and whammy repaint per touch move. So the cost argument evaporates, and what is left is that the run chart had **no antialiasing at all**: it emitted one `leRenderer_VertLine` per pixel column at alpha 255, so a steep segment was a staircase of butted vertical bars. 39 segments per paint at 1 Hz is nothing. The flash cost (`legato_vector_line.c.o`, currently gc-sectioned out — `arc_stroke` is 4,904 B for scale) is what Greg's "gobs of memory" dismisses. |
| 2026-08-06 | **The vector-rasterizer conversion is accepted on looks alone — the `marvin-perf` before/after on tilt and gauge is dropped, unmeasured.** So the item's founding premise (fixed-point-with-8-samples beats soft-float-with-1-sample) stays **unproven**, and `UI_VEC_AA = 8X` is pinned by default rather than by measurement. Greg: *"gauge is fine… not worried about this. working fine."* | The measurement is unusually expensive for what it would tell us. There is **no paint instrumentation anywhere under `src/ui/`** — the only existing instrument is `PERF_TASK_LEGATO`'s `run_time_counter`, which aggregates every widget paint plus event dispatch and damage bookkeeping, so it can only answer "Legato while dragging tilt", not "tilt's paint". And the *before* side no longer exists in the tree: `22832d9` deleted the float paint paths, so an A/B means flashing `22832d9^`, capturing, then reflashing HEAD — two flashes and four captures for a coarse number. A precise number would need new per-paint stamps, a schema bump and a lockstep host change, added to *both* builds. Against that: the panel looks right and drag lag was already fixed by the damage-rect work, so there is no symptom to chase. **Reopen if** a repaint cost ever shows up (drag lag returns, Legato CPU% climbs, or a new widget joins the vector path) — and reach for the one-line `UI_VEC_AA` 8X→4X knob in `ui/gfx/vec_draw.h` before building any instrumentation. |
| 2026-08-06 | **Tier ramp → the mockup's five anchors reordered into a monotone cool→hot hue walk, one shade family: `blue-400 · cyan-400 · green-400 · lime-400 · yellow-400 · orange-400 · red-400 · red-600`.** Replaces the green/amber/orange/red 300-500 band ramp. Tier 8 is `red-600 #DC2626` at **3.67:1** on zinc-900 — knowingly below the 4.5 floor the old ramp held, and below WCAG's large-text 3:1 allowance too (12px bold does not qualify). Bonus/unknown stays `SCHEME_TEXT_ZINC_300`. | The band ramp's defect was structural, not arithmetic: it encoded one ordinal variable with *both* hue and lightness and the two disagreed, so L\* sawtoothed `- + - + - + -` and climbing a tier alternately brightened and dimmed. Unfixable by re-pairing — monotone lightness under two-shade banding needs band N+1's light shade darker than band N's dark shade, never true for Tailwind 300/500 across green→red. So banding had to go. The five anchors are Greg's (they are the mockup's, preserved in `TIER_PALETTE`); the three fillers (cyan, lime, red-600) were chosen **on** the hue path rather than beside it, which is what repairs the two defects the 2026-08-05 entry named — no violet→yellow reversal, and T7→T8 goes ΔE2000 10.6 → 18.2. Min adjacent ΔE2000 **16.2 vs the old ramp's 10.2**. Keeping every stop in the 400 family means hue alone carries the order. **Accepted cost:** yellow is the lightest hue sRGB has and it sits mid-ramp, so L\* *arches* (67 → 84 at yellow → 48 at red) instead of stepping monotonically — one hump under a monotone hue, not four teeth. Forcing monotone lightness was measured and rejected because it turns the mid-ramp yellows to gold/olive (`#84CC16`, `#F59E0B`) and pales the cool end. Tier 8's sub-AA contrast was chosen deliberately over the alternatives: `rose-500` holds 4.83:1 but collapses T7→T8 to ΔE 9.4, as bad as the ramp we are replacing. |
| 2026-08-06 | **Correction: the old tier ramp's recorded "every adjacent pair ΔE ≥ 27" was measured with CIE76, not ΔE2000, and RGB565 was never the problem.** Re-measured: min adjacent **ΔE76 = 27.4** (so the original number was right on its own metric), min **ΔE2000 = 10.2** (amber-500 → orange-300; green-300 → green-500 is 13.5), and quantizing to the dialog's RGB565 moves it only to 10.1. | CIE76 overstates separation for high-chroma colours, which is exactly the region an amber/orange/red ramp lives in — so the constraint passed while the ramp still read muddy, and the arithmetic-was-fine framing of follow-up 2 was wrong. Worth recording as a rule: **judge palette separation with ΔE2000**, and measure it after 565 quantization even though 565 turned out to be innocent here. |
| 2026-08-06 | **The whammy's fill capsule keeps its `+ hh` padding — the fill's rounded ends deliberately land half a widget-height (32 px) outside the span from centre to thumb.** So at 25% deflection the thumb centre sits at `cx + 32` while the fill runs `cx − 32` to `cx + 64`, and at ±1 a 65-px lozenge appears at once instead of growing from zero. Raised as a defect during the drag-lag work and rejected. | The padding is what gives the fill an end radius matching the track's own instead of a stubby cap, and on the panel the thumb riding inside a swept bar reads well (Greg: *"whammy looks good. stick with what we have."*). The alternative reading — fill ends at the thumb — was inferred from a mockup not in the repo, not from anything the widget gets wrong. Recorded because it is the sort of geometry a future reader will re-flag: it is a choice, not an oversight. It does cost perf: ending the fill at the thumb would shrink the drag damage band from 70 px to ~20 and roughly triple the whammy's repaint saving, which we are knowingly not taking. |
| 2026-08-03 | **Send marvin's CV command to the fretboard as an atomic teacher label (opcode `0x04` `T1S_DET_CTRL_TEACHER`); parse the fretboard's new `commanded_mask`; perf-log schema v5→v6.** `net/t1s/t1s_link.h`: `T1S_DET_CTRL_TEACHER (4u)` + `OP_COUNT` 3→4 (staging/flush already generic). `actuator/fretboard_link.c`: `DS_FRAME_LEN` 17→18; `emit_fretboard_frame` reads `frame[16]` as `commanded_mask` and passes it to `PerfLog_EmitFretboardRaw`; `FretboardLink_Send` does an edge-triggered `T1SLink_SendFretboardCtrl(TEACHER, v)` (T1S transport only, latched only on send success). `perf_log`: `PerfLog_EmitFretboardRaw` gains a `commanded_mask` param; `perf_rec_fretboard_raw_t`'s spare `reserved` byte → `commanded_mask` (record size unchanged); `PERF_LOG_SCHEMA_VERSION` 5→6. | edge-ai needs the teacher label (marvin's CV decision) paired with the ADC scan at the source to kill the ~12 ms cross-stream skew the old actuator-join had. marvin already computes that command every frame; sending it over the existing detector control channel lets the fretboard stamp it into the same 240 Hz frame as the scan. Reused the spare record byte so only the fretboard *wire* frame grew (17→18 B) — marvin's record layout is unchanged and the schema bump is version-only. Teacher send is edge-triggered + retried to match the arm/stream send pattern; harmless outside a capture. Guarded on `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT == T1S` (no control channel on UART). |
| 2026-08-02 | **Added a `fretboard model <difficulty>` console command + detector control opcode `0x03` (`T1S_DET_CTRL_MODEL`).** `net/t1s/t1s_link.h`: `T1S_DET_CTRL_MODEL (3u)` + `T1S_DET_CTRL_OP_COUNT` 2→3 (the per-opcode staging arrays `s_fret_ctrl_arg/dirty[]` auto-resize; the `SendFretboardCtrl` range check `> OP_COUNT` and the flush's `frame[0] = i+1` opcode reconstruction were already generic). `console.c` `cmd_fretboard` gained a `model <easy|medium|hard|expert|auto>` subcommand with a **local** name→index table (marvin doesn't share the fretboard `MODEL_SEL_*` enum) → `T1SLink_SendFretboardCtrl(T1S_DET_CTRL_MODEL, idx)`. | The fretboard now carries 5 selectable inference-model slots (one per GH difficulty + a reserved `auto`); marvin should be able to pick the active model over the bus the same way it arms/streams the detector. One more opcode in the existing detector namespace — no transport change. Plumbing only today: only `hard` is trained on the fretboard, so every selection currently resolves to the hard model (fretboard journal 2026-08-02). |
| 2026-07-28 | **fauxmote command link routed over 10BASE-T1S on the coordinator side, defaulting to T1S; UART is the explicit fallback.** New `MARVIN_FAUXMOTE_TRANSPORT={FAUXMOTE_TRANSPORT_UART(0),FAUXMOTE_TRANSPORT_T1S(1)}` in `fauxmote_link.h`, wired via `user.cmake` (default 1). Because marvin has a **single** LAN8651 MAC-PHY, the fauxmote channel is a *generic controller channel inside `net/t1s`*, not a second interface: `T1S_NODE_CONTROLLER` (node id 1), ethertype `0x88B7`, a static TX FIFO drained in `t1s_task`, and an RX demux to a registered `T1SLink_ControllerHandler` (STATUS uplink). `T1SLink_Initialize` made idempotent so fretboard-T1S + fauxmote-T1S can both call it. `fauxmote_link.c` keeps its producer/latching/STATUS layer and only swaps `send_frame`/RX (STATUS latch factored into shared `latch_status`). `mf_proto.h` wire unchanged. | Matches the already-migrated ESP32/fauxmote end (`0x88B7`, controller id 1, node_type 3) and mirrors the guitar/fretboard staging pattern. A single MAC-PHY forbids a second interface, so the channel rides the existing TX/RX path beside guitar command (`0x88B5`) + heartbeats (`0x88B6`); the FIFO decouples fauxmote's multi-message-per-wake producer from the single-in-flight TC6 TX and interleaves with the latest-wins guitar command. Default T1S (Greg) since the bus is the target transport; UART fallback retained behind the flag so the point-to-point path stays buildable. Node id 1 fits the canonical table (docs/t1s-podl-link.md §7.1): controllers 1–2, guitar 3, fretboard 4. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Coordinator node table: fretboard/detector entry renumbered id 1 → 4** (`net/t1s/t1s_link.c` `s_nodes[]`: `{ 4u, DETECTOR_ADC_FRETBOARD, T1S_NODE_FRETBOARD }`). `detector_id` (`DETECTOR_ADC_FRETBOARD`) is unchanged — only the PLCA id / MAC low byte moved. Coordinated with the fretboard firmware (`t1s_detector.c` `T1S_NODE_ID` → 4); re-flash marvin **and** the fretboard together. | Completes the bus renumber to the canonical table (docs/t1s-podl-link.md §7.1): detector = 4, freeing ids 1–2 for the fauxmote controllers. Non-promiscuous MAC filtering means marvin must recognize the node's src MAC, so its table entry moves in lockstep with the node's id. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Coordinator node table: guitar entry renumbered id 2 → 3** (`net/t1s/t1s_link.c` `s_nodes[]`: `{ 3u, T1S_NO_DETECTOR, T1S_NODE_GUITAR }`). marvin now addresses its 1-byte command to `02:00:00:00:00:03`. Non-promiscuous MAC filtering means the guitar only accepts frames to its own MAC, so marvin's TX target had to move in lockstep with the node's id. |
| 2026-07-23 | **fauxmote link moved to a dedicated FLEXCOM5 USART (PA16/PA15); fauxmote made transport-independent.** New FLEXCOM5 USART in MCC (AIC source 14, ring-buffer, config byte-identical to FLEXCOM1; baud→1 Mbaud at runtime via `SerialSetup`). `fauxmote_link.c/.h` is a mechanical `FLEXCOM1_USART_*`→`FLEXCOM5_USART_*` rename. Dropped every `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT` guard that only existed to keep fauxmote off UART-guitar builds (`app.c` init, `fretboard_link.c` mirror tap, `console.c` `fauxmote` cmd, `game_controller.c` `ensure_wii_connected`). Commits `a36ccdd` (MCC) + `1872a9c` (app); verified on hardware. | fauxmote borrowing FLEXCOM1 tied it to the T1S guitar build (FLEXCOM1 was only free there). A dedicated peripheral removes the contention so fauxmote runs on every build — and since **every build drives a Wii through fauxmote** (Greg), the Wii-connect pre-flight should always run, not just on T1S. The remaining `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT` guards are the real guitar UART-vs-T1S actuator split (unchanged). |
| 2026-07-23 | **T1S host SPI moved FLEXCOM4 → FLEXCOM3 (board rework), CS0 → CS1.** New pin map: MOSI PC22, MISO PC23, nCS PC24, SCK PC26 (`FLEXCOM3_IO0/1/4/2`); `T1S_IRQ_N` PB18, `T1S_RST` PA17; AIC source 13→8. App change was a pure `FLEXCOM4_SPI_*`→`FLEXCOM3_SPI_*` rename in `t1s_link.c`; CS is hardware-driven (`FLEX_SPI_MR.PCS=NPCS1`/`CSR[1]`), so CS0→CS1 needed no app change. Re-applied patch #11 (`plib_aic.c`) after regen. Commits `70b1fc7` + `9ce4fcf` (+ `5faa2b7` MISO pull-down); verified driving the guitar on hardware. | Board rework freed the FLEXCOM4 pins for other use and put T1S on PORTC. Keeping the wrapper CS-agnostic (hardware PCS, no manual nCS toggling) is what made the CS0→CS1 move a non-event in firmware. The regen dropped the MISO absent-chip pull-down; re-created on PC23 (`5faa2b7`). |
| 2026-07-15 | **Timing pipeline is the "decide" layer, not part of the actuator — moved `timing_pipeline.{c,h}` `actuator/` → `game/`; and detector-source arbitration moved to the push side (`Detector_Publish`), so the bus carries only the active detector and the pipeline no longer filters.** Two structural cleanups after the P1/P2 re-layering made the layers explicit. **(Q1 placement)** The pipeline is a peer of detector and actuator (spec §4.4), and it's the sibling of `game_controller` — the two are the mutually-exclusive autonomous command producers (note-highway gameplay vs. menu nav, open Q11). So its home is the game-control subsystem (`game/`), leaving `actuator/` as the command **sink** (`fretboard_link` + mechanical advance). `manual_control` stays out of `game/` (operator override, not game logic). Include convention: cross-dir includes are path-qualified from `default/src`, so the move updated `#include` paths in 6 files + the `user.cmake` source list; no behavior change. **(Q2 arbitration)** Added `Detector_Publish(state)` in the detector module: it forwards to the bus only if `state->detector_id == active`, and `Detector_SetActive` drains the queue on switch. `cv_marvin_v1` publishes through it (dropped its raw `xQueueSend` + bus plumbing); the pipeline dropped `if (detector_id != Detector_GetActive()) continue`. Behavior-identical (the pipeline already acted only on the active source) but arbitration now has one home. | The `actuator/` dir had become "command producers + sink," a rule already broken by `game_controller` living in `game/`. Grouping the two autoplay producers together makes Q11 arbitration local instead of spanning dirs, and reduces `actuator/` to a clean transport sink — matching the three-layer split (detector = sense, game = decide, actuator = actuate). For Q2: the detector bus is pipeline-only (recording/UI use separate paths), so gating the push costs the recorder nothing and keeps "which source drives the robot" a single detector-module decision rather than a policy the consumer re-derives. **Follow-up smell (resolved same day):** `fretboard_link.c` had still included the scheduler header for the `TIMING_BIT_*` wire-mask layout. Extracted those defines to `actuator/guitar_cmd.h` as `GUITAR_BTN_*` and dropped the sink→scheduler include (see session log 2026-07-15). |
| 2026-07-15 | **Timing re-layered into three owners: observation-lead → detector, musical scheduling → timing pipeline, mechanical actuation-advance → actuator. Today's single `STRUM_DELAY_MS` conflates the first with the third.** The pipeline currently fuses three unrelated clocks into `STRUM_DELAY_MS` (400 ms) + friends, and the actuator (guitar T1S node, `t1s_follower.c` `buttons_apply_mask`) is a dumb "assert this mask *now*" GPIO sink with zero timing knowledge. Split them: **(1) observation lead** — the ROI sits above the strike line so a note is *seen* ~`STRUM_DELAY_MS` early; this is detector geometry, not a scheduling choice. **Decision: the detector stamps a strike-line-referenced time per pressed edge** (`strike_at ≈ timestamp_us + observation_lead`), so the pipeline schedules purely in strike-line time and carries no `STRUM_DELAY` of its own. Enables per-detector leads (CV ROI vs ADC differ). Requires adding a strike-time field to the `detector_state_t` bus record (spec §4.2.3) — a bus-record change to plan for. **(2) musical scheduling** — chord window, `FRET_EARLY_MS`, `STRUM_PULSE_MS`, release scheduling, strum-direction alternation, back-to-back hold: stays in the timing pipeline, correct where it is. **(3) mechanical actuation-advance** — solenoid rise time / GPIO propagation; 0 for the current open-drain guitar node, ~5–20 ms for a future solenoid rig. This is an actuator property, owned as declared config *by the actuator node* (may be per-bit / fret-vs-strum). **Decision: Option A now — marvin applies the node's declared advance and keeps emitting a bare "assert now" mask** (dumb wire, no shared clock, no node buffering; fits the single-emit-point + static-allocation discipline). **Option B recorded as the future escalation:** wire becomes `(mask, strike_time_T)`, node buffers and fires at `T − advance` off a shared clock — adopt only if link jitter or on-node multi-solenoid sequencing demands local scheduling; the Option-A `actuator_advance` config value *is* the number the node would apply under B, so nothing is wasted. | The muddle isn't that scheduling lives on marvin (it should) — it's that observation lead is mislabeled as a scheduler constant and mechanical advance has nowhere to live except smeared into `STRUM_DELAY_MS`, which couples marvin to one actuator's mechanics. Re-layering puts each constant with the thing that knows it: geometry with the detector, musicality with the scheduler, mechanics with the actuator. Option A over B for now because the precision budget is coarse (~±2–5 ms) and PLCA already bounds link jitter, so central application is adequate and far cheaper than a distributed clock. **T1S sync finding (for B):** PLCA (802.3cg) gives bounded latency/jitter (determinism) but **no** shared clock — it is not a time-sync protocol. Full IEEE 1588/gPTP is available in principle (the TC6 stack already surfaces a HW RX timestamp — `uint64_t *rxTimestamp` in the follower RX callback) but is overkill here. Recommended B-sync = marvin periodically broadcasts a sync frame with its `frame_epoch`/timestamp; each follower disciplines a free-running local timer to it (few-ms accuracy for near-zero infrastructure), with the LAN8651 HW timestamp as the escape hatch if tighter is ever needed. |
| 2026-07-02 | **fauxmote link on marvin uses FLEXCOM1 (PA28/PA29) and mirrors the gameplay mask to both sinks.** New `net/fauxmote/fauxmote_link.c` reuses the fretboard UART-transport peripheral (free while the guitar rides T1S); `FretboardLink_Send()` taps `Fauxmote_SendGuitarMask()` so the T1S guitar node and the ESP32 Wiimote move in lock-step. Shared `mf_proto.h` (byte-identical both sides); 1 Mbaud via `SerialSetup`. Compile-guarded to the T1S build (owns FLEXCOM1). | Zero MCC work — FLEXCOM1's USART PLIB already exists and is idle in the T1S build, so no new peripheral/pin generation. "Mirror to both" (Greg's call) keeps the real guitar and the Wii driven by the one existing choke point without a runtime multi-sink layer, and matches the spec's bit-identical `GUITAR` byte 0 == T1S mask. Latched-state TX task with a 50 ms floor gives low-latency-on-change plus dropped-frame self-heal and keeps fauxmote's watchdog fed. |
| 2026-07-01 | **UI: paint every screen canvas once at boot; show/hide is repaint-free. Fixes the ~0.5–1 s first-touch lag after opening/closing the nav drawer or song-select modal.** Root cause: Legato defers all touch input while its renderer is non-idle (`handleTouchDown`/up/move return `LE_EVENT_DEFERRED` when `leIsDrawing()`, `legato_input.c`), re-queuing the event every 10 ms `LEGATO_Tasks` tick. A screen transition kicked off a full repaint that held the renderer busy for many ticks, stranding the first tap behind it. The repaints were architecturally needless — each screen owns a RAM canvas surface and Legato renders into it regardless of the canvas's shown/bound state (`GFX_IOCTL_SET_ACTIVE_LAYER` succeeds for any canvas), so showing a screen should be a pure HW-layer bind. Three offenders removed: (1) `navigation_open()` explicit `PANEL_NAVIGATION->invalidate()` every open; (2) `songsel_set_input()` used `setEnabled()` for modal input-gating, and `_leWidget_SetEnabled` invalidates on state change → repainted the modal panels on open and the **dashboard** on close; (3) same `setEnabled(DASHBOARD)` in nav open/close. **Fix:** `panel_set_pickable()` in `ui_manager.c` toggles `LE_WIDGET_ENABLED` directly on `wgt->flags` (no invalidate — the renderer never reads ENABLED; `leUtils_PickFromWidget` descends only into ENABLED children, so clearing it gates the whole subtree). `UiManager_SetDashboardPickable(bool)` shares one dashboard gate between nav + song-select. New `paint_all_screens_once()` invalidates all four panels (dashboard/nav/song-select/album-art) behind the splash before `wait_render_idle()`, so every surface is complete before first show. Removed the now-redundant one-time invalidate in `ScreenSongSelect_Setup`. Content-change repaints (album-art swap on song tap, nav highlight scheme) are unchanged. Real reason the old startup paint "didn't fully land": MGS builds `PANEL_NAVIGATION` `setVisible(FALSE)`, so the boot root-damage painted nothing until `Setup` flipped it — the explicit boot paint makes it deterministic. Builds clean (XC32 v5.10); **pending on-hardware confirm** that first-tap lag is gone on nav open/close + song-select open/tap/close. `IGNOREPICK` was rejected for the gate: it stops a panel being the pick result but still lets its children be picked, so it wouldn't gate the subtree. The heavy 508×208 RGBA8888 album-art alpha blit remains a separate perf item. | Legato's input-while-drawing deferral is library behavior (can't edit vendor tree); the lever is to make transitions not repaint at all, which the canvas model already affords. Toggling ENABLED directly (vs `setEnabled`) drops only the invalidate side-effect while keeping identical pick semantics; it's an established idiom here (MGS sets IGNOREPICK/IGNOREEVENTS the same way) and needs no vendor edit. One-time boot paint honors the canvas model's whole point — "nothing changes while hidden, so nothing to repaint on show." Single-buffered surfaces (`bufferCount==1`, `LE_PREEMPTION_LEVEL==0`) mean one drained invalidate lands a surface completely, so the boot paint is reliable. |
| 2026-06-30 | **UI fonts: DejaVu Sans Mono family only (licensing).** All new text uses `DejaVuSansMono_*` / `DejaVuSansMonoBold_*`; do not add other faces. The figma-imported Menlo / NotoSans faces still present in the generated font set are a known cleanup item to migrate to DejaVu over time. The song-select **tier label** uses **DejaVuSansMonoBold 12pt**, which already carries the `★` (U+2605) glyph (its subset is `0x20–0x7E`, `0xA0–0xFF`, `0x2605`), so the `★★★★★ TIER n` label needs no font change. Recorded in spec §4.5. | Licensing — DejaVu is the cleared family for this project. Keeping to one mono family also keeps the font asset set small and the look consistent. The star already being in the DejaVuSansMonoBold subset means the tier label gets the mock's stars for free. |  All covers decoded once into static RGB888 DDR caches during the splash; large tier = 508×208 strip with a difficulty-colored fade baked offline; song-select wired, dashboard pending.** Two fixed-size tiers in cached DDR (`.region_ram`): small 144×144 (3.8 MB) + large 508×208 (19.3 MB) + a shared 512 KB decode scratch ≈ **23 MB**. `Art_LoadAll()` (called from `ui_boot_task` after `init_screens()`, behind the splash) walks `art/{small,large}/` with `SYS_FS_DirOpen`/`DirRead`, reads each file into scratch, parses true dims from the JPEG SOF / PNG IHDR header, validates exact tier dims, and decodes into the slot via `leImage_Render` (the JPEG/PNG decoder's `render` writes RGB888 straight into the slot — no clip-rect/active-frame dependency). Each slot is wrapped as a RAW RGB888 `leImage`; lookup is `(setlist,index)` → `leImage*` or NULL. Missing/oversize/wrong-size/corrupt → empty slot → UI blank; never fails boot. Song-select sets the **MGS-provided** `Marvin_IMAGE_ALBUM_ART_0_0` widget per selection (the maroon overlay panel MGS built stays invisible — fade is baked). **Offline (`tools/gh3-cover-art/`):** `process_gh3_cover_art.py` reworked to center-crop the middle 508×208 band, look up each song's `difficulty` from `songs.csv`, and bake a fade colored by difficulty from an 8-tier palette — `"1".."8"` green→red with blue/purple accents (`TIER_COLORS`, shared with the TIER-label schemes), `"bonus"`/unknown = gray — saved as **PNG** (lossless gradient); small stays 144×144 JPEG. `install_art.py` installs large as `<setlist>-NN.png`. `art` console command reports cache fill. **Pending:** asset regen from `./data` originals; dashboard small-thumbnail slot (no MGS widget exists for it yet). | Spec §4.8.7 always called for pre-decode-once into a static DDR cache (runtime = O(1) pointer, no card I/O/decode/alloc — honors static-allocation). RGB888 chosen per Greg; the 508×208 display window (vs full 508×~508) shrinks the large cache from ~52 MB to ~19 MB, and offline crop+fade keeps the device module to "decode→store→display" with the cached array *being* the final image. Difficulty-colored fade baked offline (Greg's call) rather than a runtime overlay: difficulty is already in `songs.csv` and static per song, so baking keeps the device trivial; cost is a re-bake if a tier changes. PNG for large because the smooth baked gradient bands under JPEG; both decoders implement the offscreen `render` path. Caches in **cached** DDR (not the 32 MB `.region_nocache`): they're decoder source/staging Legato blits into the canvas, never LCDC-scanned directly, and the 224 MB cached region has ample room. Walk-the-dir (not catalog-driven) so a missing/stale catalog never affects art (spec §4.8.7). |

Panel modules (`screen_nav.c`, `screen_song_select.c`, …) keep content + event wiring + interaction FX but drop all `leAddRootWidget`/canvas mechanics. **Splash stays separate/manual** (pre-Legato RGBA8888 scanout) on a canvas slot *outside* the layer-screen range 0..N-1 (e.g. top of the 8-slot pool). Adopting the framework model makes "each panel owns its own canvas" literally true (define up to 8 layer-screens, composite any 3 onto the HW layers), aligns with the tooling instead of fighting it, deletes two standing hacks, and is purpose-built for marvin's need (multiple coexisting overlays with preserved widget state, no redraw on transition). Splash stays out of the master screen because it must paint before Legato/the master screen is built (fast-boot path). |  (`data/ui/make-splash.sh`), not by ad-hoc shell history; the raw byte order is `[A,B,G,R]` per pixel.** The XLCDC splash canvas is `GFX_COLOR_MODE_RGBA_8888` whose pixel word is `0xRRGGBBAA` (`splash.c` `SPLASH_FILL 0x000000FF` = opaque black confirms it), so little-endian memory bytes are `[A,B,G,R]` — **not** Pillow's default `R,G,B,A`. The script splits channels and `Image.merge("RGBA",(a,b,g,r))` to get that order, resizes to 1280×800, and asserts the 4,096,000-byte size `splash.c` requires. A checked-in script with the size assertion makes the splash pipeline self-serve and drift-proof, and pairs with the existing `program-qspi.sh splash` provisioning step. |
| 2026-06-28 | **QSPI 8 MiB partition map locked — `flash/qspi_layout.h` is the single source of truth.** Splash blob `0x000000` (4 MiB) · UI assets `0x400000` (~3.98 MiB) · Settings ring `0x7FC000` (16 KiB / 4 sectors). All boundaries 4 KiB sector-aligned; contiguous, no gaps/overlaps. Splash at offset 0 (raw pre-decoded framebuffer; 4 MiB holds full-panel RGBA8888 = 3.91 MiB, or an RGB565 splash uses ~1.95 MiB — the reservation keeps both open, format decided when the loader is built). Read-mostly bulk grows upward; the small, frequently-erased settings ring sits at the very top as a stable anchor, **wear-isolated** from the bulk. Settings get 4 sectors (2× the 2-sector ping-pong minimum) for ring headroom. UI assets indexed by **compile-time offsets** for now (flashed with firmware); a manifest sector / littlefs can be carved from the front of the assets region later if assets must update independently. | One header (`qspi_layout.h`) means consumers and the `qspi` dev tools share offsets — no drift. Settings-at-top isolates erase wear and gives a fixed anchor independent of how the bulk grows. **Dev-tool caveat:** `qspi` smoke (last sector `0x7FF000`) and `qspi verify` (top-down) write into the settings region — destructive to it, but the ring-log's CRC-fallback-to-defaults makes that non-fatal (settings just reinitialize). Run heavy `qspi verify` before the settings store is populated, or accept a settings reset. | 
| 2026-06-28 | **QSPI read throughput — the serial clock (GCLK = 2×QSCK) was the lever, not per-access overhead. At 200 MHz GCLK (MCC max → 100 MHz QSCK), XDMAC mem2mem reads hit 18.2 MB/s → ~225 ms for a 4 MB splash, ~3× faster than SD (~750 ms). Read integrity verified at volume. Splash → QSPI via XDMAC mem2mem is GO.** Bench (`qspi bench`, `flash/qspi_smoke.c`) at 50 MHz GCLK → 25 MHz QSCK showed ~5 MB/s and I wrongly concluded "overhead-bound, clock maxed." Greg caught the datasheet note **GCLK must = 2×QSCK** and raised GCLK 50→200 MHz; everything scaled ~linearly (DRV_SST26_Read 4.6→14.8, XDMAC mem2mem 4.9→18.2 MB/s; the raw libc `memcpy` path stays ~2.5 — it is just bad on this region, ignore it). Read path = **XDMAC channel 1 mem2mem from `QSPIMEM_ADDR` (0x60000000)** — ch0 is the ISC histogram DMA, and the PLIB only tracks ch0, so we program ch1's registers directly. Integrity confirmed by `qspi verify 1024 4`: 1 MiB × 4 passes via **both** the driver and XDMAC, **0 mismatches** at 100 MHz, with a position-dependent pattern (catches misaddressing, not just stuck bits). **Two dead ends ruled out:** (a) making the QSPI window **cacheable** in the MMU **hangs** — cache-linefill bursts aren't serviced by the SMM slave (back out; XDMAC bypasses the MMU so it's unaffected); (b) **continuous-read mode (CRM)** only trims the instruction byte on *non-sequential* access, so it does nothing for bulk sequential reads. SMM tops out ~36% of the 50 MB/s theoretical (re-frames per access); the only path to ~50 MB/s (~80 ms) is the QSPI's RX-triggered streaming read — deferred as a mini-project, not worth it over 225 ms. | The clock fix flipped the verdict: at 25 MHz QSCK QSPI-SMM (~5 MB/s, ~800 ms) was *worse* than SD's read; at 100 MHz QSCK (18.2 MB/s) it clearly wins. 100 MHz is within the SST26's 104 MHz rating and proved data-clean at volume, so no DLL/read-training needed at this rate. 200 MHz GCLK is an MCC clock-config change (lives in MCC config → survives regen). **Gotcha for any raw SMM read (memcpy/DMA, bypassing `DRV_SST26_Read`):** the read frame must be closed with `QSPI_CR.LASTXFER` + wait-CSRA or the next QSPI command hangs with CS still asserted — the driver does this internally; raw paths must call an equivalent (`qspi_end_smm_read()` in the bench). Splash framebuffer is in `.region_nocache` (strongly-ordered 0x20000000) but XDMAC writes bypass the MMU, so DMA→framebuffer is unaffected. | 
| 2026-06-28 | **Config/operator settings (backlight %, mode toggles, calibration) persist to the onboard QSPI NOR (`SST26VF064BA`, 8 MB / 64 Mbit SQI) via flash EEPROM-emulation — a small wear-leveled ring-log, NOT a filesystem. No board EEPROM exists, so QSPI is the only non-volatile small-state store.** Reserve ≥2× 4 KB sectors as a ping-pong region: a save appends a `{seq, settings_t, CRC}` record to the next free slot in the active sector (page-program, *no erase*); when full, write the latest record into the pre-erased partner sector then erase the full one. Boot picks the highest-`seq` record with a valid CRC; a partial trailing record (power lost mid-write) fails CRC and falls back to the prior good record → **power-fail safe by construction**, no torn settings. This supersedes the spec §4.6.2/§4.7 plan to keep all config in `/marvin/config.json` on the SD card: operator/UI prefs must persist and load **even with no SD card inserted** (the SD is removable, QSPI is always present). Session/recording config that travels with the data can stay on SD. **Splash, when/if it moves to QSPI, is stored as a raw pre-decoded framebuffer blob at a fixed offset — also no filesystem.** | NOR semantics drive the design: bits only clear 1→0 on program; setting a bit back to 1 requires erasing the whole 4 KB sector, so "update a value" = erase+rewrite 4 KB. FAT makes that worse (every file write also rewrites FAT/dir sectors → write amplification) and FatFs is *not* power-loss-atomic → a mid-write power cut can corrupt the FAT itself, not just the value. The ring-log avoids both: erases are rare (~one per N saves, N = slots/sector) and alternate across sectors, giving effectively unlimited endurance for human-driven settings against the part's ~100k cycles/sector; atomicity is the seq+CRC scan. littlefs (Harmony File System can back it) is the fallback **only** if settings grow into many named, workstation-mountable keys — overkill for a handful of scalars. **Raw-only splash:** the seed `splash.jpg` carries ~2 s of on-target JPEG decode, which dwarfs any bringup savings, so a JPEG-on-QSPI splash would be *slower* than today's SD path — only a raw blob streamed straight to the framebuffer (no decode, no FS mount, no FTL — NOR has none) realizes QSPI's sub-ms bringup vs. SD's ~350 ms `ACMD41` power-up handshake. |

Touch handled by **reveal-then-promote** (parked surfaces are non-interactive; promote to a live Legato layer to interact). Phased plan P0–P5 + open questions in the doc. **Design only — no code/MCC yet.** | Reverses the earlier "back off canvas" — that call was made on the **wrong axis**: canvas was dismissed for the *slide animation* (which direct `XLCDC_SetLayerWindowXYPos` stepping handles fine, no framework needed), but the actual problem is the **off-screen surface pool** (render content decoupled from a hardware layer), and that is canvas's literal reason to exist. The two homegrown alternatives lose: DIY pixel-buffer retarget reimplements canvas's per-surface/scratch/binding bookkeeping (~half of canvas, growing once surfaces aren't full-screen); direct-layer-only can present/animate but can't get Legato content *into* a surface. Canvas's earlier objections are addressable, not fatal: it deletes the static `frame_buffer` array, but we *use* the canvas buffers as our surfaces (statically allocated, non-cached per the 2026-06-24 fix), so static-allocation holds; the dynamic `GFX_CANVAS_Task` converts to `xTaskCreateStatic` or rides alongside the existing MCC dynamic tasks. Binding constraint is **layers (2 free), not memory** (~21 MB free in the 32 MB `ram_nocache` after the ISC pool + scratch; full-screen RGB565 ≈ 2 MB, most overlays far less). Resolves spec Q5 for the *presentation* layer (Legato renderer + canvas compositor) without dictating per-screen authoring. |
| 2026-06-24 | **Legato CPU-render scratch buffer must live in non-cached DDR (`.region_nocache`) — fixed a GFX2D cache-coherency bleed; grew `ram_nocache` 16→32 MB.** Symptom (found during song-list bring-up): dashboard content streakily "bled through" an opaque overlay widget, intermittently, only where CPU-rendered text was. Active render path is **CPU software** (`leRenderer_FillArea` → `_RGBBlendFill`; glyphs too) into a **scratch buffer**, then **`DRV_GFX2D_Blit`** (the SAM9X75 2D engine) copies scratch→framebuffer. The framebuffer is non-cached (`FB_CACHE_NC` → `.region_nocache`) but the Legato scratch was **cacheable**: `LE_NO_CACHE_ATTR` was an empty no-op `#define` and `LE_COHERENT_ATTR = CACHE_ALIGN` (alignment only). The blit's `dcache_CleanInvalidateByAddr` of the cached scratch is insufficient on ARM926 → the 2D engine reads stale CPU-cached lines → streaky, cache-line-granularity bleed. **Fix:** define `LE_NO_CACHE_ATTR = __attribute__((section(".region_nocache")))` in **`user.cmake`** (the `#ifndef` guard in the generated `legato_renderer.c` lets the `-D` win → survives MGS regen, no generated-tree patch); MCC grew `ram_nocache` 16→32 MB to host it (ddram.ld + plib_mmu.c + `DDRAM_NO_CACHE_SIZE`). **Confirmed on hardware** (commits `d36640f` + `c0f316c`). **Two upstream Microchip Legato/gfx bugs to file:** (1) with "Cacheable Frame Buffers" **off**, the generator non-caches the framebuffer but leaves the Legato render scratch cacheable (`LE_NO_CACHE_ATTR` empty) — the gap that bit us; (2) `DRV_GFX2D_Fill` omits the `dcache_CleanInvalidateByAddr` that `DRV_GFX2D_Blit` has (latent — bites any GPU-fill config, e.g. `LE_WIDGET_BUFFER_ENABLE=1`). | Root-caused by elimination, not by reading (every software-logic path traced correct): a bare opaque magenta fill still bled, hiding the dashboard cleared it, and non-cached scratch fixed it → it's the cached-scratch ↔ 2D-engine coherency, below the software layer. Non-cached is the correct policy on this SoC — Microchip already chose it for the framebuffer (`FB_CACHE_NC`) rather than trusting cache maintenance for a DMA-/GPU-read buffer; the scratch is the same kind of buffer and should match. Adding cache maintenance to `DRV_GFX2D_Fill` would *not* fix this (Fill isn't on the path; the on-path `Blit` already invalidates and still leaked). System-wide rule for this board: any buffer the 2D engine or display DMA reads must be non-cached (or carry correct, sufficient maintenance — which the cacheable path here does not). |
| 2026-06-24 | **Song-select setlist = a hand-written custom Legato widget (`leWidget` subclass), not the stock list widget.** `ui/song_list.{h,c}`: subclass with overridden `_paint`/`update`/touch handlers. Stock `leListWidget` is disabled **and** its item model (icon + one string) can't render the tier-badge + two-line + right-aligned-duration row. Design: **row-provider callback** `songlist_row_fn` (widget never includes `game/catalog.h`; the screen owns catalog→row mapping + future tier grouping); **drag + kinetic inertia** computed from the **FreeRTOS tick** (`leUpdate(0)` means the framework `dt` is always 0, so the widget measures real elapsed time itself for frame-rate-independent motion); **tap-to-select** (<10 px movement) with a selection callback; **virtualized software paint** (`leRenderer_RectFill` + `leStringRenderer_DrawCString`, visible rows only, no per-row child widgets); single **pool allocation** with the vtable built by **copying the base vtable at runtime** (project builds `LE_DYNAMIC_VTABLES=0`, so `_leWidget_FillVTable` isn't linkable and the GenerateVTable path is compiled out); declared **opaque** (`BACKGROUND_FILL`) so Legato composites it. Lives entirely in `ui/` (added via `user.cmake`) — zero edits to the generated `gfx/legato/` tree. Bring-up: `ui/song_list_demo.{c,h}` + a `songlist` console command (`attach`/`off`/`hide`/`show`/`solid`/`tree`/`stats`) with a catalog-backed provider; **validated on hardware**. | Matches the project's MCC-isolation idiom (own module + `user.cmake`, like `console`/`perf_log`); the widget is the primary, reusable piece, so it was built first, ahead of the MGS screen around it. Row-provider keeps the widget generic and testable and lets the screen map `Catalog_At(i)` → row (and supply tier badges once the tier column exists). Pool allocation (not static) because Legato recursively `LE_FREE`s the whole child tree on screen teardown (`legato_widget.c`) — a static widget attached to a screen would feed a static pointer to `LE_FREE`; the Legato widget pool is the accepted heap carve-out, and the widget is one self-contained allocation (fixed internal scratch, no secondary mallocs). Time-based inertia (vs per-tick) because the update cadence is render-bound (~55 Hz) and irregular under the priority-2 UI task. Tier badges blank until the deferred catalog `tier` column lands. |
| 2026-06-24 | **Song catalog = flat CSV (`<card>/games/gh3-wii/songs.csv`); built `game/catalog.{h,c}` as a lazy static-cache loader on the SD layer. Album-art SD layout locked (two size-tier subdirs) — doc-only, no art code.** New `catalog_entry_t` + `Catalog_Lookup(setlist,index,…)` / `Catalog_LookupSong(gp_song_t*)` / `Catalog_Reload` / `Catalog_At`. On first lookup it mounts on demand, reads `songs.csv` (`setlist,index,title,artist,album,bpm,length_s,year,genre,difficulty`; `setlist`∈`main`/`bonus`), and fills a fixed `[GP_N_SONGS]` static cache, then closes the file — lookups are O(n) in RAM. The two CSV helpers (`csv_split`/`csv_quote`) were lifted from `results.c` into a shared **`util/csv.{h,c}`**; `results.c` now includes it (no behavior change). `catalog` console command (`reload`/`ls`/`<main\|bonus> <idx>`) + a checked-in seed `data/games/gh3-wii/songs.csv` (64 rows; real title/artist/album from the `SONGS` table in `tools/fetch_gh3_cover_art.py`; `bpm`/`length_s`/`difficulty` reserved-but-blank). That fetch script gained a `--catalog` mode to also source `year`/`genre` from MusicBrainz (run with `--contact`; verifiable, no hand-typed data) — `--no-enrich` reproduces the current seed byte-for-byte. Spec §4.8.3 reconciled JSON→CSV; §4.8.7 art path changed to `art/{small,large}/<setlist>-<NN>.{jpg,png}`. **Code-complete; pending Greg's MPLAB build + on-hardware test.** | Same substrate-before-consumer play as `storage`/`results`, and the *same* CSV-over-JSON call for the same reasons: on-device parse with comma-split + `atol` into static buffers (no malloc, no JSON tokenizer, fits `FF_FS_MAX_FILES=1`) while the card still opens in pandas/Excel. **Lazy static cache** (vs `results`' per-call streaming) because catalog lookups are frequent + read-mostly and shouldn't hold the single FatFs handle open against `results.csv`; 64×~120 B ≈ 8 KB BSS is cheap. Keying by the stable `(setlist,index)` the recognizer emits means a missing/stale catalog degrades to "Unknown song" and never affects recognition (templates stay compile-time in flash). Art deliberately **not** referenced from `songs.csv` — firmware derives the art path straight from `(setlist,index)`, so the two are independent (missing catalog still shows art, missing art still shows the title); size tiers as sibling subdirs map 1:1 to fixed-size DDR caches. Shared `util/csv` over duplicating the splitter is the right reuse now that two SD consumers parse CSV. |
| 2026-06-24 | **Results stored as flat CSV (`<card>/players/results.csv`); built `results` module on the SD/RTC layer ahead of the gameplay write path.** New in-tree `results/results.{h,c}` (added via `user.cmake`): `Results_Append(record)` (mount-on-demand, header-if-new, RTC UTC timestamp, CSV-quoted song), `Results_TopN(setlist,index,difficulty,…)` (streaming line scan + quote-aware `csv_split` + bounded score-descending insert, all static buffers), and `Results_Set/GetPlayer` (operator string, default "p1"). Console gains `player`, `scores <set> <idx> [diff]`, and `results add …` (synthetic-row injection for testing). Paths build from `Storage_MountPoint()` so files land at the card root next to `sdtest.bin`. **Code-complete; pending Greg's MPLAB build + on-hardware test.** | Same substrate-before-consumer play as `storage`/`time`: the writer + on-device reader + display validate the CSV format decision (spec §4.8.6) against synthetic rows **now**, entirely on the proven SD/FatFs/RTC stack — no gameplay dependency. CSV chosen so on-device high-score display parses with comma-split + `atol` into static buffers (no malloc, no JSON tokenizer, fits `FF_FS_MAX_FILES=1`) while the card still opens in pandas/Excel. The gameplay engine wires in later as a one-line `Results_Append` once M9 Phase 3 score readers exist; player-id scheme (operator string vs profiles) is the remaining Q12 open item. |
| 2026-06-23 | **RTC enabled in Gregorian mode, kept in UTC; FatFs file timestamps wired to it.** Added the SAM9X75 internal RTC via MCC (Gregorian/`struct tm` plib `RTC_TimeSet/Get`, `RTC_Initialize` in `SYS_Initialize`); `RTC_MR.UTC=0` (calendar register format) but the clock *value* is set in UTC. Console gains `time` / `time set YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` (prints ISO-8601 `…Z`, computes weekday). A strong `get_fattime()` in `storage.c` overrides the generated `__WEAK` stub (which hardcoded 2013-08-09) so files carry RTC time (year-1980, sec/2 DOS packing; <1980 falls back to the epoch). Resolves the timestamp half of Q12. **No backup cell populated yet** → RTC resets on power-off and must be re-set at boot (host via `time set`); persistent wall-clock needs VDDBU backup hardware. | "UTC mode" (the `RTC_MR.UTC` seconds-counter format) vs Gregorian is orthogonal to keeping a UTC *value* — Gregorian was chosen because FatFs `get_fattime` (mandatory, `FF_FS_NORTC=0`) wants broken-down fields, so calendar registers feed it directly with no epoch conversion, while a UTC value gives DST/timezone-proof timestamps matching the spec's `session_started_utc` `…Z` convention (§4.6.5). `get_fattime` as a `__WEAK` override keeps it out of the regenerated diskio.c. Player identity + whether to add VDDBU backup remain open under Q12. |
| 2026-06-23 | **SD write buffers must be cache-line aligned (`__ALIGNED(CACHE_LINE_SIZE)`, 32 B) or writes drop to ~10x-slow single-sector transfers.** First `sd bench` on hardware: read 5.7 MB/s but write **0.4 MB/s** (verify ok). Root cause is in the generated FatFs diskio (`system/fs/fat_fs/hardware_access/diskio.c`): for a buffer not aligned to `CACHE_LINE_SIZE`, `disk_write` bounces through a `SYS_FS_FAT_ALIGNED_BUFFER_LEN`-sized (512 B = **one sector**) buffer in a loop → single-sector writes; `disk_read` for the same unaligned buffer still does a single multi-block transfer (reads sector 0 via the bounce buffer, the rest directly into the app buffer) → stays fast. Hence the write-slow / read-fast asymmetry with one shared buffer. Fix: aligned the bench scratch (`storage.c s_chunk`). **Confirmed on hardware: write 0.4 → 2.9 MB/s (7x), read 6.7 MB/s, verify ok.** 2.9 MB/s already clears the default recording load (§4.6.3: ~1.05 MB/s at 1 keyframe/s) with ~2.7x headroom; the §4.6.2 "≥5 MB/s" was a ceiling the format sits well under, not the workload. | The `sd bench` smoke test earned its keep on day one — caught a silent 14x write penalty before any recorder was built on top. **System-wide implication:** every buffer handed to `SYS_FS_FileWrite` for throughput (M4 keyframe DDR buffers, config/catalog/results writers) wants 32 B alignment, else it silently runs at single-sector speed. The ISC capture buffers are DMA-aligned already, but app-side staging buffers are the risk. Speed-mode-DEFAULT is *not* the bottleneck (reads prove the link); revisit High-Speed mode only if aligned writes still fall short of the §4.6.2 ≥5 MB/s target. |
| 2026-06-22 | **SD bring-up = manual `SYS_FS_Mount` now + an `sd` console command; automount deferred to a SYS_FS event handler.** New in-tree `storage/storage.{h,c}` module (added via `user.cmake`, MCC kept out) owns the FAT mount lifecycle (`Storage_Mount`/`Unmount`/`IsMounted`, `/dev/mmcblka1` → `/mnt/marvin`, bounded retry since the polled SDMMC driver needs a moment post-insert) and the bring-up diagnostics (`Storage_DiagInfo`/`List`/`Bench`). Console gains one `sd <info|ls|bench|mount|unmount>` command (binding-table row + `cmd_sd`, dispatch into the storage diag API via a `storage_print_fn` adapter so storage doesn't depend on the console). `Storage_Initialize` runs in `APP_Initialize` (state only — no pre-scheduler I/O). **Code-complete; pending Greg's MPLAB build + on-hardware test.** MCC config observed: SDMMC card-detect = `DRV_SDMMC_CD_METHOD_POLLING`, `isFsEnabled=true`, speed `DRV_SDMMC_SPEED_MODE_DEFAULT`, 4-bit; SYS_FS `AUTOMOUNT=false`, `VOLUME_NUMBER=1`, `MAX_FILES=1`. | SDMMC + FatFs failures are hardware/clock/format/cache-coherence issues; a standalone smoke test isolates them from the first real consumer (M4 record task / catalog loader), which would otherwise debug mount + FatFs + its own logic at once. Console-command form (vs throwaway code) is near-free in the embedded-cli idiom and stays a permanent field diagnostic, like `t1s`/`nodes`. `sd bench` (write→read-back→verify, integer MB/s) directly validates the ≥5 MB/s the recording format assumes (spec §4.6.2) **before** building the recorder — note speed mode is DEFAULT (not High-Speed), so that's the lever to pull if the number falls short rather than reshaping the keyframe stride. Manual mount needs no further MCC regen (we just did one); the `Storage_Mount` seam is isolated so the production path swaps to an automount event handler (correct hotplug for the removable card) without touching the diagnostics. `MAX_FILES=1` is fine for sequential catalog/art loads; bump if concurrent opens are needed. |
| 2026-06-22 | **SD-card data architecture decided (spec §4.6.2 layout, §4.7, §4.8.3, new §4.8.6/§4.8.7).** The flash-vs-SD line is drawn by *what the data is coupled to*. **Flash (compile-time):** recognizer signatures (centroids, menu baselines, song bitmap templates, thresholds) **and** the menu graph — all *algorithm-coupled* (only valid with the exact grid/sampling/normalization compiled into `gameplay_classify.c`/`gameplay_select.c`), generated by `tools/gameplay/gameplay/export_c.py` into `gameplay_metadata.h`. **Recognition never reads the SD card.** **SD (FAT32, `/marvin/`):** `config.json` (resolves Q9 — config on SD, not flash); `games/<game>/songs.json` song-catalog *labels* keyed by the recognizer's `(setlist,index)` (`gp_song_t`), carrying a `metadata_version` stamp so the UI can warn on a stale catalog; `games/<game>/art/<setlist>-<NN>.{jpg,png}` album artwork (named by the same key, decoded once at startup into a static DDR cache via Legato's already-enabled JPEG/PNG decoders — no new library); `players/results.jsonl` append-only per-player results; and `recordings/`. **Rejected: moving recognition data to SD** — it's an algorithm-coupled build artifact, not user-meaningful, and a stale SD blob would silently misclassify; keeping it compile-time removes that failure mode entirely. New open Qs: Q12 (results timestamps — enable SAM9X75 RTC for wall-clock vs `session`+`frame_epoch`; player identity) and Q13 (artwork target dimensions + cache pixel format). **Doc-only change; no firmware** — all loaders/writers build on the M4 FAT mount (results also need M9 Phase 3 score readers). | This is the design Greg raised (song metadata + artwork + per-player results on SD; could recognition be data-driven from a file?). Recognition turned out to already be data-driven, but coupled to the C algorithm, so the right split is the one spec §4.8.3 always anticipated: compile-time tables for the algorithm-coupled graph/signatures, SD for the user-meaningful, independently-changing data. Keying catalog/artwork/results by the stable `(setlist,index)` the recognizer emits means a missing/stale card degrades gracefully (Unknown song / placeholder) and never breaks recognition. Legato's runtime JPEG+PNG decoders are already enabled (`legato_config.h`), so no TJpgDec; the internal RTC exists (32.768 kHz + VDDBU backup on the Hybrid board) but is currently disabled, so wall-clock timestamps are an opt-in cost (MCC regen + backup-cell), not assumed. |
| 2026-06-17 | **T1S actuator command now targets the guitar node (id 2), not the fretboard (id 1).** `net/t1s/t1s_link.c`: added `T1S_NODE_GUITAR` (id 2, MAC `02:00:00:00:00:02`, `detector_id = T1S_NO_DETECTOR`) to the node table; the service-task command flush now sends to `node_for_type(T1S_NODE_GUITAR)`. Renamed `T1SLink_SendToFretboard` → `T1SLink_SendToGuitar` (one caller, `fretboard_link.c`). The fretboard node (id 1) stays in the table as the **detector/RX** source for when it moves to T1S; only the actuator TX target moved. | The actuation role split out to the dedicated `guitar` node, which is now proven on the bus (guitar G1: `LAN8651 up … PLCA follower id=2/8`). marvin was still addressing its 1-byte command to the fretboard node; with the guitar running non-promiscuous (MAC-filtered), commands had to be re-pointed at `02:..:02` to actuate it. Single guitar today, so the target is `node_for_type(T1S_NODE_GUITAR)`; an **active-guitar selector** (mirror of `Detector_SetActive`) is deferred until multiple guitar nodes share the bus. RX/ADC over T1S is unaffected (no T1S detector node exists yet — the real fretboard is still UART). The "FretboardLink" module keeps its name for now (broader rename deferred). |
| 2026-06-16 | **T1S inter-node link — scope locked to *multi-node*, marvin-side first; gated on two prerequisites (held).** Multidrop is now a first-class requirement (several guitars + phototransistor fretboard-detector nodes sharing one PLCA pair), not just headroom: **design the node-ID / addressing / demux + PLCA-coordinator abstraction for N nodes now, physically bring up the single marvin↔fretboard link.** Addressing (static, no discovery — keeps the M0+ node dumb): marvin = PLCA **coordinator, ID 0**, MAC `02:00:00:00:00:00`; node *k* = PLCA **ID k**, MAC `02:00:00:00:00:0k`; one custom ethertype (~`0x88B5`, TBC); a static **node table** maps src MAC → `detector_id` (the single fretboard stays `detector_id = 1`); existing 17-byte/1-byte frames ride inside the payload unchanged. Driver: **adapt [`MicrochipTech/oa-tc6-lib`](https://github.com/MicrochipTech/oa-tc6-lib)** (Greg adding as a subproject) with thin Harmony FLEXCOM-SPI + FreeRTOS glue — **not** from-scratch. **Correction: marvin is FreeRTOS + Harmony, not "bare-metal"** as earlier notes said — but still has no netdev, so the own-TC6-driver conclusion stands; integration is a service task woken by an `IRQ_N` PIO interrupt (reuse `fretboard_link.c`'s `xSemaphoreGiveFromISR` pattern). Keep FLEXCOM1 UART **in parallel**, select via a `MARVIN_FRETBOARD_TRANSPORT={UART,T1S}` build flag (default UART; T1S opt-in for bring-up). `FretboardLink_{Initialize,Send,IsConnected}` stays public; `Send` gains a node target. Hand-authored code lands in `default/src/net/t1s/` (out of the MCC `config/` tree, added via `user.cmake`). **Two hard prereqs before any firmware code (both held):** (1) a free FLEXCOM regenerated in **SPI-master mode in MCC** wired to the LAN8651 EVB pins — *no SPI PLib exists today*, only USART/TWI; (2) `oa-tc6-lib` on disk as a subproject. Bring-up gate 1 = control-transaction read of the LAN8651 device-ID over SPI. Plan file: implementation phases + verification. | The link runs on a LAN8651 EVB/Click wired to the SAM9X75 Curiosity *now*, so target runnable code. Designing addressing for N up front (even with one physical node) means a second guitar/detector joins with only a node-table row, not a transport refactor — the whole point of choosing PLCA over point-to-point. `oa-tc6-lib` **adapted, not rewritten**, for proven chunk/credit logic and the "OA SPI driver scales across the family (Cortex-A5 + Cortex-M0+)" demo story. Build-flag parallelism keeps the working UART link alive while T1S is unproven, matching the cut-over-when-solid approach. `net/t1s/` outside the generated tree follows the same MCC-isolation discipline as `perf_log`/`console` (no re-apply patches). Resolves the topology open item (→ PLCA coordinator on marvin) and the driver/UART-fallback open items from the 2026-06-09 direction note. |
| 2026-06-14 | **Full-frame snapshot capture = on-demand host→device perf-log command (`PERF_CMD_SNAPSHOT`, id 0x02) that streams the current frame back as banded `STRIP` records (new kind `PERF_STRIP_SNAPSHOT`=2).** First step toward the M9 gameplay engine (spec §4.8): get real GH3 screens off the device so screen/number/letter detection can be prototyped offline. `perf_log_rx.c` latches the request; the perf-drain task copies the live frame once into a static 1280×720×3 staging buffer (coherent — the 4-deep nocache ring laps in ~50 ms, less than the ~0.4–1 s emit), then writes full-width horizontal bands (≤ `PERF_STRIP_MAX_BYTES`, ~30 rows @480p / ~16 @720p) **directly via `PerfLogSinkCdc_WriteFramed`, bypassing the 6-slot strip pool** (which would drop most of 16–45 bands) and naturally paced by the wire. The final band sets `PERF_STRIP_FLAG_LAST` (a repurposed strip `reserved[0]`); host groups bands by `frame_epoch` and finalizes on LAST. Snapshot emit is **not** gated by the STRIP type mask — the command is the request. Schema bumped 4 → 5 (firmware + `tools/marvin-perf` in lockstep). Host: `marvin-perf snapshot --port … --out …` reassembles and saves `.bgr` + `.json` sidecar (+ `.png` via Pillow). **Detection library to be built host-side in Python first, then proven algorithms ported to a firmware C `gameplay_engine` module — neither built yet (out of scope here).** | Reused the existing `STRIP` wire path + host→device command channel rather than inventing a new transport or waiting on M4 SD recording (not started): a snapshot is just a full frame sliced into bands the host decoder already understands `(x,y,w,h,kind)`. One USB cable, scriptable. Drain-task + staging-copy chosen over the strip pool to guarantee a tear-free frame and zero drops without resizing the pool. Full-resolution (not downsampled) because the point is OCR/glyph analysis of fixed GH3 assets. Brief (~0.4–1 s) drain-task monopolization is acceptable for an on-demand debug capture; a dedicated low-prio task is a possible later refinement. |
| 2026-06-12 | **Serial operator console = in-tree module on FLEXCOM2 @ 115 200 using vendored embedded-cli; fretboard link moved FLEXCOM2 → FLEXCOM1 @ 500 000.** New `console/console.{h,c}` (spec §4.9) owns the FLEXCOM2 USART plib directly in ring-buffer mode (modeled on `fretboard_link.c`): a task drains the RX ring on a 1-byte read-threshold notification, feeds [embedded-cli](https://github.com/funbiscuit/embedded-cli) (vendored verbatim at `third_party/embedded-cli/`, MIT, pinned commit `8e796cb`), and dispatches a static binding table (`status`, `detect`, `active`, `timing`, `manual`, `fret`, `strum`) into the existing `Detector_*`/`TimingPipeline_SetEnabled`/`ManualControl_*` setters. embedded-cli runs in static-allocation mode (fixed `CLI_UINT` buffer → no malloc; `embeddedCliRequiredSize` asserted at init). Sources + include dir added via `user.cmake`; `Console_Initialize()` called from `APP_Initialize`. Fretboard link is a mechanical `FLEXCOM2_USART_*` → `FLEXCOM1_USART_*` rename in `fretboard_link.c` (the only non-generated consumer). | Rejected Harmony `SYS_CONSOLE`/`SYS_COMMAND`: MCC-config-coupled, lands in regenerated files (we already re-apply ~10 patches per regen — no 11th), heavy indirection for "read a line, split it, dispatch." The hand-rolled-module + small-lib path matches the repo ethos (`perf_log`, `log`, `fretboard_link` all own their transport directly, fully static, MCC kept out via `user.cmake`). Console deliberately on its **own channel** (FLEXCOM2), separate from DBGU log/printf chatter, so neither pollutes the other. Channel split decided by Greg's board mod: console = FLEXCOM2 @ 115 200, fretlink = FLEXCOM1 @ 500 000 (the FLEXCOM1 6 Mbps in commit `b236806` was only a bring-up test). **Prerequisite (Greg, in MCC):** regenerate FLEXCOM1 into ring-buffer mode @ 500 000 and FLEXCOM2 @ 115 200 (TX ring ≥ 512 B for `status` dumps); code references the ring-buffer plib API and won't build until then. Also corrected a stale spec row: the log channel is the dedicated **DBGU** peripheral, not "FLEXCOM4" (confirmed by `log.h`: `printf → xc32_monitor → DBGU`). |
| 2026-06-11 | **NAND programming from macOS = u-boot RAM-loaded over JTAG, as a PMECC-aware flasher; the boot region needs a hand-prepended PMECC header.** New flow in [`openocd/program-nand.{sh,cfg,md}`](../openocd/program-nand.md): the init-and-stop at91bootstrap brings up DDR (as `load-ram`), then we `load_image` the at91bootstrap + `harmony.bin` + `u-boot.bin` into DDR and jump to u-boot; u-boot's `atmel_nand` (`nand erase`/`nand write`) burns NAND with PMECC. Built `u-boot.bin` from `sam9x75_curiosity_pro_nandflash_defconfig` (macOS: `gmake`, OpenSSL via the **environment** so dtc's bundled-libfdt include isn't clobbered). **Validated on hardware: marvin boots standalone from NAND** (RomBOOT → at91bootstrap copies `0x100000` from `0x40000` → marvin runs, HDMI captured). | Closes the 2026-06-09 "macOS-native NAND is Phase-2 R&D" item. OpenOCD's `at91sam9` NAND driver can't do PMECC, so it can't write a bootable boot region; u-boot's driver uses the same PMECC IP and its DT (hw ECC, **8-bit/512, eccOffset 152** = `oobsize − 8·13`) matches both the chip's ONFI (`nand info`: 4096/256/8/512) and the at91bootstrap header generator — so `nand write` is RomBOOT-correct. **Two non-obvious gotchas, both bench-found:** (1) The SAM9X7 ROM reads page 0 *without ECC* expecting a NAND header — 52 copies of a 32-bit word, code at offset `0xD0` (DS60001813D p.159/162). SAM-BA's `writeboot` synthesizes this; a plain `nand write` does not, so headerless writes give RomBOOT-only (no boot). Fix: prepend the header with `binaries/make-pmecchead.sh` (wraps at91bootstrap `addpmecchead.py`) using the **real** chip params → word `0xc2605007`; *not* that script's SAM9X7 defaults (2048/64/4), which don't match this board. (2) u-boot's default `bootcmd` reads the empty kernel partition into `0x22000000`/`0x21000000`; a 3 s autoboot overwrites staged blobs with `0xff` before you can program. Fix: stage into `0x21100000`/`0x21200000` (outside its read windows) and stop autoboot on connect. QSPI path still SAM-BA (no OpenOCD driver). |
| 2026-06-09 | **Display refresh raised 50 → 55 Hz on the Curiosity Hybrid board.** XLCDC LVDSPLL set to `Mul=37, Frac=2738881, DivPMC=2` (451.84 MHz LVDS, 64.55 MHz pixel, 55.00 Hz) in `le_gfx_driver_xlcdc.yml` + emitted `plib_xlcdc.c`. | The board port (same 10.1″ panel + cable) widened the LVDS signal-integrity margin enough to move the yellow-tinge cliff up from the original board's 52–54 Hz window to ~57–58 Hz. Re-swept on hardware: 60 Hz mild yellow (was *severe*), 57 Hz clean at rest but cable-handling-sensitive (the new "closest clean"), 55 Hz clean and handling-robust. Picked 55 Hz for ~3.5% margin under the 57 Hz edge — same back-off-from-the-cliff logic that chose 50 Hz before, now +5 Hz. Improvement is purely transmit-side (routing/connector/power + USB-host EMI aggressor gone); root cause + overlay-only signature unchanged. Full sweep in `yellow_tinge.md` §9. |
| 2026-06-09 | **No-power-cycle dev loop = correct nSRST reset config + single-session `load-ram.sh`.** Adopted the known-good Microchip class-material reset machinery into `sam9x75-chybrid.cfg`: push-pull `nSRST -data 0x20 -oe 0x20`, `reset_config srst_only srst_pulls_trst`, `adapter srst delay 500` + `pulse_width 500`, a `reset-init` event (disable WDT + MMU/caches), and an SRAM work area at `0x300000`. (The example also defines `nTRST` on AD4, but AD4 is N/C on this board per the schematic, so nTRST is omitted — verified the loop still works without it.) `marvin_load_ram` now does `reset init` → at91bootstrap (DDR) → load + run marvin, one session. Repeatable with **no physical power-cycle** (validated twice back-to-back from a running marvin). | **Earlier "needs a power-cycle / DDR3L not re-runnable / nSRST can't reset a running marvin" were all misdiagnoses of one root cause: our hand-rolled reset config never actually reset the SoC.** `-oe`-only didn't drive AD5; even when driven, the missing `adapter srst pulse_width` meant the pulse was far too short to reset. So `reset halt` left marvin running (`MMU enabled`), at91bootstrap ran against the live MMU/clocks, and DDR came up scrambled. The example's push-pull `-data` + 500 ms pulse + `srst_pulls_trst` resets reliably (incl. a running marvin); `reset init` then disables the WDT so it stays halted; the reset re-inits the MPDDRC so at91bootstrap brings DDR up fresh each run. The work area fixes the slow `No working memory` load path. Discarded two same-day workarounds for the broken reset: a `soft_reset_halt` preserve-DDR reload (core-only, didn't stop DMA → SYSCOMP timeout) and a software-PROCRST (`RSTC_CR=0xA5000001`) two-session loop (worked, but unnecessary once the reset config was correct). |
| 2026-06-09 | **VS Code debug = Native Debug or Microsoft cppdbg attach (not cortex-debug); macOS SD prep via `make-sdcard.sh`; J-Link not adopted; QSPI stays SAM-BA.** `.vscode/{launch,tasks}.json` start OpenOCD (`:3333`) and attach `arm-none-eabi-gdb`. | cortex-debug is Cortex-M-centric; a generic gdb front-end fits ARM926. `arm-none-eabi-gdb` (Arm GNU 15.2) is already installed at `/opt/homebrew/bin`. J-Link is a separate probe with its own flashloaders (no clear Microchip-supplied SAM9X75 external-NAND/QSPI loaders) → no win over the working FTDI+OpenOCD, so kept only as a fallback. QSPI has no OpenOCD driver for SAM9X7 → stays SAM-BA-on-Linux (or SD); macOS-native NAND via OpenOCD `at91sam9` is Phase-2 R&D. |
| 2026-06-08 | **Wired nSRST + RTCK into the OpenOCD config; `reset halt` and adaptive clocking verified.** Channel-A reset/clock pinout (from Greg/schematic): AD5=nRST, AD7=RTCK, AD4/AD6=N/C (no nTRST). Added `ftdi layout_signal nSRST -oe 0x0020` (open-drain), `reset_config srst_only srst_open_drain srst_nogate`, `adapter srst delay 300`. Verified on `W16-2026-413`: `reset halt` drops the core into boot ROM (pc=0x00000044, ARM, cpsr=0x…d3) vs the running-firmware SRAM pc; `adapter speed 0` engages `RCLK (adaptive)` and still scans/halts. | Supersedes the `reset_config none` placeholder in the JTAG-bring-up entry below. nSRST as open-drain via `-oe` (drive low to assert, tristate to release — board pulls up) matches the SAM9 NRST electrical behavior. `srst_nogate` because the ARM926 TAP/EmbeddedICE stay alive across a system reset, so OpenOCD can keep JTAG up and catch the core immediately after release (confirmed: TAP re-scanned fine post-reset). No nTRST is fine — ARM9 TAP reset via 5×TMS works. RTCK/adaptive clocking left available but **not** the default (`adapter speed 1000` stays default for reproducibility; the SoC runs fast enough that fixed TCK is reliable). |
| 2026-06-08 | **Programmed the FT4232H's 93LC46B EEPROM with board identity + per-channel driver flags; keeping it despite macOS not honoring the VCP bit.** Image authored via [`firmware/marvin/ftdi/ft4232h-chybrid.conf`](../ftdi/ft4232h-chybrid.conf): strings `Microchip` / `SAM9X75 cHybrid` / serial `W16-2026-359`, and per-channel driver A=D2XX, B=D2XX, C=VCP, D=D2XX. Channel map (sch sheet 14): A=JTAG (MPSSE), C=DBGU console (populated via R149/R151), B+D unconnected (B's bridge resistors are DNP). Flashed with `ftdi_eeprom --flash-eeprom`; verified write read-back at the chip and re-enumeration of the new strings/serial. JTAG re-confirmed working afterward. | The board shipped with a **blank** 93LC46B (all `0xFFFF`), so all four channels enumerated as generic "Quad RS232-HS" serial ports. Goal was to stop the JTAG channel (A) and the unconnected channels (B/D) from appearing as `/dev/cu` ports by marking them D2XX. **Outcome: macOS 26 (Tahoe) `AppleUSBFTDI` ignores the per-channel VCP/D2XX EEPROM flag and still creates a serial node for all 4 interfaces** — the port-hiding goal is not achievable via EEPROM on macOS.
