## Session log

### 2026-08-08 — The boot freeze was a `LOG_*` from UDPHS ISR context; the log lock now refuses exception callers

Closes the previous day's hunt. The nesting detector added on 2026-08-07 fired on the first reproduction and named the culprit outright — **no JTAG, no forensics**:

```
PerfLog: MCC task 'USB_DEVICE_TASKS' not foun
*** LOG NESTED task=PerfDrain site=0x23f59fcc ***
PERF: USB device CDC configured
d
```

`xc32-addr2line` → `device_event_handler` at `perf_log_sink_cdc.c:294`. The split outer line is the proof of shape: the outer `LOG_INFO` had emitted 14 characters and was still holding the lock when the inner one arrived.

- **The mechanism, and why it looked impossible.** `device_event_handler` runs in **UDPHS ISR context**: `AIC` source 23 → `DRV_USB_UDPHS_Handler` → `F_DRV_USB_UDPHS_DEVICE_Tasks_ISR` → `pEventCallBack` = `F_USB_DEVICE_EventHandler` ([usb_device.c:1881](../default/src/config/default/usb/src/usb_device.c)) → `callBackFunc(USB_DEVICE_EVENT_CONFIGURED)` (:2848) → our handler → `LOG_INFO`. In an ISR `xTaskGetCurrentTaskHandle()` returns the **interrupted** task, so `xSemaphoreTake` blocked *that* task on the log mutex. When the interrupted task already held it (mid-`LOG_*`), a non-recursive mutex does not detect self-take — `vTaskPlaceOnEventList` put `pxCurrentTCB` on the mutex's own wait list, leaving it simultaneously `xMutexHolder` and waiter. It never ran again, never released, and every logger queued behind it until all tasks were blocked and the CPU sat in `prvIdleTask`.
- **It also explains the one contradiction the post-mortem could not resolve.** On the frozen board the saved PC was inside `xQueueSemaphoreTake` while the saved LR pointed just past `bl DBGU_Write` in `write()`. Those cannot belong to one call chain — and they don't: **PC is the ISR's, LR is VideoTask's own**, stitched together by `portSAVE_CONTEXT`. The "the word scan is lying" call was right; the reason was wrong.
- **Why the detector attributed it to the wrong task.** The marker named `PerfDrain` this time and the frozen board implicated `VideoTask` — same defect both times. Whichever task happens to be interrupted gets the blame, so *the task name in a `LOG NESTED` marker is not the culprit*; the `site` address is. Worth remembering before chasing a task that is merely the victim.
- **Fix, in two parts.** (1) `device_event_handler` no longer logs: the ISR-context handlers only bump `s_ev_configured` / `s_ev_deconfigured`, and `PerfLogSinkCdc_ServiceRx` — already called once per drain-task pass — reports the change from task context. Latency is one drain pass; the counters remain the record of how many, and a previously-silent "deconfigured" line comes along free. No new plumbing and no `perf_log.c` change, so the whole fix is one file. (2) `log_emit` now *enforces* the ISR contract rather than trusting it (decision-log row).
- **Tree-wide ISR audit, done by registration API rather than by name-guessing** (`*CallbackRegister`, `*EventHandlerSet`, `PIO_PinInterruptCallbackRegister`, plus the ISC hooks `on_frame_done` / `heo_frame_latch` which are wired differently): `perf_log_sink_cdc.c:293` was the **only** ISR-context `LOG_*` in the tree. `cdc_event_handler` was already correct — it uses `xSemaphoreGiveFromISR` / `portYIELD_FROM_ISR` — which is what makes the single lapse next door easy to miss.
- **Parallel-agent note.** `log.c` / `log.h` / `journal.md` were being edited concurrently by the agent building the activity-log screen (`ed91896`). Their `log_set_sink` layered cleanly on the recursive/bounded lock, so the ISR guard here was held back until that landed rather than risk clobbering it; the sink is skipped on the ISR path for the same reason it must not block or log.

### 2026-08-08 — Telemetry refresh raised to 2 Hz; publish cadence and averaging window separated

With `LEGATO_Tasks` at 3% on the bus screen (from 35%), Greg asked for the titlebar widgets and the bus stats screen to refresh at 2 Hz. Spending the optimization headroom on responsiveness rather than banking it.

The cadence itself is three constants:

- `titlebar.c`: `SAMPLE_MS` 1000 → **500**. `TICK_MS` stays 100 — it is the LED pulse's step, not the sample's.
- `screen_bus.c`: a new `REFRESH_MS 500u` now drives `bus_task`'s `vTaskDelay`, replacing the literal 1000.
- `health_monitor.c`: `HM_SUP_SAMPLE_MS` 1000 → **500**.

**The supervisor had to move too, or the CPU plot would have stair-stepped** — `HealthMonitor_CpuPermille()` is only recomputed by the supervisor's own sample, so polling at 2 Hz while it publishes at 1 Hz plots each reading twice: double the resolution carrying half the information. But halving the sample period also halves the *averaging window*, and Greg's read on the first attempt was that 500 ms is too short a window for the figure `health` prints — it reads jumpy.

Those are two independent knobs that had been welded together, so they were separated. **The supervisor now publishes every 500 ms but measures across the last `HM_SUP_WINDOW_N` (2) samples**, i.e. a 1 s window sliding in half-second steps: fresh enough to plot, steady enough to read. Successive readings overlap by half.

The implementation is a two-slot snapshot ring instead of a single "previous" table, and it needs no ageing pass or timestamps because of one observation: **the slot a sample is about to overwrite is the one taken `HM_SUP_WINDOW_N` samples ago, which is exactly the far end of its own window.** So `ref = &s_snap[seq % HM_SUP_WINDOW_N]`, diff against it, then overwrite it. Widening the window later is a one-constant change and costs one 352-byte slot.

Two values expressed in samples but *tuned in time* moved with it:

- `HM_SUP_SUMMARY_N` 10 → 20, holding one `SUP:` line per 10 s.
- runaway confirmation `starve_streak >= 2` → `>= HM_SUP_STARVE_N` (**3**). Because windows now overlap, N samples span `(N-1) * HM_SUP_SAMPLE_MS + window` — 3 covers 2 s, matching what two 1 s samples covered before. 2 would have tripped at 1.5 s.

**A consumer polling faster than the source now says so mechanically, not in a comment.** `HealthMonitor_CpuSeq()` counts publications, and `metric_sample()` appends CPU only when the count changes — keyed on the sequence, not the value, since two equal readings a second apart are still two samples. The plot therefore tracks the supervisor's true resolution whatever either period is set to, and this stops being a pair of constants that must be kept in agreement. It also fixed a present-day wart: the zeros `CpuPermille()` returns before the first reading no longer enter the history, so the CPU plot starts at its first real sample instead of scrolling a false flat line at zero off the left edge. Cost is that the CPU tile's value reads blank rather than `0` for the first second after the UI is revealed — and that first reading now arrives at 1 s rather than 2 s, since filling the window is faster.

`metric_sample()` returns a bitmask of which metrics gained a sample and `tiles_refresh()` takes it, so a tile whose metric did not move is not invalidated at all. A view change (`s_dirty`) still forces both, since a new instance's labels hold whatever the last one left in them.

**The bus simulator was integrating per-tick, not per-second.** `sim_advance()` did `tx += tx_rate` and `s_sim_uptime++` on every call — correct only while a call *was* one second. At 2 Hz the totals would have climbed at twice the TX RATE displayed next to them, and the uptime clock would have run at 2×. Fixed by giving it a time delta: `sim_advance(REFRESH_MS)` scales the accumulators, the CRC/SYM error probabilities (now in thousandths, so 4%/2% per second survives the truncation), and a new `s_sim_uptime_ms` accumulator that `s_sim_uptime` derives from. The simulator no longer cares what the refresh period is.

**Two labels became wrong.** The bus run chart's axis said `"<- 40s ago"`; 40 samples at 2 Hz is 20 s. And the titlebar's `PLOT_WINDOW` of 24 samples now spans 12 s instead of 24 s — the window is chosen for *pitch* (24 across 74 px ≈ 3 px each, the mockup's proportion; all 40 would be under 2 px and read as a comb), so the sample count is what's load-bearing and the time span follows. Both documented at the constants rather than left implicit.

**Measured on hardware.** Per-frame costs are unchanged, which is the result that matters — nothing in the paint paths regressed, so the whole change is a rate change:

```
titlebar probe                      bus probe
  fixed ~134 us/frame, 24 ns/px       PANEL   1280x800 = 166868 us  <- old refresh
  dot   12x12 =   302 us              REFRESH (targeted) = 19489 us
  plot  80x28 =  1994 us              gauge    76x44   =   1365 us
  card 158x42 =  6225 us              spark   358x204  =   8745 us
                                      txbar[0] 46x202  =   5686 us
```

The bus refresh at 19.5 ms twice a second is **~3.9% of one core**, so that screen's `LEGATO_Tasks` share goes ~3% → ~6% — the cost of the doubling, paid as expected. The titlebar splits: only the tiles half doubles, the 10 Hz LED pulse (302 µs/frame) is untouched by any of this.

One number to keep an eye on: the bus `spark` measured 8745 µs against 5450 µs before this change. The sparkline painter's cost tracks the polyline's *vertical excursion per column* (it narrows the row scan to what the candidate segments can reach), so it is genuinely data-dependent and the two runs plotted different random series — but the simulated feed's value distribution did not change, only its rate, so that does not explain a 60% jump on its own. Not chased. If it is stable across repeated `bus probe` runs rather than variance, it is real and unexplained.

### 2026-08-07 — Every bullet in the marvin UI was ~3px high: Legato centres the baseline, not the font box

Greg, after two failed fixes: *"you need to check your math because they all show high, even in the .built lists. It is NOT just a visual artifact of 2 lines."* Correct on both counts. The `.built` list has single-line items, so the two-line explanation I had offered was worthless — and the real cause was that **the baseline formula I had been reasoning from was wrong.**

**What Legato actually does.** Traced through the library rather than assumed this time:

| step | effect |
| --- | --- |
| `_leString_GetLineRect` | `rect.height = fontHeight` |
| `leStringUtils_KerningRect` | `height -= fontHeight; height += fontBaseline` → for one line, **`height == fontBaseline`** (descender dropped) |
| `leUtils_ArrangeRectangleRelative` | `LE_VALIGN_MIDDLE`: `rect_y = H/2 - rect.height/2` |
| `leStringRenderer_DrawString` | glyph at `rect_y + (fontBaseline - glyph.bearingY)` → `rect_y` **is** the glyph-box top |

So `rect_y = H/2 - fontBaseline/2` and `baseline = rect_y + fontBaseline`. I had `fontHeight` in place of `fontBaseline`, **wrong by half the descender** — 3px at `DejaVuSansMono_16`. That is why the model kept saying the dot was a half-pixel *low* while the panel showed it clearly high: the error and the correction pointed opposite ways.

**Why it hid for three rounds.** The mistake is invisible in the text itself — Legato positioned the glyphs correctly the whole time. Only things aligned from *my* arithmetic were off, with nothing looking broken. Lesson: for a vendor widget, read the draw path; a plausible centring formula in a comment is not evidence.

- **Fixed by construction, not by tuning.** `TEXT_BASELINE(h, base)` and `DOT_Y(h, base, xh, d)` in [`screen_system.c`](../default/src/ui/screens/system/screen_system.c) now derive every bullet from the font's own metrics, with `MONO{12,16,20}_{BASE,XH}` decoded from `le_gen_fonts.c`. `STORY_DOT_Y` is gone — there is no tuned constant left to drift.
- **Every dot in the UI was wrong, not just the story list** — all eight were box-centred, so this was one defect with eight instances rather than anything screen-specific. Net movement once the landmark was settled: system chip bullet +2, parts list +3, story list +2, bus NODE +2, bus STATUS +2, dashboard state LED +2, actuator LED +2, NO SIGNAL +1. **The parts-list bullet had been ~3px high on every node page since that screen was built.**
- **Two dots are deliberately left box-centred**, and should stay that way: the navigation footer dot answers a *two-line* block (a 16px caption over a 20px value), so it is centred on the block on purpose; and the titlebar status LED sits beside the Microchip logo *image*, with no text to align to.
- **The landmark took three tries, so record where it landed.** Row-box centre reads ~3px high (it ignores the descender). Pure **x-height middle** — `baseline - xHeight/2`, right for running lowercase — read 1px *low*, because these labels are caps- and digit-heavy (`PIC32CM6408PL10048`, `IDLE`, `NO SIGNAL`, `41,000 lines of...`) and their mass sits higher. What reads level is the **mean of the cap-height and x-height middles**, which the `- 1` in `DOT_Y` produces to within 0.5px for every DejaVu Mono 12–20 at diameters 6–9 — hence a constant, not a per-site tweak.
- **Macros moved to [`ui/ui_text_metrics.h`](../default/src/ui/ui_text_metrics.h)** now that three screens use them, carrying the Legato call chain, the `MONO*_BASE`/`_XH` table, and a note to re-run `font_metrics.py` after any font change.
- **Two things verified rather than assumed**, having been burned once already: the *button* skin centres text through the same `KerningRect` path a label does (so the actuator LED's formula is valid, and with no image set `VALIGN_MIDDLE` ignores that button's margins); and the three dashboard captions are `leTableString`, so their fonts come from the **MGS binding, not from C** — resolved with the skill's own `audit_strings_fonts.py` (`PLAYER_ROBOT_Status` and `ACTUATOR_*` are Bold_12, `VIDEO_NO_SIGNAL` is Mono_12). Those three constants are only right while the design keeps those bindings.
- **The skill's `REFERENCE.md` and `font_metrics.py` carried the wrong formula and are corrected** — that mattered more than the screen, since it had been committed as reference. `font_metrics.py` now prints an **`optical`** column (the cap/x mean, i.e. the target) plus `boxctr err` (how many px high a box-centred marker sits), so the next person reads the answer off the table instead of re-deriving it.

### 2026-08-07 — guitar's card stops claiming it plays; project card joins the "The X -" pattern

Greg: *"it is correct that the guitar is now just an indicator with LEDs at the buttons - it doesn't actually play."* The card said **"guitar is the board that actually plays"**, which was the one outright false sentence on the screen — the firmware has targeted the ATE_2026 board since 2026-07-23 and its output stage is active-high status LEDs, no Wii guitar (see [`firmware/guitar/SPEC.md`](../../guitar/SPEC.md) §1–2). Also dropped the *"a couple of milliseconds behind the call"* latency claim, which was never measured.

- **The tag and tagline stay exactly as they were** — "The hands - presses the buttons in perfect time". Greg's call, and a fair one: the lamps show which buttons are being pressed, so the phrase describes what a visitor sees. The prose now opens `"guitar is the hands."` so it picks the tag up instead of contradicting it.
- **The lamps are not a mock-up of the command path — they are the command path.** SPEC §1: the mask → GPIO mapping is shared across output-stage variants. So the prose closes on *"The same mapping drives a real controller's switches, unchanged"*, which stops the LED board reading as a downgrade and pairs with the second paragraph's "swappable". "Lamp" rather than "LED" in the prose, since lightshow's card owns the LED language.
- **An earlier draft added the software-open-drain detail** (assert = drive low, release = tri-state) as the replacement for the removed 5 V line. Rejected — *"the open drain gpio isn't interesting"*. Left both lists short rather than filling them: guitar is prose 8/8, built 5/8.

Separately, the project card's tag was the only one of the eight not opening `"The X - "` (it read "A robot band that plays…", tagline "The whole rig - …"). Now **"The band - plays Guitar / Hero all by itself"**, tagline **"The band - all seven boards, playing Guitar Hero by itself"** — "robot band" dropped because "The band - a robot band" stutters, and "all seven boards" ties the card to the screen headline. All eight tags and taglines now share the pattern; a check for that is cheap to re-run and worth doing after any copy edit.

### 2026-08-07 — Node card copy: guitar drops its 5 V claim, lightshow names MVIO

Greg removed `"5 V I/O drives the guitar's buttons with no level shift"` from guitar's parts list — that node drives open-drain into the controller, so the 5 V I/O headroom is not what makes it work and the line was selling the wrong feature.

Lightshow's is the card where that silicon feature *is* load-bearing, and it was under-sold: the old copy said "This MCU's 5 V outputs drive the strips directly", which states the symptom without naming the capability. Both its prose and its parts list now name **MVIO (Multi-Voltage I/O)** and, more importantly, say what it *is* — a second supply rail (`VDDIO2`), independent of `VDD`, powering a subset of the pins at its own voltage, 5 V here. Confirmed against the datasheet before writing: [§15.4.4.2](https://onlinedocs.microchip.com/oxy/GUID-DE09DA5A-1CBB-49A8-9DA0-B2EB94E57E56-en-US-11/GUID-23DA1B16-810A-4484-891F-37E103B6C940.html) (Dual Supply mode, `MVIO.VDDIO2CFG`) and [§40.3.1.3](https://onlinedocs.microchip.com/oxy/GUID-DE09DA5A-1CBB-49A8-9DA0-B2EB94E57E56-en-US-11/GUID-1161AB62-565B-4749-BD0E-26EE9A8CF15A.html) (VDDIO2 as a supply pin pair). The acronym is spelled out in both places — a visitor at the panel will not know it.

Lightshow is now at capacity on both lists (**prose 8/8, built 8/8**), so anything further on that card trades something out. Guitar's list has room, at 5/8.

### 2026-08-07 — guitar-pic story rewritten around an audit of how much of the repo the agent wrote

Greg: *"I want to focus on the extent of AI assisted coding. There is virtually zero human hand coding in this project."* Paragraph 1 kept, paragraphs 2–4 replaced by an emphasised lead-in and a list of measured figures. Builds clean.

**The audit lives in [`docs/ai-authorship-audit.md`](../../../docs/ai-authorship-audit.md), not here** — a dated snapshot with its own exclusion list, reproduction recipe and snapshot-history table, and the single source for the card. Headline at commit `6da9caf`: **41,116** lines of application C over 7 boards (241 files, 4 silicon families, 3 toolchains), **19,315** lines of Python in 7 host-side tools, **22,989** lines of specs/docs/journals, **626** commits and **110** recorded sessions across six months. Authored source is only **7.1 %** of the C in the repo; the rest is MCC/pack/CMSIS/FreeRTOS/Legato output. Card values are rounded **down** to the nearest thousand.

Three numbers stay off the card, and the audit doc says why: the 464 `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailers (the trailer only starts in May, so it floors rather than measures), the ~6.9 M transcript word count (`wc -w` counts echoed file contents, so it measures log volume), and the "fewer than 100 lines ever hand-typed" figure — **that one is testimony, not a count.** Git has one author across all 626 commits and the trailer attributes no lines, so nothing in the repo apportions authorship between the two hands. The kicker is therefore *"This is what it wrote:"*: it presents the magnitudes as evidence and makes no claim the counts cannot back. Two earlier drafts were rejected for the opposite failure — "Almost none of it was typed by a person" hedges, and "A human typed fewer than 100 of these lines" invites the arithmetic ("100 out of what?").

The **[`mgs-legato-design`](../../../.claude/skills/mgs-legato-design/) skill gets its own audit section** (§4) rather than being buried in the Python subtotal, because it is a different kind of authorship: tooling the agent wrote *for its own use*, to reach a GUI-only authoring database it otherwise could not edit at all. **3,396 lines — 2,675 Python across 17 scripts + 721 of prose — accreted over 15 commits in three days** (2026-08-04 → 08-06), from 2 scripts and 177 lines of prose. Two things the numbers show: 800 of the 2,675 lines are *read-only auditing* against 1,534 that mutate anything (the split round-trip's failure modes are all silent, so verification had to outweigh action), and the prose is a failure-mode list rather than API docs — the white-flashing-button scheme bug, RGB565 read-back, runtime glyphs being on loan from unrelated captions, `LE_*_WIDGET_ENABLED` compiling out a type when the last design instance is stripped. It earns a bullet on the card too. Growth curve reproducible from the recipe in audit §7.

**Card structure changed, so the geometry is worth knowing before editing it again.** `story[]` went 19 rows → 5 (the lead paragraph uses 4, so the spare row *is* the paragraph break); the emphasised line is its own `story_kicker` field because it takes a different font and scheme from the rows around it (`DejaVuSansMonoBold_18` in `SCHEME_NODE_PROJECT`); and the figures are `story_list[STORY_ITEMS]`, each a `{head, gloss}` pair. `gloss == NULL` gives a one-row item; a NULL `head` hides its dot, which is what keeps the other seven node entries clean under zero-initialisation.

- **Sizes.** Headlines are `DejaVuSansMono_20` (was 18), glosses `DejaVuSansMono_16`. The lead paragraph deliberately stayed at 18: at 20 the wrap drops to 52 columns and the paragraph would have needed rewrapping. Wrap limits are `PROSE_COLS` 57 / `STORY_HEAD_COLS` 51 / `BUILT_COLS` 61.
- **Vertical.** `STORY_ITEM_PITCH` 72 against a 56px item block leaves a 16px gap between items and 0 inside one (the rows abut, so a gloss reads as part of its headline). Five items land the last gloss bottom at **584 in a 620px card — the same 36px pad** the two-card shape uses. Derived from `STORY_MAX`, so the chain re-solves if the lead changes.
- **Dots read high. My first two explanations were both wrong; see the follow-up entry above (2026-08-07, "every bullet on the screen was 2.5–3.5px high") for the actual root cause** — Legato centres the *kerning* rect, whose height is `fontBaseline`, not `fontHeight`. I had blamed the row box, then the cap-height-vs-x-height distinction, and dismissed the `.built` list as a two-line-item visual artifact. It was none of those: the baseline formula I was reasoning from was simply wrong, so every number in this bullet's original version was off by half a descender. Kept only `STORY_DOT_D` 8 (was `CHIP_DOT_D` 6, to answer a 20px headline).
- **All of it generalised into the [`mgs-legato-design`](../../../.claude/skills/mgs-legato-design/) skill**, since it is Legato-wide rather than marvin-specific: a new `scripts/font_metrics.py` (parses `le_gen_fonts.c` → height / baseline / advance / cap / x-height / descender per font, and `--box H` solves baseline and both midpoints for a label of that height, flagging fonts that would clip), plus a *Vertical metrics* section in `REFERENCE.md` with the struct parse, the x-height alignment rule, the worked 2px example, and the box-too-short clipping rule. It is the one script in the skill that reads generated output rather than the zip — read-only, so it does not breach the never-edit-the-generated-tree rule. Bonus finding from the table: Bold matches Regular on every vertical metric at 14/16/18/20/24, but **the 12px pair does not** (Bold_12 is `h` 15 / `base` 11 against 16 / 12), so a weight swap at 12px shifts the baseline 1px.

### 2026-08-07 — Intermittent boot freeze "on mounting card" is a log-mutex self-deadlock in VideoTask

Greg: *"marvin has been freezing occasionally on boot. Usually a second attempt will get past the freezeup… Always happens approximately the same time although the amount of data getting out the log before freeze can vary. Splash screen always stops on mounting card."* Root cause confirmed on hardware by JTAG halt. **The SD card is not involved at all** — `MOUNTING CARD` is simply the note that happens to be on screen, and the LCDC keeps scanning DDR by DMA after the CPU stops, so the panel stays lit on the last frame.

- **What the freeze actually is.** `VideoTask` holds the `log.c` mutex *and* is blocked waiting for it. Four independent, ELF-offset-independent reads agree: `xMutexHolder == s_task_tcb` (VideoTask), `xStateListItem.pvContainer == &xSuspendedTaskList` (blocked, **no timeout**), `xEventListItem.pvContainer == &s_mutex->xTasksWaitingToReceive` (exact address match — waiting on *that* mutex), and `R4 == 0x267fe384` (the mutex handle, in the callee-saved register `xQueueSemaphoreTake` keeps its queue argument in). Everything downstream follows: the perf-log drain task queues behind it, then the boot task blocks in `LOG_INFO("STG: mounted …")` — **which is why that line never appears and the mount looks like the culprit**. With every logger blocked, all tasks are blocked and the CPU parks in `prvIdleTask`.
- **Why the log goes quiet rather than continuing.** Four lines *do* get out after the wedge (`PERF: USB device opened…`, `TC358743: init complete…`, `PerfLog: MCC task 'DRV_MAXTOUCH_Tasks' not found`, `TC358743: post`) — proof it is not a CPU-pinning interrupt storm. It then stops because nothing else has anything to print: the only periodic logger is the health monitor, and `HealthMonitor_NotifyReady()` is the *last* statement of the boot sequence, so it never arms. The varying amount of output between freezes is just how far the concurrent service tasks got.
- **Fix (this commit):** recursive + bounded log lock, see the decision-log row. The nesting is now survivable *and* self-reporting, which is the only way left to find the inner call site — see the open question below.
- **Four dead ends, all killed by hardware data rather than by reasoning — don't re-walk them.**
  1. **UDPHS/AIC boot interrupt-storm** (the [known bug](udphs_aic_boot_storm_bug.md)). Plausible: `DRV_USB_UDPHS_Initialize` leaves the controller attached with `IEN=0x10`, and the driver's `pEventCallBack` is only installed when the app calls `USB_DEVICE_EventHandlerSet` from the CDC sink — a wide no-client window ending right around the mount, where the ISR returns without acknowledging a level-sensitive source. **Killed:** the freeze happens *after* `PERF: USB device opened, waiting for host enumeration`, which is printed after the handler is registered. (Patch #13, the pre-AIC quiesce, is intact at `initialization.c:442`.)
  2. **SD mount stall.** `disk_checkCommandStatus` ([`diskio.c:59`](../default/src/config/default/system/fs/fat_fs/hardware_access/diskio.c)) spins unbounded with no timeout, which *would* defeat `Storage_Mount`'s deliberate 3 s bound. **Killed:** the CPU is in the idle task, so nothing is spinning — the boot task is *blocked*. (Still a real latent hazard; noted as an open question.)
  3. **VideoTask stack overflow.** Genuinely attractive, because the linker puts `s_task_stack` (0x1000) immediately above `s_task_tcb` (0x68) immediately above `s_mutex_buf` (0x50) — a downward overflow would scribble exactly the two structures that looked wrong, and stack words are full of plausible TCB pointers (`pvOwner`). **Killed:** `pxTopOfStack` = `0x267ff270` against `pxStack` = `0x267fe450` and a `0x1000` stack — only **480 of 4096 bytes used**, with the bottom 128 bytes still `0xa5a5a5a5`.
  4. **Reading the call chain off a raw stack-word scan.** Produced a coherent-looking `log_printf → log_vprintf → vprintf → … → write → xQueueSemaphoreTake` chain, but `DBGU_Write` is a pure polled TXRDY spin that cannot block on a semaphore, and saved LR (`write+40`) and saved PC (`xQueueSemaphoreTake+396`) cannot both belong to one live frame. **Lesson: `info symbol` on stale words inside printf's large frames invents plausible chains.** Trust struct field reads over word scans.
- **Process rule this cost us: archive the ELF with every capture.** `load-ram.sh` preserves nothing, and the rebuild for the MCC regen moved `.bss` by `0x70000` while leaving `.text` *almost* identical — `log_vprintf` landed at the same address but `write` shifted by 4 bytes. That is the worst case: offset arithmetic looks like it is working right up until it silently isn't.
- **Halt-and-inspect recipe** captured as [`../openocd/inspect-wedge.gdb`](../openocd/inspect-wedge.gdb), since reading TCB and mutex fields directly is what actually cracked this. Includes the ARM926 `portSAVE_CONTEXT` layout (`[0]`=`ulCriticalNesting`, `[1]`=SPSR, `[2..16]`=R0–R14, `[17]`=PC), which is not written down anywhere else.

### 2026-08-07 — The splash fade never faded: `XLCDC_SetLayerOpts(update=true)` does not wait for a frame

Greg: *"did we attempt to implement fade from splash screen to dashboard… I don't think it's working if so. Just instant switch."* It was implemented and it was mine to get wrong. Fixed, **confirmed on hardware**.

- **The blend was never the problem.** OVR1's blender is `SFACTC = A0·As` / `DFACTC = 1−A0·As` and `A0` is a real 8-bit multiplier at `OVR1CFG9[23:16]` with no separate enable bit — and the modal scrim already proves that exact equation works on this LCDC (§18.1: `out = src·ADEF + dst·(1−ADEF)` with `A0 = 255`). So ramping A0 is sound.
- **The bug was pacing, and the trap is that the vendor call *looks* like it waits.** `XLCDC_SetLayerOpts(…, update=true)` ends in `XLCDC_UpdateLayerAttributes`, which does two things that read as "block until live" and are neither: `WAIT_ATTRS_EQ(LCDC_ATTRS_SIP_Msk)` waits on **Synchronization In Progress**, a *clock-domain* handshake of a few LCD-clock cycles (the field's own description: "access to `LCDC_ATTRE` has no effect" while set) — **not** vsync; and the leading `while(XLCDC_REGS->LCDC_ATTRE);` polls a register the DFP header declares **write-only** (`__O`). So the call returns in well under a microsecond. All 17 alpha writes landed inside one frame, only the last (`A0 = 0`) ever latched, and the result was an instant cut.
- **My error, and worth recording as a process note:** the first version of `FadeOut` had `vTaskDelay(FADE_STEP_MS)` per step and would have worked. I removed it in the same pass that fixed the glitches, as a "the hardware already paces this, one call is one frame" tidy-up — an inference I never verified, supported by mis-citing the nav-drawer journal note about a busy-wait (that was the *canvas* update path, not this PLIB call). The lesson is narrow and concrete: **do not infer blocking semantics from a spin loop without reading what the flag means.** Both facts were one grep away in the DFP header.
- **The frame-accurate primitive, if one is ever needed:** `LCDC_ATTRS` carries per-layer update-status bits (mask `0x80000F0F`) and *those* clear at a frame boundary. Polling the layer's own bit would pace exactly; a delay just over one frame is adequate and cheaper, and a dropped step is invisible in a fade. Written up in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §12.1 so the next layer animation does not re-learn it.

### 2026-08-06 (evening) — Two splash glitches from the loading bar: an erase-then-draw race and scanout underrun

Greg: *"I notice splash screen glitches very occasionally while loading. It never did before loading bar… Is the entire bar redrawn every time it fills?"* Then, asked what the artifact looked like: *"both text blinks briefly and whole screen glitch occasionally… I think I've only seen the screen glitch happen towards the end of the load (e.g. when rendering screens)."* Two separate bugs; both fixed, **confirmed gone on hardware**.

- **The question was the right one, but the answer was a red herring.** Yes, the whole fill was redrawn every tick (`draw_fill` looped `0..w`, deliberately, so repaints were idempotent against the `s_track` backup). But that could not *tear*: interior columns were rewritten with **byte-identical** values (coverage 1.0 → `px_blend(track, rgb, 255)` → exactly `rgb`), and the left cap's SDF is independent of `w` (`dx = |px − w/2| − (w/2 − R)` collapses to `R − px` for small `px`), so only the ~4 columns at the leading cap ever changed value. A torn scanout cannot show a difference that isn't there.
- **Bug 1, the text blink: erase-then-draw over live scanout.** `draw_left`/`draw_right` did `art_restore` (memcpy the pristine art back) and *then* `GlyphBlit_Text`, as two passes over rows the beam was crossing. A frame caught between them shows the text **missing**. Estimated exposure ≈ (0.2–0.4 ms of unbuffered erase + 0.54 ms band transit) / 16.7 ms ≈ **5% per redraw**, and with ~140 counter ticks plus ~100 percentage changes that is ~10 blank frames per boot — which is exactly "very occasionally". Fix: compose the field in **cached** scratch and blit once, so a pixel only ever goes old-final → new-final. Both the write and the beam run top-to-bottom, so the worst case is old-above/new-below — invisible for a digit change. `art_restore` deleted; nothing needs a bare erase now.
- **Bug 2, the whole-screen glitch: LCDC scanout underrun, and Greg's timing observation is what identified it.** "Towards the end of the load" is the `RENDERING SCREENS` stage, where `paint_all_screens_once` has the 2D engine blitting every canvas — on top of OVR1's full-screen RGBA8888 splash at **245 MB/s** and BASE's RGB565 dashboard at **117 MB/s**. Into that, the ticker was issuing ~7200 px × 25 Hz = **180k strongly-ordered writes per second**: small in bytes (720 KB/s) but each one an unbuffered, un-coalesced DDR transaction serialized against everything else. Same bandwidth region that has knocked CSI-2 out of lock before (2026-07 note on the RGBA8888 dialog at ~174 MB/s). Fix: `draw_fill(from, to)` starts at `s_fill_w − BAR_R`, since only the cap's width of columns depends on the leading edge — **~48 px per tick instead of ~7200, 150× fewer transactions.**
- **Two bigger bandwidth levers identified and deliberately not taken** (the two fixes above were enough): **(1)** BASE is enabled by `bind_canvas(CANVAS_DASH, …, show=true)` *before* the paint, so the dashboard layer reads 117 MB/s through the whole paint window while completely invisible under the opaque splash — Legato paints a canvas regardless of its layer state, so the `show` could move to just before the fade (it must precede the fade, or the cross-dissolve has nothing to dissolve into). **(2)** The splash blob could be **dithered RGB565**: it is opaque and the fade uses the layer's *global* alpha (A0), not per-pixel alpha, so the alpha channel earns nothing — RGB565 halves 245 → 122 MB/s, halves the blob (4 MB → 2 MB, and the 263 ms QSPI load with it), and frees 2 MB of `ram_nocache`. Banding on the brushed-metal gradient is the risk, but `make-splash.sh` is an offline Pillow script and can dither at conversion time. Together those take the paint-window layer load from ~362 MB/s to ~122. **Reach for these** if any scanout artifact reappears, or before adding another overlay that draws into a live layer.

### 2026-08-06 (evening) — `node_art` moved `game/` → `ui/`

Pure move, no behaviour change: `git mv` both files, `#include "game/node_art.h"` → `"ui/node_art.h"` in `app.c` / `ui_manager.c` / `screen_system.c` (and its own), include guard `MARVIN_NODE_ART_H` → `UI_NODE_ART_H` to match the `UI_*` convention its new neighbours use (`ui/dashboard_feed.h`, `ui/titlebar.h`, `ui/song_detail.h`), one path in `user.cmake`, and `ui_compositor.md` §6.1. Builds clean; `harmony.bin` is byte-for-byte the same size, as a move should be.

- **The rule this establishes, written into §6.1:** an asset cache belongs beside the screens that consume it, not in the domain modules. `node_art` is keyed by **T1S PLCA node id** and read only by `screens/system` — nothing about it is game domain. `game_art` stays in `game/` because its key *is* game data, `(setlist, index)` from the recognizer. The only reason `node_art` was ever in `game/` is that it was bootstrapped from `game_art.c` and inherited its neighbourhood along with its shape.
- §6.1's layout block also gained the `gfx/` line it had been missing since those primitives were added. The rest of that section is still stale (its "current contents" list predates several screens) — left alone rather than turned into a drive-by rewrite.
- **Earlier journal entries still say `game/node_art.c`.** Deliberately not rewritten: they are dated records of what was true when written, and this entry is the forward pointer.

### 2026-08-06 (evening) — Splash bar sub-notes inside the artwork stage (and why they don't drive the bar)

Greg, after two hardware runs: *"might be nice to have a few more message updates within loading artwork. this might have been the 2 option."* It was — and his own log then argued for doing **less** than option 2 proposed. Committed the bar first (`7aacb66`), then this.

- **The log made the case for sub-notes overwhelming and the case for count-driving nonexistent.** `ART` was **18.36 s of a 23.36 s boot — 78% of the bar** behind one unchanging label. But the same log shows `LOADING ARTWORK 18360 ms (was 18351)`: a **0.05% prediction error** over 18 s. So the time interpolation across the stage is already excellent, and level 2's original pitch — drive the span off `files_done / files_total` — would have made the motion **worse**: small covers are 144×144 JPEG and large are 508×208 PNG, so progress-per-file is not progress-per-second and a count-linear bar would visibly change speed at the tier boundary. A bar should be linear in time. Counting is an information win, not an accuracy win.
- **So the counter is display-only.** `SplashProgress_SetNote(note, done, total)` overrides the stage label (`LOADING ALBUM ART 42/70`) and touches nothing in the progress model. No settings change, no version bump, no new stages — a far smaller change than the option as scoped.
- **`load_tier` was already shaped for this:** phase 1 collects the whole file list and closes the directory, phase 2 decodes with `n` known, so an exact total is available with no restructuring and no second walk. `NodeArt_LoadAll` has a fixed `PHOTO_N` and no walk at all.
- **Layering:** the loaders take a registered progress callback and report their own tier name (`"small"`/`"large"`) plus counts; `ui_manager` maps those to display strings. `game/` gains no UI vocabulary — same shape as `Video_SetFrameLatchCallback` / `UiManager_SetSplashShownCallback`.
- **Mount got its own note** by calling `Storage_Mount()` from the boot task before the loaders (it is idempotent, and both loaders already call it defensively). Worth 657 ms of the boot that was previously labelled "loading artwork".
- **Text is composed by hand** (`append_str`/`append_u32`, no `snprintf`) because the label is now rebuilt on every counter tick on the 768-word ticker task; and the label's erase box is now `max(outgoing, incoming)` width instead of a precomputed worst case, so a counter tick copies back tens of pixels of art rather than the whole left half of the band.
- **Noticed in passing, not acted on:** `NODEART: 0 of 7 board photo(s) loaded` — the board photos aren't on the card yet, so the system screen shows blank frames. The new note will make that obvious on screen (`LOADING BOARD PHOTOS 7/7` with nothing to show for it).

### 2026-08-06 (evening) — Splash bar milestones grounded in measurement (settings v2 → v3)

First hardware run looked right (Greg: *"it's looks pretty good on first run"*), then: *"but how do we ground the various milestones?"* — a fair challenge, because the five permille marks were my apportionment guesses, and there was no way to check them: `log.c` calls `vprintf` with **no timestamp prefix**, so no existing boot log carries a time. Greg took levels 0 + 1 of the three offered.

- **Level 0 — instrument.** `SplashProgress_SetStage` now logs `stage → elapsed ms (predicted ms, permille)`, and `_Calibrate` logs the full measured-vs-stored table. The grounding is now readable off a boot log instead of inferred.
- **Level 1 — the marks are derived, not chosen.** `settings_t` carries `boot_stage_ms[4]` (one per *work* stage) instead of a single `boot_ms`; `mark[i] = cum_ms[i] × 990 / total`, and within a stage the bar interpolates on `elapsed_in_stage / predicted_stage_ms`. The bar's **speed** now tracks what each stage costs rather than being uniform in time, which is the part a single total could never do — with one number, the bar necessarily crossed the 1–3 s artwork decode at the same rate as the 400 ms service spawn. The five constants demote to a compiled seed profile `{400, 3000, 700, 700}`, replaced after one boot.
- **The trap in this scheme, and why the last stage is special.** `READY` is **slack**, not work — it waits out `SPLASH_MIN_MS`. Storing its measured duration would invert the calibration: speed the work up and the idle wait *grows*, so the next boot would give the idle stage a bigger share and train the bar to crawl through the stages that actually do something. So only the four work stages are stored and the slack stage is derived as `max(Σwork, min_hold) − Σwork` (0 when the work overruns the hold). Everything else falls out of that: the total is no longer stored at all, because it *is* `max(Σwork, min_hold)` — which is exactly what the old `boot_ms` measured.
- **Second-order effect worth watching on hardware:** with work at ~3.9 s against a 5 s floor, `READY` legitimately owns ~20% of the bar, so the last fifth is spent crawling through an idle wait. That is honest, but it is also an argument for **lowering `SPLASH_MIN_MS`** now — the 5 s floor exists so "a fast boot doesn't flash the splash away", and a progress bar that fills to 100% is a better answer to that than a fixed wait. Not changed; noted for when the measured profile is in.
- **Record v3** (12 B). Version bumped again before v2 ever left Greg's board, so the only cost is one more one-time backlight reset. A compile-time check (negative-array typedef, matching the `splash_fits_qspi_region` idiom) ties `SETTINGS_BOOT_STAGES` to `SPLASH_STAGE_COUNT − 1`, so adding a stage cannot silently outgrow the record.
- **Also answered (no code):** Greg asked why `node_art` sits in `game/`. It shouldn't — board photos keyed by **T1S PLCA node id**, consumed only by `screen_system.c`; it landed there because it was bootstrapped from `game_art.c` (same dir-walk → fixed-slot → `leImage*` shape). `game_art` belongs in `game/` because its key *is* game data, `(setlist,index)` from the recognizer. Target is `ui/node_art.{c,h}` — mechanical (4 includes + `user.cmake` + `ui_compositor.md` §6.1), deferred to its own commit until the in-flight system screen lands rather than moving a file that work is actively including.

### 2026-08-06 (evening) — Splash loading bar: pre-Legato glyph blitting, calibrated progress, alpha fade

Greg: *"I'd like to implement a loading bar on the marvin splash screen… note that this is probably before legato graphics is running so we may need to implement from scratch. I'm also thinking that we can make it self calibrating by timing the boot and storing in settings."* Then, on the text: *"do we have legato functions that could draw into a buffer even if the entire stack isn't up? We could conceivably draw on overlay if don't want on RGBA8888?"* Design in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §12.1; decision log has the reasoning. **Builds clean per-TU but the tree does not link** — `ui_manager.c` and `screen_system.c` both fail on `Marvin_PANEL_SYSTEM`, which is the *other* in-flight item awaiting an MGS Generate (entry below), not this work. Nothing seen on hardware yet.

- **"From scratch" turned out to be half right.** Legato's font *data* layer is fully usable with the stack down — `leFont_GetGlyphInfo` is a linear search over a `const` glyph table with no renderer, globals or init, and the 8bpp AA coverage bitmap is at `font->base.header.address + glyph->dataOffset`, `dataRowWidth` bytes per row, one alpha byte per pixel, placed at `x + bearingX`, `top + (baseline − bearingY)` (read out of `legato_string_renderer.c`). So the bar draws real `DejaVuSansMono_12` text: no hand-rolled 5×7 table, no new asset, no MGS change. New `ui/gfx/glyph_blit.c` is ~80 lines and sits beside `aa_corners.c`/`gradient.c` for the same reason those exist.
- **The overlay idea fails on timing, not feasibility.** A Legato layer-screen can't paint until `init_screens()` — after `GameArt_LoadAll`/`NodeArt_LoadAll` — so it would miss the 1–3 s the bar is most needed for. Writing into `s_fb` costs nothing extra since it is already `.region_nocache`.
- **Two backdrop copies make every repaint idempotent** (~220 KB in cached `ram`): pristine art for the band `y 724..763`, plus the rendered track. Text erases to the art; the fill and its AA leading cap draw onto the track. Without them a growing bar re-blends its own output and the caps darken.
- **The capsule's distance field is nearly free at this size.** Height 8, radius 4 ⇒ the straight section is fully covered on *every* row (the SDF's `min(max(dx,dy),0) − r` collapses to `−r`), so `sqrtf` only runs in the two 4-px cap boxes — ~64 pixels a frame instead of 9216.
- **Ticker task, because `configUSE_TIMERS` is 0** in the MCC-owned FreeRTOSConfig (and it is not ours to edit). Static task, 25 Hz, **priority 3** — it has to be above the boot task's 2, which spends most of the window blocked in the art decode and the render-idle wait. Repaints only on a real change (fill pixel width or a changed string), and `_Complete()` stops it and waits for it to exit so the framebuffer has one writer again before the final draw.
- **Calibration bookkeeping worth remembering:** the measured span runs splash-shown → reveal decision, so it *includes* the `SPLASH_MIN_MS` hold and *excludes* the 100% dwell and the 300 ms fade. Measure past the fade and every boot would store a slightly larger number than the last.
- **Settings record is now v2** (`boot_ms` + an explicit `reserved1` so there is no implicit padding before the crc). `record_valid` now also tests `version`, which makes future bumps explicit instead of relying on the crc offset moving. Expect one `SETTINGS: no valid record, defaults` on the first boot after flashing, and backlight back at 50%.

### 2026-08-06 — QSPI splash corruption traced to non-uniform Block-Erase; partition map remapped

Greg: *"I'm trying to program qspi flash but getting error"* — `program-qspi.sh splash` failed its `cmp.b` verify, and on boot the new splash showed a corrupted band near the top. **Not built or flashed yet** (Greg builds in MPLAB); the fix is a layout change, so it needs a firmware rebuild + NAND flash before the new splash offset is live.

- **The one-byte proof.** `cmp.b` reported flash `0x78` vs staged `0x79` at offset `0x2001`. A page-program can only clear bits, so no write can turn the source's `1` into a `0` — the readback had to be `old AND new`, meaning that region was **never erased**. That ruled out the plausible-but-wrong suspects (flaky DDR staging, QSPI read integrity, block protection) without touching the board: protection would have left *pure* old data, and a bad DDR or read path wouldn't be constrained to clearing bits.
- **Root cause.** `SST26VF064B` Block-Erase (`D8h`) covers 8, 32 or 64 KiB **depending on address**: four 8 KiB blocks + one 32 KiB at each end, 64 KiB only mid-array. u-boot's spi-nor uses a flat 64 KiB `erase_size`, so `sf erase 0 0x3f0000` erased `0x0–0x1FFF`, then advanced to `0x10000` and reported `Erased: OK` — `0x2000–0xFFFF` (56 KiB) stayed programmed. Confirmed against DS20005119K §3.0 and the Block-Protection table (whose 8 KiB granularity at both ends mirrors the erase geometry).
- **Why it matched the symptom exactly.** `0x2000–0xFFFF` ÷ 5120 B/row = rows **1.6–12.8** — a ~11-row band at the top. ANDing the two renders alters 37,717 of 57,344 bytes at mean delta 24.6/255 (~10% brightness noise), which reads as corruption rather than a stale image. And every 4th byte is `0xFF` (the X channel, 16384/16384 checked in the low 64 KiB), which is why the first mismatch landed on `0x2001` and not `0x2000`.
- **Why it only surfaced now.** The first-ever splash write went onto a blank part, where a missed erase is invisible; assets at `0x400000` are entirely mid-array. It took a *second* write to the same low offsets to expose it.
- **Fix: remap rather than work around** (decision log). Splash → `0x010000`, assets top → `0x7F0000`, both non-uniform bands reserved, host window `[0x010000, 0x7F0000)` declared in `qspi_layout.h` and enforced by the script. `program-qspi.sh` now also refuses non-64 KiB-aligned offsets — the `raw` mode could previously aim anywhere and earn an `ERROR -22` at best, silent corruption at worst. Guard logic exercised across the boundary cases off-hardware (old offset 0 refused, assets-to-exactly-`0x7F0000` allowed, one byte more refused, unaligned refused).
- **Also added:** a compile-time check in `screen_splash.c` that `sizeof s_fb <= QSPI_SPLASH_SIZE` — the read is one shot, so a future panel-size bump would otherwise run silently into the assets region. Used a negative-array-size typedef, not `_Static_assert`, since nothing else in the tree relies on C11.
- **Left alone:** `settings.c` and `qspi_smoke.c` — settings' base and size are unchanged, and smoke's scratch is computed from the device top, so it still lands in the settings region exactly as before (documented, CRC-fallback makes it non-fatal).
- **Known latent gap, accepted:** u-boot's `sf erase` still cannot clear `0x7F8000–0x7FFFFF`, so the settings ring is not host-erasable. Firmware's 4 KiB `20h` handles it, and the script now refuses that range outright. The recovery hatch if the reserved bands ever need clearing from u-boot is `sf erase 0 0x800000`, which matches the device size and takes the uniform `C7h` whole-chip path (wipes everything).

### 2026-08-06 — Bus-screen run chart converted to AA vector lines (sparkline, the fifth widget)

Greg: *"on bus stats screen, can we use AA lines (legato vector functions?) on the run chart?"* → *"we have gobs of memory available. do it."* One file, one paint function; no design-DB, schema or screen changes. **Not yet built or seen on hardware** — Greg builds in MPLAB, so the flash delta is unmeasured.

- **What it replaced.** `sparkline_paint` walked the pixel columns of each segment and emitted one `leRenderer_VertLine` per column, spanning this column's y to the next column's so steep slopes stayed connected. Hard-edged at alpha 255 — the chart had **no AA at all**, which is the real reason to convert it (see decision log; the recorded "most repainted widget" objection was wrong, the bus screen refreshes at 1 Hz).
- **Now:** one `leDraw_VectorLine` per sample pair (39 for a full 40-sample ring), `LE_CAPSTYLE_ROUND` so adjacent caps overlap at each shared vertex and form a join rather than a notch. Colour still `leScheme_GetRenderColor(wgt->scheme, LE_SCHM_BASE)`, which is already in the render colour mode — no `leColorConvert` needed, unlike the widgets that hard-code an `RGB_888` constant.
- **Four things this needed care on, all vendor-behaviour, all worth knowing before the next conversion:**
  - `hardness` pinned to `LE_REAL_I16_ONE`, per the sticky-shader bug — `legato_vector_line.c` has its own static `_kernel` and `_drawLine` memsets `_context` but **not** `_kernel`, so one soft-edged call installs a `shadeFragment` on that module permanently.
  - **`attr->width == 0` silently takes `_drawHardLine`** — plain Bresenham through `leRenderer_BlendPixel_Safe`, no supersampling. A zero width is not "hairline", it is "no AA".
  - **The kernel treats integer coordinates as pixel *edges*** (`pixelCenter = FromInt(scanPoint) + ONE_OVER_TWO`), and a stroke is centred on its path, so an **even** width lands crisply on integer coordinates and an odd one wants a `+0.5`. `LINE_THICK 2` therefore needs no half-pixel offset — but it did need the old `span = height - LINE_THICK` trick dropped, since that assumed the run was drawn *downward* from `y`.
  - **The plot is inset a full `LINE_THICK` on all four sides.** Round caps extend the OBB by width/2 beyond each endpoint, and `leDraw_VectorLine`'s flat-segment short-circuit into `leDraw_VectorHLine` adds `LE_REAL_I16_ONE` to the length (an inclusive-pixel convention the AA body doesn't share), so both ends can reach ~2 px past a naive bound. That matters here specifically because `refresh_all()` calls `PANEL_BUS->invalidate()` — the **whole panel** — so the clip rect during paint is the panel, not the widget, and an overshoot would land in the axis gutter rather than being clipped away. Costs 4 px of 204; the y domain is 600‰ so it is invisible.

### 2026-08-06 — Tier colour ramp reordered to one monotone spectral arc (follow-up 2, DONE)

- Greg's objection to the old band ramp was that it *"doesn't read as ordered"* — he wanted the
  mockup's cool-to-hot progression fitted to eight steps. Diagnosis: the old ramp encoded one
  ordinal variable with **both** hue and lightness and they disagreed (L\* direction per step
  `- + - + - + -`). Proved unfixable by re-pairing: monotone lightness under two-shade banding
  needs band N+1's light shade darker than band N's dark shade, never true for Tailwind 300/500
  across green→red.
- New ramp `blue-400 · cyan-400 · green-400 · lime-400 · yellow-400 · orange-400 · red-400 ·
  red-600`. Min adjacent ΔE2000 **16.2**, up from 10.2; repairs the violet→yellow reversal and
  lifts T7→T8 from ΔE 10.6 to 18.2. Accepted cost: L\* *arches* (67 → 84 at yellow → 48 at red)
  because yellow is the lightest hue sRGB has and sits mid-ramp, so a blue→yellow→red path
  cannot be monotone in lightness. Hue is monotone, which is what carries the ordering.
- Also corrected the record: the old ramp's "ΔE ≥ 27" was **CIE76**, which overstates separation
  for high-chroma colours. On ΔE2000 the true minimum was 10.2 (amber-500 → orange-300). RGB565
  quantization moves it only to 10.1, so 565 was innocent. **Judge palette separation with
  ΔE2000.**
- Landed as three changes and nothing in C: the eight `SCHEME_TEXT_TIER_1..8` `text` roles in
  `default_design.zip`, `TIER_PALETTE`'s accents + `<hue>-900` tints in
  `tools/gh3-cover-art/process_gh3_cover_art.py`, and a re-bake of all 70 covers (only **24
  larges** moved — tiers 5–8 already carried yellow/orange/red-900 and red-950). This also closed
  an art/label disagreement live since 2026-08-05, where `TIER_PALETTE` still held the rejected
  spectral sweep. The two are now cross-checked programmatically.
- **Confirmed on hardware** after MGS Generate — all eight generated `text` roles verified as
  `0xRRGGBBAA` in `le_gen_scheme.c` (`SCHEME_TEXT_TIER_1` = `0x60A5FAFF`). Tier 8 stays at
  red-600 / 3.67:1, knowingly sub-AA but legible on the panel; `red-500` remains the fallback at
  the cost of the T7→T8 separation we just bought.
- Two `mgs-legato-design` findings added to the skill: MGS's serializer is
  `json.dumps(indent=4, sort_keys=True)` *except* empty containers render `[\n]`, so a naive
  round-trip rewrote ~1200 unrelated lines — the fix is to edit the member as **text** and assert
  the *parsed* diff is exactly the intended fields (11 bytes changed, verified member-by-member
  against `git show HEAD:<zip>`); and editing the zip retriggers MCC's autosave manifest.

  match the gap indices ascending. Verified each capture visually against the highlighted title and

- Note on the corpus/art renames and repo size: the index shift is a *permutation* of paths, so
  `git status` shows most files as `M` rather than `R` — git's rename detection needs a delete/add

### 2026-08-06 — Drag lag: tilt + whammy repaint only the damaged band

- Cause: `value_set` in both widgets called `invalidate`, damaging the whole widget rect for a
  ~2 px thumb move. Send path exonerated (non-blocking); Legato already coalesces touch moves.
- `widget_tilt`: damage = union of the round-cap boxes at the old and new angle (also bounds the
  thumb and the swept sector). 355 808 → 38 288 point evaluations for a 1° step, **9×**. Only
  possible because the vector kernel clips its *scan*, which the old float loop did not.
- `widget_whammy`: paint loop bounded to `rect ∩ clip` (free, invisible) plus a column-band
  damage rect. 50 824 → 13 440 `rrect_sdf` calls, **3.8×**.
- Both proven bit-identical to a full repaint over 13 angle/value transitions on the host harness,
  including the extremes (tilt 0→90, whammy −50→50, spring-back 60→0).
- +992 B flash. **Confirmed on hardware** — Greg: *"significantly improved!"*.
- Raised the whammy's fill overshoot (its capsule ends land 32 px outside the span it covers) as a
  defect; it is deliberate and stays. See the decision log.

### 2026-08-06 — Vector rasterizer adopted for tilt / gauge / dot / fret (follow-up 4)

- Four widgets converted from hand-rolled per-pixel soft-float coverage to `leDraw_Vector*`,
  one step at a time in the review's order. New header-only `ui/gfx/vec_draw.h` carries the
  project-wide `UI_VEC_AA = 8X` pin, `UI_VEC_DEG16`, a whole-pixel `leRectF` builder and an
  arc-point helper; header-only so no `user.cmake` edit is needed.
- `tilt`: 4 calls (2 `ArcStroke` + 2 `ArcFill`) replace a 24.6 k-pixel loop doing ~8 soft-float
  transcendentals each. `gauge`: 2 `ArcStroke`. `panel_aa` dot: a capsule (band + end discs) that
  also covers `screen_bus`'s pill tracks and now owns the widget's background. `fret`: 1–2
  `RectFill`. Paint paths verified float-free in the disassembly.
- **+7 912 B flash** (`.text` 1 364 140 → 1 372 052): ~9.8 kB of vector library that `--gc-sections`
  used to discard, less ~1.9 kB of widget code deleted. Ran on the panel the same day (see the
  drag-lag entry) and signed off there.
- A/B'd on a host harness (vendor vector tree compiled natively behind a shimmed
  `legato_renderer.h`): all four within AA noise except the gauge, whose feathered ring becomes a
  crisp one by design; no pixel painted outside any widget rect. The harness caught a
  `LE_REAL_I16_FROM_FLOAT` macro-parenthesis bug that had shrunk the tilt thumb to 1.25 px.
- Found and documented: the rect fill's corner radius clamps to `min(w,h)/4` (capsules
  inexpressible), arcs scan the full circle not the span, and `widget_whammy` is blocked outright
  (needs a clip-rect setter the API lacks). Review doc carries a corrections block.
- **On hardware the same day** (the drag-lag entry above was measured on it) and **signed off**:
  all four look right, the gauge's crisp ring is accepted as the intended reading (no `thickness`
  drop to 5). The `marvin-perf` before/after was **dropped unmeasured** — see the decision log for
  why and what would reopen it. The premise that fixed-point-with-8-samples beats
  soft-float-with-1-sample therefore stands unproven, and `UI_VEC_AA = 8X` is pinned by default
  rather than by measurement.

### 2026-07-31 — beatbox heartbeat awareness (node table entry) + broadcast-tolerance check

- **marvin now recognizes beatbox (beat-source node, id 5) on the bus.** Added `T1S_NODE_BEATSOURCE`
  to the node-type enum, a `{ 5u, T1S_NO_DETECTOR, T1S_NODE_BEATSOURCE }` row to the static node table,
  and a `"beatbox"` display name (`net/t1s/t1s_link.c`). Same table-driven pattern as the lemmy/lightshow
  rows: presence, seq capture, and the `nodes` display key off the source MAC, so beatbox's `0x88B6`
  heartbeat now marks it present and `nodes` lists it — no other change needed. beatbox advertises
  `node_type = 6` (beat source) in its payload; as with the other nodes marvin maps id→type via its
  static table and does not decode the byte. Awareness only; beatbox is a producer, so marvin sends it
  nothing.
- **Broadcast messages need no change on marvin or any other node.** beatbox's planned bus output splits
  into a broadcast beat frame (ethertype `0x88B8`, dst `FF:FF:FF:FF:FF:FF`, for lightshow + any future
  consumer) and a unicast lemmy position command (`0x88B9`). Checked the whole bus for broadcast
  tolerance: followers filter at the MAC layer to self-MAC + broadcast (not promiscuous), so a broadcast
  frame is *received* by every node; each then filters by ethertype and drops what isn't its type. marvin
  runs promiscuous during bring-up and drops any ethertype not in {`0x88B5`,`0x88B6`,`0x88B7`} at
  `TC6_CB_OnRxEthernetPacket`. So a `0x88B8`/`0x88B9` frame is harmlessly received-and-dropped everywhere
  until a consumer opts in (lightshow at beatbox B4). No node needs a change to *tolerate* the new
  broadcast traffic — only the eventual consumer needs new code.

ACCEL slice, transport-independent ESP32 side). Approach + verification are in the approved plan;

  fauxmote-T1S build), `T1S_ETHERTYPE_CTRL 0x88B7`, `send_to_node` generalized to take an ethertype,
  a static ctrl TX FIFO (depth 8, item `type+len+≤8B`) drained one-per-wake in `t1s_task`, RX demux
  on `0x88B7` → registered `T1SLink_ControllerHandler`, ctrl tx/rx counters, and an idempotent
  `T1SLink_Initialize` (static `s_initialized`). Header symbols always exist (stubs when the channel
  is compiled out) so a UART fauxmote build still links.

  `send_frame`→`T1SLink_SendToController`, `ctrl_rx`→`latch_status` on STATUS, `T1SLink_Initialize`
  + `T1SLink_SetControllerHandler` in `Fauxmote_Initialize`, TX task only (no FLEXCOM5, no RX task).
  UART build: today's file verbatim behind `#else`. STATUS latch factored into shared `latch_status`
  (pure move — UART parity preserved).
- **Build + console.** `user.cmake` documents `MARVIN_FAUXMOTE_TRANSPORT=1` beside the fretboard flag;
  `fauxmote status` prints the active transport + ctrl tx/rx; `nodes` lists the controller row for free.

No MCC/Legato edits; `mf_proto.h` wire unchanged. Self-reviewed both build configs for missing/unused
