### 2026-07-15 — `game/` module naming cleanup + `fret.h` relocation (pending build)

Made the `game/` module fully consistent — symbols, filenames, and header guards. Documented the
full file inventory + external/internal split (see below). No behavior change — renames + one file
move only.

**Convention (decided w/ Greg):** two tiers keyed on visibility, and **the filename prefix mirrors
the symbol tier**:
- **Public API → `game_*.{c,h}` files, `Game<Area>_Verb` symbols** (types `game_*_t`). Every public
  symbol starts with `Game`; every public file starts with `game_`:
  - `art`→`game_art` (`Art_`→`GameArt_`), `catalog`→`game_catalog` (`Catalog_`→`GameCatalog_`,
    `catalog_entry_t`→`game_catalog_entry_t`), `selection`→`game_selection` (`Selection_`→
    `GameSelection_`, `selection_t`→`game_selection_t`, `sel_difficulty_t`/`sel_mode_t`→
    `game_difficulty_t`/`game_mode_t`, `SEL_DIFF_*`/`SEL_MODE_*`→`GAME_DIFF_*`/`GAME_MODE_*`),
    `timing_pipeline`→`game_timing` (`TimingPipeline_`→`GameTiming_`),
    `gameplay_engine`→`game_engine` (`GameplayEngine_`→`GameEngine_`; `game_state_t` unchanged).
  - `game_controller` was already conformant.
- **Internal recognizer → `gameplay_*.{c,h}` files, `gp_*`/`GP_*` symbols** (the pure, Python-mirrored
  library: `gameplay_classify`/`_select`/`_score`/`_present`/`_metadata`; consumed only by
  `game_engine.c`, zero external includes). Reverted the presence detector's public-styled name back
  to this tier: `GameplayPresent_Classify` → **`gp_present`** (sits with `gp_classify`/`gp_fingerprint`).
  Keeping the recognizer on `gp_` also leaves `gameplay_metadata.h` generation (`export_c.py`) and the
  host cross-check trivial.

So the file prefix now *is* the boundary marker: **`game_*` = public module API; `gameplay_*` = the
vision recognizer internals.** Header guards standardized to `MARVIN_<FILENAME>_H` across all game/
headers (incl. the generated `gameplay_metadata.h`, via `export_c.py`).

**`fret.h` moved out of `game/`** → `src/fret.h` (guard `MARVIN_FRET_H`; include `"fret.h"`).
`fret_t`/`FRET_*` is a cross-cutting domain primitive used mostly by detector/actuator/timing/
perf-log (21 files, 324 refs) — the widest-shared symbol and not "game" logic; it now lives at the
src root beside `app.h`/`log.h`. Names unchanged, only the location + ~6 include paths + guard.

Rule of thumb going forward: **`game_*`/`Game*` = call it from outside `game/`; `gameplay_*`/`gp_*`
= recognizer internals.** Verified: no dangling old symbols/includes/filenames anywhere in the
firmware tree; `user.cmake` updated; host cross-check + present + export tests pass (15);
`gameplay_present.c` clean under `-Wall -Wextra`. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.**

### 2026-07-15 — Gameplay screens classified by scoreboard-chrome presence (fixes intermittent 2p dropout; pending build)

The 2-player `in_song_2p` screen classification was intermittently dropping out. Root cause: every
screen — menu *and* gameplay — was classified by one whole-frame 12×8 centroid (`gp_classify`).
Menus are ~static so that's solid; a gameplay frame is mostly *dynamic* (two scrolling note
highways, animated crowd, changing score/amp digits), and the `in_song_2p` centroid was trained on
5 frames from one session, so a different song/stage/character drifts many grid cells → the L1
crosses `t_abs` or the margin collapses → UNKNOWN. The fix keys gameplay detection on the one static
element, whose **layout is itself the 1p/2p signature**: one bottom-left score block (1p) vs two top
amp panels (2p). Highways are irrelevant to *classification* (they belong to the CV detector).

- New `game/gameplay_present.{c,h}` (pure C, no RTOS/video deps; `-Wall -Wextra` clean): three
  masked, per-frame-normalized SAD probes at **fixed nominal coords** (no offset search, no
  registration). Each block's luma is standardized to mean128/std48 over its masked static-chrome
  pixels (same quantize as `gp_fingerprint`), integer L1 vs a baked uint8 reference over the mask,
  / npix. `gp_present()` returns `GP_SCREEN_in_song` / `in_song_2p` / `UNKNOWN`. Decision: `1p iff
  p1≤TAU & p1≤max(pL,pR)`; `2p iff max(pL,pR)≤TAU`. TAU = 18.
- `gameplay_engine.c` observe path is now **probe-first**: `gp_present()` runs first; on UNKNOWN it
  falls through to `gp_classify()` for the static screens. The `in_song`/`in_song_2p` centroids stay
  in the classifier as a harmless fallback — the probe is authoritative for gameplay. `game_task`
  score/streak/multiplier readers stay gated on `GP_SCREEN_in_song`; `game_controller.c` already
  accepts `in_song_2p` and auto-selects the 2p-left highway — both unchanged.
- Tables baked into `game/gameplay_metadata.h` by `export_c.py`: `gp_probes[]` (block coords,
  bw/bh, npix, uint8 mask + uint8 mean128/std48 reference) + `GP_PRESENT_TAU`. Header regen only —
  no MCC/Legato hand-edits.

Why no registration (I initially over-modeled it): the classifier needs none. marvin captures
pixel-locked native BGR888 at the Wii's 720×480 (`capture_pipeline.md`); the component→HDMI
converter re-samples to a fixed raster, so there's no per-frame position jitter and the real 2p
snapshots already sit at nominal offset. Any static per-rig offset is a one-time concern, deferred
with the 2p digit reader (`amp2p.calibrate`, host-only) — the present probe is fixed-offset.

**Proven offline first** (host `tools/gameplay`; see its journal same date): 0/187 corpus frames
misclassified; value-slop envelope worst present 10.1 vs best absent 28.8 → **2.9× margin** around
TAU 18; pause-overlay partial chrome stays clearly absent; C `gp_present` == Python on every corpus
frame (new `test_firmware_classify.py` `present` mode). Full host suite **80 passed**.

**Pending Greg's MPLAB build + hardware validation** (a live 2p game should now hold `in_song_2p`
without dropout; 1p unaffected). **Data follow-up:** the in-sample present margin (~0.04) is
optimistic — capture career/quickplay 1p and cross-session 2p to measure the true out-of-sample
margin.

### 2026-07-15 — CV strike time anchored to capture, not detect time; two small cleanups (pending build)

Area review of the game-pipeline / detector / actuator turned up three fixable items (a fourth — multi-producer wire arbitration — deferred to Open questions).

- **CV strike time now anchors to frame capture, not detect time.** `cv_marvin_v1` stamped `timestamp_us` from `xTaskGetTickCount()` at the moment the *CV task processed* the frame, so capture→detect latency (queue wait + task scheduling) folded into `strike_at_ms` as timing jitter that `observation_lead_ms` couldn't remove. `Video_FrameInfo` gains `uint64_t timestamp_us`, stamped in `on_frame_done` (the ISC frame-done IRQ) via `xTaskGetTickCountFromISR()`; CV copies `frame->timestamp_us` instead of resampling. Same ms-tick domain, just sampled at the right end of the pipeline. Removed the now-unused `CV_US_PER_TICK`; set `timestamp_us = 0` in the synchronous `Video_GetFrameInfo` (state read, not a capture event) so the new field is never garbage.
- **Fixed a stale comment:** `TP_TICK_MS` block said "5 ms" while the value is `2u`.
- **Removed dead `s_last_frame_us`** in `timing_pipeline.c` (written every frame, never read).

Files: `video/video.h`, `video/video.c`, `detector/cv_marvin_v1.c`, `game/timing_pipeline.c`. No behavior change beyond the capture-time anchoring (which reduces timing jitter). **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.**

### 2026-07-15 — Wire-mask defines extracted to actuator/guitar_cmd.h (pending build)

Resolved the layering smell flagged in the Q1/Q2 work: the guitar command bit layout no longer lives in the scheduler header.

- New `actuator/guitar_cmd.h` — a dependency-free header owning the guitar controller command mask (mirrors the receiver's `firmware/fretboard cmd_receive.h` / guitar T1S command bits). Renamed `TIMING_BIT_*` → `GUITAR_BTN_*` (they were never a timing concept — they're the wire command).
- Removed the `#define` block from `game/timing_pipeline.h` and refreshed its stale header comment (dropped the "filtered by Detector_GetActive()" line — the bus is now pre-arbitrated; noted the strike-line time base).
- **`fretboard_link.c` no longer includes `timing_pipeline.h`** — the sink→scheduler dependency is gone; it includes the neutral `guitar_cmd.h`. `screen_dashboard.c` likewise. `timing_pipeline.c`, `manual_control.c`, `game_controller.c` include `guitar_cmd.h` for the bits (the latter two keep `timing_pipeline.h` for the `TimingPipeline_*` API). `console.c`/`app.c` are API-only, untouched.
- Comments in `dashboard_feed.h`, `screen_dashboard.h`, `perf_log_records.h` updated to the new name. No behavior change; `guitar_cmd.h` is header-only (no `user.cmake` entry).

**Pending Greg's MPLAB build.**

### 2026-07-15 — Timing pipeline → game/ (it's the decide layer); detector-side source arbitration (pending build)

Two structural follow-ons to the P1/P2 re-layering (decision log 2026-07-15).

- **Q1 — moved `timing_pipeline.{c,h}` from `actuator/` to `game/`.** The pipeline is the "decide" layer, sibling to `game_controller` (the two mutually-exclusive autoplay producers, Q11), not a member of the actuator sink. `git mv` + include-path fixes in `app.c`, `console/console.c`, `ui/screens/dashboard/screen_dashboard.c`, `game/game_controller.c`, `actuator/fretboard_link.c`, `actuator/manual_control.c`, and the moved `timing_pipeline.c` (`fretboard_link.h` → `actuator/fretboard_link.h`); `user.cmake` source path updated. No behavior change. `actuator/` is now the command sink (`fretboard_link` + advance) + `manual_control` (operator override; a candidate to move to `ui/` later).
- **Q2 — detector-source arbitration moved to the push side.** New `Detector_Publish(state)` (`detector/detector.c`) forwards to the bus only when `state->detector_id == active`; `Detector_SetActive` now `xQueueReset`s the bus so no stale-source frames survive a switch. `cv_marvin_v1` publishes through it (removed its raw `xQueueSend` and the `bus` local/param); `timing_pipeline` dropped its `Detector_GetActive()` filter. The bus now means "the active detector's stream." Behavior-identical; recording still captures all enabled detectors via the separate `PerfLog_EmitDetector` path.
- Spec §4.2.4 (bus mechanics) and §4.4 (pipeline is the decide layer, home in `game/`, Q11) updated to match.
- **Deferred:** `fretboard_link.c` still pulls `TIMING_BIT_*` from the scheduler header — those wire-mask defines belong in the actuator/wire layer (match `firmware/fretboard/cmd_receive.h`); relocating is a follow-up.

**Pending Greg's MPLAB build.**

### 2026-07-15 — Timing re-layering P2: detector stamps strike time (pending build)

Landed P2. The observation lead (how early the sensor sees a note) now lives with the detector, and the pipeline schedules purely in strike-line time — no delay constant of its own.

- **`detector_state_t` gains `uint32_t strike_at_ms`** (`detector/detector.h`) = `timestamp_us/1000 + observation_lead`. Placed right after `frame_epoch`, filling the existing 4-byte alignment hole before the u64 `timestamp_us` — **struct size unchanged**. No host-tooling breakage: the host consumes the separate `perf_rec_detector_t`, and the `state.bin` recorder (§4.6.5) isn't built yet.
- **CV detector owns the lead as per-config calibration** — `observation_lead_ms` added to `cv_marvin_v1_config_t`, set to **250** in both `CFG_1P` / `CFG_2P_LEFT` (the value P1 had left in `TP_STRUM_DELAY_MS`). `detect_frame` stamps `state.strike_at_ms`.
- **`timing_pipeline.c`:** removed `TP_STRUM_DELAY_MS`; added `s_strike_at_ms` (the current frame's strike time, advanced 1:1 with `s_now_ms` on between-frame ticks). `chord_commit` → `assert_at = strike_at − FRET_EARLY`, `strum_at = strike_at`; release scheduling → `s_release_at_ms[i] = strike_at`. `s_now_ms` still drives only input aggregation (chord window) and the emit-anchored strum pulse.
- **Behavior-preserving:** with `observation_lead_ms == old STRUM_DELAY (250)`, every deadline resolves to the same value, and the TIMING perf snapshot (`assert_at_ms`/`strum_at_ms`) is unchanged. Composes cleanly with P1's actuator advance (`fire_now = now + advance`).
- **Spec updated to match code** (§4.2.3 record shape, §4.4 pipeline ownership + actuator advance, §2.4 latency rows now name `observation_lead_ms`).

Follow-ups (not needed for the firmware re-layer, deferred): surface `observation_lead_ms` to host tooling — a field in `PERF_REC_DETECTOR_CONFIG` + `marvin-perf/records.py` — so offline replay/`state.bin` can reconstruct strike time; and give the future `adc_fretboard` detector its own (different) lead. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.**

### 2026-07-15 — Timing re-layering P1: actuator-advance seam (pending build)

Landed P1 of the timing re-layering (model + phases in the decision log / open questions, 2026-07-15). Goal: stop conflating the actuator's mechanical latency with the pipeline's scheduling clock, and put the mechanical-advance knowledge where it belongs — the actuator layer.

- **`FRETBOARD_ACTUATOR_ADVANCE_MS`** (new, `actuator/fretboard_link.h`) declares the active actuator node's mechanical advance in ms. Open-drain guitar node = **0**; a future solenoid rig sets its rise time here. Static per-node constant for now (resolved 2026-07-15).
- **`timing_pipeline.c`** now schedules its three strike-line deadlines — note assert, strum fire, fret release — off an advanced firing clock `fire_now = s_now_ms + FRETBOARD_ACTUATOR_ADVANCE_MS`, so the command is emitted `advance` ms before the strike-line moment. `process_notes/process_strums/process_releases` take `fire_now`; the wire byte stays a bare "assert now" mask (Option A — marvin applies the advance).
- Left on the real clock (`s_now_ms`): the **chord-accumulation window** (input aggregation, not an output deadline) and the **strum-pulse release** (anchored to the already-advanced emit instant, so the wire pulse width is preserved rather than double-advanced).
- At advance = 0 the change is **behavior-preserving byte-for-byte** for the current guitar node — `fire_now == s_now_ms`.

**Pending Greg's MPLAB build.** Not yet exercised with a nonzero advance (no solenoid node exists). P2 (detector stamps strike time; move `STRUM_DELAY_MS` out of the pipeline; `detector_state_t` bus-record change) and P3 (Option B timestamped wire) remain in Open questions.

### 2026-07-14 — CV detector: config-selectable 1p / 2p-left highway geometry (pending build)

Made `cv_marvin_v1` read the note-detector geometry from a **passed-in config** so the one detector
can sample either the single-player centered highway or the **2-player left (robot) highway** (the
right player is a human — no second detector, no two-guitar actuation, no bus/perf schema change).
2p-left coords calibrated host-side 2026-07-14 (see `tools/gameplay/docs/journal.md`).

- `detector/cv_marvin_v1.h`: new public `cv_marvin_v1_config_t` (per-fret `cv_sensor_xy_t` sample
  points + SENSING/STRIKE strip ROIs + `name`), two `extern const` configs `CV_MARVIN_CFG_1P` /
  `CV_MARVIN_CFG_2P_LEFT`, and `CvMarvinV1_SetConfig()`/`GetConfig()`.
- `detector/cv_marvin_v1.c`: replaced the file-static `s_sensor_coords` + `CV_SENSING_*`/`CV_STRIKE_*`
  `#define`s with the two config tables + a `s_active_cfg`/`s_pending_cfg` pointer pair. The task picks
  up a pending swap at the top of the loop (even while disabled), **resets the per-fret latch state**
  (`s_pressed`/`s_edge_active`/dists/press_count) so stale latches don't leak across highways, and
  re-publishes detector config. `detect_frame`/`draw_overlay`/`publish_detector_config` + the strip
  copy all read geometry from the active config. SENSING scratch sized to the max strip
  (`CV_SENSING_MAX_W/H`) so a swap can't overflow it. Detector identity (`DETECTOR_CV_MARVIN_V1`) and
  the color/threshold constants stay shared — geometry is the only per-highway difference for now
  (thresholds can move into the config later if the 2p highway needs different values). Config swap is
  lock-light (volatile pointer; a benign read-then-clear race just delays a rare swap by a frame).
- `console/console.c`: `cvcfg <1p|2pl>` command (bare `cvcfg` prints the active config name) — the
  primary bring-up/validation tool, mirroring the 1p sensor bring-up.
- **`play attach`** (new `GameController_StartAttach`, `gc_mode_t{NAV,ATTACH}`): a playthrough that
  **skips all menu navigation** — the operator sets the game up by hand (a 2-player match), and marvin
  owns the wire, waits for a gameplay screen (`GC_ATTACH_WAIT_MS` = 2 min budget), auto-selects the
  highway config from the observed `in_song`/`in_song_2p`, then actuates the whole song via
  `play_until_done` and goes idle. `play` (unchanged) still auto-navigates the 1p practice path from
  the committed Selection. Both share the wire-ownership prologue + `ensure_wii_connected` pre-flight.
- `game/game_controller.c`: at gameplay entry it now accepts `GP_SCREEN_in_song_2p` as gameplay and
  **auto-selects** the matching config (`in_song_2p` → 2p-left, `in_song` → 1p); the 2p branch in
  `play_until_done` keeps the loop alive without running the 1p score/multiplier/streak readers (2p
  uses the separate amp scoreboards — different WIP). `gameplay_engine.c` unchanged (1p readers stay
  gated on `GP_SCREEN_in_song`; `in_song_2p` just publishes screen state + logs generically).
- Screen-classifier side (host `tools/gameplay`): added the `in_song_2p` class + regenerated
  `game/gameplay_metadata.h` (`GP_N_SCREENS` 13→14). Detail in the gameplay journal, same date.

**Fauxmote/actuation unchanged** — marvin still drives its single guitar exactly as in 1p; the
`ensure_wii_connected()` pre-flight in `run()` is the existing fauxmote path.

**Bring-up plan (Greg, MPLAB build):** on a live 2-player game, `detect cv on` + `cvcfg 2pl`, open the
host viewer, confirm the SENSING overlay rings sit on the left highway's five gems and the fret P/E
flags fire on notes — the on-hardware analog of the host `sense_marked.png` overlay. `cvcfg 1p`
restores single-player behavior. Then the integrated path (auto-select from `in_song_2p`). **Pending
Greg's build + hardware validation.** Open: 2p-highway note-scroll *lead time* at the same y=311 may
differ from 1p (shorter highway) — may need to drop the sense row after watching real 2p scroll; and
the automated 2p *menu* navigation is out of scope (the controller's practice-run plan is 1p; 2p is
human-set-up + console/timing for now).

### 2026-07-09 — gameplay_engine: score multiplier reader + dashboard 1X-4X buttons (pending build)

Added the in-song score **multiplier** (1x/2x/3x/4x) alongside the score. `gp_read_multiplier`
(`game/gameplay_score.c`, pure C) classifies it by **colour** — the medallion glyph is 2x gold /
3x green / 4x purple (1x = no digit) — counting bright saturated pixels of each hue in a small
16×24 ROI (`GP_MULT_ROI`) and taking the argmax (floor → 1x). No segmentation/templates;
mode-independent. `game_state_t.multiplier` read on `GP_SCREEN_in_song` (log `score N x<m>`). The
game controller posts it (`DashboardFeed_PostMultiplier`, reset 1x at song start) and the dashboard
feed consumer applies `DASH_EVT_MULTIPLIER` → `ScreenDashboard_ApplyMultiplier`, highlighting the
active `Marvin_BUTTON_DASHBOARD_ROBOT_{1,2,3,4}X` button and clearing the rest (the ApplyFret latch
pattern). Host cross-check confirms `gp_read_multiplier` == the prototype on all 54 corpus frames;
validated on the 6549-frame capture (multiplier only climbs by 1 / resets, 1 anomalous transition).
**Pending Greg's MPLAB build.** Detail in the gameplay journal (2026-07-09).

### 2026-07-09 — dashboard: live CV score → ROBOT score label (pending build)

Wired the ported score reader's output to the dashboard `Marvin_LABEL_DASHBOARD_ROBOT_Score`
(added in MGS), reusing the already-stubbed feed path: `game_controller.play_until_done` observes
the full `game_state_t` in its ~3 Hz play poll and `DashboardFeed_PostScore(gs.score)` on
`in_song` (resets to 0 at song start); the dashboard-feed consumer applies `DASH_EVT_SCORE` via a
new `ScreenDashboard_ApplyScore` that formats the value into a `leFixedString` and `setString`s the
label (mirrors `ApplyPlaytime`/`ApplyStatus`). Files: `game/game_controller.c`, `ui/dashboard_feed.c`,
`ui/screens/dashboard/screen_dashboard.{c,h}` — all our own source (no generated edits;
`DASH_EVT_SCORE`/`DashboardFeed_PostScore` already existed). **Pending Greg's MPLAB build**: on a
training run the label should track the on-screen score (~3 Hz), reset at song start, hold the final
value. Follow-ups: multiplier/streak labels (feed stubs exist), career-font score mode.

### 2026-07-09 — gameplay_engine: in-song score reader ported (M9 Phase 3, pending build)

Added the in-song score reader to the observer, mirroring the classifier/selection/song ports.
New pure-C `game/gameplay_score.{h,c}`: `gp_read_score(frame, w, h, mode, *out)` — proportional
GH3 score OCR (relative-threshold ink mask, gap-based column segmentation into digit blobs, per-cell
ink-coverage glyph, **integer L1 argmin over 10 per-digit coverage-mask templates**). Driven by a
new `export_c` score block in `gameplay_metadata.h` (`GP_SCORE_*` geometry/params, per-mode digit
band + a `[10][140]` uint8 template array). **Mode-parameterized** (`GP_SCORE_MODE_TRAINING` now;
career green font appends as a second mode with no API change). Started as a 270-exemplar float 1-NN
(554 KB header, soft-float) but Greg rightly flagged it as overkill for a fixed 10-glyph font;
simplified to the coverage-mask match (integer-only, header 157 KB, same accuracy). **No chrome
registration in v0** — the rig is stable and the host
registration always resolved to (0,0), so a fixed digit band suffices (deferred on-device
registration if drift appears). `gameplay_engine` gained `game_state_t.score`, reads it on
`GP_SCREEN_in_song` (logs `GAME: in_song / score N`); `gameplay_score.c` added to `user.cmake`.

Proven before build: the host cross-check (`tools/gameplay/tests/test_firmware_classify.py`, new
`score` driver mode) compiles `gameplay_score.c` and confirms `gp_read_score` matches the prototype
`read_score` on all 54 corpus frames. The prototype reads all 6549 real capture frames with 0
monotonic violations. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build + on-hardware check.** Detail in the gameplay
journal (2026-07-09).

### 2026-07-09 — perf_log: region-stream command (host-selected sub-region at full frame rate)

Added a `PERF_CMD_REGION_STREAM` command + `PERF_STRIP_REGION` strip kind so the host can stream
a fixed video **sub-region** back as one STRIP record per frame, continuously, tear-free — built
to gather scoring-block training data for the gameplay score reader (`tools/gameplay`), but the
rect is host-parameterized so it's a generic region streamer (repointable without a reflash).

Reuses the existing machinery: `PerfLog_EmitStripFromFrame` already coherently row-copies an
arbitrary `w×h` window per frame (the same path as the 60 Hz SENSING/STRIKE strips), and 96×105×3
= 30 KB fits one STRIP record, so no banding/staging. New: `perf_cmd_region_stream_t` (enable +
x,y,w,h), a `volatile` flag/rect in `perf_log.c` (mirrors the overlay-flags pattern) with
`PerfLog_SetRegionStream` + a bounds-checked `PerfLog_EmitRegionIfEnabled`, an RX dispatch case,
and one call in the `cv_marvin_v1` per-frame loop beside the SENSING/STRIKE emits. Default off;
pooled/drop-on-full path (never the snapshot drain monopoly); STRIP-mask gated. **No schema bump**
— a new strip kind is forward-compat (records.h says so) and a host→device command doesn't change
any record layout (SET_OVERLAY precedent). Files: `perf_log/{perf_log_records.h,perf_log.c,
perf_log.h,perf_log_rx.c}`, `detector/cv_marvin_v1.c`. **Pending Greg's MPLAB build.**

Host side (`tools/marvin-perf`): `StripKind.REGION` + `encode_region_stream_payload`, no schema
bump. **Fretboard vs scoreboard are independent** (Greg): the three strip kinds were all gated by the
single `PERF_REC_STRIP` type mask, coupling them. Fixed by making the REGION strip **command-gated
only**, bypassing the type mask (a `honor_mask` flag on `strip_slot_claim`; REGION passes false —
its own start/stop command, default off, is the gate). So the STRIP mask now gates only the fretboard
SENSING+STRIKE pair, and the region command gates the scoreboard, toggled separately. (Frontend
mirrors this: the live-record mask filter exempts REGION-kind strips.)

Two capture paths: (1) the **web viewer** (`serve`) — a **SCORE checkbox in the types panel**
(grouped with the record-type checkboxes; `POST /api/live/region` → `LiveSession.set_region_stream`)
starts the stream, independent of the STRIP box; the block renders live in the strip pane at its
frame position like the sensing/strike strips (`REGION` added to the JS `STRIP_KIND_REGISTRY`), and
the existing **Record** button captures the REGION strips into the `.bin` like any other record
(the mirror path has no type filter, so zero changes there); (2) the headless **`score-capture`** verb
that streams straight to `scores/score-NNNN.png` (`--count`/Ctrl-C, `--rect`). Offline,
**`export-region`** pulls REGION strips out of a recorded capture into `score-NNNN.png`
(`save_region_strips`), and the offline viewer already renders them (`kind_name`="region"). 153
tests pass. See gameplay journal for the downstream template-enrichment plan.

June 17 onward stays here; Decision log + Open questions untouched. Archive chain preserved (active → Q2 → the May `journal-archive.md`).
- **Docs audited against code across all subprojects** (parallel read-only auditors → findings → fixes). Applied all high+medium factual corrections; per-doc findings + the authoritative completion record are in [`../../../docs/doc-audit-2026-07.md`](../../../docs/doc-audit-2026-07.md). marvin-side corrections: `spec.md`/`SPEC.md` (T1S is the shipping default not FLEXCOM1 UART; config persistence is on QSPI flash not SD; M10 controller / SDMMC / multi-screen UI further along than the status tables claimed; fauxmote link documented); `capture_pipeline.md` (scrubbed BGRX32/4-byte/`.region_cache_aligned`/RMS=0/1080p residuals → BGR888 3B/px, `.region_nocache`, RMS=1, 1280×720); `display_path.md` **rewritten in place** (HEO scanout + PWM backlight + scaler now owned by `ui_manager`, cross-links `ui_compositor.md` §14–16); `ui_compositor.md` (QSPI raw-RGBA8888 splash not SD JPEG, PWM backlight, `LE_LAYER_COUNT`=4, HW-layer table); `gh3_navigation.md` (classifier/controller now exist on-device); tooling docs (bootstrap issues `BKPT_NOTIFY_DONE` not "just loops"; QSPI-over-JTAG toolchain added to the openocd README; NAND addrs/sizes/chip-erase). `yellow_tinge.md` verified accurate — no change. **Docs only; no marvin source touched by this effort.**

### 2026-07-01 — UI first-touch lag after screen change: paint canvases once at boot, make show/hide repaint-free

Greg noticed a ~0.5–1 s lag on the **first** touch after a "screen change" (open/close nav drawer, open song-select then tap a song); after that first tap, touch is snappy again.

**Root cause (traced through the Legato core).** Legato refuses to dispatch touch while its renderer is busy: `handleTouchDown` (and up/move) return `LE_EVENT_DEFERRED` when `leIsDrawing()` (`legato_input.c:396`), and `leEvent_ProcessEvents` leaves a deferred event queued to retry on the next `LEGATO_Tasks` tick (10 ms). A screen transition kicked off a large repaint that held the renderer non-idle for many ticks, so the first tap in that window was deferred repeatedly → the lag. The transitions repainted for no good reason: each screen renders into its own RAM canvas surface, and Legato paints into a canvas independent of its shown/bound (`active`) state (`gfx_canvas.c` `SET_ACTIVE_LAYER` succeeds for any canvas), so showing a painted screen should be a pure HW-layer bind.

**Three repaint sources removed.** (1) `navigation_open()` did an explicit `Marvin_PANEL_NAVIGATION->fn->invalidate()` on every open (full 320×800 repaint). (2) `songsel_set_input()` toggled `setEnabled()` on the dashboard + song-select panels for **modal input-gating**, but `_leWidget_SetEnabled` invalidates whenever the enabled state changes — so opening repainted the modal panels and closing repainted the whole dashboard. (3) nav open/close toggled `setEnabled(PANEL_DASHBOARD)` the same way → dashboard repaint on nav close.

**Fix.** Added `panel_set_pickable(leWidget*, leBool)` in `ui_manager.c` that toggles `LE_WIDGET_ENABLED` directly on `wgt->flags` — the renderer never reads ENABLED, and `leUtils_PickFromWidget` descends only into ENABLED children, so this gates a panel's whole subtree from picking with **zero** repaint. Exposed `UiManager_SetDashboardPickable(bool)` so nav and song-select share one dashboard gate. Reworked `songsel_set_input` onto it. In `screen_navigation.c` dropped the `invalidate()` and swapped the dashboard `setEnabled` calls for the gate. Added `paint_all_screens_once()` (invalidate dashboard/nav/song-select/album-art) called in `ui_boot_task` right before the existing `wait_render_idle()`, so every canvas surface is fully painted behind the splash and is never repainted on show again. Removed the now-redundant one-time `invalidate()` at the end of `ScreenSongSelect_Setup`.

**Why the old startup paint "didn't fully land"** (the comment that motivated the per-open invalidate workaround): MGS builds `PANEL_NAVIGATION` with `setVisible(FALSE)`, so `leAddRootWidget`'s boot root-damage painted nothing inside the drawer until `ScreenNavigation_Setup` later flipped it visible — fragile/incidental. The explicit `paint_all_screens_once()` makes it deterministic. Verified single-buffering (`bufferCount==1`, `LE_PREEMPTION_LEVEL==0`) so one drained invalidate lands a surface completely.

**Left intact:** content-change repaints — album-art swap on song tap (`setImage`) and nav highlight (`setScheme`) — are legitimate and unchanged. Rejected `LE_WIDGET_IGNOREPICK` for the gate (it still lets a panel's children be picked). The heavy 508×208 RGBA8888 album-art alpha blit is noted as a separate perf item.

**Status:** builds clean (XC32 v5.10, ninja). **Pending Greg's on-hardware check** that first-tap latency is gone on nav open/close, nav-close→dashboard, song-select open, song tap, and song-select close→dashboard. Files: `ui/ui_manager.{c,h}`, `ui/screens/navigation/screen_navigation.c`, `ui/screens/song_select/screen_song_select.c`.

### 2026-07-01 — Video-collapse recovery: watchdog (task #1) + blank HEO/BASE behind modal (Phase 2) + 594 Mbps lane rate (task #2)

**Task #1 — capture stall-watchdog (confirmed recovering on hardware).** New `capture_watchdog()` in `video.c`, called each video-task tick after `reconcile()`: while `s_capture_armed && s_capture_enabled && TC358743_IsLocked()`, it watches `ISC_Capture_FrameCount()`; if the count hasn't advanced for `VIDEO_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (500 ms) it logs a `LOG_WARN` and cycles the chain (`capture_disarm()` → `capture_arm()`), which re-runs `DRV_CSI2DC/CSI/ISC_Configure` and re-inits/re-locks the D-PHY once the bus is quiet. Timing via `xTaskGetTickCount()`; state is two function-static locals (`last_count`, `last_advance_tick`). Self-healing without spam: (a) when not actively capturing the reference is held fresh so a fresh arm gets a full window; (b) `ISC_Capture_Configure` zeroes `g_frame_count`, so after each re-arm the reference re-reads from 0 → a stuck source retries at most once per 500 ms window, not every tick. On hardware the watchdog fires and re-locks, but it fires on **every song switch** (log shows `capture stalled … re-arming` on each select) — recovery works, but the ~0.5–1 s freeze each time is a visible glitch. That motivated Phase 2.

**Task #2 — CSI-2 lane rate 972 → 594 Mbps/lane: TRIED, REVERTED (hypothesis wrong).** Theory was FIFO underrun: the bridge was hard-wired to 972 Mbps/lane (`tc358743.c PLL_FBD=144`) but the source is 720×480p60 (~27 MHz pixel clock, ~250 Mbps/lane needed), and the kernel `tc358743.c` `fifo_level` comment warns that when "CSI reads out faster than the data is arriving" it's underrun-prone → a GFX/DDR perturbation tips it → RX lanes to LP-11 (STOPSTATE=0x3). So I dropped to **594 Mbps** (kernel's characterized 2-lane default; the only lower timing set Linux fully tabulates): `PLL_FBD` 144→88, the nine TX timing `*_VAL` macros → kernel `case 594000000`, `ISC_CAP_CSI_BITRATE` 0x0A→0x07 (Gen3 band 550–600 from `dw-dphy-rx.c range_gen3[]`). **On hardware this made things worse:** boot now failed to hold lock and the watchdog re-armed 5–6× (stalled at frames 161/152/114/7/19) before finally catching a stable lock; reboot reproduced it. At **972** boot had been rock-solid (only the dialog stalled, now fixed by Phase 2). So on *this board* the 972 lane rate locks the D-PHY RX far more reliably at boot than 594 — boot-time lock margin dominates over any steady-state FIFO-underrun benefit. Briefly reverted to 972, then **re-examined** — Greg pushed back (rightly): a slower PHY clock should lock *more* easily, so "594 is just worse" is not an explanation. Clarified symptom: at 594 the frame counter advanced (frames arriving) but the **picture rolled/stuttered** and only turned clean after several re-arms, then stayed stable — i.e. **marginal data-sampling** (byte slips shifting the raster), not lock-loss. Root-cause dig (see also the code comments in `isc_capture.c`/`tc358743.c`): the RX rate knob is a single per-lane hsfreqrange code written by `plib_csi.c csi_phy_hs_rx_init` (plus a *fixed* `0x94` offset-cal validated at 972). Found **two disagreeing hsfreqrange tables**: Linux `dw-dphy-rx.c range_gen3` (594→**0x07**, 972→0x0A) vs the vendor `drv_csi.c CSI_BitRateLookUp` (594→**0x17**, 972→0x1A; vendor = Linux + 0x10 across bands). We used 0x07 (Linux); reality follows Linux at 972 (0x0A works) so 0x07 *should* be right — but 594 is far less forgiving of a near-miss. **hsfreqrange band ruled out (2026-07-01).** Tested 594 with 0x07 then **0x17** (vendor value); *no difference* — both flaky. So the RX band code is not the cause. Sharper symptom read with `VIDEO_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS`=100 ms: **each arm is bimodal** — the D-PHY locks, passes 1–30 frames, then the lanes collapse (watchdog re-arms); *most* arms collapse, but *occasionally* one lands a good calibration and then runs **stable for minutes**. So 594 steady-state is fine; only the **bring-up handshake/calibration is marginal and lands bad most tries**. Since RX band is out and the RX offset-cal (`plib_csi.c` fixed `0x94`) is rate-independent, the differentiator vs rock-solid-972 is **TX-side** (TC358743 CSI PLL at FBD=88/594 MHz, or the 594 D-PHY TX timing counts — esp. `LINEINITCNT` 0xE80 vs 972's 0x1B58, the LP-11 init window the RX must catch), or a PLL-settling race at EnableStream.

**Instrumenting the collapse (2026-07-01).** Added `ISC_Capture_DumpDiag()` (unconditional LOG_WARN variant of the DEBUG-gated `diag_dump_rx`; refactored both onto a shared `emit_rx_diag(tag, lvl)`), fired from `capture_watchdog` at each stall (first 8 only, to not flood UART) *before* the re-arm resets the latches — kept in place as a general stall diagnostic.

**Datasheet nailed the mechanism (2026-07-01 — Greg pointed at SAM9X75 DS §48.6.1.3 / §5.6.6.1.3 Analog Initialization).** Two facts: (1) the bit-rate table confirms **0x17 = 550–599 Mbps** (so our RX code is *correct*, and the vendor `csi_phy_hs_rx_init` matches the datasheet's test-code-0x44 procedure) — the RX band is definitively **not** the fault; (2) *"The initialization period … has a **minimum of 100 µs** … the D-PHY starts decoding low-power commands **after** the analog initialization."* The **RX** needs ≥100 µs to init/calibrate; the **TX** (TC358743) provides that window by holding the opening **LP-11 for `LINEINITCNT`** before the first HS burst. `LINEINITCNT` at 594 (`0xE80`) is much shorter than at 972 (`0x1B58`) — if it's too short the RX per-lane calibration lands *marginal*, giving the rolling picture, and only an occasional re-arm hits a good calibration → then stable forever (**exactly** the bimodal symptom). This is TX-side + rate-dependent, which fits: RX band ruled out, and 972 works because its `LINEINITCNT` window is long enough. **RESOLVED: 972 is the correct rate for this source; keep it (2026-07-01).** Web research (sourced from mainline `tc358743.c`, its header, the FIFO-374 patch, and RPi forum threads by the driver author) settled it: the **TC358743 has a single 512×32-word line FIFO and cannot rate-convert** — the CSI-2 output must drain faster than the HDMI fills. A 27 MHz-pixel 480p source is the *worst* mismatch (drain/fill 1.8× @594, 3× @972). **`FIFOCTL` (0x0006) is the trigger level in 32-bit words (0–511); there is no published formula** (Toshiba's REF_02 `.xls`), 374 is empirical. Crucially, **Raspberry Pi's universal default is 972 Mbps + FIFO 374** — they run 972 for everything and don't fight 594; our working config mirrors theirs exactly. Our `ErrSotSyncHS` is a D-PHY HS-lock failure (not a clean FIFO underflow); the kernel's **594 TX timing counts are marked FIXME/approximate**, and 972's are better-conditioned for this TC358743↔SAM9X75 pairing. **No 594 erratum, no hsfreqrange band-boundary defect** (that hypothesis had zero support). Conclusion: 972 is not a fallback — it's the right operating point for a low-pixel-clock source on this bridge, and the "combined config that works" is the one we already have (972 + FIFO 374 + the 972 timing set). Reverted all three files to 972 / 0x0A / 972-timing. The stall-diagnostic (`ISC_Capture_DumpDiag`, first 8 stalls) is left in as a general D-PHY stall tool; `VIDEO_STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` left at Greg's 100 ms. Task #2 closed — the marginality it aimed to reduce is already gone at 972 (watchdog + Phase 2).

**Rate is not the lever (2026-07-01).** Data points now: 972 = clean/stable; 594 = ErrSotSyncHS, ~1–30 frames/attempt; 540 (FBD=80, band 0x07, FRS=0) = **same ErrSotSyncHS signature**, ~7–173 frames/attempt (marginally longer, so *not* monotonic "lower=worse"). Every sub-972 rate fails identically (`PHY_FATAL=0x3`, `PKT_FATAL=0x1`, `CSI_PHY_RX=0x00030000` invariant); only 972 works. So the working point isn't about bandwidth (540 is ample for 480p's ~324 Mbps/lane need) — something makes only the ~972 region lock reliably. Prime remaining un-validated knob: **FIFOCTL** (see below); research agent still out on the formula + the known-480p issue.

**TC358743 TX config faithfully matches the kernel's proven 594 setup (2026-07-01).** Compared our `tc358743_set_csi()` register-by-register against Linux `drivers/media/i2c/tc358743.c` `tc358743_set_csi()`: CTXRST reset, unused-lane disables (D2W/D3W for 2-lane), all 9 PPI timing counts, HSTXVREGEN (CLM+D0M+D1M), TXOPTIONCNTRL=CONTCLKMODE, STARTCNTRL, CSI_START, and CSI_CONTROL (CSI_MODE|TXHSMD|NOL_2) + err/int enables — **all identical**. PLL (FBD=88→594) and timing counts match the kernel `case 594000000`. So we are *not* missing or misprogramming a TX register; our 594 config is the kernel's known-good 594 config. The **one** setting the kernel author explicitly punted on is **`FIFO_LEVEL` (FIFOCTL, hardcoded 374)** — their comment says it should be computed from input/output data rates but the formula is "buried in Toshiba's REF_02 spreadsheet." That's the prime remaining suspect for a **480p-specific (low ~27 MHz pixel clock)** failure. Greg's recollection: TC358743 is reportedly known-bad with 720×480@60 in Linux (which defaults to 594) — so 972 working for us may be *the* right config for a low-pixel-clock source, not a mere fallback. Launched a web-research agent to find the FIFOCTL formula + the known 480p issue + whether a higher lane rate is the accepted workaround.

**`LINEINITCNT`=0x1B58 did NOT help — but the stall dump nailed the failure class (2026-07-01).** Every collapse dump is identical: `CSI_INT_ST_PHY_FATAL=0x3` (**ErrSotSyncHS on both data lanes**), `PKT_FATAL=0x1` (**header ECC double-bit error**), `STOPSTATE` 0x3→0x0, CSI2DC/ISC otherwise idle+armed (`GSR=0`, `DCTRL=1`). So the RX *does* enter HS (SoT detected — not the 80–110 default, not TX-stopped) but **mis-samples the HS bits** → can't sync the leader byte → ECC-garbage headers. It's a bit-sampling failure. Combined with 0x07≡0x17 (the two bands program *different* THS-SETTLE, yet behaved identically ⇒ settle/band is **not** the dominant error), the other half of the SoT handshake is implicated: the **TX HS timing** (`THS_HEADERCNT` = THS-PREPARE/ZERO) that the RX settle races. Kernel counts: 594 → `0x0103` (prepare=3/zero=1), 972 → `0x0806` (prepare=6/zero=8). At 594 prepare-cnt 3 ≈ 40 ns ≈ *under* the MIPI THS-PREPARE min (~47 ns) → RX can't lock the SoT. **Fix under test:** run 594 rate (`FBD=88`) + 0x17, but with the **full 972 TX timing set** (prepare=6/zero=8, LINEINITCNT 0x1B58, etc.) — longer, known-good-on-this-link, and the lengthened prepare stays under the MIPI max at 594's slower byte clock. Diag dump still armed. **Awaiting hardware.** If clean → the aggressive 594 counts were it; narrow to `THS_HEADERCNT` and compute proper 594 values. If still ErrSotSyncHS → the mis-sample is independent of TX timing (byte-clock/skew at 594, or a CSI/CSI2DC rate assumption) and 594 likely isn't viable via this vendor driver → stay at 972.

**Phase 2 — blank the video (HEO) + discard BASE behind the modal (confirmed on hardware).** Root of the per-switch stall: the album-art RGBA8888 decode/blit + repaint on each selection is a heavy DDR burst that, layered on top of HEO's 720×480×3×60 read and BASE's full-panel read, knocks the marginal D-PHY out of lock. The dialog fully covers the video window, so displaying either behind it is pure wasted bandwidth. `UiManager_OpenSongSelect` now `UiManager_VideoHide()`s (HEO output off — **capture keeps running** for the detector/gameplay) and sets `s_dialog_discard`; `CloseSongSelect` clears both and re-`VideoShow`s. **BASE DISCEN is now reconcile-owned** (journal's `{video rect | dialog rect | none}`): the DISCEN writes were pulled out of `heo_bind`/`heo_unbind` into a single `base_discard_apply` (writes only on change, one `s_base_disc` cache) driven by `base_discard_reconcile()` at the end of `heo_reconcile` — video rect while HEO bound, else the 1100×660 dialog rect while `s_dialog_discard`, else off. Both HEO-hide and BASE-discard are UI-task *intent* consumed by the video-task reconcile, so HEO/BASE stay single-writer. Dialog is opaque RGB565 with square corners (no BASE showing through), so discarding the full rect is safe. **To confirm on hardware:** open/close + song-switching should no longer stall capture (watchdog quiet), and video should reappear cleanly on close. Task #2 (right-size the D-PHY lane rate) still open — if Phase 2 makes stalls rare enough it may not be needed.

### 2026-06-30 — HEO (video display) control moved into ui_manager; video.c → capture producer

Refactor so the compositor owns the HEO hardware layer and video.c is a pure capture producer — enabling screens that don't show video, future overlay-on-video (a separate OVR layer, **not** drawing into the RGB888 capture buffer), and HEO reuse when video is idle. **Seam:** video.c keeps ISC/TC358743/ring/subscribers and exposes two registered callbacks; **all HEO/BASE register writes moved to `ui_manager`** but execute in the same contexts as before (so timing/threading is unchanged and no locks are added): `Video_SetDisplayReconcileCallback` fires each video-task tick with `(source_valid, w, h)` → ui_manager's `heo_reconcile` (re)binds/unbinds HEO; `Video_SetFrameLatchCallback` fires from the ISC IRQ with the freshest buffer → ui_manager's `heo_frame_latch` re-points HEO. The UI task only sets volatile intent (`UiManager_VideoShow(rect)` / `UiManager_VideoHide()`), consumed by the reconcile, so HEO/BASE stay single-writer. `heo_bind`/`heo_unbind`/`scaler_factor` + BASE DISCEN moved out of video.c (was `lcd_bind`/`lcd_unbind`/`display_apply`). Video-window layout constants moved app.c → ui_manager (compositor owns layout). Boot: ui_manager parks HEO off after the dashboard binds and calls `UiManager_VideoShow` at reveal; `app.c` now only `Video_CaptureEnable()`s (capture is needed for the detector/gameplay regardless of display). Callbacks registered in `UiManager_Initialize` before the video task starts.

**Root-caused a long-standing "video collapses to ~1 fps" bug — it was a CSI-2 D-PHY bring-up ordering issue, NOT this refactor.** Symptom: capture ran ~60 fps until the video went live, then dropped to ~0–1 fps with a near-frozen, vertically-wrapping frame. `git bisect` pinned it to **d0763197** (the earlier "rewrite UI boot — splash + ui_manager, retire loader" commit — which is C-only and never touched `video.c`/`isc_capture.c`), working at its parent `0314c8fc`. A staged diagnostic walked it down: `cap` counter (capture IRQ rate) → confirmed the collapse; `LCDC commits/s` ≈ 0 and removing the ISC-IRQ HEO latch didn't help → **ruled out** the compositor/`LCDC_ATTRE` busy-wait and DDR bandwidth (a clean 60→<1 is a hard stall, not contention); a capture-pipeline register dump while stalled was the tell — `ISC_DCTRL=1` (DMA armed), `CSI2DC_GSR=0` (healthy), but **`CSI_PHY_STOPSTATE=0x3`** (both data lanes parked in LP-11, only flicking to 0 occasionally). So the *input* was dead: the CSI-2 D-PHY RX was locking only marginally — no pixels arriving, the whole downstream chain idle-waiting. **Cause:** `d0763197` reordered capture bring-up to fire *early* (in the splash-shown callback, during the boot task/SD/paint contention and before the display clocks settle); the D-PHY RX is timing-sensitive at init (must catch the source's LP11→HS transition) and locks marginally in that window. `0314c8fc` armed capture *last*, after reveal. **Fix:** arm `Video_CaptureEnable()` at the **end of `ui_boot_task`** (after `UiManager_VideoShow`), not in `app_on_splash_shown` — matching the old ordering. Confirmed on hardware (60 fps, solid).

Separately, `heo_frame_latch` was made **non-blocking** (write `LCDC_HEO[0].LCDC_HEOYFBA` + `LCDC_ATTRE |= LCDC_ATTRE_HEO_Msk`, no `while(LCDC_ATTRE)` wait) and **unconditional** — a correctness hardening (an ISC IRQ should never busy-wait on the display), not the fix for the above.

**OPEN — resume 2026-07-01: video drops again after closing the song-select dialog.** The late-capture-arm fix cures the *boot* case, but the CSI-2 D-PHY lock is **marginal**: a UI-transition GFX burst (dialog open/close — OVR1/OVR2 bind+hide, album-art RGBA8888 decode/blit, full-dashboard repaint on close) knocks the RX lanes back to stop-state, and it does **not** self-recover (TC358743 still reports locked, so `reconcile` never re-arms → capture sits dead). `0314c8fc` never had a dialog to open/close, so this interaction is new, not a regression. Two tasks queued (agreed with Greg):

1. **Capture stall-watchdog (robust fix, do first).** ✅ Implemented 2026-07-01 (`video.c` `capture_watchdog`, called each video-task tick after `reconcile`) — **not yet confirmed on hardware.** In the video task: if `s_capture_enabled && TC358743_IsLocked()` but `ISC_Capture_FrameCount()` hasn't advanced for ~500 ms, `capture_disarm()` + `capture_arm()` **once** (not every tick). `capture_arm` re-runs `DRV_CSI2DC/CSI/ISC_Configure` → re-inits the D-PHY and re-locks cleanly once the transition is quiet. Self-heals any disturbance (this dialog, future screens, transient glitches); cost is a sub-second video freeze on re-lock after a heavy transition.
2. **Revisit the CSI-2 D-PHY clock rate (reduce marginality at the source).** ✅ Resolved 2026-07-01 — **keep 972**. 594/540 both fail HS SoT-sync; RX band and TX timing don't move it. Root cause: the TC358743's single-line FIFO makes a low-pixel-clock 480p source *want* the higher CSI rate — 972 + FIFO 374 is the correct, RPi-standard operating point, not a fallback. Lower is the wrong direction on this bridge. Full write-up in the session log.

**Phase 2 (✅ implemented 2026-07-01, confirmed on hardware — see the session-log entry above):** while the song-select dialog is open, `UiManager_VideoHide()` (HEO output off, capture continues) + set the effective BASE DISCEN to the dialog rect (skip BASE DMA behind the opaque 1100×660 dialog); DISCEN is now reconcile-owned (`{video → video rect | dialog → dialog rect | none}`) to stay single-writer. **Still deferred/possible:** dim-dashboard by repurposing HEO under OVR1 while a modal is open.

**Considered, not done — fully disabling the capture pipeline while a modal is open.** Phase 2 stops the HEO *read* but capture keeps running: the ISC still DMA-*writes* ~1 MB/frame × 60 ≈ 62 MB/s to DDR that nobody consumes while the dialog is up (detector/gameplay idle during song-select). We *could* `Video_CaptureDisable()` on open / `Video_CaptureEnable()` on close to reclaim that write bandwidth too. **Deliberately not doing it:** disabling capture tears down the D-PHY, so re-enabling on close re-runs `..._Configure` → a marginal re-lock, reintroducing the ~0.5–1 s close-time freeze Phase 2 + the watchdog exist to avoid. It trades idle write bandwidth for a *guaranteed* hitch on every close. Revisit only if that write traffic is later shown to matter (a heavier future screen, or if leaving capture armed itself provokes stalls); the watchdog keeps correctness either way.

### 2026-06-30 — Song-select dialog is now a modal (open on header tap, close on X/SELECT)

The dialog + its OVR2 cover strip no longer show at boot — the dashboard reveals alone and the dialog opens on demand. `ui_manager` gained `UiManager_OpenSongSelect()`/`UiManager_CloseSongSelect()`: open binds + shows the pair (`bind_canvas` SONGSEL→OVR1 RGB565, ALBUM_ART→OVR2 RGBA8888); close `gfxcHideCanvas` both (freeing OVR1 for the nav drawer). State tracked by a `s_songsel_open` guard (idempotent). At reveal the two boot `bind_canvas(...,true)` show calls were removed and replaced by a single `XLCDC_SetLayerRGBColorMode(OVR1, RGB565)` poke so a first nav-open (nav shares OVR1 and doesn't set RGBMODE itself) still scans out correctly before any dialog open. **Open trigger:** a tap on the dashboard **top header bar** — `screen_dashboard.c` re-points the (shared) vtable of `PANEL_BASE_TOP` + `PANEL_SYSTEM_LEFT/TITLE/RIGHT` at a copy whose `touchDownEvent` calls `UiManager_OpenSongSelect` (same per-vtable override pattern as `widget_song_list.c`/`widget_panel_aa.c`). Legato delivers a touch only to the topmost picked widget without bubbling, so the hamburger button (→ nav) and the title labels keep their own behaviour; only taps on the bare header panels open the dialog. **Close:** `screen_song_select.c` wires `BUTTON_SONG_SELECT_CLOSE` (the X) and `_SELECT` release callbacks to `UiManager_CloseSongSelect` (SELECT is a placeholder until it starts gameplay).

**Touch pass-through (MGS layer-screen "background panel" pattern).** First cut blocked dashboard touches under the *closed* dialog: Legato treats every layer-screen as stacked + pickable regardless of canvas visibility, so the topmost layer's first pickable widget eats touches even when its canvas is hidden ([MGS layer-screen guide → Using Background Panel Widgets](https://developerhelp.microchip.com/xwiki/bin/view/software-tools/mgs/mgs-harmony-guide/how-to/gfx-canvas/layer-scr/)). Our roots are all `IGNOREPICK`, so the capturing widgets are the layers' **background panels** — `PANEL_SONG_SELECT` (layer 2, 1100×660 centered) and `PANEL_SONG_SELECT_ALBUM_ART` (layer 3, 508×208); while enabled they swallowed dashboard touches in those rects (header above the dialog still worked — hence "mostly working"). Fix: `ui_manager` `songsel_set_input(on)` toggles `setEnabled` on the background panels — the dialog panels enabled while shown / disabled while hidden (so input falls through to the dashboard), **and `PANEL_DASHBOARD` (layer 0) inversely disabled while the dialog is open** so it's a true modal (the uncovered header/hamburger behind the dialog don't react). Disabling a panel only gates pick for its subtree; child rendering is unaffected (per-widget VISIBLE/flags). Nav (layer 1) passes *its own* layer through a different way — its canvas window slides fully off-screen on close (`LE_DRIVER_LAYER_MODE` pick rect follows the window), so its panel needs no toggle. Both modals (dialog and nav) also **disable `PANEL_DASHBOARD` while open** and re-enable on close, so nothing behind reacts; this makes them mutually exclusive from the open side (dialog open → hamburger inert → can't open nav; nav open → header inert → can't open dialog; the open drawer also covers the hamburger, so nav still closes via its Dashboard entry).

**Out of scope / follow-up:** opening the nav drawer *while the dialog is open* is unhandled — both target OVR1, and the hamburger sits in the uncovered header strip so it can still be tapped. Uncommitted; needs a build + on-hardware check.

### 2026-06-30 — Album-artwork pre-cache (`game/art.{c,h}`) + offline difficulty-fade pipeline

Built the cover-art cache that spec §4.8.7 called for; resolves Q13. New `game/art.{c,h}`: two fixed-size **RGBA8888** tiers in cached DDR (`.region_ram`) — small 144×144 (5.3 MB), large 508×208 (27 MB) — plus a 512 KB shared decode scratch (~33 MB total). (Originally RGB888 ~23 MB; switched to RGBA8888 so the cover source matches the RGBA8888 display layer and is 2D-engine-blittable — see the full-color entry below.) `Art_LoadAll()` runs in `ui_boot_task` **before** `init_screens()` (Legato decoders are up from SYS_Initialize; it mounts storage itself) so the cache is populated when `ScreenSongSelect_Setup` → `song_detail_show(0)` paints the initial selection's cover; the ~1–3 s of JPEG/PNG decode is hidden behind the splash. It walks `art/{small,large}/` via `SYS_FS_DirOpen`/`DirRead` in **two phases** — collect the `<setlist>-<NN>.<ext>` entries, **close the dir**, then open+decode each (FatFs here is `FF_FS_MAX_FILES=1`, so a dir handle and a file handle can't be open at once; holding the dir open made every `SYS_FS_FileOpen` return `FR_TOO_MANY_OPEN_FILES`=18 — the bug that made the first cut load 0/0 despite the files being present). Phase 2 reconstructs each path from the `(setlist,index,fmt)` key, which also sidesteps macOS `._` AppleDouble cruft on the card. It reads each file into scratch, reads true dims from the JPEG `SOF`/PNG `IHDR` header (the destination raster is sized from these), requires an exact tier-dimension match, then decodes into the slot with `leImage_Render` — the JPEG/PNG decoder `render` path writes RGB888 straight into the slot via `lePixelBufferSet`+`leColorConvert`, with no dependence on the screen clip rect or an active paint frame (verified by reading the decoder). Each slot is wrapped as a RAW RGB888 `leImage`; `Art_Large/Small(setlist,index)` is a linear scan → `leImage*` or NULL. Missing/oversize/wrong-size/corrupt → empty slot → UI blank, never a boot failure.

Wiring: `user.cmake` (+`game/art.c`), `Art_Initialize()` in `app.c`, `Art_LoadAll()` in `ui_manager.c`, `art` console command (cache fill / per-key presence). Song-select (`screen_song_select.c`) sets the **MGS-provided** `Marvin_IMAGE_ALBUM_ART_0_0` (508×208 image widget already in the `Marvin` layout) on every selection via a `song_art_show()` folded into `song_detail_show()`; the maroon `PANEL_ALBUM_ART_OVERLAY` MGS built stays invisible (fade is baked offline).

Offline (`tools/gh3-cover-art/`): `process_gh3_cover_art.py` reworked — large output is now a center-cropped 508×208 strip with a difficulty-colored top/bottom fade baked in, read per song from `songs.csv` (`"1".."8"` → an 8-tier palette green→red with blue/purple accents via `TIER_COLORS`, `"bonus"`/unknown → gray), saved as PNG (lossless gradient). Small stays 144×144 letterboxed JPEG. `install_art.py` installs large as `<setlist>-NN.png`. Fade tunables (`FADE_TOP_ALPHA`/`FADE_BOTTOM_DARK`/`FADE_GAMMA`) at the top of the script.

The song-select **TIER label** (`SONG_LEVEL`) now renders "★…★ TIER n" (n stars, career tier) / "BONUS", in DejaVuSansMonoBold 12pt (carries U+2605), colored by tier via MGS schemes `SCHEME_TEXT_TIER_1..8` (text color = the `TIER_COLORS` palette; bonus → existing `SCHEME_TEXT_GRAY_D4D4D8`). C side wired in `screen_song_select.c`; schemes created in MGS 2026-06-30. DejaVu-only font policy recorded (decision log + spec §4.5).

Status / bring-up bugs found & fixed (all on hardware): (1) **FF_FS_MAX_FILES=1** — the dir handle + a file handle can't both be open, so the first cut logged `0 small, 0 large` (`FR_TOO_MANY_OPEN_FILES`=18); fixed by the two-phase walk above. (2) **`png_dims` read the wrong IHDR bytes** (high 2 of the 4-byte width/height, always 0) → every large PNG parsed 0×0; fixed to read the low two bytes. (3) **`leImage_Create` stamps `header.size` with the *decoded* size, but the PNG decoder passes it to lodepng as the *compressed* input length** → lodepng read past the file into stale scratch → silent decode failure (black); fixed by setting `src.header.size = compressed file size`. (4) **GFX2D cache coherency** — the decode writes the slot via the CPU (write-back), but Legato draws the image with `leGPU_BlitBuffer` (2D engine DMA-reads DDR), so it saw stale/zero DDR → black; fixed with a one-time `dcache_CleanByAddr(slot)` after decode (sufficient because the slot is write-once, unlike the per-frame scratch that had to go non-cached in 2026-06-24). A per-tier diagnostic logs files-seen/loaded + the first cover's center pixel (kept through bring-up). With these, both tiers decode to valid pixels and the strip renders.

**Full-color via a dedicated layer-screen (done; 2026-06-30).** Song-select stays **RGB565** (DDR bandwidth); the large art is its **own MGS layer-screen** — Marvin **layer 3, RGBA8888**, a 508×208 canvas composited on **OVR2 above the dialog on OVR1**, so only the art rect pays 32bpp (~0.42 MB). `LE_LAYER_COUNT` is now 4. **RGBA8888, not RGB888:** the 2D engine has **no RGB888 support** (`gfx2dFormats[GFX_COLOR_MODE_RGB_888] = -1`; only `RGB_565`→`GFX2D_RGB16` and `RGBA_8888`→`GFX2D_ARGB32`), so a 24bpp canvas can't be GFX2D-blitted scratch→canvas and renders as static garbage (confirmed on hardware: OVR2 showed a fixed white-ish pattern). MGS authored the layer as `RGB_888`; `ui_manager` overrides it to `LE_COLOR_MODE_RGBA_8888` right after `screenShow_Marvin` (set the layer-screen to RGBA8888 in MGS to make the override redundant). The cover **cache** (`game/art.c`) is also **RGBA8888** (4 B/px) so the source matches the layer and the 2D engine can blit it natively (RGB888 is unblittable as *source* too — `gfx2dFormats[RGB_888] = -1`). Covers are opaque (fade baked into RGB), so `decode_one` forces the alpha byte to 0xFF after `leImage_Render` (a JPEG/PNG-without-alpha decode into RGBA8888 can leave alpha 0 → fully transparent → invisible). Cache size rose to ~33 MB cached DDR (large 27 MB + small 5.3 MB + 0.5 MB scratch) — fine in the 224 MB region. MGS hosts the cover image **plus the TIER/title/artist labels** on this layer (they overlay the art at full color); album/year/genre/duration stay on the RGB565 dialog. New firmware: `ui/screens/album_art/screen_album_art.{c,h}` (canvas `CANVAS_ALBUM_ART`=3, `GFX_COLOR_MODE_RGBA_8888` 4 B/px buffer in `.region_nocache`, cleared to opaque black since NOLOAD, window at screen (433,160)); `ui_manager` reveal now binds **song-select→OVR1**, **album-art→OVR2** (`XLCDC_RGB_COLOR_MODE_RGBA_8888`), and **no longer binds nav at boot** — `navigation_open()` grabs OVR1 only while shown (nav and the dialog share OVR1, mutually exclusive). MGS widget rename sweep (`_0_0` dropped; `SongTier`/`SongTitle`/`Artist`) reconciled in `screen_song_select.c`.

**PNG decode: two stock-Legato issues fixed (confirmed full-color on hardware 2026-06-30).** Pre-decoding the PNG covers via `leImage_Render` hit (a) a real **bug in `legato_imagedecoder_png.c` `_render()`** — it copies the *compressed* `src->buffer` and skips the byte-swap that `_draw()` does → garbage (re-apply patch #9; to be filed upstream), and (b) **lodepng heap exhaustion** — a 508×208 RGBA decode peaks ~740 KB vs the 512 KB `LE_VARIABLEHEAP_SIZE`, giving intermittent `err 83` on ~25–30% of covers (raised to **2 MB**, re-apply patch #10). With both, all 60 covers decode and the strip renders full-color. Diagnostics that proved this (a direct-write RGBA test pattern, per-cover decode logging) have been removed.

**Per-selection blit: use `LE_IMAGE_DIRECT_BLIT` (the cover slots set it).** First cut had a visible hesitation on each song selection — the cover was being re-blitted **per-pixel in software**. Cause: `leGPU_BlitBuffer` sets `SRC_OVER` blend whenever the source is an *alpha mode* (RGBA8888 always is, regardless of actual opacity — `LE_COLOR_MODE_IS_ALPHA`), and the GFX2D driver's blit only implements `PLIB_GFX2D_Copy` (returns `GFX_FAILURE` for any blend), so `leGPU_BlitBuffer` fails and the RAW decoder falls back to its per-pixel software stage over all 508×208 px. There's no full-color *non-alpha* GFX2D-blittable source mode (RGB888 is unsupported; RGB565 is lossy), so a true GPU copy isn't available for the cover. Fix: set `LE_IMAGE_DIRECT_BLIT` on the cached `leImage`s — with the source mode matching the RGBA8888 layer, the RAW decoder's `_draw` takes `_directBlit`, a per-row `memcpy` (no per-pixel convert/blend). Covers are opaque + fill the widget, so the overwrite is correct; labels still composite on top. Removes the hesitation.

**GPU blit not reachable without a driver change (noted, not done).** Confirmed the cover can't be true-GPU-blitted as-is: `DRV_GFX2D_Blit` returns `GFX_FAILURE` for every non-NONE blend and never wires `PLIB_GFX2D_Blend`, so the GFX2D driver does **zero** hardware blending — *all* alpha-blended blits in the UI silently fall to CPU, not just covers. Reachable only by forcing NONE for opaque RGBA8888 (loosens the global `IS_ALPHA` test — risky) or implementing `SRC_OVER` via `PLIB_GFX2D_Blend` in the driver (the right, general fix; candidate second Microchip bug alongside the PNG one). Not worth it for a one-shot per-selection copy — left on `_directBlit`.

**Rounded album-art corners (radius 14) — opaque backdrop fill, not transparency (2026-06-30).** The art is alone on OVR2 over the dialog on OVR1, and the dialog area behind it is a **known solid gray** (`SCHEME_PANEL_GRAY_18181B` — the same gray the song list AA-blends against). That turns the hard *transparency* case (punch OVR2 corners transparent to reveal OVR1 — needs per-pixel layer alpha, deferred when the AA panel widget landed) into the easy *opaque* case: paint the corner wedges opaque with that gray. New `AaCorners_RenderRoundImage(rect, radius, bg)` — same arc math as `AaCorners_Render`, but keeps the inside of the arc (the image) untouched and only fades the *outside* wedge to `bg` with an AA edge; `PanelAA_EnableRoundImage()` drives it from the empty `PANEL_ALBUM_ART_OVERLAY` (sibling painted **after** the cover, so the image is in the buffer when it runs), `bg` = the overlay's BASE scheme color. Reusable for the dashboard thumbnail (same call, `bg` = that card's color). **Dialog corners are NOT covered** — they sit over layer 0 (not a known solid color) and OVR1 is RGB565 (no per-pixel alpha to reveal layer 0); rounding them would need a cross-layer variant that samples the real BASE-layer pixels behind each corner. Deferred. (One thing to confirm on hardware: the overlay repaints when `setImage` swaps the cover, so corners re-round per selection — Legato should mark the overlapping overlay dirty.)

**Follow-ups (open):** dialog open/close is now wired (modal: header-tap open, X/SELECT close — session log 2026-06-30); remaining is **nav-vs-dialog OVR1 coexistence** (opening nav while the dialog is open is unhandled — both target OVR1) and **SELECT should start gameplay** rather than just close. The dashboard small thumbnail can reuse this dedicated-layer trick (and `PanelAA_EnableRoundImage` for its corners). **Delete patch #9 once the upstream Legato PNG `_render` fix ships**, and reconsider the 2 MB heap (patch #10) if PNG is ever swapped for JPEG. Dashboard thumbnail still deferred.

### 2026-06-30 — UTF-8 for dynamic strings (song-select + song-list)

Non-ASCII chars in dynamically-set labels were rendering as two glyphs each. Root cause: Legato decodes UTF-8 only on the *string-table* path (`leDecodeCodePoint` keyed off the table's `encodingMode`, which we switched to UTF-8 in MGS). Fixed/dynamic strings have no encoding mode — `setFromCStr` and the C-string renderer (`drawCString`) are byte-oriented: each UTF-8 byte becomes its own `leChar`/glyph. `leChar` is `uint16_t`, so strings are really UCS-2 code-point arrays.

Fix: new shared helper `default/src/util/legato_utf8.{c,h}` — `utf8_to_lechar()` decodes a UTF-8 C string into a `leChar` buffer via `leDecodeCodePoint(LE_STRING_ENCODING_UTF8, …)`; `lestring_set_utf8()` wraps it + the base `setFromChar` vtable call (works for any fixed/dynamic string). Song-select detail labels now use `lestring_set_utf8` instead of `setFromCStr`. The song-list widget renders rows through the C-string renderer, which can't be made UTF-8-aware, so `draw_str` now decodes into a stack `leChar` buffer and renders via `leStringRenderer_DrawUString` (the leChar-buffer path; alignment logic is identical to the C-string path). BMP-only (code points >U+FFFF truncate) — fine for song metadata. Not yet confirmed on hardware.

### 2026-06-29 — Song-select detail labels mirror the selected song (confirmed on hardware w/ SD)

Wired the dialog's detail labels to the selected catalog entry: `SONG_LEVEL`←difficulty,
`SONG_TITLE`←title, `SONG_ARTIST`←artist, `SongAlbum`←album, `SongYear`←year,
`SongGenre`←genre, `SongDuration`←`m:ss`. Updated on the default (first-song) selection
and on every list tap (the `SongList` select handler calls `song_detail_show(index)`).

Legato runtime-text mechanics worth remembering: `leLabelWidget` `setString` only
*references* the string (and installs invalidate callbacks on it), so each label gets a
file-scope `leFixedString` over a static `leChar` buffer (no heap — honours the
static-allocation rule). Legato strings carry their own font, so `song_detail_init`
copies the font from each label's original MGS table string (`getString`→`getFont`) onto
its fixed string before `setString`, so the dynamic text renders exactly as designed.
`set_detail` rewrites via `setFromCStr`; the label repaints through the installed
invalidate callback. Empty/unknown fields render "-" (em-dash avoided — label fonts may
lack the glyph). Confirmed on hardware with an SD card; minor tweaks deferred.

### 2026-06-29 — Song list dropped into the song-select dialog (catalog-backed, transparent, empty-state)

Wired the catalog-backed `SongList` widget into the song-select dialog's LEFT panel
(`ScreenSongSelect`), below the 33px SETLIST header: DejaVu Mono 12 — bold title over
regular artist/right-aligned duration — 44px rows, catalog row provider (`Catalog_At`),
tap logs the selection, defaults to the first song (`SongList_SetSelected(list, 0)`,
no-op while empty). Two `SongList` widget additions driven by this:

- **Empty state** — when `count == 0` the widget draws a centred placeholder over its
  background (default "No songs", settable via `SongList_SetEmptyText`). Previously an
  empty model just left a black void.
- **Transparency toggle** — `SongList_SetTransparent(w, on)`: skips the hardcoded
  `SL_BG` fill and flips `backgroundType` to `NONE` so the parent panel shows through.
  The dialog list uses it + sets the list's scheme to the dialog's
  `SCHEME_PANEL_GRAY_18181B` so the glyph anti-alias blends against the real backdrop
  (`0x18181B`). (Row/text colours are still hardcoded `SL_*`, not scheme-driven; the
  scheme only feeds the glyph AA lookup — full scheme-ification is a separate option.)

Catalog/SD timing: the count is latched at `SetModel` time; the dialog currently builds
at boot before the SD mounts (and the dev board has no card), so the list shows the
empty state. Not a real-flow problem — long-term the catalog is loaded before the dialog
is opened; the model reload belongs in the (future) dialog-open path. Confirmed on
hardware (empty state renders; transparency blends with the panel).

### 2026-06-29 — AA rounded-corner panel widget (`PanelAA_Enable`), applied to the dashboard robot-controls card (confirmed on hardware)

Added `ui/widgets/panel_aa/widget_panel_aa.{c,h}` — `PanelAA_Enable(leWidget*)`, the
panel counterpart to `ButtonAA_Enable`, over the shared `ui/gfx/aa_corners` renderer.
Same vtable-re-point trick on the base `leWidget` vtable; fill = panel `BASE`, 1px
`SHADOWDARK` ring if `LINE` border, any radius. Backdrop is sampled from the panel's
own corner, so this is for **rounded child panels on an opaque parent** (the common
case); the top-level overlay/transparency case (song-select dialog over the dashboard)
is explicitly out of scope (needs per-pixel alpha on the layer — deferred; bandwidth
trade-offs discussed). First use: `ScreenDashboard_Setup` rounds `PANEL_ROBOT_CONTROLS`
(256×259, `LINE` border, child of the opaque `BASE_LEFT`) at radius 4 — which also
validates the variable-radius path (≠ the 12 default) and the border path on a panel.
Builds clean; confirmed on hardware.

### 2026-06-29 — AA rounded corners: border support + variable radius, renderer split out (confirmed on hardware)

Applied anti-aliased rounded corners to the song-select buttons, which forced two
improvements to the AA path. The difficulty + mode buttons are `LE_WIDGET_BORDER_LINE`
(1px rounded line border in the scheme's `SHADOWDARK`); the old AA blended fill only,
so the stepped border arc showed through. Reworked it:

- **Split the corner renderer into a widget-agnostic helper** `ui/gfx/aa_corners.{c,h}`
  — `AaCorners_Render(rect, radius, borderWidth, fill, border, mode)`. It repaints the
  four corner boxes with an analytic per-pixel blend: backdrop → (optional border ring)
  → fill, with 1px AA bands at the outer (radius) and inner (radius−border) arcs.
  Borderless collapses to backdrop↔fill (the old behaviour). Reusable by a future
  panel-AA wrapper (same shape: re-point the panel `_paint`, pull fill/border from its
  scheme, call the renderer).
- **Variable radius** — coverage is computed analytically from `cornerRadius`, so the
  fixed radius-12 precomputed mask + the `cornerRadius == BUTTON_AA_RADIUS` constraint
  are gone. `BUTTON_AA_RADIUS` (12) is now just a shared default. `widget_button_aa.c`
  shrank to a thin button wrapper that extracts fill (BASE/BACKGROUND by state), border
  (1px SHADOWDARK if LINE), and radius, then calls the shared renderer.
- Cost is a non-issue: the AA only runs on a widget repaint = a state change (the screen
  is otherwise pre-rendered/static), never per frame — so the analytic per-pixel blend
  (a `sqrtf` per corner pixel, ~hundreds of px) is paid only on a tap.

Song-select difficulty/mode/select buttons now AA via a small `round_button()` helper
(`setCornerRadius(BUTTON_AA_RADIUS)` + `ButtonAA_Enable`); SELECT is borderless, the
others LINE-bordered. Nav buttons (radius 12, no border) unchanged. Confirmed on hardware.

### 2026-06-29 — Song-select difficulty + mode radio groups (confirmed on hardware)

Wired the song-select dialog's two button groups as single-select radios in
`screen_song_select.c` (same released-event + scheme-swap pattern as the nav's
single-active highlight). DIFFICULTY (Easy/Medium/Hard/Expert): shared unselected
scheme `SCHEME_BUTTON_DIFFICULTY`, **per-button** selected scheme
(`SCHEME_BUTTON_EASY/MEDIUM/HARD/EXPERT`). MODE (1P robot / 1P human / 2P robot-vs-human):
shared unselected `SCHEME_BUTTON_MODE`, shared selected `SCHEME_BUTTON_MODE_SELECTED`.
Selection persists in `s_difficulty`/`s_mode` (defaults Easy / 1P robot, applied at
`ScreenSongSelect_Setup`); getters to be exposed when the SELECT action is wired.
Re-picking the active entry is a no-op (no deselect). AA/rounded corners on these
buttons deferred.

### 2026-06-29 — UI screen module-layout + naming cleanup (behavior-preserving)

Made the screen modules uniform now that there are four of them. (1) Dashboard got its own
module `screens/dashboard/screen_dashboard.{c,h}` — its framebuffer + window setup moved out of
`ui_manager` (it had been the one panel special-cased inline). (2) `screens/nav/screen_nav` →
`screens/navigation/screen_navigation` (file + folder + all our `nav_*`/`NAV_*`/`CANVAS_NAV`
symbols → `navigation_*`/`NAVIGATION_*`/`CANVAS_NAVIGATION`; MGS-generated `Marvin_BUTTON_NAV_*_0`
/ `SCHEME_NAV_BUTTON_*` / `event_Marvin_*` left alone). (3) `screens/splash/splash.{c,h}` →
`screen_splash.{c,h}`. (4) Public functions renamed to a `Screen<Name>_` prefix (PascalCase of
the file basename): `ScreenDashboard_*`, `ScreenNavigation_*`, `ScreenSongSelect_*`,
`ScreenSplash_*` — replacing the inconsistent `Dash_`/`SongSel_`/`Navigation_`/`Splash_`. The
convention is now documented in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §6.1: a screen lives in
`screens/<name>/screen_<name>.{c,h}` and exposes `Screen<Name>_InitSurface()` + `Screen<Name>_Setup()`
(splash, not being a canvas, exposes `Load`/`Show`/`Hide`). `ui_manager` calls them symmetrically;
no panel is special-cased. `git mv` preserved history; `user.cmake` + includes updated. Builds
clean (xc32 v5.10); pure rename/move, no behavior change.

### 2026-06-29 — Splash decoupled from GFX_CANVAS: drives its XLCDC layer directly (confirmed on hardware)

Follow-up to the Marvin migration. The splash had been parked on **canvas slot 7** (outside the
layer-screen range) just so it could ride the GFX_CANVAS show/hide path — a magic number for a
thing that isn't a canvas. It now owns its static framebuffer and **drives its XLCDC hardware
layer directly via the PLIB**, the same way `video.c` drives the camera on HEO, so it's no longer
in the canvas pool at all (pool is now exactly the three layer-screens 0/1/2). `splash.c`
`Splash_Show(XLCDC_LAYER)` mirrors the XLCDC GFX driver's own commit sequence
(`drv_gfx_xlcdc.c`): `SetLayerRGBColorMode` → `SetLayerAddress` → `SetLayerOpts(255, dma=true)` →
window pos/size → `SetLayerXStride(0)` → `SetLayerEnable(true, update=true)` — the final
`update=true` latches all the deferred attributes at vsync (handles the SIP latch we fought during
splash bring-up). `Splash_Hide` just disables the layer; the hand-off to the nav is clean because
`bind_canvas(CANVAS_NAV, OVR1)` reprograms OVR1 from scratch. `ui_manager` deleted `CANVAS_SPLASH`
and now calls `Splash_Show(xlcdc_layer(SPLASH_HW_LAYER))` / `Splash_Hide(...)`; it still owns the
layer choice. The **`enable_dma=true` in `SetLayerOpts` is load-bearing** — without it the layer
never fetches from DRAM (found by reading the driver's own commit path). Boots and reveals
identically on hardware.

### 2026-06-29 — UI migrated to the Marvin single-master-screen / layer-screen model (confirmed on hardware)

Regenerated the MGS design as **one master Legato screen `Marvin`** with three layers — L0 dashboard (full-screen), L1 nav (320×800), L2 song-select (1100×660) — each built by MGS directly onto its own Legato layer/canvas. Deleted the four standalone screens (Dashboard/Navigation/SongSelect/LayerBudget); `LE_LAYER_COUNT`=3 is now **derived** from the layer count (the `LayerBudget` pin hack is gone). Widget names are now `Marvin_*` (MGS dedup suffixes `_0` on nav, `_0_0` on song-select); generated event hooks renamed (`event_Marvin_BUTTON_SYSYEM_NAVIGATION_OnPressed`, `event_Marvin_BUTTON_GUITAR_STRUM_*`). Non-persistent, no OnShow — only `screenInit_Marvin`/`screenGetRoot_Marvin` + event handlers are generated.

Code side: `ui_manager` no longer builds/disconnects/re-hosts per screen — it calls `screenInit_Marvin()` once, then per-panel `Nav_Setup()`/`SongSel_Setup()` (window + event wiring on already-placed widgets), and binds canvases to HW layers at display time. New `ui/screens/song_select/screen_song_select.c` (owns CANVAS_SONGSEL=2, content-sized RGB565 buffer, centered window at (90,70)). `screen_nav.c` ported to `Marvin_*_0` widgets, re-host dropped, `Navigation_OnShow`→`Nav_Setup`. `manual_input.c` strum handlers renamed. **Splash moved off canvas slot 2 → slot 7** (outside the layer-screen range 0..2, since L2 now renders into canvas[2]). Retired `widget_song_list_demo` + its `songlist` console command (its job was bring-up before a real song-select screen existed). At reveal the song-select dialog is brought up on OVR2 over the live dashboard — the initial "on screen at startup" milestone. Builds clean (xc32 v5.10) and **boots on hardware** — splash → dashboard with the song-select dialog centered on OVR2 over the live dashboard.

**Gotcha that cost a debug round (silent prefetch-abort, splash never revealed):** for the regenerated Marvin screen, `screenInit_Marvin` only flips the one-time `initialized` flag — the **widget tree is constructed in `screenShow_Marvin`** (the `leWidget_New` calls + `leAddRootWidget(rootN, N)` + `leSetLayerColorMode`). Calling only `screenInit_Marvin()` built nothing, so every widget global stayed NULL and `Nav_Setup`'s first `widget->fn->...` call dereferenced NULL → `prefetch_abort` to PC `0x60` (fault handlers are silent `while(1)` spins, so it just froze with the log stuck mid-line). Fix: `ui_manager` calls `screenInit_Marvin()` **then `screenShow_Marvin()`**. (So the earlier "only `screenInit` + `screenGetRoot` needed" was wrong — `screenShow_` does the construction.) Diagnosed by dumping the widget pointers over the console (all `(nil)`) after marker-bisecting the boot task to between "marvin built" and "nav setup".

**Pending:** open/close wiring, the song_list widget inside the dialog, and difficulty/mode button schemes + AA corners. Authoritative model in [`ui_compositor.md`](ui_compositor.md) §0.

### 2026-06-28 — Splash loads from QSPI (host→QSPI provisioning over JTAG) — end-to-end on hardware

The splash now lives in QSPI NOR, not on the SD card — **confirmed booting with no SD inserted**, splash up in **263 ms** (`SPLASH: 4096000 B from QSPI in 263 ms`).

- **Read side (`ui/screens/splash/splash.c`):** `Splash_Load()` dropped the SD/FatFs path (`Storage_Mount` + `SYS_FS_*` + chunked file read) and now does `DRV_SST26_Open` → `DRV_SST26_Read(s_fb, 4,096,000, QSPI_SPLASH_OFFSET)` → `Close`, falling back to the opaque fill only on a flash error. This also removes the SD bring-up (~300 ms) from the splash path entirely — SD still mounts later for catalog/recordings. `DRV_SST26_Read` (CPU) not XDMAC: 263 ms is ~as fast as XDMAC (~225 ms) since the QSPI read dominates and the strongly-ordered framebuffer writes overlap, so XDMAC-for-splash is dropped (~40 ms, not worth it).
- **Provisioning (`openocd/program-qspi.{sh,cfg,md}`, `qspi_console.py`):** host→QSPI over JTAG, mirroring the NAND flow — stage an init-and-stop at91bootstrap (DDR up) + a u-boot SF flasher + the blob into DDR, then drive the u-boot console `sf probe`/`erase`/`write`/verify. `splash` / `assets` / `raw` targets (settings region guarded); `shell` mode stages u-boot only for interactive `sf` poking. The **committed NAND-flasher u-boot already has SF** (`sf probe` detects the SST26) — it's the default `UBOOT_BIN`, so no qspiflash build was needed. Bugs found + fixed on first run: u-boot parses args as **hex** (pass lengths in hex), `sf erase` needs **64 KiB-aligned** length for this part (`ERROR -22` otherwise), and the verify must key on `: OK` / absence of `!=` (terse u-boot output substring-matched `Erased`/`were the same` even on failure). Heartbeat dots added — the multi-MB erase/write is silent for ~10-25 s (expected, one-time).
